**Trap:** Thorium is fertile, not fissile; "huge thorium means huge fuel" is a half-truth.
**Mains hook:** Resource endowment is potential, not available fuel, until the breeder and reprocessing chain works.
**Back:** *"Fissile fissions, fertile breeds."* (✅ owner 04)

### Flashcard SNT-1085 — Thorium is fertile, not fissile (trap)
**Front:** Why is "India has thorium so it has nuclear fuel" a half-truth?
**Definition (learn):** Th-232 is fertile; it becomes fissile U-233 only after neutron capture and beta decay in a reactor.
**Breakdown:**
- Needs a fissile driver (e.g., Pu-239 from Stage 1 spent fuel).
- Needs reprocessing and remote fuel fabrication.
- U-232 contamination makes U-233 gamma-active.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 §6, §7.
**Links:** SNT-1084, SNT-1088, SNT-1110.
**Trap:** Thorium is not a ready-to-use fuel for India's electricity system today.
**Mains hook:** Thorium is a long-horizon strategic objective, not the current mainstream base.
**Back:** *"Thorium breeds, it does not burn yet."* (✅ owner 04)

### Flashcard SNT-1086 — Stage 1: PHWRs on natural uranium
**Front:** What is Stage 1 of India's programme and why is it the practical backbone today?
**Definition (learn):** Pressurised heavy-water reactors run on natural uranium and produce plutonium in spent fuel.
**Breakdown:**
- Heavy water as both moderator and coolant.
- Output: electricity plus plutonium-bearing spent fuel.
- Indigenous 700 MWe PHWR expansion is the current civilian capacity backbone (✅ owner 04 §5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 §5, §8.
**Links:** SNT-1089, SNT-1097, SNT-1113 (700 MWe line), SNT-1087.
**Trap:** Stage 1 is not the thorium stage.
**Mains hook:** Stage 1 is the practical backbone; Stage 2 is the strategic bridge; Stage 3 is the long-term horizon.
**Back:** *"Stage 1: natural uranium, heavy water, plutonium spoils."* (✅ owner 04)

### Flashcard SNT-1087 — Stage 2: Fast Breeder Reactors
**Front:** What is Stage 2, and how does a breeder create fissile material?
**Definition (learn):** Fast breeder reactors use plutonium fuel and breed more fissile material than they consume.
**Breakdown:**
- U-238 blanket captures neutrons and becomes Pu-239.
- Thorium blanket becomes U-233 for Stage 3.
- Doubling time depends on reprocessing throughput as much as physics.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 §3, §5.
**Links:** SNT-1092, SNT-1096, SNT-1111, SNT-1086.
**Trap:** Breeder success is Stage 2 progress, not Stage 3 completion.
**Mains hook:** A breeder claim needs sustained power operation plus reprocessing, not just criticality.
**Back:** *"Stage 2 breeds plutonium from U-238 and thorium from Th-232."* (✅ owner 04)

### Flashcard SNT-1088 — Stage 3: thorium and U-233, with U-232 handling
**Front:** What is Stage 3 and what makes U-233 hard to handle?
**Definition (learn):** The long-term thorium-linked stage using U-233 bred from Th-232.
**Breakdown:**
- U-233 is contaminated with U-232, whose decay chain emits hard gamma radiation.
- Needs remote, shielded fuel fabrication.
- Engineering, not physics, is the main difficulty.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 §3, Advanced §1.
**Links:** SNT-1084, SNT-1085, SNT-1087.
**Trap:** Stage 3 is not complete merely because Stage 2 reached criticality.
**Mains hook:** Thorium matters for India's resource profile (modest uranium, large thorium).
**Back:** *"Stage 3: thorium to U-233, gamma-hot to handle."* (✅ owner 04)

### Flashcard SNT-1089 — PHWR (pressurised heavy-water reactor)
**Front:** What defines a PHWR and why does it fit India's Stage 1?
**Definition (learn):** A reactor using heavy water (D₂O) as both moderator and coolant, typically with natural uranium fuel.
**Breakdown:**
- Heavy water allows natural (unenriched) uranium.
- Core of Stage 1.
- Indigenous 700 MWe line is the practical backbone.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 §2, §5.
**Links:** SNT-1086, SNT-1090, SNT-1091, SNT-1093.
**Trap:** PHWR is not PWR; PHWR uses heavy water and natural uranium.
**Mains hook:** Heavy-water reactors align with India's resource logic.
**Back:** *"PHWR: heavy water, natural uranium, India's workhorse."* (✅ owner 04)

### Flashcard SNT-1090 — BWR (boiling water reactor)
**Front:** What is a BWR and what is India's example?
**Definition (learn):** Water boils inside the reactor vessel and steam goes directly to the turbine.
**Breakdown:**
- Light water as moderator and coolant.
- Direct steam to turbine.
- Example in owner: Tarapur 1-2.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 §2 table.
**Links:** SNT-1089, SNT-1091, SNT-1093.
**Trap:** BWR boils in the vessel; PWR does not.
**Mains hook:** Reactor type affects safety and maintenance design.
**Back:** *"BWR boils in the pot; steam goes straight out."* (✅ owner 04)

### Flashcard SNT-1091 — PWR (pressurised water reactor)
**Front:** What is a PWR and what is India's example?
**Definition (learn):** High-pressure light water transfers heat to a separate steam-generator loop; the reactor vessel does not boil.
**Breakdown:**
- Light water under pressure.
- Separate steam loop.
- Example in owner: Kudankulam VVER units.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 §2; Kudankulam units 1–2 (SNT-1112).
**Links:** SNT-1089, SNT-1090, SNT-1112.
**Trap:** Pressurised water appears in PHWR too; PWR uses light water and a different design.
**Mains hook:** Light-water reactors typically need enriched uranium.
**Back:** *"PWR: pressure keeps the water from boiling."* (✅ owner 04)

### Flashcard SNT-1092 — FBR (fast breeder reactor) design
**Front:** What is a fast breeder reactor's coolant and moderator, and why is sodium used?
**Definition (learn):** Fast neutrons are used; there is no moderator; liquid sodium is the coolant.
**Breakdown:**
- Sodium has excellent heat transfer and barely slows neutrons.
- Sodium reacts violently with water and air; sodium-system engineering is the hard part of Stage 2.
- Central to Stage 2; PFBR at Kalpakkam.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 §2, §6.
**Links:** SNT-1082, SNT-1087, SNT-1096, SNT-1101 (IGCAR).
**Trap:** "Every reactor is moderated" is wrong.
**Mains hook:** Scaling breeders needs sodium handling, fabrication and reprocessing mastery.
**Back:** *"FBR: no moderator, sodium cools, neutrons stay fast."* (✅ owner 04)

### Flashcard SNT-1093 — Reactor-type comparison matrix (PHWR, PWR, BWR, FBR)
**Front:** Set out moderator, coolant, fuel and India example for PHWR, PWR, BWR and FBR.
**Definition (learn):** A single matrix for Prelims.
**Breakdown:**
- PHWR: heavy water moderator + heavy water coolant; natural uranium; RAPP line.
- PWR: light water moderator + coolant under pressure; enriched fuel typical; Kudankulam VVER.
- BWR: light water boils in vessel; enriched fuel typical; Tarapur 1-2.
- FBR: no moderator; sodium coolant; plutonium-based fuel; PFBR.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 §1 table; "enriched fuel typical" is ⚠️ analytical.
**Links:** SNT-1089 to SNT-1092.
**Trap:** Matching by the words "pressurised" or "water" is the common error.
**Mains hook:** Reactor choice reflects resource and fuel-cycle logic.
**Back:** *"Heavy PHWR, light PWR/BWR, sodium FBR."* (✅ owner 04; ⚠️ enrichment note)

### Flashcard SNT-1094 — AHWR (300 MWe thorium-oriented design)
**Front:** What is the AHWR and what is its status?
**Definition (learn):** Advanced Heavy Water Reactor: heavy-water moderated, light-water cooled, thorium-oriented, 300 MWe, designed by BARC.
**Breakdown:**
- Design with supporting test facilities.
- Thorium-oriented concept.
- No AHWR power plant has been built.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 §2, §6 (BARC AHWR page in grounding).
**Links:** SNT-1088, SNT-1100 (BARC), SNT-1095.
**Trap:** AHWR is not an operating plant.
**Mains hook:** Thorium reactor designs remain at R&D and test stage.
**Back:** *"AHWR: 300 MWe thorium design, still on paper and test rigs."* (✅ owner 04)

### Flashcard SNT-1095 — SMRs, BSR and BSMR-200
**Front:** Distinguish the Bharat Small Reactor (BSR) and BSMR-200, and state their status.
**Definition (learn):** BSR = 220 MWe PHWR for captive industrial power; BSMR-200 = indigenous modular pressurised-water reactor line.
**Breakdown:**
- BSR: DAE proposal 20 Mar 2025; NPCIL RFP inviting private finance and build.
- BSMR-200: AEC in-principle approval (11 Mar 2026); Tarapur identified for lead units.
- Neither has been constructed or commissioned.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 §6, §8.
**Links:** SNT-1089, SNT-1091, SNT-1102 (NPCIL).
**Trap:** BSR is a PHWR, not a PWR; "BSR = SMR" conflates two lines.
**Mains hook:** India is hedging between PHWRs, breeders and small modular designs.
**Back:** *"BSR is heavy water for factories; BSMR is light water, still approvals."* (✅ owner 04)

### Flashcard SNT-1096 — PFBR at Kalpakkam: first criticality
**Front:** What is the PFBR, who designed and built it, and when did it reach first criticality?
**Definition (learn):** Prototype Fast Breeder Reactor: a 500 MWe sodium-cooled fast breeder at Kalpakkam.
**Breakdown:**
- Designed by IGCAR; built and to be operated by BHAVINI.
- First criticality at 20:25 on 6 Apr 2026 (DAE).
- Not grid synchronisation and not commercial operation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 6 Apr 2026 (📰 owner 04; DAE release grounding; ⚠️ not fetched).
**Links:** SNT-1087, SNT-1092, SNT-1101, SNT-1102.
**Trap:** PFBR criticality does not mean Stage 2 is operational or Stage 3 complete.
**Mains hook:** Stage 2 is strategically alive; it still needs power operation and reprocessing to prove breeding.
**Back:** *"PFBR: critical on 6 April 2026, not yet on the grid."* (✅ owner 04; ⚠️ time-varying)

### Flashcard SNT-1097 — RAPP-7 first criticality (20 Sep 2024)
**Front:** What did RAPP-7 achieve on 20 Sep 2024?
**Definition (learn):** The indigenous 700 MWe PHWR reached first criticality, the start of a controlled fission chain reaction.
**Breakdown:**
- Indigenous PHWR maturity marker.
- Part of the Stage 1 expansion.
- Criticality is a start point, not commercial operation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 20 Sep 2024 (📰 owner 04; PIB note).
**Links:** SNT-1086, SNT-1113, SNT-1081.
**Trap:** Criticality of one unit is not the whole fleet.
**Mains hook:** Current expansion rests on PHWR maturity.
**Back:** *"RAPP-7: 700 MWe, critical, Sep 2024."* (✅ owner 04)

### Flashcard SNT-1098 — India's operating fleet (24 reactors / 8,780 MW)
**Front:** How many operating reactors does India have and what is the MW total, with its inclusion rule?
**Definition (learn):** A parliamentary reply of 12 Mar 2026 stated 24 operational reactors totalling 8,780 MW.
**Breakdown:**
- Excludes RAPS-1 (100 MW) in long shutdown.
- An NPCIL 2025 corporate profile cites 25 reactors / 8,880 MW because it includes RAPS-1.
- Always quote with date and inclusion rule.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 12 Mar 2026 (📰 owner 04 §8).
**Links:** SNT-1102, SNT-1086.
**Trap:** 24 vs 25 is a scope difference, not a contradiction.
**Mains hook:** Nuclear is a small share of generation; argue it as firm low-carbon capacity complementing renewables.
**Back:** *"24 running, 8,780 MW; RAPS-1 sits out."* (✅ owner 04)

### Flashcard SNT-1099 — DAE (Department of Atomic Energy)
**Front:** What is DAE's role in India's nuclear programme?
**Definition (learn):** The apex governmental department for nuclear policy, research coordination and strategic direction.
**Breakdown:**
- Umbrella for BARC, IGCAR, NPCIL, BHAVINI, NFC, UCIL, IREL and IPR.
- Owner records DAE replies on SHANTI Act non-commencement (see SNT-1107).
- Policy and strategy, not day-to-day operation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 §4.
**Links:** SNT-1100, SNT-1102, SNT-1105, SNT-1107.
**Trap:** DAE is not a company; it is a department.
**Mains hook:** Governance of nuclear power lies under DAE's policy umbrella with regulatory oversight by AERB.
**Back:** *"DAE steers; BARC thinks; NPCIL runs."* (✅ owner 04)

### Flashcard SNT-1100 — BARC (Bhabha Atomic Research Centre)
**Front:** What is BARC's role in India's nuclear programme?
**Definition (learn):** The core R&D institution for reactor technology, fuel cycle, advanced reactor concepts and reprocessing knowledge.
**Breakdown:**
- Designed the AHWR (SNT-1094).
- Participates in the BSMR line.
- Fuel-cycle and reprocessing-linked knowledge.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 §4, §5.
**Links:** SNT-1094, SNT-1095, SNT-1099.
**Trap:** BARC is R&D, not the commercial operator.
**Mains hook:** R&D backbone sustains the Stage 3 thorium horizon.
**Back:** *"BARC: the brain of the fuel cycle."* (✅ owner 04)

### Flashcard SNT-1101 — IGCAR vs BHAVINI (trap pair)
**Front:** Distinguish IGCAR and BHAVINI with respect to the PFBR.
**Definition (learn):** IGCAR (Kalpakkam) is the fast-reactor and sodium-technology design centre; BHAVINI is the DAE company that builds and operates the PFBR.
**Breakdown:**
- IGCAR: design R&D; designed PFBR.
- BHAVINI: project execution and operation.
- Stage 2 implementation runs through BHAVINI.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 §4 and Advanced §5.
**Links:** SNT-1092, SNT-1096, SNT-1099.
**Trap:** Conflating the design centre with the implementing company is a common institutional error.
**Mains hook:** Separate design R&D from execution to avoid vague "DAE built it" statements.
**Back:** *"IGCAR designs, BHAVINI builds and runs."* (✅ owner 04)

### Flashcard SNT-1102 — NPCIL (Nuclear Power Corporation of India Ltd)
**Front:** What does NPCIL do and what is its role in Bharat Small Reactor solicitation?
**Definition (learn):** Operator and developer of commercial nuclear power plants; public-facing utility side of the civilian fleet.
**Breakdown:**
- Operates the civilian reactor fleet.
- Vehicle for the BSR solicitation inviting private industry to finance and build.
- Publishes plant lists and corporate profiles.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 §4.
**Links:** SNT-1098, SNT-1095, SNT-1099.
**Trap:** NPCIL runs commercial reactors; BHAVINI is the PFBR/Stage 2 body.
**Mains hook:** Private participation via NPCIL frameworks depends on the SHANTI Act's commencement.
**Back:** *"NPCIL: the utility that keeps the lights on."* (✅ owner 04)

### Flashcard SNT-1103 — Fuel chain: NFC, UCIL, IREL
**Front:** Name the three DAE fuel-chain bodies and their functions.
**Definition (learn):** The physical supply chain beneath the three-stage narrative.
**Breakdown:**
- NFC (Hyderabad): fabricates fuel assemblies and zirconium components.
- UCIL: uranium mining and milling.
- IREL (India) Ltd: processes beach-sand monazite for thorium and rare earths.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 §4.
**Links:** SNT-1080, SNT-1084, SNT-1110.
**Trap:** Mining is not milling and not fuel fabrication; monazite gives thorium.
**Mains hook:** Thorium's physical basis is monazite, which links nuclear and critical-minerals policy.
**Back:** *"Mine (UCIL), process (IREL), fabricate (NFC)."* (✅ owner 04)

### Flashcard SNT-1104 — AERB and regulatory independence
**Front:** What is AERB's role, and why is its independence contested?
**Definition (learn):** Atomic Energy Regulatory Board: the safety regulator for siting, design, operation review and corrective oversight.
**Breakdown:**
- Technical safety regulator.
- Not a statutorily independent authority; works under the same governmental umbrella that promotes nuclear power.
- CAG and others have criticised it; SHANTI Act deems AERB constituted under the Act (⚠️ owner Advanced §5/§9).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 §4 (contest from owner analysis).
**Links:** SNT-1099, SNT-1105, SNT-1106, SNT-1107.
**Trap:** AERB is the safety regulator, not the liability law and not an independent statutory regulator.
**Mains hook:** Regulatory independence is a standing governance reform demand for nuclear power.
**Back:** *"AERB checks safety, not compensation, and is not independent by statute."* (✅ owner 04)

### Flashcard SNT-1105 — Atomic Energy Act, 1962
**Front:** What does the Atomic Energy Act, 1962 provide, and when does it cease to apply?
**Definition (learn):** The statute creating the state monopoly over atomic energy; operative until the SHANTI Act commences.
**Breakdown:**
- State monopoly over atomic energy.
- Repealed on commencement of the SHANTI Act, 2025 (s.91).
- Still in force as of owner's verified position.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 §4 (India Code reference in grounding).
**Links:** SNT-1106, SNT-1107, SNT-1099.
**Trap:** The 1962 Act is still in force until the 2025 Act commences.
**Mains hook:** A statutory monopoly and a licensing regime are different legal architectures.
**Back:** *"1962: the State owns the atom."* (✅ owner 04)

### Flashcard SNT-1106 — CLND Act, 2010 and Section 17(b)
**Front:** What does the Civil Liability for Nuclear Damage Act, 2010 provide, and what is the s.17(b) controversy?
**Definition (learn):** Operator-channelled liability with a statutory cap, but s.17(b) preserves a right of recourse against suppliers.
**Breakdown:**
- Liability channelled to the operator.
- s.17(b): supplier recourse; the provision international vendors most object to.
- Global practice channels liability to the operator only.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 §4; Advanced §3. Act year 2010 per owner.
**Links:** SNT-1105, SNT-1107, SNT-1109.
**Trap:** Liability law is not the same as safety regulation.
**Mains hook:** Reform of supplier recourse trades investment against supplier accountability.
**Back:** *"CLND 2010: operator pays, but suppliers can be sued."* (✅ owner 04)

### Flashcard SNT-1107 — SHANTI Act, 2025: enacted, not commenced
**Front:** What does the SHANTI Act, 2025 provide, and what is its commencement status?
**Definition (learn):** Sustainable Harnessing and Advancement of Nuclear Energy for Transforming India Act, 2025 (Act 39 of 2025): assent 20 Dec 2025, published 21 Dec 2025.
**Breakdown:**
- Licensing for government entities, government companies, other companies and qualifying JVs (s.3(1)).
- Reserves enrichment, reprocessing/HLW management and heavy-water production to government bodies (s.3(5)).
- Per-incident liability cap 300 million SDR equivalent (s.13); graded operator caps ₹100–3,000 crore (Second Schedule).
- Repeals the Atomic Energy Act 1962 and CLND Act 2010 on commencement (s.91).
- DAE reply 12 Feb 2026: rules and timelines not yet notified.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 20 Dec 2025 assent (📰 owner 04 §8, Advanced §5; SHANTI text and PIB reference in grounding; ⚠️ not fetched). Commencement status after Feb 2026 not verified here.
**Links:** SNT-1104, SNT-1105, SNT-1106, SNT-1108, SNT-1110.
**Trap:** "SHANTI has replaced the Atomic Energy Act" is wrong until commencement.
**Mains hook:** Act is not rules, not commencement and not private investment; three separate events.
**Back:** *"Act 39 of 2025: passed, not yet in force."* (📰 owner 04; ⚠️ time-sensitive)

### Flashcard SNT-1108 — Indo-US 123 Agreement, NSG waiver and NPT status
**Front:** What diplomatic framework enabled civil nuclear commerce with India, and what is India's NPT status?
**Definition (learn):** The Indo-US 123 Agreement (bilateral), the 2008 NSG waiver, and India's status as a non-NPT party.
**Breakdown:**
- 123 Agreement: bilateral civil nuclear cooperation.
- NSG waiver (2008) enabled civil commerce without NPT membership.
- India is not a party to the NPT; the waiver did not change that.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 §4; MEA FAQs (2008) in grounding.
**Links:** SNT-1109, SNT-1107.
**Trap:** NSG waiver did not make India an NPT signatory.
**Mains hook:** Diplomacy widened fuel and reactor options; it did not replace the indigenous fuel-cycle strategy.
**Back:** *"123 and NSG: commerce without NPT."* (✅ owner 04)

### Flashcard SNT-1109 — IAEA safeguards and civil–strategic separation
**Front:** How do IAEA safeguards apply to India's facilities, and what is the civil–strategic separation?
**Definition (learn):** Safeguards verify declared material is not diverted; India designates some facilities civilian and under safeguards, while keeping others outside.
**Breakdown:**
- Safeguards apply to designated civilian facilities, not automatically to all Indian reactors.
- Separation plan of 2008 allowed NSG-waiver commerce.
- Constrains which reactors can use imported fuel.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 Advanced §3, PYQ routing (2020 GS-I Q55; key not held locally).
**Links:** SNT-1108, SNT-1106.
**Trap:** Not every Indian reactor is under the same IAEA regime.
**Mains hook:** Safeguards preserve transparency while protecting strategic autonomy.
**Back:** *"Civil reactors watched, strategic ones not."* (✅ owner 04; ⚠️ answer not inferred)

### Flashcard SNT-1110 — Enrichment and reprocessing (concepts)
**Front:** What do enrichment and reprocessing mean, and why are they strategic?
**Definition (learn):** Enrichment raises the proportion of fissile U-235; reprocessing recovers fissile material from spent fuel.
**Breakdown:**
- Enrichment is a fuel-cycle property, not a synonym for power generation.
- Reprocessing has civilian and proliferation dimensions.
- SHANTI reserves enrichment and reprocessing to the State (s.3(5)).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 Advanced §3, §5 and PYQ routing (2023 GS-I Q11).
**Links:** SNT-1111, SNT-1107, SNT-1103.
**Trap:** Do not guess enrichment percentages from Prelims statements without context.
**Mains hook:** State control of the back end of the fuel cycle matters for private operators.
**Back:** *"Enrich adds U-235; reprocess recovers plutonium and uranium."* (✅ owner 04; ⚠️ percentages not given)

### Flashcard SNT-1111 — Closed fuel cycle and doubling time
**Front:** What is the closed fuel cycle and what determines how fast a breeder can scale?
**Definition (learn):** A cycle that reprocesses and reuses fissile material; doubling time is how long a breeder takes to produce surplus fissile material for a second reactor.
**Breakdown:**
- Bottlenecks: reprocessing throughput, sodium handling, remote fuel fabrication for U-233.
- Doubling time depends on reprocessing as much as reactor physics.
- ⚠️ Doubling time concept from owner Advanced §3.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 Basic §2–3.
**Links:** SNT-1087, SNT-1088, SNT-1110.
**Trap:** A breeder that is critical is not a closed cycle.
**Mains hook:** Scaling breeders depends on the closed cycle's engineering.
**Back:** *"Close the loop, double the fuel."* (✅ owner 04; ⚠️ concept)

### Flashcard SNT-1112 — Kudankulam VVER units
**Front:** What is the status of Kudankulam units 1–6?
**Definition (learn):** Russian VVER (PWR-type) reactors at Kudankulam.
**Breakdown:**
- Units 1–2: commercial operation.
- Units 3–6: under construction (owner).
- Example of PWR in India.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 §6 (NPCIL overview in grounding).
**Links:** SNT-1091, SNT-1098.
**Trap:** Kudankulam is a PWR, not a PHWR.
**Mains hook:** Foreign-technology reactors sit alongside indigenous PHWRs in India's mix.
**Back:** *"Kudankulam: Russian pressure water, 1–2 running."* (✅ owner 04)

### Flashcard SNT-1113 — Indigenous 700 MWe PHWR line
**Front:** What is the indigenous 700 MWe PHWR line and what did the DAE 2024 review record?
**Definition (learn):** A standardised PHWR series of 700 MWe units forming India's expansion backbone.
**Breakdown:**
- Kakrapar's first two indigenous 700 MWe units in commercial operation (DAE review 24 Dec 2024).
- RAPP-7 first criticality 20 Sep 2024 (SNT-1097).
- Core of current civilian capacity growth.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 24 Dec 2024 (📰 owner 04 §8).
**Links:** SNT-1086, SNT-1097, SNT-1089.
**Trap:** Indigenous 700 MWe is PHWR, not PWR or fast breeder.
**Mains hook:** Nuclear capacity growth rests on PHWR maturity.
**Back:** *"700 MWe PHWR: Kakrapar proved, RAPP follows."* (✅ owner 04)

### GROUP — Nuclear Fusion and ITER

### Flashcard SNT-1114 — Fusion is experimental: no electricity from ITER
**Front:** What is the status of fusion power, and does ITER generate electricity?
**Definition (learn):** Fusion is an experimental, research-stage energy frontier; ITER is a feasibility facility with no turbine and no electricity-generating equipment.
**Breakdown:**
- No operational commercial fusion power plant exists anywhere (owner).
- ITER targets fusion thermal power, not grid electricity.
- Later DEMO-class plants are the next step.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 §2, §6; ✅ Wikipedia (ITER power targets are thermal).
**Links:** SNT-1115, SNT-1125, SNT-1126, SNT-1137.
**Trap:** "ITER will feed the grid soon" is wrong.
**Mains hook:** Argue that fusion matters now as science, engineering and industrial capability, not as grid supply.
**Back:** *"ITER: heat without a turbine."* (✅ owner 05)

### Flashcard SNT-1115 — Fusion vs fission contrast
**Front:** Contrast fusion and fission in process, present use and key device.
**Definition (learn):** Fusion combines light nuclei; fission splits heavy nuclei.
**Breakdown:**
- Fusion: experimental/research stage; tokamak or inertial devices.
- Fission: commercially used in nuclear power plants; reactor core.
- India's syllabus link: ITER, IPR, SST-1, Aditya-U versus the three-stage programme.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 §1 table.
**Links:** SNT-1081, SNT-1114, SNT-1116.
**Trap:** Fusion and fission are not interchangeable in answers.
**Mains hook:** Draw the fusion–fission line sharply before discussing plasma confinement.
**Back:** *"Fusion joins light, fission splits heavy."* (✅ owner 05)

### Flashcard SNT-1116 — The D-T reaction and its energy split
**Front:** What is the D-T fusion reaction and how is its energy shared?
**Definition (learn):** Deuterium + tritium -> helium-4 + neutron + 17.6 MeV.
**Breakdown:**
- 14.1 MeV neutron carries about 80% of the energy and heats the blanket (and damages walls).
- 3.5 MeV helium (alpha) stays in the plasma and heats it: alpha self-heating.
- Burning plasma: when alpha heating sustains most of the heating (⚠️ owner 05 Advanced §3).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 17.6 MeV, 14.1 MeV, 3.5 MeV (✅ owner 05 §2 and standard D-T reaction; ⚠️ not fetched).
**Links:** SNT-1117, SNT-1123, SNT-1124.
**Trap:** The neutron, not the alpha, carries most energy out of the plasma.
**Mains hook:** Neutron damage and tritium breeding are the consequences of the D-T choice.
**Back:** *"D-T: 17.6 MeV, neutrons take 80%, alphas stay and heat."* (✅ owner 05; ⚠️ MeV values standard)

### Flashcard SNT-1117 — Why D-T: deuterium abundant, tritium bred
**Front:** Why is D-T the first-generation fuel, and why must tritium be bred?
**Definition (learn):** D-T has the highest cross-section at the lowest attainable temperature (~150 million °C class).
**Breakdown:**
- Deuterium abundant in seawater.
- Tritium is radioactive with ~12.3-year half-life; not available naturally in useful quantity.
- Breeding from lithium blanket is required.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 §2; 12.3-year half-life (⚠️ standard).
**Links:** SNT-1116, SNT-1123.
**Trap:** Deuterium and tritium are not equally available.
**Mains hook:** Fuel self-sufficiency is the defining requirement of a D-T power plant.
**Back:** *"Deuterium from sea, tritium from lithium."* (✅ owner 05)

### Flashcard SNT-1118 — Lawson criterion (n·T·τ_E)
**Front:** What is the Lawson criterion and why does it make fusion hard?
**Definition (learn):** Net energy requires plasma density × temperature × energy-confinement time to exceed a threshold.
**Breakdown:**
- You can raise one factor easily, but not all three at once.
- Machine size, field strength and stability enter through τ_E.
- Triple product explains the central challenge: confinement.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 §2 (⚠️ analytical concept).
**Links:** SNT-1119, SNT-1120, SNT-1121.
**Trap:** Temperature alone is not the test.
**Mains hook:** Confinement, not temperature, is the crux; plasma stability is the engineering challenge.
**Back:** *"Density, temperature, time: all three or none."* (⚠️ owner 05)

### Flashcard SNT-1119 — Fusion gain Q and ITER's target
**Front:** What is Q, what is ITER's target, and what are the common errors?
**Definition (learn):** Q = fusion power / injected heating power; ITER targets Q ≥ 10 (500 MW fusion from 50 MW heating) per owner.
**Breakdown:**
- Q > 1 = scientific breakeven.
- Engineering breakeven (net electricity after plant consumption) is a higher bar.
- ⚠️ Wikipedia gives Q = 10 for brief periods and Q = 5 in steady state.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 §6 (Q ≥ 10 target); ⚠️ Wikipedia (Q = 10 brief, Q = 5 steady state) — conflict flagged, not resolved.
**Links:** SNT-1118, SNT-1125, SNT-1126.
**Trap:** Q = 10 does not mean ten times the electricity consumed.
**Mains hook:** Separate plasma gain from net plant electricity in any claim about fusion economics.
**Back:** *"Q is fusion over heating, not over the plug."* (✅ owner 05; ⚠️ Q conflict)

### Flashcard SNT-1120 — Tokamak (magnetic confinement)
**Front:** What is a tokamak and how does it confine plasma?
**Definition (learn):** A toroidal magnetic-confinement device that holds plasma away from walls with strong magnetic fields.
**Breakdown:**
- Plasma is ultra-hot; cannot touch walls.
- ITER and India's SST-1 and Aditya-U are tokamaks.
- Induced plasma current makes operation inherently pulsed.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 §2, §3.
**Links:** SNT-1121, SNT-1122, SNT-1132, SNT-1133.
**Trap:** Tokamak is one magnetic approach, not "any fusion".
**Mains hook:** Pulsed operation is why steady-state research matters.
**Back:** *"Tokamak: doughnut of magnets holding fire."* (✅ owner 05)

### Flashcard SNT-1121 — Stellarator (trap)
**Front:** How does a stellarator differ from a tokamak?
**Definition (learn):** Confinement achieved with twisted external coils, not a large induced plasma current.
**Breakdown:**
- Harder to build, easier to run steadily.
- Strategically interesting for steady-state operation.
- Not the same as a tokamak.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 §2; Advanced §3.
**Links:** SNT-1120, SNT-1122.
**Trap:** Do not call every magnetic device a tokamak.
**Mains hook:** Steady operation is a route to practical power, but design trade-offs differ.
**Back:** *"Stellarator: twisted coils, no plasma current."* (✅ owner 05)

### Flashcard SNT-1122 — Magnetic vs inertial confinement
**Front:** Distinguish magnetic and inertial confinement.
**Definition (learn):** Magnetic: hold plasma with magnetic fields (tokamak, stellarator). Inertial: compress a tiny fuel pellet rapidly with lasers or beams.
**Breakdown:**
- Magnetic: long confinement.
- Inertial: very short fusion burst.
- ITER uses magnetic confinement, not inertial.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 §2, §3, §6.
**Links:** SNT-1120, SNT-1121, SNT-1125.
**Trap:** ITER does not follow inertial confinement.
**Mains hook:** Choice of confinement shapes machine size, duty cycle and engineering.
**Back:** *"Magnetic holds, inertial bursts."* (✅ owner 05)

### Flashcard SNT-1123 — Tritium breeding and the lithium blanket
**Front:** How is tritium bred in a fusion reactor, and why is tritium breeding ratio above one essential?
**Definition (learn):** Fusion neutrons strike a lithium-bearing blanket: ⁶Li + n -> tritium + helium.
**Breakdown:**
- TBR = tritium bred per tritium consumed; must exceed 1 with margin for decay and losses.
- Test blanket modules on ITER validate breeding.
- Without breeding, a D-T plant cannot fuel itself.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 §2, §3, §6; Advanced §3.
**Links:** SNT-1116, SNT-1117, SNT-1124.
**Trap:** Deuterium is abundant but tritium is not; breeding is not optional.
**Mains hook:** Self-sufficiency in tritium is a precondition for commercial D-T plants.
**Back:** *"Lithium feeds neutrons, neutrons feed tritium, TBR above one."* (✅ owner 05)

### Flashcard SNT-1124 — Neutron damage, activation and the divertor
**Front:** What are neutron damage, activation and the divertor, and why do they matter?
**Definition (learn):** 14.1 MeV neutrons displace atoms and transmute steel (swelling, embrittlement, activation); the divertor exhausts helium ash and heat at the plasma edge.
**Breakdown:**
- Reduced-activation ferritic-martensitic steels and tungsten armour are candidate materials (⚠️ owner 05 Advanced).
- A high-flux neutron source is needed to validate materials for DEMO.
- Divertor faces some of the highest sustained heat fluxes in any engineered system.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 §2; ⚠️ material names analytical.
**Links:** SNT-1116, SNT-1123, SNT-1135.
**Trap:** Fusion is not radiation-free; activation is real.
**Mains hook:** Materials resilience is the first-order engineering constraint for any future plant.
**Back:** *"Neutrons wound the walls; the divertor bears the heat."* (⚠️ owner 05)

### Flashcard SNT-1125 — ITER: members, site and design target
**Front:** Name ITER's members, its site, and its design target.
**Definition (learn):** ITER is the flagship experimental tokamak project at Saint-Paul-lez-Durance, France.
**Breakdown:**
- Members: China, European Union, India, Japan, Russia, South Korea, United States (seven parties) (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 05).
- Target: 500 MW fusion from 50 MW injected heating (Q ≥ 10 per owner; ⚠️ Wikipedia Q = 10 brief, Q = 5 steady state).
- The UK left via Fusion for Energy in 2023 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 §4; ✅ Wikipedia.
**Links:** SNT-1114, SNT-1119, SNT-1126, SNT-1128.
**Trap:** ITER is not a power plant; the UK is not a current member (✅ Wikipedia).
**Mains hook:** ITER is the anchor of global experimental fusion; India's role is in kind.
**Back:** *"Seven flags, Cadarache, 500 MW of heat."* (✅ owner 05; ✅ Wikipedia)

### Flashcard SNT-1126 — ITER 2024 new baseline
**Front:** What dates did the July 2024 ITER baseline set?
**Definition (learn):** The new baseline replaced the "First Plasma" framing with staged operational milestones.
**Breakdown:**
- Start of Research Operations: 2034.
- Full magnetic energy: 2036.
- Deuterium-tritium operation: 2039.
- Wikipedia adds deuterium-deuterium operation in 2035 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 8 Jul 2024 adoption (✅ owner 05 §6, §8; ✅ Wikipedia for 2034/2035/2039).
**Links:** SNT-1125, SNT-1127, SNT-1137.
**Trap:** Quote these as scheduled targets of an under-construction project; "first plasma" is no longer the headline milestone.
**Mains hook:** Schedule realism dates any "implications of success" answer to the second half of the century.
**Back:** *"2034 research, 2036 magnetic energy, 2039 D-T."* (✅ owner 05; ✅ Wikipedia)

### Flashcard SNT-1127 — ITER Council meeting 36 (19 Jun 2025)
**Front:** What did the ITER Council record in June 2025?
**Definition (learn):** The ITER Council welcomed ahead-of-schedule sector-module installation and completion of all major magnets.
**Breakdown:**
- Still an experimental facility under construction.
- Progress shows execution, not commercial readiness.
- Owner: Council Meeting 36, 19 Jun 2025.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 19 Jun 2025 (📰 owner 05 §8).
**Links:** SNT-1126, SNT-1125.
**Trap:** Construction progress is not operational fusion.
**Mains hook:** Use progress with honesty about schedule and status.
**Back:** *"Council 36: magnets done, still building."* (📰 owner 05)

### Flashcard SNT-1128 — ITER-India vs IPR (trap pair)
**Front:** Distinguish IPR, Gandhinagar from ITER-India.
**Definition (learn):** IPR is a DAE-aided plasma research institute; ITER-India is the Domestic Agency for ITER housed as a dedicated project within IPR.
**Breakdown:**
- IPR: research institute; operates ADITYA-U and SST-1.
- ITER-India: contracts Indian industry, manages delivery to ITER Organization.
- ⚠️ "IPR / ITER-India" as one entity blurs research and procurement.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 §4 and Advanced §5.
**Links:** SNT-1129, SNT-1130, SNT-1125.
**Trap:** Do not use the two names interchangeably.
**Mains hook:** Institutional clarity is a UPSC-penalised distinction.
**Back:** *"IPR researches, ITER-India delivers the parts."* (✅ owner 05)

### Flashcard SNT-1129 — Institute for Plasma Research (IPR), Gandhinagar
**Front:** What is IPR and which devices does it operate?
**Definition (learn):** An aided institute of the Department of Atomic Energy and India's principal plasma and fusion research institution.
**Breakdown:**
- Operates ADITYA-U and SST-1.
- Hosts ITER-India as its Domestic Agency project.
- Gandhinagar location (✅ owner 05).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 §4.
**Links:** SNT-1128, SNT-1132, SNT-1133.
**Trap:** IPR is not a power reactor operator.
**Mains hook:** Domestic plasma capability sits in IPR, complementing ITER participation.
**Back:** *"IPR: plasma home in Gandhinagar, DAE-aided."* (✅ owner 05)

### Flashcard SNT-1130 — India's nine in-kind packages for ITER
**Front:** List India's nine in-kind ITER procurement packages.
**Definition (learn):** India contributes largely in kind (manufactured hardware).
**Breakdown:**
- Cryostat; in-wall shielding; cooling-water system; cryogenic system.
- Ion-cyclotron RF heating; electron-cyclotron RF heating; diagnostic neutral beam.
- Power supplies; a set of diagnostics.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 §4, §6.
**Links:** SNT-1131, SNT-1128, SNT-1136.
**Trap:** In-kind is manufacture, not only cash.
**Mains hook:** In-kind contributions build domestic capability but tie India to multi-party schedules.
**Back:** *"Nine packages: cryo, shield, cool, RF, beam, power, diagnose."* (✅ owner 05)

### Flashcard SNT-1131 — The cryostat
**Front:** What is the cryostat, and why is it the most quotable Indian ITER contribution?
**Definition (learn):** The large high-vacuum pressure chamber around the ITER machine, one of India's nine in-kind packages.
**Breakdown:**
- Owner calls it the largest stainless-steel high-vacuum pressure chamber ever built (⚠️ owner claim, not verified).
- Largest of India's procurement packages (ITER, 17 Feb 2025).
- Built by Indian industry.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 17 Feb 2025 (📰 owner 05 §8).
**Links:** SNT-1130, SNT-1128.
**Trap:** "Largest ever built" is an owner claim; quote with attribution.
**Mains hook:** Cryostat shows Indian industry at nuclear-grade tolerances.
**Back:** *"Cryostat: India's giant vacuum vessel."* (⚠️ owner claim)

### Flashcard SNT-1132 — SST-1: steady-state superconducting tokamak
**Front:** What is SST-1 and what is its purpose?
**Definition (learn):** India's Steady State Superconducting Tokamak-1 at IPR, for long-pulse plasma research.
**Breakdown:**
- Superconducting magnets; steady-state-relevant operation.
- Research device, not a power reactor.
- Hands-on plasma experience for Indian labs.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 §2, §4; IPR page in grounding.
**Links:** SNT-1120, SNT-1129, SNT-1133.
**Trap:** SST-1 is not an Aditya-U upgrade; they are different platforms.
**Mains hook:** Steady-state research is strategically important for practical tokamak operation.
**Back:** *"SST-1: long-pulse, superconducting, research only."* (✅ owner 05)

### Flashcard SNT-1133 — Aditya and Aditya-U
**Front:** What are Aditya and Aditya-U, and what work does Aditya-U describe?
**Definition (learn):** Aditya was India's earlier tokamak; Aditya-U is its upgraded form operated by IPR.
**Breakdown:**
- Aditya began India's tokamak journey (✅ owner 05 Must-Know).
- Aditya-U: deuterium-plasma, divertor and shaped-plasma campaigns (IPR).
- Research platform, not a power reactor.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 §4, §8; IPR Aditya-U page in grounding.
**Links:** SNT-1120, SNT-1129, SNT-1132.
**Trap:** Aditya (Sun-observing) and Aditya-L1 are different missions; do not conflate with Aditya tokamak.
**Mains hook:** Domestic tokamak experience builds sovereignty in plasma physics.
**Back:** *"Aditya-U: tokamak upgrade, not Aditya-L1."* (✅ owner 05)

### Flashcard SNT-1134 — Fusion vs Aditya-L1 name collision (trap)
**Front:** Why must "Aditya" be disambiguated between the tokamak and the solar mission?
**Definition (learn):** Aditya-U is a fusion tokamak at IPR; Aditya-L1 is a solar observatory at Sun-Earth L1 (SNT-1062).
**Breakdown:**
- Aditya-U: plasma physics; deuterium plasma.
- Aditya-L1: solar observation from a halo orbit.
- Both Indian; different purposes.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 and owner 03.
**Links:** SNT-1133, SNT-1062.
**Trap:** Name-based confusion in Prelims.
**Mains hook:** Use exact names in answers.
**Back:** *"Aditya-U burns plasma; Aditya-L1 watches the Sun."* (✅ owner 05 / owner 03)

### Flashcard SNT-1135 — Fusion waste profile and radiation
**Front:** What radioactive waste and hazards does fusion produce, and what does it avoid?
**Definition (learn):** Fusion avoids long-lived fission-product high-level waste and runaway chain reaction, but neutron activation makes structures radioactive and tritium is a radioactive gas.
**Breakdown:**
- No long-lived HLW.
- Loss of confinement stops the reaction; no meltdown-style runaway.
- Neutron activation of structures; tritium containment needed.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 §6.
**Links:** SNT-1124, SNT-1123, SNT-1114.
**Trap:** Fusion is not radiation-free or waste-free.
**Mains hook:** Be precise: "safer waste profile" is not "no waste".
**Back:** *"No long-lived HLW, but activated steel and tritium."* (✅ owner 05)

### Flashcard SNT-1136 — 2025 GS-III PYQ: India's ITER contribution (Q5, 150 words)
**Front:** How should a 150-word answer on India's ITER contribution and the implications of success be structured? (2025 GS-III Q5)
**Definition (learn):** Exact PYQ asked for the evolution of fusion, India's ITER contribution and implications for global energy.
**Breakdown:**
- Why fusion matters: D-T, deuterium abundance, no long-lived HLW.
- Evolution: Aditya -> SST-1 -> ITER membership -> ITER-India within IPR.
- Name packages and note industrial manufacture; close with implications conditional on tritium breeding, materials and cost.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 GS-III Q5 (10 marks, 150 words) per owner routing.
**Links:** SNT-1130, SNT-1126, SNT-1137.
**Trap:** Claiming ITER will generate electricity; giving no named Indian package; asserting a commercial fusion date.
**Mains hook:** Close with the honest status: implications belong to the second half of the century.
**Back:** *"Name the packages, date the power, qualify the promise."* (✅ owner 05 PYQ routing; ⚠️ answer skeleton is owner analytic)

### Flashcard SNT-1137 — DEMO, post-ITER path and private-fusion hype
**Front:** What comes after ITER, and how should private fusion ventures be treated?
**Definition (learn):** DEMO-class plants are the next step after ITER; private ventures change funding more than technical readiness.
**Breakdown:**
- DEMO: first machine intended to put fusion electricity on a grid.
- Prerequisites: validated tritium-breeding blanket, neutron-materials test facility.
- Private compact-tokamak ventures claim faster timelines; none has shown a burning plasma or closed tritium cycle (⚠️ owner 05 Advanced §5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 §8, Advanced §5; private-venture claim ⚠️ analytical, not fetched.
**Links:** SNT-1114, SNT-1126, SNT-1135.
**Trap:** A venture's timeline claim is not a plant.
**Mains hook:** Funding landscape versus technical readiness: separate them.
**Back:** *"DEMO next, hype later, tritium first."* (✅ owner 05; ⚠️ private-venture claim)

### Flashcard SNT-1138 — In-kind model: double edge
**Front:** What are the strengths and weaknesses of the in-kind contribution model for India's ITER participation?
**Definition (learn):** Members supply components rather than only cash; this builds domestic capability but couples India to a multi-party schedule.
**Breakdown:**
- Strength: Indian firms build first-of-kind nuclear-grade hardware.
- Weakness: delivery failure by one party delays all.
- 2024 re-baselining is the practical illustration.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 Advanced §5 (2024 re-baseline per SNT-1126).
**Links:** SNT-1130, SNT-1126, SNT-1128.
**Trap:** "In-kind = symbolic" is wrong; the value is industrial capability.
**Mains hook:** Convert participation into durable technological gain by building industry depth.
**Back:** *"In-kind builds Indian hands and binds Indian timelines."* (✅ owner 05; ⚠️ analytical)

## SCIENCE AND TECHNOLOGY — Part 2: Defence R&D, Digital India, AI and Quantum

### GROUP — Defence R&D, DRDO and Missile Systems (basic 06 + advanced 06)

### Flashcard SNT-2001 — Core theme: development is not induction
**Front:** In defence technology, what is the difference between a successful DRDO test and a weapon being in service?
**Definition (learn):** DRDO develops and tests; the armed services set requirements, run user trials and take the induction decision; production is handled by DPSUs or private/JV partners. A test is one rung on a longer ladder.
**Breakdown:**
- Requirement (Services) → design, subsystems and integration (DRDO labs/clusters) → developmental trials → user evaluation → induction decision (Services) → production (DPSU/industry) → deployment.
- DRDO "facilitates" production and induction; it cannot induct its own product.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Chain and roles ✅ (owner 06, DRDO About page, verified 16 Jul 2026 per owner). Exact rung vocabulary used in official material is in SNT-2018.
**Links:** SNT-2002, SNT-2003, SNT-2018, SNT-2035 (DAP 2020), SNT-2043 (AoN), SNT-2055.
**Trap:** Treating "DRDO developed it" as "the army has it." Development/testing belongs to Topic 06; procurement/manufacture belongs to Topic 07.
**Mains hook:** Use "requirement → R&D → trials → induction → production" to show that strategic autonomy depends on converting test success into reproducible production; close with a qualified conclusion that R&D is necessary but not sufficient.
**Back:** *"Build, test, trial, induct, produce — DRDO owns only the first two."*

### Flashcard SNT-2002 — DRDO (Defence Research and Development Organisation)
**Front:** What is DRDO, when was it formed, and what is its role?
**Definition (learn):** The R&D wing of the Ministry of Defence (under the Department of Defence R&D) that designs and develops defence systems and facilitates their production and induction.
**Breakdown:**
- Formed 1958 by amalgamation of TDEs, DTDP and DSO (✅ owner 06, DRDO About page).
- Works through about 41 laboratories and 05 DRDO Young Scientist Laboratories (✅ owner 06; count is as stated on DRDO site, verified 16 Jul 2026 — re-check later).
- Parent: Department of Defence R&D, Ministry of Defence (✅ owner 06).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Formed 1958 (✅ owner 06). Labs: ~41 + 05 YSLs (✅ owner 06, as-of 16 Jul 2026).
**Links:** SNT-2001, SNT-2003, SNT-2004, SNT-2025 (DIA-CoE).
**Trap:** DRDO is not the production agency (that is DDP and DPSUs), and not the procurement agency (that is the Services under DAC approval).
**Mains hook:** Present DRDO as the R&D and testing backbone, then show where its mandate stops (production, induction) and who takes over.
**Back:** *"Born 1958: designs, tests, facilitates — never buys."*

### Flashcard SNT-2003 — DRDO vs Department of Defence Production (R&D–production split)
**Front:** Why must DRDO and the Department of Defence Production (DDP) never be confused?
**Definition (learn):** DRDO = technology creation; DDP = production infrastructure, DPSUs and manufacturing indigenisation. The R&D-production split is the institutional fact most often missed in answers.
**Breakdown:**
- DRDO: design, development, testing (Topic 06).
- DDP, MoD: DPSUs, production infrastructure, indigenisation of manufacture (Topic 07).
- Defence Acquisition Council (DAC) sits above both for approvals (SNT-2044).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 06 and owner 07.
**Links:** SNT-2002, SNT-2033, SNT-2047 (DPSUs), SNT-2044 (DAC).
**Trap:** "Indian defence R&D is done by DDP" — wrong; DRDO does R&D; DDP handles production policy and DPSUs.
**Mains hook:** Show that a missile programme's real test of self-reliance lies in the handoff from DRDO to DPSU/private production; end with the caveat that the handoff is where many indigenisation efforts stall.
**Back:** *"DRDO designs the missile; DDP builds the factory."*

### Flashcard SNT-2004 — MSS cluster and its laboratories
**Front:** Which DRDO cluster handles missiles and strategic systems, and which labs does it name?
**Definition (learn):** The Missiles and Strategic Systems (MSS) cluster is DRDO's official cluster for missile and strategic-system technologies for deterrence and defence.
**Breakdown:**
- Named core labs: DRDL, RCI, ASL, TBRL, ITR (✅ owner 06, MSS cluster page).
- Technology areas: aerodynamics, airframe design, solid/liquid/ramjet/scramjet propulsion, guidance, homing, launch systems and command-and-control (✅ owner 06 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** DRDL = Defence Research & Development Laboratory (Hyderabad); ITR = Integrated Test Range (Chandipur, Odisha). ⚠️ Lab city names from general knowledge; confirm on DRDO site before quoting.
**Links:** SNT-2002, SNT-2013 (Pinaka tested from ITR), SNT-2008 (scramjet).
**Trap:** Naming DRDO labs as if they were production factories; MSS labs are R&D and test establishments.
**Mains hook:** Use the cluster to show that missile capability is a bundle of propulsion, seekers, guidance and test infrastructure, not a single "programme."
**Back:** *"Dr. Rama's RCI, ASL and ITR — DRDL leads the missile team."*

### Flashcard SNT-2005 — IGMDP (Integrated Guided Missile Development Programme)
**Front:** What was IGMDP, which systems did it cover, and when did it end?
**Definition (learn):** The umbrella programme that developed the first indigenous family of guided missiles and their subsystem base.
**Breakdown:**
- Systems: Prithvi, Trishul, Akash, Nag and the Agni technology demonstrator (✅ owner 06).
- Trishul was closed without induction (✅ owner 06 basic says so).
- Start year: 1983 (owner) — Wikipedia says 1982–83 (✅ Wikipedia, fetched).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner: 1983 to March 2012 (✅ owner 06, citing PIB relid=85986). **Conflict:** the fetched Wikipedia article says DRDO announced completion on 8 Jan 2008, and Trishul's project was officially shut on 27 Feb 2008. ⚠️ Owner-vs-source conflict on the end date — re-check PIB before quoting "2012".
**Links:** SNT-2015 (Prithvi/Trishul), SNT-2016 (Nag), SNT-2011 (Akash), SNT-2009 (Agni-5 is outside IGMDP).
**Trap:** "All Indian missiles came from IGMDP." Wrong: BrahMos, Astra, Agni-5, QRSAM, MRSAM and the hypersonic programmes lie outside it.
**Mains hook:** IGMDP built the subsystem base (propulsion, seekers, guidance, materials) on which later non-IGMDP systems became possible; say that and flag the end-date conflict if pressed.
**Back:** *"Five sanctioned, one shut, the rest took its base: Prithvi, Trishul, Akash, Nag, Agni-demo."*

### Flashcard SNT-2006 — Ballistic missile
**Front:** What defines a ballistic missile?
**Definition (learn):** A missile whose main flight path after powered boost is largely ballistic — unpowered and gravity-shaped — on a predictable trajectory.
**Breakdown:**
- Propulsion: typically solid or liquid rocket (✅ owner 06).
- Flight: powered boost, then exo-atmospheric mid-course, high speed, fixed trajectory.
- Interception: predictable path makes it trackable, but speed and short flight time make it hard to intercept (✅ owner 06 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Agni series is publicly associated with the ballistic deterrence role (✅ owner 06).
**Links:** SNT-2007 (cruise), SNT-2008 (boost-glide), SNT-2021 (canisterisation), SNT-2014 (Agni-Prime).
**Trap:** "Ballistic and cruise differ only by range." The key difference is flight profile and propulsion logic in the main phase.
**Mains hook:** Link the ballistic flight profile to deterrence: a predictable path helps tracking, but speed and survivability (canisters, rail mobility) matter for second-strike credibility.
**Back:** *"Boost, then fall on a rail of gravity."*

### Flashcard SNT-2007 — Cruise missile
**Front:** What is a cruise missile and what propulsion does it use?
**Definition (learn):** A guided missile that sustains powered flight and cruises inside the atmosphere, usually at low altitude, with continuous aerodynamic guidance.
**Breakdown:**
- Propulsion: turbojet/turbofan for subsonic; ramjet for supersonic types (BrahMos); scramjet for hypersonic cruise (developmental) (✅ owner 06 advanced, ⚠️ propulsion-class mapping is analytical).
- Trade-off: slower than ballistic missiles, but low-flying terrain masking and accuracy (✅ owner 06 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** BrahMos classed by DRDO as a universal long-range supersonic cruise missile (✅ owner 06).
**Links:** SNT-2006, SNT-2012 (BrahMos), SNT-2020 (propulsion-to-class mapping).
**Trap:** BrahMos should not be analysed with ballistic logic; official DRDO material classes it as cruise.
**Mains hook:** Distinguish by flight logic, not by label. Cruise missiles trade speed for accuracy and low-radar concealment; ballistic missiles trade predictability for speed.
**Back:** *"Cruise is low, powered and steered; ballistic is high, boosted and falling."*

### Flashcard SNT-2008 — Boost-glide vs scramjet hypersonic cruise (HSTDV and the 2024 trial)
**Front:** What separates a boost-glide hypersonic vehicle from a scramjet hypersonic cruise missile, and what did India demonstrate?
**Definition (learn):** A boost-glide vehicle is rocket-boosted, then glides unpowered but manoeuvrably at hypersonic speed. A scramjet cruise missile is powered throughout by supersonic-combustion ramjet.
**Breakdown:**
- Boost-glide: neither classically ballistic nor cruise; defeats predictability assumptions (⚠️ owner analytical, 06 advanced).
- Scramjet: air-breathing; technology barriers are thermal management, materials, sustaining combustion in supersonic air (✅ owner 06 advanced).
- HSTDV scramjet demonstration: 7 Sep 2020 (✅ owner 06, PIB PRID=1651956).
- First long-range hypersonic missile flight trial: 16 Nov 2024 per owner body text, 17 Nov 2024 per owner's PIB reference (PRID=2073994) — ⚠️ date conflict inside owner; re-check PIB before quoting the day. Designed for payloads beyond 1,500 km per DRDO (✅ owner 06).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Neither HSTDV nor the 2024 trial is an inducted weapon (✅ owner 06).
**Links:** SNT-2006, SNT-2007, SNT-2009.
**Trap:** "Hypersonic" is a speed, not a missile class. A re-entering ballistic warhead is also hypersonic. Also, a demonstration is not an induction.
**Mains hook:** India is entering the hypersonic race at the demonstration rung; say so explicitly and separate air-breathing from boost-glide.
**Back:** *"Glide without engine, scram with engine: two hypersonic roads, one speed."*

### Flashcard SNT-2009 — MIRV and Mission Divyastra (Agni-5)
**Front:** What is a MIRV and when did India first flight-test indigenous MIRV technology?
**Definition (learn):** Multiple Independently targetable Re-entry Vehicles: one missile carrying several warheads that can be directed at separate targets.
**Breakdown:**
- Mission Divyastra: Agni-5 flight test with indigenous MIRV technology, 11 Mar 2024 (✅ owner 06, PIB PRID=2013549).
- Agni-5 was tested again on 20 Aug 2025 (✅ owner 06).
- Formal induction of MIRV capability is not officially recorded (✅ owner 06).
- First Agni-5 test: 19 Apr 2012 (✅ Wikipedia, fetched).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 11 Mar 2024 (first MIRV flight test on Agni-5); 20 Aug 2025 (second test).
**Links:** SNT-2022 (MIRV deterrence stability), SNT-2008, SNT-2018.
**Trap:** A first flight test is not fleet MIRV capability. Do not write "India has deployed MIRVs."
**Mains hook:** MIRV raises the number of warheads per launcher and complicates missile defence, which can strengthen assured retaliation; a balanced answer also notes destabilising "use-it-or-lose-it" pressure.
**Back:** *"One rocket, many re-entries, Divyastra showed the tests, not the fleet."*

### Flashcard SNT-2010 — Surface-to-air vs air-to-air vs anti-tank missile (trap pair)
**Front:** How do SAM, AAM and ATGM differ, and which trap pairs do examiners set?
**Definition (learn):** SAM = ground-launched weapon against aerial threats. AAM = launched from an aircraft against aerial targets. ATGM = guided anti-tank missile for armoured targets.
**Breakdown:**
- Akash = SAM (not AAM) (✅ owner 06).
- Astra = air-to-air family (✅ owner 06).
- Nag = anti-tank guided missile (✅ owner 06).
- Different seeker, launch platform and guidance logic (✅ owner 06 basic says so; analytical).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner's Prelims practice item: "Akash — air-to-air" is wrong.
**Links:** SNT-2011 (Akash), SNT-2016 (Nag), SNT-2017 (Astra).
**Trap:** "Akash is an air-to-air missile." Wrong: Akash is surface-to-air. Matching Nag with SAM is also wrong.
**Mains hook:** Explain that air defence, air combat and anti-armour need different sensors and platforms, which is why India's families are role-based.
**Back:** *"Ground-to-air is SAM; air-to-air is AAM; tank-killer is ATGM."*

### Flashcard SNT-2011 — Akash (surface-to-air missile system)
**Front:** What is Akash, when was it inducted, and what is its status now?
**Definition (learn):** A short-range surface-to-air missile system, mobile, for air defence.
**Breakdown:**
- Official DRDO class: short-range SAM (✅ owner 06).
- Operational with IAF and Indian Army (✅ owner 06, DRDO Akash page, verified 16 Jul 2026).
- Inducted into the IAF: July 2015 (✅ owner 06).
- Akash-NG user evaluation trials completed 23 Dec 2025; described as "paving the way" for induction; induction not confirmed (✅ owner 06, PIB PRID=2207971).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Akash IAF induction July 2015; Akash-NG user trials 23 Dec 2025.
**Links:** SNT-2019 (air-defence ladder), SNT-2010, SNT-2027 (Sudarshan Chakra).
**Trap:** "Paved the way for induction" is not induction. Legacy Akash ≠ Akash-NG.
**Mains hook:** Use Akash to show the status discipline: fielded legacy system, next-generation system at user-trial rung.
**Back:** *"Akash flies for the IAF since 2015; Akash-NG has only paved the road."*

### Flashcard SNT-2012 — BrahMos and BrahMos Aerospace (DRDO-NPOM JV)
**Front:** What is BrahMos and who develops it?
**Definition (learn):** A universal long-range supersonic cruise missile for land, sea and air launch, produced by BrahMos Aerospace Private Limited.
**Breakdown:**
- BrahMos Aerospace is a DRDO (India) and NPO Mashinostroyeniya (NPOM, Russia) joint venture (✅ owner 06, PIB PRID=1791199).
- Operational with Indian Navy and Army (✅ owner 06, DRDO BrahMos page, verified 16 Jul 2026).
- Ramjet propulsion for supersonic cruise (✅ owner 06 advanced propulsion mapping).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Joint venture: DRDO-NPOM; supersonic cruise; land/sea/air launch.
**Links:** SNT-2007, SNT-2020, SNT-2025 (DRDO-industry route).
**Trap:** "BrahMos is a purely domestic Indian project." Also "written as developed by DRDO, India and NPOM" reads as if India were a separate developer. Write "BrahMos Aerospace, a DRDO-NPOM joint venture."
**Mains hook:** A JV can deliver a fielded system faster than pure indigenous work, at the cost of shared IP and partner dependence. Use this in a strategic-autonomy answer.
**Back:** *"Indo-Russian JV, supersonic cruise, navy and army in service."*

### Flashcard SNT-2013 — Pinaka and guided rocket artillery
**Front:** What is Pinaka, and how does rocket artillery differ from ballistic or cruise missiles?
**Definition (learn):** Pinaka is a multi-barrel rocket / guided rocket artillery family for area fire support, distinct from ballistic and cruise missile logic.
**Breakdown:**
- Guided Pinaka: launched from in-service Pinaka launcher; trial results met PSQR parameters (✅ owner 06, DRDO page).
- Pinaka LRGR 120 maiden flight test from ITR, Chandipur, 29 Dec 2025 (✅ owner 06, PIB PRID=2209588).
- Shows launcher commonality across variants (✅ owner 06 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 29 Dec 2025 (LRGR 120 maiden flight test; status: tested).
**Links:** SNT-2004 (ITR), SNT-2010 (SAM vs AAM vs ATGM), SNT-2023 (missile vs launch vehicle).
**Trap:** Memorising Pinaka as "just another missile." It is a rocket-artillery family.
**Mains hook:** Pinaka shows artillery modernisation and precision long-range fires blurring the missile binary; say so explicitly.
**Back:** *"Pinaka rains rockets; it is artillery, not a missile binary."*

### Flashcard SNT-2014 — Agni-Prime and the rail-mobile launch
**Front:** What was the significance of the rail-mobile Agni-Prime launch, and what is its induction status?
**Definition (learn):** Intermediate-range Agni-Prime launched from a rail-based mobile launcher, a survivability and second-strike development.
**Breakdown:**
- Flight trial: 3 Apr 2024 (✅ owner 06).
- First rail-mobile launch: 24 Sep 2025 per owner basic (⚠️ owner's current anchor gives PIB release date 25 Sep 2025 — launch day vs release day; check PIB before quoting).
- PIB: road-mobile Agni-P already inducted; rail-based system enables future induction (✅ owner 06).
- Canisterisation/mobility improves survivability (✅ owner 06 advanced, analytical).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 3 Apr 2024 flight trial; 24/25 Sep 2025 rail-mobile launch.
**Links:** SNT-2021 (canisterisation), SNT-2006 (ballistic), SNT-2018 (status ladder).
**Trap:** Same missile, two different statuses: road-mobile inducted, rail-mobile tested. Do not merge.
**Mains hook:** Use Agni-P to show that status depends on launch platform, and tie survivability to second-strike credibility.
**Back:** *"Same Agni, two statuses: road is in, rail is on trial."*

### Flashcard SNT-2015 — Prithvi and Trishul (IGMDP legacy systems)
**Front:** What role did Prithvi and Trishul play in India's missile programme?
**Definition (learn):** Prithvi = early short-range surface-to-surface ballistic missile family; Trishul = short-range surface-to-air system developed under IGMDP.
**Breakdown:**
- Prithvi: linked to IGMDP legacy and indigenous capability building (✅ owner 06).
- Trishul: closed without induction (✅ owner 06 basic).
- Wikipedia: Trishul was de-linked from user service in 2003 and continued as a technology demonstrator (✅ Wikipedia, fetched).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Trishul project officially shut 27 Feb 2008 (✅ Wikipedia, fetched; conflicts with owner's "2012" end date for IGMDP as a whole, see SNT-2005).
**Links:** SNT-2005, SNT-2011.
**Trap:** Trishul is not an inducted air-defence system. Prithvi is not a SAM.
**Mains hook:** IGMDP's failures (Trishul) are useful evidence that development does not guarantee induction.
**Back:** *"Prithvi flew the early road; Trishul was shut before the army took it."*

### Flashcard SNT-2016 — Nag (anti-tank guided missile)
**Front:** What is Nag, and why does its design matter?
**Definition (learn):** An anti-tank guided missile family for armoured-target engagement.
**Breakdown:**
- ATGM class (✅ owner 06).
- Seeker-intensive missile development; anti-armour specialisation (✅ owner 06 advanced).
- Part of IGMDP (✅ owner 06; Wikipedia lists Nag among IGMDP systems, fetched).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** IGMDP member system.
**Links:** SNT-2005, SNT-2010, SNT-2017.
**Trap:** Nag is not a SAM; ATGM logic differs from air-defence logic.
**Mains hook:** Use Nag to show that anti-armour needs seeker and guidance specialisation, which explains multiple parallel families.
**Back:** *"Nag hunts tanks, not planes."*

### Flashcard SNT-2017 — Astra (air-to-air missile)
**Front:** What is Astra and what does it illustrate?
**Definition (learn):** The air-to-air missile family developed indigenously for air combat.
**Breakdown:**
- Air-to-air class (✅ owner 06).
- Indigenous air-combat capability distinct from ground-based air defence (✅ owner 06 advanced).
- Not an IGMDP product (✅ owner 06 basic, "outside IGMDP").
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not an IGMDP system.
**Links:** SNT-2005, SNT-2010, SNT-2011.
**Trap:** Astra is not a surface-to-air system, and Akash is not an air-to-air system.
**Mains hook:** Use Astra to show that air combat requires different seeker and launch-platform logic from ground-based defence.
**Back:** *"Astra fights in the air; Akash guards the ground."*

### Flashcard SNT-2018 — The development-to-induction status ladder
**Front:** Which rung does each official phrase describe — tested, trials completed, ready for induction, contracted, inducted, deployed?
**Definition (learn):** Official vocabulary for weapon status, from earliest to latest: developmental trial → user evaluation → "ready for induction" → contract (AoN/contract in procurement) → induction → deployment at scale.
**Breakdown:**
- Tested / flight-tested: technical result only.
- User trials completed / "paving the way": user-side validation.
- Ready for induction: validated but not yet inducted.
- Contracted: order signed, not delivered.
- Inducted: formally into service. Deployed: at operational scale.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Examples: Agni-5 MIRV tested (2024); Akash-NG user trials (23 Dec 2025); QRSAM "ready for induction" (Sep 2022 trials); MRSAM contract (16 Jan 2025); legacy Akash inducted (July 2015) (✅ owner 06 and 07).
**Links:** SNT-2001, SNT-2009, SNT-2011, SNT-2019, SNT-2055.
**Trap:** "A successful test means the weapon is in service." UPSC rewards the exact rung.
**Mains hook:** Each example in a Mains answer should carry its rung. This is how you show analytical precision.
**Back:** *"Test, trial, ready, contract, induct, deploy: six rungs, one ladder."*

### Flashcard SNT-2019 — Air-defence status ladder: QRSAM and MRSAM
**Front:** What is the status of QRSAM and MRSAM, and what contract is linked to MRSAM?
**Definition (learn):** QRSAM (quick reaction surface-to-air missile) and MRSAM (medium range SAM) are air-defence systems at different points on the status ladder.
**Breakdown:**
- QRSAM: Army trials completed Sep 2022; described as "ready for induction" (✅ owner 06, PIB PRID=1857724).
- MRSAM: fitted on Indian Navy ships; ₹2,960-crore BDL supply contract signed 16 Jan 2025 (✅ owner 06, PIB PRID=2093286).
- QRSAM and MRSAM are outside IGMDP (✅ owner 06).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ₹2,960 crore (BDL, 16 Jan 2025); QRSAM trials Sep 2022.
**Links:** SNT-2018, SNT-2011, SNT-2049 (offsets), SNT-2045 (PIL).
**Trap:** "MRSAM is in service because it is fitted to ships" — the owner says fitted and contracted, not that every unit is operational; keep the rung.
**Mains hook:** The MRSAM contract with a DPSU (BDL) is the missing middle between DRDO trial and induction; that is where indigenisation succeeds or fails.
**Back:** *"QRSAM was ready, MRSAM was ordered: know which rung you are on."*

### Flashcard SNT-2020 — Propulsion-to-class mapping (advanced)
**Front:** Which propulsion system maps to which missile class?
**Definition (learn):** The propulsion type largely determines the missile's flight profile and role class.
**Breakdown:**
- Solid rockets → ballistic missiles, SAMs, rocket artillery (storable, instant readiness).
- Liquid rockets → some early ballistic systems (higher performance, harder handling).
- Turbojet/turbofan → subsonic cruise missiles.
- Ramjet → supersonic cruise (BrahMos).
- Scramjet → hypersonic cruise (developmental).
(⚠️ owner analytical mapping, 06 advanced.)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Solid propulsion's instant readiness is an operational point, not a range figure.
**Links:** SNT-2006, SNT-2007, SNT-2008, SNT-2012.
**Trap:** Propulsion overlap does not make a missile a launch vehicle; launch vehicles are space-access systems (SNT-2023).
**Mains hook:** Use the mapping to argue that the missile class is a design decision, which is why R&D depth in propulsion is strategic.
**Back:** *"Solid for speed of readiness, ramjet for supersonic, scram for hyper."*

### Flashcard SNT-2021 — Canisterisation and second-strike survivability (advanced)
**Front:** Why does canisterisation or rail mobility matter for deterrence?
**Definition (learn):** Storing a missile sealed in a canister on a road- or rail-mobile launcher improves survivability and reduces launch preparation time.
**Breakdown:**
- Survivability: harder to locate before launch.
- Reduced preparation time.
- Rail-mobile Agni-Prime (2025) as a second-strike credibility development (⚠️ owner analytical, 06 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 24/25 Sep 2025 rail-mobile launch (see SNT-2014).
**Links:** SNT-2006, SNT-2014, SNT-2022.
**Trap:** Treating the rail launch as "just another test" — the owner argues it is a survivability signal.
**Mains hook:** Link dispersal and mobility to assured second-strike and stability in deterrence.
**Back:** *"Hide the missile, keep the retaliation."*

### Flashcard SNT-2022 — MIRV and deterrence stability (advanced)
**Front:** Does MIRV make deterrence more or less stable?
**Definition (learn):** MIRV increases warheads per launcher, complicating missile defence (stabilising for retaliation credibility), but a MIRVed launcher is a high-value target (destabilising, use-it-or-lose-it pressure).
**Breakdown:**
- Stabilising: penetration of adversary missile defence.
- Destabilising: counterforce target incentives, use-it-or-lose-it pressure.
- A first flight test is not fleet capability.
(⚠️ owner analytical, 06 advanced.)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Mission Divyastra, 11 Mar 2024 (SNT-2009).
**Links:** SNT-2009, SNT-2021.
**Trap:** Claiming India has MIRV fleet deployment on the basis of one flight test.
**Mains hook:** Give one balanced line on both effects; conclude with the verdict that MIRV strengthens credibility only if command-and-control and safety are robust.
**Back:** *"More warheads deter, but a juicy target invites the first strike."*

### Flashcard SNT-2023 — Missile is not a launch vehicle (trap)
**Front:** Why is a missile system not the same as a space launch vehicle?
**Definition (learn):** Missile systems are weapons for military delivery; launch vehicles are space-access systems. They may share propulsion engineering but differ in purpose, policy and legal framing.
**Breakdown:**
- Missile: weapon, deterrence/air defence/anti-armour/fires.
- Launch vehicle: space access (see Topic 01 owner).
- Overlap in engineering foundations; different strategic purposes (✅ owner 06 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner's Prelims pairs use "Pinaka — launch vehicle" as a wrong option.
**Links:** SNT-2020, SNT-2013, Topic 01 owner (ISRO launch vehicles).
**Trap:** "Pinaka is a launch vehicle" or "Agni is a space launch vehicle." Neither is a launch-vehicle classification.
**Mains hook:** Keep weapon and space-access programmes in separate sentences; note overlap only in propulsion.
**Back:** *"Missiles strike; launch vehicles lift."*

### Flashcard SNT-2024 — Loitering munition (boundary card)
**Front:** What is a loitering munition and where does it sit between missiles and drones?
**Definition (learn):** A one-way attack system that loiters over an area before striking.
**Breakdown:**
- Sits between a drone and a missile (⚠️ owner analytical, 06 basic).
- Belongs in Topic 19 (drones) as much as here.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** No specific Indian programme date is given in owner 06 — ⚠️ do not quote one.
**Links:** Topic 19 owner (drones and robotics), SNT-2007.
**Trap:** Treating every loitering drone as a cruise missile.
**Mains hook:** Use it as a dual-use example when discussing drones and missiles together.
**Back:** *"Circles, then strikes once: half drone, half missile."*

### Flashcard SNT-2025 — DIA-CoE (DRDO-Industry-Academia Centres of Excellence)
**Front:** What are DIA-CoEs and why do they matter?
**Definition (learn):** DRDO's scheme route for collaboration with academia, industry and research institutions on frontier defence problems.
**Breakdown:**
- Official DRDO scheme route (✅ owner 06).
- Shows that defence innovation is not lab-only (✅ owner 06 Mains framework).
- Translating research into rugged fielded systems is institutionally demanding (⚠️ owner advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** No date in owner — ⚠️ do not invent one.
**Links:** SNT-2002, SNT-2033, SNT-2047 (DPSUs).
**Trap:** DIA-CoE is a research collaboration scheme, not a production contract.
**Mains hook:** Use DIA-CoEs as evidence of an ecosystem beyond DRDO labs; add the translation-to-field caveat.
**Back:** *"Labs, campuses, companies: one DRDO door."*

### Flashcard SNT-2026 — Stealth, radar cross-section and absorbing materials (PYQ 2026 Q43)
**Front:** Which distinctions does a stealth question test (radar cross-section, absorbing materials, detection)?
**Definition (learn):** Low radar cross-section (RCS) is pursued through shaping and radar-absorbing materials; detection can rely on sensor or frequency choice, not on assuming invisibility.
**Breakdown:**
- Shaping: reduce reflective surfaces.
- Radar-absorbing materials: absorb incident energy.
- Detection: frequency and sensor choice matters; no platform is invisible at all frequencies.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2026 Prelims GS-I Q43. Provisional Set-A key is present locally; the answer letter is **not** recorded or inferred here (✅ owner 06 PYQ block).
**Links:** SNT-2027, SNT-2010.
**Trap:** Metamaterials must not be described as increasing radar scattering for stealth; public programme detail may be classified.
**Mains hook:** Use stealth to show that detection and counter-detection are coupled; avoid claiming any specific Indian stealth capability without official sources.
**Back:** *"Shape it, absorb it, but never call it invisible."*

### Flashcard SNT-2027 — Mission Sudarshan Chakra (PYQ 2026 Q60)
**Front:** What is Mission Sudarshan Chakra, and what does an announcement not prove?
**Definition (learn):** An announced integrated air and missile defence mission linking sensors, command-and-control and interceptors.
**Breakdown:**
- Air defence (defensive) ≠ aerial offensive capability.
- Stated objective year: 2035 — a future target (✅ owner 06 PYQ block, announced objective).
- Sensor → command-and-control → interceptor chain.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2035 target (owner-stated; time-varying, as-of owner 06 PYQ block).
**Links:** SNT-2011 (Akash), SNT-2026, SNT-2032 (S-400 route).
**Trap:** Do not call a nationwide shield deployed, or infer coverage of every public place from an announcement.
**Mains hook:** Integrated air defence needs detection, decision, interceptor and industrial support; distinguish announced from deployed.
**Back:** *"Sudarshan promises 2035; today it is a plan, not a shield."*

### Flashcard SNT-2028 — THAAD vs FOBS (PYQ 2018 Q43, 2022 Q34)
**Front:** What is THAAD, and what is FOBS?
**Definition (learn):** THAAD (Terminal High Altitude Area Defense) is a US anti-ballistic-missile defence system. FOBS (Fractional Orbital Bombardment System) puts a payload in partial orbit before de-orbiting toward a target.
**Breakdown:**
- THAAD: defensive interceptor for terminal phase, US system.
- FOBS: a space-weapon concept, not an ordinary satellite or ICBM label.
- Neither is an Indian demonstrated capability (✅ owner 06 PYQ block; ✅ owner 06 advanced for FOBS as concept).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018 Q43 (THAAD); 2022 Q34 (FOBS). Keys unavailable locally; answer letters not inferred.
**Links:** SNT-2023, SNT-2027.
**Trap:** THAAD is not an Israeli radar system nor an Indian missile programme.
**Mains hook:** Use the pair to separate defensive interception (THAAD) from space-based bombardment concepts (FOBS) in a strategic-stability answer.
**Back:** *"THAAD catches; FOBS circles then drops."*

### Flashcard SNT-2029 — IS 19445:2025 bomb-disposal standard (PYQ 2026 Q80)
**Front:** What does IS 19445:2025 cover, and what does it not prove?
**Definition (learn):** BIS standard titled Bomb Disposal Systems — Performance Evaluation and Requirements, covering uniform testing and assessment of protective systems.
**Breakdown:**
- Standard supports interoperable evaluation and procurement.
- Source: BIS Standard of the Month, Feb 2026 (✅ owner 06 audit source).
- Standard does not prove that every agency has compatible equipment.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** IS 19445:2025 (✅ owner 06 audit, BIS SOM PDF).
**Links:** SNT-2030, SNT-2033.
**Trap:** Do not repeat collaboration claims absent a primary BIS or DRDO source.
**Mains hook:** Standards are the plumbing of interoperability; link to procurement and certification (Topic 07).
**Back:** *"Standard tests the bomb-kit; it does not make every agency compatible."*

### Flashcard SNT-2030 — CL-20, HMX and LLM-105 (PYQ 2025 Q46)
**Front:** What are CL-20, HMX and LLM-105, and what should a candidate not infer?
**Definition (learn):** High-energy chemical substances discussed as military explosives.
**Breakdown:**
- Category fact: high-energy explosives.
- Do not turn a category fact into handling, composition or deployment claims.
- Official Set-A key available locally; the answer letter is not recorded here (✅ owner 06).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 Prelims GS-I Q46.
**Links:** SNT-2029.
**Trap:** Treating a chemical name as a missile or a warhead. These are substances.
**Mains hook:** Keep the high-energy-materials point at category level in any answer.
**Back:** *"Three high-energy names, one category: explosives, not missiles."*

### Flashcard SNT-2031 — Operation Sindoor MoD release (advanced)
**Front:** What did the MoD officially say about Operation Sindoor, and what can an answer use it for?
**Definition (learn):** MoD release of 7 May 2025 stated the operation struck nine terrorist-infrastructure sites in Pakistan and PoJK and described it as focused, measured and non-escalatory.
**Breakdown:**
- Official release date: 7 May 2025 (✅ owner 06 advanced, PIB PRID=2127370).
- Use only for the doctrinal point that precision stand-off capability shapes India's response options.
- Do not attribute specific weapons beyond the official release.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 7 May 2025; nine sites.
**Links:** SNT-2027, SNT-2026.
**Trap:** Naming specific missiles used in the operation from media claims.
**Mains hook:** Use the operation to illustrate doctrine (precise, calibrated response) rather than weapon inventory.
**Back:** *"Nine sites, measured tone: doctrine, not weapon inventory."*

### Flashcard SNT-2032 — S-400 air-defence route (advanced, 2021 GS-III Q5)
**Front:** How should a 10-mark answer compare an integrated air-defence system like S-400?
**Definition (learn):** S-400 is a mobile, layered surface-to-air and anti-missile system with a radar-command-interceptor architecture (owner evidence).
**Breakdown:**
- Compare: sensor/radar coverage → command-and-control/fusion → interceptor family and envelope → mobility/networking → training/logistics/interoperability.
- Evidence: layered architecture; significance: area and asset defence against multiple aerial threats.
- Qualification: saturation, electronic warfare, terrain, readiness and network integration limit effectiveness; no system is invulnerable.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2021 GS-III Q5 (routed), 10 marks, 150 words (✅ owner 06 PYQ block).
**Links:** SNT-2027, SNT-2011.
**Trap:** Asserting unverified range figures or classified Indian deployment detail.
**Mains hook:** The structure is architecture first, then a qualified verdict.
**Back:** *"Sensors see, command decides, interceptors hit, logistics keeps it alive."*

### GROUP — Defence Indigenisation, Atmanirbhar and Procurement (basic 07 + advanced 07)

### Flashcard SNT-2033 — Core theme: procurement and indigenisation are not R&D
**Front:** Why is defence self-reliance a procurement and industrial problem as well as an R&D problem?
