**Definition (learn):** Topic 06 asks what India develops and tests; Topic 07 asks how India procures, manufactures, scales, sustains and exports it.
**Breakdown:**
- Development and testing: DRDO and labs (Topic 06).
- Categorisation and approval: DAP 2020 and DAC (in-principle only).
- Production: DPSUs, private firms, JVs and MSMEs.
- Supply- and demand-side instruments must both work (⚠️ owner analytical, 07 basic).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** DAP 2020 published 14 Dec 2020 (✅ owner 07, DDP node).
**Links:** SNT-2001, SNT-2003, SNT-2034, SNT-2035, SNT-2055.
**Trap:** An answer that mentions only supply-side or only demand-side instruments is incomplete.
**Mains hook:** Answer thesis: indigenisation succeeds only when R&D is matched by procurement reform, predictable orders, supplier development, corridors and export-capable depth.
**Back:** *"Topic 06 builds it; Topic 07 buys it, builds it again and keeps it alive."*

### Flashcard SNT-2034 — Atmanirbhar Bharat in defence
**Front:** What does Atmanirbhar (self-reliant) defence mean in official policy?
**Definition (learn):** A policy push for greater domestic design, development, production and supply-chain depth in defence.
**Breakdown:**
- Design → development → acquisition → production → sustainment → exports (⚠️ owner Mains framing, 07 advanced).
- Measured by control of design, value addition and lifecycle support, not by assembly alone.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Policy framing ✅ owner 07.
**Links:** SNT-2033, SNT-2042, SNT-2064.
**Trap:** Self-reliance equals assembly. Mere assembly, licensed production and genuine manufacturing depth are different.
**Mains hook:** Define Atmanirbhar as a spectrum, then place a specific programme on it.
**Back:** *"Self-reliance is a spectrum from drawing to delivery, not a slogan."*

### Flashcard SNT-2035 — DAP 2020 (Defence Acquisition Procedure)
**Front:** What is DAP 2020 and what capital-acquisition categories does it recognise?
**Definition (learn):** The Defence Acquisition Procedure, the MoD rulebook for capital procurement, published 14 Dec 2020.
**Breakdown:**
- Capital schemes: Buy; Buy and Make; Leasing; Make; Design and Development; Strategic Partnership Model (✅ owner 07).
- Buy has five sub-categories in priority order (SNT-2037).
- Official DDP node records 14 Dec 2020 publication (✅ owner 07).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** DAP 2020, 14 Dec 2020.
**Links:** SNT-2036, SNT-2037, SNT-2040, SNT-2041, SNT-2043.
**Trap:** DAP is a procedure, not a policy slogan and not a list of approved purchases.
**Mains hook:** DAP is the procurement skeleton; positive lists and corridors are muscles around it.
**Back:** *"Six schemes, five buy rungs, one procedure dated 14 Dec 2020."*

### Flashcard SNT-2036 — Buy (Indian-IDDM)
**Front:** What is the highest-priority DAP category and what does IDDM mean?
**Definition (learn):** Buy (Indian-IDDM): an Indian vendor supplying a product indigenously designed, developed and manufactured (IDDM) with prescribed minimum indigenous content.
**Breakdown:**
- Highest priority rung in the Buy ladder (✅ owner 07).
- Requires indigenous design, not only Indian assembly.
- Contrast with Buy (Indian), which covers Indian vendors without the full IDDM design test (⚠️ owner definition).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Rank 1 of five Buy sub-categories (✅ owner 07, DAP 2020).
**Links:** SNT-2035, SNT-2037, SNT-2042.
**Trap:** "IDDM" is not the same as "made in India." A product can be manufactured in India and still not be indigenously designed.
**Mains hook:** Use IDDM to separate design indigenisation from manufacturing indigenisation.
**Back:** *"IDDM means Indian design, development and manufacture: the top rung."*

### Flashcard SNT-2037 — The five-rung DAP 'Buy' ladder
**Front:** What is the complete DAP Buy priority ladder?
**Definition (learn):** Buy (Indian-IDDM) → Buy (Indian) → Buy & Make (Indian) → Buy (Global – Manufacture in India) → Buy (Global), in descending order of indigenisation preference.
**Breakdown:**
- Rung 1: Indian-IDDM.
- Rung 2: Buy (Indian).
- Rung 3: Buy & Make (Indian): Indian vendor manufactures after technology transfer from a foreign OEM (SNT-2038).
- Rung 4: Buy (Global – Manufacture in India): foreign vendor with a minimum share manufactured in India through a subsidiary or JV (SNT-2039).
- Rung 5: Buy (Global).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Five rungs ✅ owner 07 (DAP 2020 text).
**Links:** SNT-2036, SNT-2038, SNT-2039.
**Trap:** Stopping at three rungs (Indian-IDDM, Indian, Global) misses the two middle categories where tech transfer is negotiated.
**Mains hook:** The middle rungs are the mechanism of technology absorption; trade speed of induction against depth of domestic value capture.
**Back:** *"IDDM, Indian, Make, Manufacture, Global — five rungs down."*

### Flashcard SNT-2038 — Buy & Make (Indian)
**Front:** What is the Buy & Make (Indian) route?
**Definition (learn):** An Indian vendor manufactures a product after technology transfer from a foreign OEM.
**Breakdown:**
- Rung 3 of the Buy ladder (✅ owner 07).
- Technology transfer is the central element.
- Sits between indigenous design and foreign-buy routes.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** DAP 2020 category (✅ owner 07).
**Links:** SNT-2037, SNT-2039, SNT-2041.
**Trap:** Buy & Make is not Make (Indian-designed-and-built) — the design stays with the OEM.
**Mains hook:** Middle rungs are where absorption is negotiated; the answer should ask whether ToT produces design depth or only assembly.
**Back:** *"Licence the design, build it here, and learn the craft."*

### Flashcard SNT-2039 — Buy (Global – Manufacture in India)
**Front:** What is Buy (Global – Manufacture in India)?
**Definition (learn):** A foreign vendor sells to India, but a minimum share of the product must be manufactured in India through a subsidiary or JV.
**Breakdown:**
- Rung 4 of the Buy ladder (✅ owner 07).
- Local manufacture obligation via subsidiary or JV.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** DAP 2020 (✅ owner 07).
**Links:** SNT-2037, SNT-2038, SNT-2053.
**Trap:** "Buy (Global)" and "Buy (Global – Manufacture in India)" are different rungs; the latter carries a local-manufacture share.
**Mains hook:** Use it as an example of the middle ground between import dependence and full indigenous design.
**Back:** *"Global vendor, Indian factory: buy abroad, build at home."*

### Flashcard SNT-2040 — Make, Design & Development and Leasing routes
**Front:** How do the Make, Design and Development, and Leasing routes differ from Buy?
**Definition (learn):** Make: Indian industry designs and produces, with government support; Design & Development: Indian firms develop prototypes with government funding; Leasing: the armed forces lease equipment rather than buying it outright.
**Breakdown:**
- DAP 2020 chapter structure: procurement, strategic partnership, leasing, make and innovation routes are distinct channels (✅ owner 07 advanced).
- Buy is the procurement route; Make and Design & Development are the development routes.
- ⚠️ Descriptions beyond the category label are from general knowledge; check the DAP text before quoting detail.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Category names ✅ owner 07.
**Links:** SNT-2035, SNT-2036, SNT-2041, SNT-2050 (iDEX).
**Trap:** A Make route is not a Buy route; do not treat leasing as purchase.
**Mains hook:** Show that DAP offers several pathways, and the category choice determines the domestic-preference logic.
**Back:** *"Buy it, make it, design it, or rent it: four doors in DAP."*

### Flashcard SNT-2041 — Strategic Partnership Model
**Front:** What is the Strategic Partnership Model in DAP 2020?
**Definition (learn):** A DAP route to build long-term domestic capacity in major defence segments through selected private partners.
**Breakdown:**
- Long-term partnership with selected private firms (✅ owner 07).
- Can coexist with DPSU anchors (⚠️ owner advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** DAP 2020 category (✅ owner 07).
**Links:** SNT-2035, SNT-2047, SNT-2064.
**Trap:** SPM and DPSUs are not mutually exclusive approaches.
**Mains hook:** Present SPM as a capacity-building tool, not an end; assess it on the capacity it builds.
**Back:** *"Long partners for big segments, chosen with care."*

### Flashcard SNT-2042 — Indigenous content and the three senses of "indigenous"
**Front:** What is indigenous content (IC), and how does it differ from indigenous design?
**Definition (learn):** IC is the percentage of contract value that must be Indian; it is the operative measure of DAP categories.
**Breakdown:**
- (a) Indigenous design: IP and system architecture are Indian.
- (b) Indigenous manufacture: production occurs in India, possibly under licence.
- (c) Indigenous value addition: the DAP IC percentage (a value-share test).
- A system can score high on (b) and (c) while remaining dependent on (a) (⚠️ owner analytical).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** No IC threshold figure in owner — ⚠️ do not invent a percentage.
**Links:** SNT-2036, SNT-2064, SNT-2058.
**Trap:** "High indigenous content = indigenous design." Wrong. IC is a value-share test; engines, seekers and semiconductors may remain foreign.
**Mains hook:** Ask which of the three senses is being claimed; India's aero-engine, seeker and semiconductor gaps show the design-sense weakness.
**Back:** *"Content counts value, not authorship: design, make and value are three senses."*

### Flashcard SNT-2043 — Acceptance of Necessity (AoN)
**Front:** What does an Acceptance of Necessity mean, and what does it not mean?
**Definition (learn):** In-principle administrative approval, granted by the DAC, of a requirement and its categorisation.
**Breakdown:**
- Not a contract, not a delivery, not an induction (✅ owner 07 and 06).
- Chain: service requirement → DAC AoN → RFP/tender → evaluation → negotiation → contract → delivery → induction (⚠️ owner analytical sequence).
- MoD material of 3 Jul 2026 restated that AoN is in-principle only (✅ owner 07, PIB PRID=2280728).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 3 Jul 2026 MoD restatement (owner-cited).
**Links:** SNT-2044, SNT-2035, SNT-2055.
**Trap:** Confusing AoN with a purchase is the commonest factual error in defence-procurement answers.
**Mains hook:** Track AoN-to-contract conversion rates as an honest performance measure.
**Back:** *"AoN is permission, not a purchase order."*

### Flashcard SNT-2044 — Defence Acquisition Council (DAC)
**Front:** What is the DAC and what does it chair and grant?
**Definition (learn):** The apex body for defence acquisition approvals, chaired by the Raksha Mantri; it grants Acceptance of Necessity and approves categorisation.
**Breakdown:**
- Chair: Raksha Mantri (✅ owner 06 and 07).
- Grants AoN and approves categorisation (✅ owner 07).
- Distinct from DDP (production) and DRDO (R&D) (✅ owner 07).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Chair: Raksha Mantri (as stated in owner; time-varying office-holder not recorded).
**Links:** SNT-2043, SNT-2003, SNT-2035.
**Trap:** DAC is not a production or R&D body.
**Mains hook:** DAC approves what to acquire; it does not guarantee that anything is bought or delivered.
**Back:** *"DAC nods, the contract follows much later."*

### Flashcard SNT-2045 — Positive Indigenisation List (PIL)
**Front:** What is a Positive Indigenisation List and how do Services PILs differ from DPSU PILs?
**Definition (learn):** An official list of items to be procured from Indian industry after notified timelines.
**Breakdown:**
- Services PILs: platforms or weapons the armed forces may import only up to a notified date (import embargo) (✅ owner 07, ⚠️ detail in advanced).
- DPSU PILs: sub-systems, components and raw materials DPSUs must source domestically; tracked on SRIJAN (SNT-2046).
- Fifth DPSU PIL notified 25 Jun 2024 (✅ owner 07 current anchor, ⚠️ item-by-item deadlines).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Fifth DPSU PIL: 25 Jun 2024.
**Links:** SNT-2046, SNT-2019 (MRSAM), SNT-2064.
**Trap:** A notified list is not proof of full domestic value addition; each item has its own deadline. A list is a sourcing tool, not complete self-reliance.
**Mains hook:** Judge PILs by items actually indigenised, not by list length.
**Back:** *"Services list bans imports; DPSU list localises parts."*

### Flashcard SNT-2046 — SRIJAN portal
**Front:** What is SRIJAN and what does it track?
**Definition (learn):** The official public dashboard for DPSU positive-indigenisation items and their stage of progress.
**Breakdown:**
- Tracks stages such as EOI, RFP, trials and indigenised progress (✅ owner 07).
- Monitoring is staged progression, not one-time announcement (⚠️ owner advanced analytical).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Dashboard verified 16 Jul 2026 per owner.
**Links:** SNT-2045, SNT-2047, SNT-2055.
**Trap:** SRIJAN is a tracker, not a procurement category or a regulator.
**Mains hook:** Use SRIJAN to show measurable indigenisation rather than political announcements.
**Back:** *"SRIJAN tracks items from EOI to indigenised."*

### Flashcard SNT-2047 — DPSUs (Defence Public Sector Undertakings)
**Front:** Which DPSUs are named in the owner files, and what is their role?
**Definition (learn):** Public-sector defence manufacturers or integrators such as HAL, BEL, BDL, MDL, GRSE, GSL, BEML and MIDHANI.
**Breakdown:**
- HAL (aerospace), BEL (electronics), BDL (missiles/ammunition supply, MRSAM contract), MDL and GRSE (shipbuilding), GSL (shipbuilding) (✅ owner 06/07 for named list; sector roles from general knowledge ⚠️ where not in owner).
- DPSUs are production enterprises, not DRDO labs (✅ owner 06).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** DDP products page exposes filters for HAL, BEL, BDL, MDL, GRSE, GSL (✅ owner 07, verified 16 Jul 2026).
**Links:** SNT-2003, SNT-2048, SNT-2019.
**Trap:** DPSUs are not DRDO laboratories; a DPSU is a production partner.
**Mains hook:** DPSUs remain anchors for sovereign capability, while private firms add agility; the answer should show both.
**Back:** *"Public firms build the iron; DRDO only designs the sketch."*

### Flashcard SNT-2048 — Ordnance Factory Board corporatisation (2021)
**Front:** What happened to the Ordnance Factory Board in 2021, and which seven DPSUs were created?
**Definition (learn):** The Ordnance Factory Board (OFB) was corporatised in 2021 into seven new DPSUs, converting a departmental production organisation into corporate entities with balance-sheet accountability.
**Breakdown:**
- Seven DPSUs: Munitions India, Armoured Vehicles Nigam, Advanced Weapons and Equipment India, Troop Comforts, Yantra India, India Optel, Gliders India (✅ owner 07).
- Success judged on order books and productivity, not on the reorganisation itself (✅ owner 07 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2021 (✅ owner 07).
**Links:** SNT-2047, SNT-2033.
**Trap:** "The Ordnance Factory Board still exists" as a current producer — outdated.
**Mains hook:** Corporatisation is a governance reform; judge it by productivity and accountability outcomes.
**Back:** *"OFB became seven DPSUs in 2021: from department to company."*

### Flashcard SNT-2049 — Offsets (narrowed in DAP 2020)
**Front:** What are offsets and how did DAP 2020 change them?
**Definition (learn):** An obligation on a foreign vendor in large contracts to plough back a share of contract value into Indian defence and aerospace industry through purchases, investment or technology transfer.
**Breakdown:**
- DAP 2020 removed offsets for government-to-government, inter-governmental and single-vendor procurements (✅ owner 07).
- Reason: criticism that offsets rarely produced critical technology absorption (✅ owner 07).
- Treat offsets as a policy case study, not a success story (⚠️ owner advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** DAP 2020 narrowing.
**Links:** SNT-2035, SNT-2037, SNT-2064.
**Trap:** Offsets are not the same as Buy (Global – Manufacture in India); offsets are reciprocal obligations on the vendor.
**Mains hook:** Offsets show that obligations without absorption capacity do not create technology.
**Back:** *"Offsets promised tech, often delivered cheques."*

### Flashcard SNT-2050 — iDEX and ADITI (MoD Defence Innovation Organisation)
**Front:** What do iDEX and ADITI do, and why do they matter?
**Definition (learn):** Innovations for Defence Excellence (iDEX) is the MoD–DIO route that funds startups, MSMEs and individual innovators against defence problem statements; ADITI supports deep-tech within it.
**Breakdown:**
- Opens defence R&D to entrants outside the DRDO–DPSU system (✅ owner 07 basic says it; ⚠️ its funding details not verified).
- Attacks the entry-barrier problem that PILs and IC rules do not (⚠️ owner advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** DIO = Defence Innovation Organisation (✅ owner 07).
**Links:** SNT-2051 (TDF), SNT-2040, SNT-2052.
**Trap:** iDEX is not DRDO's TDF; they are separate channels.
**Mains hook:** Supply-side innovation policy complements demand-side PILs.
**Back:** *"iDEX opens the door to startups; ADITI takes them deep."*

### Flashcard SNT-2051 — Technology Development Fund (TDF)
**Front:** What is the Technology Development Fund?
**Definition (learn):** A DRDO-executed fund that finances Indian industry, especially MSMEs and startups, to develop defence technologies not yet available domestically.
**Breakdown:**
- Executed by DRDO (✅ owner 07).
- Supply-side instrument alongside iDEX/ADITI (✅ owner 07).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** No date in owner — ⚠️ do not invent one.
**Links:** SNT-2050, SNT-2002.
**Trap:** TDF is not a procurement contract.
**Mains hook:** Pair TDF with DAP to show that development funding and demand guarantees must move together.
**Back:** *"TDF pays the builders to build what India does not yet have."*

### Flashcard SNT-2052 — Defence industrial corridors (Uttar Pradesh and Tamil Nadu)
**Front:** Where are the two defence corridors and what is a corridor?
**Definition (learn):** Cluster-based industrial ecosystems intended to attract investment, ancillaries, testing and supply chains.
**Breakdown:**
- Two corridors: Uttar Pradesh and Tamil Nadu (✅ owner 07).
- Tamil Nadu corridor vision: a global defence-manufacturing destination (✅ owner 07, portal verified 16 Jul 2026).
- Corridors are place-based industrial policy, not an acquisition category (✅ owner 07 trap).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Two corridors (UP, TN).
**Links:** SNT-2035, SNT-2040, SNT-2047.
**Trap:** A defence corridor is not a DAP category.
**Mains hook:** Corridors aim at agglomeration: vendor clustering, logistics, skilling and proximity to primes; test them on order flow.
**Back:** *"Two corridors, one ecosystem goal: cluster to compete."*

### Flashcard SNT-2053 — Defence FDI routes
**Front:** What is the FDI limit and route for defence, and what must the government route consider?
**Definition (learn):** Up to 74 per cent is under the automatic route; beyond 74 per cent and up to 100 per cent requires government approval, considered where it is likely to result in access to modern technology, or for other reasons to be recorded.
**Breakdown:**
- Automatic: up to 74% (✅ owner 07, official policy search, 16 Jul 2026).
- Government route: above 74% up to 100% (✅ owner 07).
- Modern-technology access is the explicit test (✅ owner 07).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 74% automatic; 100% cap (as stated in owner's official-policy search, 16 Jul 2026 — re-check DPIIT for later changes).
**Links:** SNT-2039, SNT-2041, SNT-2064.
**Trap:** "FDI up to 100% is automatic" — wrong. And "FDI automatically means loss of self-reliance" — also wrong; test technology absorption.
**Mains hook:** FDI is a tool, judged by technology absorption and domestic capacity.
**Back:** *"Seventy-four is automatic; beyond that, prove the technology."*

### Flashcard SNT-2054 — Defence Exim portal
**Front:** What is the Defence Exim portal?
**Definition (learn):** The official digital platform for defence export and import authorisation processes.
**Breakdown:**
- Official portal under the defence-production ecosystem (✅ owner 07, verified via web search 16 Jul 2026).
- Exports matter for production runs, order-book stability and learning effects (✅ owner 07 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Portal URL in owner; no date needed.
**Links:** SNT-2058, SNT-2033.
**Trap:** Exports alone do not prove indigenous design; some exports depend on imported subsystems.
**Mains hook:** Exports matter for industrial economics, not only diplomacy; qualify with depth of domestic content.
**Back:** *"Export portal counts shipments; design depth is a separate test."*

### Flashcard SNT-2055 — Procurement status chain
**Front:** What is the usual chain from requirement to induction in defence procurement?
**Definition (learn):** Services' requirement → DAP category choice → DAC Acceptance of Necessity → RFP/tender → technical and field evaluation → commercial negotiation → contract → delivery → induction, with an ecosystem of EOI, trials and indigenised progress on SRIJAN.
**Breakdown:**
- AoN is early, not final.
- Contract and delivery are later rungs.
- MRSAM: DRDO design → BDL production contract (16 Jan 2025) → Navy induction chain (✅ owner 06 and 07).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** MRSAM contract ₹2,960 crore, 16 Jan 2025 (✅ owner 07 current anchor).
**Links:** SNT-2018, SNT-2043, SNT-2046.
**Trap:** Stopping at AoN and calling it procurement.
**Mains hook:** Use the chain to show where indigenisation actually succeeds or fails.
**Back:** *"Need, category, approval, tender, contract, delivery, induction."*

### Flashcard SNT-2056 — Public vs private role debate (advanced)
**Front:** Should defence self-reliance rely on DPSUs, private firms or a mix?
**Definition (learn):** A governance debate: over-centralising in DPSUs may reduce agility; over-romanticising private entry may ignore sovereign-capacity needs.
**Breakdown:**
- DPSUs: anchors for missiles, electronics, shipbuilding and aerospace (✅ owner 07 advanced).
- Private firms: components, integration, software, drones and niche platforms (✅ owner 07).
- Mixed ecosystem is the most defensible position (⚠️ owner analytical).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None beyond owner.
**Links:** SNT-2047, SNT-2041, SNT-2046.
**Trap:** DPSUs and private firms as either-or substitutes.
**Mains hook:** Argue for a calibrated mix measured by order certainty, quality, export credibility and sovereign control.
**Back:** *"Public anchors, private agility, measured by output."*

### Flashcard SNT-2057 — Lifecycle indigenisation: repair, overhaul, spares, upgrades
**Front:** Why does lifecycle support count as indigenisation?
**Definition (learn):** Repair, overhaul, spares and upgrade capacity are part of indigenisation; they determine whether a platform remains sovereign after delivery.
**Breakdown:**
- Design-control: drawings, testing standards and future upgrades (⚠️ owner analytical).
- Assembly can scale faster than deep materials, electronics and engine control (✅ owner 07 advanced analytical).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2042, SNT-2064, SNT-2035.
**Trap:** Counting delivery as the end of indigenisation.
**Mains hook:** Use lifecycle support to test whether self-reliance survives beyond the first delivery.
**Back:** *"Keep it flying: spares, overhaul and upgrades are self-reliance too."*

### Flashcard SNT-2058 — Procurement speed vs scrutiny (advanced)
**Front:** What trade-off does fast defence procurement face?
**Definition (learn):** Faster buying is desirable, but opaque shortcuts can weaken competition or lifecycle assessment.
**Breakdown:**
- Speed: shorter induction cycles.
- Scrutiny: competition, cost, quality, lifecycle costing.
- Balance is a governance design question (⚠️ owner analytical).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2043, SNT-2044, SNT-2042.
**Trap:** Assuming speed is always the same as efficiency.
**Mains hook:** Argue for transparent fast-tracks with post-award audit.
**Back:** *"Fast buying, slow shortcuts: keep scrutiny in the bargain."*

### Flashcard SNT-2059 — Fighter generation guardrail (LCA Tejas Mk1)
**Front:** What generation does HAL give LCA Tejas Mk1, and what must you avoid saying?
**Definition (learn):** HAL describes LCA Tejas Mk1 as a 4.5-generation fighter. It is not an Indian fifth-generation operational aircraft.
**Breakdown:**
- Tejas Mk1: 4.5-generation (✅ owner 07, HAL LCA page, audit 14 Aug 2026).
- AMCA is a future programme, not an inducted fleet at the verified date (✅ owner 07).
- PYQ 2024 Q35 lists Rafale, MiG-29 and Tejas Mk1 in a fifth-generation question; check keys before answering (owner: key available locally, answer not recorded here).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Tejas Mk1 = 4.5 generation (HAL, owner-audited).
**Links:** SNT-2060, SNT-2033.
**Trap:** Calling Tejas Mk1, Rafale or MiG-29 "fifth-generation Indian" aircraft.
**Mains hook:** Generation labels reflect stealth, avionics and sensor-fusion capability, not just year of design.
**Back:** *"Mk1 is 4.5, AMCA is tomorrow: never promote the future."*

### Flashcard SNT-2060 — Platform status: what "made in India" means (PYQ 2026 Q68)
**Front:** How should you describe Indian fighter aircraft, tanks and submarines without collapsing their status?
**Definition (learn):** For each named platform distinguish design origin, licensed or indigenous manufacture, contract, delivery and operational service.
**Breakdown:**
- Tejas: indigenous fighter design and production programme (✅ owner 07).
- Arjun: indigenous tank programme (✅ owner 07).
- Submarines/naval platforms: domestic construction is a shipbuilding capability (✅ owner 07).
- 2026 Prelims GS-I Q68 (Indian manufacture of fighters, tanks, submarines): provisional Set-A key present; answer not recorded or inferred here.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2026 Q68 (owner routing).
**Links:** SNT-2059, SNT-2047, SNT-2055.
**Trap:** "Made in India" is not one fact; it can mean licensed production, not design.
**Mains hook:** Use the design-manufacture-contract-delivery-service ladder for every platform.
**Back:** *"Designed, built, contracted, delivered, serving: five words for one proud platform."*

### Flashcard SNT-2061 — Equivalent service ranks (PYQ 2024 Q69)
**Front:** Which ranks are equivalent across the Army, Navy and Air Force?
**Definition (learn):** Service-rank comparisons across the three services, not procurement categories.
**Breakdown:**
- Captain / Lieutenant / Flight Lieutenant.
- Major / Lieutenant Commander / Squadron Leader.
- Colonel / Captain (Navy) / Group Captain.
- Brigadier / Commodore / Air Commodore.
- Major General / Rear Admiral / Air Vice Marshal.
- Lieutenant General / Vice Admiral / Air Marshal.
- General / Admiral / Air Chief Marshal.
(✅ owner 07 rank-equivalence card.)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024 Prelims GS-I Q69; key available locally, answer not recorded here.
**Links:** SNT-2062, SNT-2063.
**Trap:** Army Major equals Navy Lieutenant Commander and Air Force Squadron Leader, not Navy Commander.
**Mains hook:** Rank-equivalence is an organisational fact; use it in defence governance answers only where needed.
**Back:** *"Captain, Major, Colonel — navy and air have their own names for the same step."*

### Flashcard SNT-2062 — CDS as Head of the Department of Military Affairs (PYQ 2024 Q77)
**Front:** What is the Chief of Defence Staff's role in relation to the DMA?
**Definition (learn):** The CDS heads the Department of Military Affairs within the Ministry of Defence and is Permanent Chairman of the Chiefs of Staff Committee.
**Breakdown:**
- DMA is within MoD (✅ owner 07).
- CDS does not erase the separate command roles of the three Service Chiefs (✅ owner 07).
- Linked Polity owner for constitutional detail (✅ owner 07 cross-link).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024 Q77; key available locally, answer not recorded here.
**Links:** SNT-2061, SNT-2044.
**Trap:** CDS is not the head of DRDO or DDP.
**Mains hook:** Use the CDS to discuss integrated operational planning without collapsing service roles.
**Back:** *"CDS chairs the chiefs and heads the DMA — not the services."*

### Flashcard SNT-2063 — Aircraft roles: Dornier-228, IL-76 and C-17 (PYQ 2025 Q84)
**Front:** What role does each transport aircraft play?
**Definition (learn):** Dornier-228 is a light utility or transport-type aircraft; IL-76 and C-17 are heavy strategic-airlift types.
**Breakdown:**
- Dornier-228: light utility.
- IL-76 and C-17: heavy airlift.
- Do not infer manufacturer, service, generation or cargo figure from a role label without an official source (✅ owner 07 aircraft-role card).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 Prelims GS-I Q84; official key available locally, answer not recorded here.
**Links:** SNT-2059, SNT-2060.
**Trap:** Mixing up light utility and heavy airlift roles in a type-description pair.
**Mains hook:** Role-based classification is the shared logic across aircraft, missiles and vehicles.
**Back:** *"Dornier is light, IL-76 and C-17 are heavy lifters."*

### Flashcard SNT-2064 — Trap pack: SPM vs DPSU, IC vs design, PIL vs self-reliance (advanced)
**Front:** Which three procurement claims must an answer never make?
**Definition (learn):** (1) SPM and DPSUs are not mutually exclusive. (2) IC percentage is not indigenous design. (3) A PIL listing does not prove full value addition.
**Breakdown:**
- Coexistence of routes in a mixed ecosystem (✅ owner 07 advanced).
- IC value-share test vs design authorship (SNT-2042).
- PIL is a sourcing tool; deadlines per item (SNT-2045).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None beyond owner.
**Links:** SNT-2041, SNT-2042, SNT-2045.
**Trap:** The three claims above.
**Mains hook:** A stronger answer names what each instrument can and cannot do.
**Back:** *"Routes mix, content is not design, lists are not localisation."*

### GROUP — Digital India and India Stack, UPI, Aadhaar (basic 08 + advanced 08)

### Flashcard SNT-2065 — Core theme: India Stack layers are distinct, not one system
**Front:** How are Aadhaar, UPI, Account Aggregator, DigiLocker and eSign related, and why must they stay separate?
**Definition (learn):** India's digital public infrastructure (DPI) is a set of interoperable layers: identity (Aadhaar), payments (UPI), consented financial data (AA), trusted documents (DigiLocker) and signatures (eSign), each with its own institution and legal base.
**Breakdown:**
- Identity: UIDAI under Aadhaar Act, 2016 (✅ owner 08).
- Payments: UPI run by NPCI within RBI-regulated system (✅ owner 08).
- Data: AA framework, RBI-regulated NBFCs (✅ owner 08).
- Documents and signatures: DigiLocker (NeGD/MeitY) and eSign (CCA, IT Act) (✅ owner 08).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner's architecture diagram; no single database.
**Links:** SNT-2066, SNT-2067, SNT-2074, SNT-2080, SNT-2081, SNT-2082, SNT-2086, SNT-2087.
**Trap:** Treating the stack as one legal or technical bucket.
**Mains hook:** Separate layers give interoperability and accountability; end with the caveat on inclusion and consent.
**Back:** *"Identity, payment, consent, documents, signatures: five rails, five institutions."*

### Flashcard SNT-2066 — Digital India programme
**Front:** What is Digital India, and what does it cover in the owner files?
**Definition (learn):** The government programme to expand digital infrastructure, digital governance and digital service delivery; an umbrella for initiatives such as Aadhaar, UPI, DigiLocker and the national AI programme.
**Breakdown:**
- Umbrella: MeitY ecosystem (✅ owner 08).
- Digital India AI page used as framing (✅ owner 08, verified 16 Jul 2026).
- Launch year not stated in owner — ⚠️ do not quote unverified year.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Programme, no launch date recorded in owner.
**Links:** SNT-2065, SNT-2142.
**Trap:** Digital India is an umbrella programme, not a single statute or regulator.
**Mains hook:** Present Digital India as an architecture of rails and services, not a list of apps.
**Back:** *"Digital India: the umbrella over identity, payments and services."*

### Flashcard SNT-2067 — Aadhaar: 12-digit identity, residency not citizenship
**Front:** What is Aadhaar, and what does it prove and not prove?
**Definition (learn):** A unique 12-digit identity number and authentication ecosystem governed by the Aadhaar Act, 2016 and run by UIDAI.
**Breakdown:**
- 12-digit number (✅ Wikipedia, fetched).
- Enacted under Aadhaar Act, 2016 (✅ owner 08; Wikipedia says the Bill was introduced as a Money Bill in March 2016).
- Residency-linked identity, not citizenship or domicile proof (✅ owner 08; UIDAI statement).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Act 2016; 12 digits.
**Links:** SNT-2068, SNT-2069, SNT-2070, SNT-2072.
**Trap:** "Aadhaar is proof of citizenship" — wrong. It is residency-based identity.
**Mains hook:** Argue for identity-as-service and identity-as-citizenship being separate questions; cite the Act's residency basis.
**Back:** *"Twelve digits prove you live here, not that you belong here."*

### Flashcard SNT-2068 — UIDAI
**Front:** What is UIDAI and under which ministry does it sit?
**Definition (learn):** The statutory authority under the Aadhaar Act, 2016, under MeitY, which issues Aadhaar and runs authentication.
**Breakdown:**
- Statutory authority (✅ owner 08).
- Under MeitY (✅ owner 08).
- Operates Aadhaar and is also the statutory authority, an asymmetry worth noting (⚠️ owner advanced analytical).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Statutory under MeitY.
**Links:** SNT-2067, SNT-2069, SNT-2086.
**Trap:** RBI does not run Aadhaar; UIDAI does.
**Mains hook:** Accountability of a DPI operator is a governance design question.
**Back:** *"UIDAI issues the number; RBI does not."*

### Flashcard SNT-2069 — Authentication vs e-KYC vs offline verification
**Front:** What are the three Aadhaar verification workflows?
**Definition (learn):** Authentication = yes/no match against the CIDR (demographic, biometric or OTP). e-KYC = consented return of demographic data (and photo) from UIDAI. Offline verification = QR/XML/paperless e-KYC without any CIDR call.
**Breakdown:**
- Authentication: yes/no against CIDR.
- e-KYC: consented data return.
- Offline verification: privacy-preserving route encouraged after the 2018 judgment; introduced by the 2019 amendment (⚠️ owner analytical; 2019 link in SNT-2073).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** No count here (see SNT-2085).
**Links:** SNT-2067, SNT-2072, SNT-2073.
**Trap:** Offline verification does not call the CIDR; authentication does.
**Mains hook:** Offline verification is the statutory response to privacy concerns; say whether the design reduces data exposure.
**Back:** *"Yes-no to CIDR, data back with consent, or verify offline without calling home."*

### Flashcard SNT-2070 — Section 7 of the Aadhaar Act
**Front:** What does section 7 of the Aadhaar Act allow?
**Definition (learn):** Permits requiring Aadhaar for subsidies, benefits and services financed from the Consolidated Fund of India.
**Breakdown:**
- Constitutional hinge of the mandatory-use scheme (✅ owner 08).
- Mandate is limited to Consolidated Fund-financed benefits, not private contracts (✅ owner 08).
- Legitimacy rests on s.7 plus proportionality (⚠️ owner advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Section 7, Aadhaar Act, 2016.
**Links:** SNT-2067, SNT-2072.
**Trap:** s.7 does not authorise private entities to demand Aadhaar under contract; that was s.57, struck down.
**Mains hook:** Legitimate use of identity depends on the public-benefit link and proportionality.
**Back:** *"Section seven: subsidies yes, contracts no."*

### Flashcard SNT-2071 — Puttaswamy I: privacy as a fundamental right (2017)
**Front:** What did the 2017 Puttaswamy judgment establish?
**Definition (learn):** The nine-judge bench recognised privacy as a fundamental right under Article 21 (owner citation, ⚠️ date and bench not re-fetched).
**Breakdown:**
- Privacy under Art. 21 (✅ owner 08).
- Foundation for the later Aadhaar challenge (✅ owner 08).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2017, nine-judge bench (✅ owner 08; ⚠️ not separately fetched).
**Links:** SNT-2072, SNT-2089.
**Trap:** Confusing Puttaswamy I (2017, privacy) with Puttaswamy II (2018, Aadhaar).
**Mains hook:** Privacy as a right is the constitutional yardstick for DPI safeguards.
**Back:** *"Puttaswamy one: privacy is a right; Puttaswamy two: Aadhaar is tested."*

### Flashcard SNT-2072 — The 2018 Aadhaar judgment (26 Sep 2018)
**Front:** What did the 2018 Aadhaar judgment decide?
**Definition (learn):** Upheld the Aadhaar Act but struck down section 57 (private use under contract), read down s.33(2), and barred Aadhaar for school admissions and competitive exams.
**Breakdown:**
- Majority opinion dated 26 Sep 2018 (✅ Wikipedia, fetched).
- Section 57 declared unconstitutional (✅ Wikipedia, fetched).
- Owner: Act upheld as Money Bill; s.33(2) read down; school admissions, CBSE/NEET/UGC barred (✅ owner 08; ⚠️ details not separately verified).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 26 Sep 2018; s.57 struck.
**Links:** SNT-2070, SNT-2073, SNT-2071.
**Trap:** Saying the 2018 judgment struck down the Aadhaar Act; it was upheld.
**Mains hook:** The judgment made Aadhaar a public-benefit tool with a consent-and-proportionality boundary.
**Back:** *"Twenty-six September 2018: Act stands, section 57 falls."*

### Flashcard SNT-2073 — Aadhaar and Other Laws (Amendment) Act, 2019
**Front:** What did the 2019 amendment add for banking and telecom?
**Definition (learn):** Created a voluntary route for banking and telecom using offline verification or voluntary authentication, with civil penalties for entity misuse (owner).
**Breakdown:**
- Voluntary route for banking and telecom (✅ owner 08).
- Offline verification promoted (✅ owner 08).
- Penalties for entity misuse (✅ owner 08).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2019 (✅ owner 08; ⚠️ exact date not fetched — Wikipedia fetch truncated).
**Links:** SNT-2069, SNT-2072.
**Trap:** The 2019 amendment did not make Aadhaar compulsory for banks; it made the route voluntary.
**Mains hook:** Use the 2018 → 2019 sequence to show legal response to privacy.
**Back:** *"Two thousand nineteen: banks and telecom go voluntary."*

### Flashcard SNT-2074 — UPI (Unified Payments Interface)
**Front:** What is UPI, who runs it, and when did the pilot start?
**Definition (learn):** An interoperable real-time retail-payment rail run by NPCI, addressed by virtual payment address (VPA) and built over IMPS.
**Breakdown:**
- Operator: NPCI (✅ owner 08; ✅ Wikipedia).
- Regulator: RBI (✅ Wikipedia).
- Pilot launch: 11 Apr 2016 in Mumbai with 21 member banks (✅ Wikipedia, fetched). Wikipedia's infobox credits Narendra Modi, while its history section says RBI Governor Raghuram Rajan inaugurated the pilot — ⚠️ inconsistency inside the source.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 11 Apr 2016 pilot.
**Links:** SNT-2075, SNT-2076, SNT-2077, SNT-2078.
**Trap:** UPI is not a currency; it is a payment interface between bank accounts.
**Mains hook:** UPI shows the interoperable-rail model; cite its operator and regulator separately.
**Back:** *"NPCI runs the rail, UPI moves the money, RBI watches the gate."*

### Flashcard SNT-2075 — UPI beyond bank accounts: PPIs and credit lines
**Front:** Which instruments can now be linked to UPI beyond bank accounts?
**Definition (learn):** Full-KYC prepaid payment instruments (PPIs) through third-party UPI apps (RBI, 27 Dec 2024), and pre-sanctioned credit lines and credit cards (RBI, 4 Sep 2023, updated 12 Feb 2025).
**Breakdown:**
- Full-KYC PPIs discoverable via third-party apps (✅ owner 08; RBI circular, 27 Dec 2024).
- Pre-sanctioned credit lines from scheduled commercial banks with consent (✅ owner 08; RBI circular 4 Sep 2023, updated 12 Feb 2025).
- Savings, overdraft, PPIs and credit cards can be linked (✅ owner 08).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 27 Dec 2024; 4 Sep 2023 (as updated 12 Feb 2025).
**Links:** SNT-2074, SNT-2091.
**Trap:** Defining UPI as bank-account-only is out of date.
**Mains hook:** The move to credit is a change in risk profile, not only in convenience.
**Back:** *"UPI now carries wallets and credit, not just bank balances."*

### Flashcard SNT-2076 — NPCI: operator, not regulator
**Front:** What is NPCI and what is its regulatory status?
**Definition (learn):** National Payments Corporation of India: an umbrella not-for-profit company owned by banks; an operator of payment rails, regulated by RBI under the Payment and Settlement Systems Act, 2007.
**Breakdown:**
- Operates UPI, RuPay, IMPS, AePS, BBPS and NACH (✅ owner 08).
- Owned by banks, not a government department (✅ owner 08).
- Not a regulator (✅ owner 08; ✅ Wikipedia: UPI regulated by RBI).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PSS Act 2007 (✅ owner 08; ✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SNT-2077, SNT-2074, SNT-2086.
**Trap:** "NPCI regulates digital payments" — wrong; NPCI operates, RBI regulates.
**Mains hook:** Operator-regulator separation is a conflict-of-interest safeguard.
**Back:** *"NPCI operates; RBI regulates."*

### Flashcard SNT-2077 — RBI and the Payment and Settlement Systems Act, 2007
**Front:** What role does RBI play in payments, and under which Act?
**Definition (learn):** RBI regulates and authorises payment-system operators under the Payment and Settlement Systems Act, 2007, and issued the Account Aggregator Master Direction.
**Breakdown:**
- Regulator and authoriser (✅ owner 08).
- PSS Act 2007 (✅ owner 08; ✅ Wikipedia).
- AA Master Direction (✅ owner 08; RBI page).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PSS Act 2007.
**Links:** SNT-2076, SNT-2080, SNT-2086.
**Trap:** RBI does not run Aadhaar or DigiLocker.
**Mains hook:** Use RBI's regulatory role to explain why UPI's consumer protection and credit risk fall under RBI.
**Back:** *"RBI guards the payment gate under the 2007 Act."*

### Flashcard SNT-2078 — Payment rail taxonomy: IMPS, NEFT, RTGS, NACH, BBPS
**Front:** How do IMPS, NEFT, RTGS, NACH and BBPS differ from UPI?
**Definition (learn):** UPI: mobile-first, VPA-addressed, 24x7 instant retail, built over IMPS. IMPS: interbank instant transfer. NEFT: 24x7 batch-settled interbank transfer operated by RBI. RTGS: real-time gross settlement for large-value transactions. NACH: bulk and recurring debits/credits. BBPS: interoperable bill payment.
**Breakdown:**
- UPI over IMPS (✅ owner 08).
- NEFT: 24x7 batch (✅ owner 08).
- RTGS: ₹2 lakh minimum (⚠️ not verified in owner; general knowledge — check RBI before quoting).
- NACH: subsidy, salary and EMI mandates (✅ owner 08).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** RTGS minimum ₹2 lakh (⚠️ unverified).
**Links:** SNT-2074, SNT-2079, SNT-2076.
**Trap:** Treating "UPI" as a synonym for all digital payments.
**Mains hook:** Rail taxonomy matters for fraud and inclusion analysis.
**Back:** *"UPI moves now, NEFT batches, RTGS settles big, NACH repeats, BBPS bills."*

### Flashcard SNT-2079 — AePS (Aadhaar Enabled Payment System)
**Front:** What is AePS and why is it not a UPI feature?
**Definition (learn):** Aadhaar-authenticated banking at a business correspondent (cash withdrawal, balance enquiry, mini statement) — an inclusion rail.
**Breakdown:**
- Uses Aadhaar authentication (✅ owner 08).
- Served by business correspondents (✅ owner 08).
- Distinct fraud profile: biometric replay and agent misuse (⚠️ owner advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None beyond owner.
**Links:** SNT-2078, SNT-2088.
**Trap:** "AePS is a UPI feature" — wrong.
**Mains hook:** AePS shows rural inclusion and its specific fraud risk.
**Back:** *"AePS is Aadhaar at the agent's counter, not UPI on your phone."*

### Flashcard SNT-2080 — Account Aggregator (AA) framework
**Front:** What is the AA framework and why is it a "data blind"?
**Definition (learn):** An RBI-regulated NBFC-based framework for consented sharing of financial information; the AA cannot retain the financial information it moves.
**Breakdown:**
- Explicit customer consent; participation voluntary (✅ owner 08; DFS).
- AA is a data blind; the data is not its property (✅ owner 08; RBI AA Master Direction).
- AA Master Direction updated 6 Sep 2024 (✅ owner 08).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** AA Master Direction, updated 6 Sep 2024.
**Links:** SNT-2077, SNT-2092.
**Trap:** AA owns or monetises customer data — wrong.
**Mains hook:** The AA model separates data flow from data ownership, a consent-architecture lesson.
**Back:** *"AA moves your money data with consent and forgets it."*

### Flashcard SNT-2081 — DigiLocker
**Front:** What is DigiLocker and who maintains it?
**Definition (learn):** A government-backed platform to access, share and verify digital documents.
**Breakdown:**
- Maintained by NeGD/MeitY ecosystem (✅ owner 08).
- Trust and document layer, not an Aadhaar database (✅ owner 08).
- 31 Aug 2025: pan-India integration of nearly 2,000 e-government services on DigiLocker and e-District (✅ owner 08, PIB PRID=2162403).
- 08 Nov 2025: national DigiLocker conference (✅ owner 08, PIB).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ~2,000 services (31 Aug 2025). Site user counts are dated Sep 2023 — do not quote as current (owner).
**Links:** SNT-2082, SNT-2065.
**Trap:** DigiLocker is not an identity database.
**Mains hook:** A trust layer multiplies governance services; regulatory harmonisation is the next step (NeGD high-level committee, owner advanced).
**Back:** *"DigiLocker stores trust, not identity."*

### Flashcard SNT-2082 — eSign
**Front:** What is eSign and how does it differ from a scanned signature?
**Definition (learn):** An online electronic-signature service integrated through APIs, using digital-signature infrastructure and e-KYC-style authentication; legally recognised under the IT Act, 2000.
**Breakdown:**
- CCA under MeitY is the policy authority (✅ owner 08).
- No physical cryptographic token needed for the end user (✅ owner 08).
- Not a scanned handwritten signature (✅ owner 08).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** IT Act 2000 (✅ owner 08).
**Links:** SNT-2081, SNT-2086.
**Trap:** eSign is not an image of your signature.
**Mains hook:** eSign enables paperless legal transactions; mention trust framework.
**Back:** *"eSign signs with keys, not with ink images."*

### Flashcard SNT-2083 — Digital Public Infrastructure (DPI): the three tests
**Front:** What makes something DPI rather than merely government-owned?
**Definition (learn):** Shared, interoperable, minimalist digital rails (identity, payments, data exchange) governed by public rules and usable by public and private actors; the three tests are interoperability, openness of standards and a governance/trust framework.
**Breakdown:**
- Digital public good: open-source or open-standard software or data (⚠️ owner definition).
- Ownership can be public, private or hybrid (NPCI is bank-owned) (✅ owner 08).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2065, SNT-2084, SNT-2092.
**Trap:** DPI simply means government-owned digital systems — wrong.
**Mains hook:** Judge DPI by interoperability and governance, not ownership.
**Back:** *"Interoperable, open, governed: ownership is not the test."*

### Flashcard SNT-2084 — ONDC and OCEN (network-layer protocols)
**Front:** What do ONDC and OCEN do?
**Definition (learn):** ONDC is an open network protocol for e-commerce that unbundles buyer apps from seller apps; OCEN is an open credit protocol connecting lenders, loan service providers and borrowers, typically using AA-sourced data.
**Breakdown:**
- ONDC: competition on a network, not inside a platform (✅ owner 08 advanced analytical).
- OCEN: small-ticket cash-flow lending (✅ owner 08).
- Both are network-layer additions to India Stack (✅ owner 08).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None in owner.
**Links:** SNT-2083, SNT-2080, SNT-2091.
**Trap:** ONDC is not a payment rail; OCEN is not a payment rail.
**Mains hook:** Network protocols are the strongest counter to platform concentration (⚠️ analytical).
**Back:** *"ONDC opens shops, OCEN opens credit: both on a network."*

### Flashcard SNT-2085 — Aadhaar authentication scale (dated figures)
**Front:** What dated figures show the scale of Aadhaar authentication?
**Definition (learn):** UIDAI passed 150 billion authentications (PIB, 16 May 2025); UIDAI's monthly dataset showed about 182.9 billion cumulative through July 2026, with about 2.69 billion in July 2026 alone.
**Breakdown:**
- 16 May 2025: 150 billion (✅ owner 08, PIB PRID=2129121).
- July 2026: ~182.9 billion cumulative; ~2.69 billion in July (✅ owner 08, UIDAI dashboard dataset, dated).
- Rising e-KYC usage highlighted (✅ owner 08).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Dated as per owner. Always quote with the month.
**Links:** SNT-2069, SNT-2064.
**Trap:** Quoting a cumulative total without a date; cumulative figures are volatile.
**Mains hook:** Run-rate matters: even a small failure percentage means large absolute exclusion at monthly volumes of this order.
**Back:** *"One hundred fifty billion by May 2025; always date the count."*

### Flashcard SNT-2086 — Who operates, who regulates: the institution matrix
**Front:** Who does what among UIDAI, NPCI, RBI, DFS, CCA and NeGD?
**Definition (learn):** A matrix of operator, regulator and policy roles in the India Stack.
**Breakdown:**
- UIDAI: operates and is statutory authority for Aadhaar (MeitY).
- NPCI: operates UPI and other rails (bank-owned).
- RBI: regulates payment systems and AA framework.
- DFS (Ministry of Finance): explanatory and policy page for AA.
- CCA (MeitY): eSign policy and trust framework.
- NeGD / Digital India Corporation (MeitY): DigiLocker governance.
(✅ owner 08.)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None beyond owner.
**Links:** SNT-2068, SNT-2076, SNT-2077, SNT-2082.
**Trap:** RBI runs Aadhaar; NPCI regulates payments; UIDAI regulates UPI — all wrong.
**Mains hook:** Institutional separation improves accountability: errors and disputes are assigned to the right domain.
**Back:** *"UIDAI issues, NPCI runs, RBI regulates, CCA signs, NeGD keeps records."*

### Flashcard SNT-2087 — Four distinctions: identity, payments, data, documents
**Front:** What must a high-quality India Stack answer always separate?
**Definition (learn):** Aadhaar is identity; UPI is payments; AA is data sharing; DigiLocker is document trust.
**Breakdown:**
- Identity: Aadhaar Act / UIDAI.
- Payments: PSS Act 2007 / NPCI operator / RBI regulator.
- Data: AA Master Direction / RBI / DFS.
- Documents: DigiLocker / NeGD / eSign / CCA.
(✅ owner 08 PYQ application.)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None beyond owner.
**Links:** SNT-2065, SNT-2086.
**Trap:** Merging the four into "digital India."
**Mains hook:** Use these four lines as the spine of a 10-mark answer.
**Back:** *"Identity, payments, data, papers: four lines, one stack."*

### Flashcard SNT-2088 — Fraud typologies differ by rail (advanced)
**Front:** Why is a single "digital fraud" framing analytically weak?
**Definition (learn):** UPI faces social-engineering and collect-request fraud; AePS faces biometric replay and agent misuse; card-not-present fraud is a separate profile.
**Breakdown:**
- Remedies map to rails: 2FA design, transaction limits, biometric locking (⚠️ owner advanced).
- Helpline 1930 and I4C are cited in owner as reporting routes (⚠️ not verified in this card).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1930 helpline (owner-cited, ⚠️ re-check).
**Links:** SNT-2079, SNT-2074, SNT-2064.
**Trap:** One remedy for all rails.
**Mains hook:** Map each fraud type to its remedy; this is a stronger answer than "more awareness."
**Back:** *"Each rail, its own fraud; each fraud, its own fix."*

### Flashcard SNT-2089 — DPDP sequencing seam (advanced)
**Front:** Why is India's DPI ahead of its enforceable data-rights regime?
**Definition (learn):** DPI has scaled to hundreds of billions of authentications, while the DPDP Act's substantive obligations are phased to commence later.
**Breakdown:**
- India built infrastructure before the enforceable rights regime; the EU sequence was the reverse (⚠️ owner analytical).
- Faster inclusion but weaker ex-ante accountability (⚠️ owner).
- DPDP detail belongs to Topic 12.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None verified; DPDP phasing not re-verified here.
**Links:** SNT-2071, SNT-2072, Topic 12 owner.
**Trap:** Saying DPDP is fully in force for all obligations.
**Mains hook:** Argue for sequencing rights and infrastructure together.
**Back:** *"Built the road before the traffic rules."*

### Flashcard SNT-2090 — Zero-MDR and the sustainability question (advanced)
**Front:** Why does near-zero MDR make UPI success a sustainability question?
**Definition (learn):** UPI's near-zero merchant discount rate drove adoption but makes the rail's economics dependent on subsidy and cross-subsidy.
**Breakdown:**
- Adoption metrics and financial sustainability differ (✅ owner 08 advanced analytical).
- Economics depend on subsidy or cross-subsidy.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2074, SNT-2091.
**Trap:** Quoting adoption as proof of financial sustainability.
**Mains hook:** Separate adoption from sustainability in a balanced answer.
**Back:** *"Free for merchants, costly for someone; ask who pays."*

### Flashcard SNT-2091 — UPI as a credit-distribution rail (advanced)
**Front:** Why has UPI's move into credit changed its risk profile?
**Definition (learn):** With pre-sanctioned credit lines and credit cards on UPI, the rail distributes credit, importing credit-risk, over-indebtedness and consumer-protection questions.
**Breakdown:**
- Transfer rail → credit-and-payment rail (✅ owner 08 advanced).
- New consumer-protection needs: liability, grievance, over-indebtedness (⚠️ analytical).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** RBI circular 4 Sep 2023 (SNT-2075).
**Links:** SNT-2075, SNT-2084.
**Trap:** Treating credit on UPI as just another payment.
**Mains hook:** Credit distribution needs safeguards beyond payments.
**Back:** *"From transfer to borrowing: the risk moved with it."*

### Flashcard SNT-2092 — DPI export as diplomacy (advanced)
**Front:** How should India's DPI export diplomacy be judged?
**Definition (learn):** India positions its DPI stack as an exportable governance product through bilateral MoUs and multilateral fora.
**Breakdown:**
- Judge on interoperability and local institutional capacity of the adopting country (✅ owner 08 advanced analytical).
- Do not judge by number of MoUs signed.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2083, SNT-2080.
**Trap:** Counting MoUs as success.
**Mains hook:** Assess DPI export by how well the partner can run it.
**Back:** *"Count the institutions built, not the MoUs signed."*

### Flashcard SNT-2093 — FASTag and electronic toll collection (PYQ 2024 GS-III Q6)
**Front:** What is FASTag's technology, and what did the 2024 question demand?
**Definition (learn):** FASTag is an RFID-based electronic toll system within the National Electronic Toll Collection (NETC), with an interoperable payment, clearing and dispute-management architecture.
**Breakdown:**
- Tag-reader system, not satellite distance tolling (✅ owner 08 routed PYQ; IHMCL NETC page).
- Advantages: less cash handling, less queueing and fuel waste, audit trail (✅ owner 08).
- Limits: read failures, inactive or duplicate tags, outages, dispute redress (✅ owner 08).
- 2024 GS-III Q6, "Explain", 10 marks, 150 words.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024 GS-III Q6.
**Links:** SNT-2094, SNT-2065.
**Trap:** Answering with a generic UPI paragraph instead of the toll technology.
**Mains hook:** Structure: present technology → advantages → limitations → proposed change → risk and safeguards.
**Back:** *"Tag, read, debit, clear, dispute: FASTag's five beats."*

### Flashcard SNT-2094 — ANPR-FASTag and GNSS tolling: pilot vs deployment (PYQ 2024 Q6)
**Front:** What status does ANPR-FASTag barrierless tolling and GNSS distance-based tolling have?
**Definition (learn):** ANPR (number-plate recognition) combined with RFID is in pilots; GNSS distance-based tolling is a policy or pilot direction, not a nationwide substitute for FASTag at the verified date.
**Breakdown:**
- ANPR-FASTag: pilot (✅ owner 08 routed audit, PIB material).
- GNSS: proposed (✅ owner 08).
- Risks: location tracking, spoofing and data governance (⚠️ owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Status as of audit (owner, 14 Aug 2026 audit).
**Links:** SNT-2093, SNT-2087.
**Trap:** "GNSS tolling is nationwide now" — wrong per owner.
**Mains hook:** Pair any seamless-flow claim with data minimisation, retention limits and non-digital grievance access.
**Back:** *"Pilot plates, proposed satellites: never call the future deployed."*

### Flashcard SNT-2095 — AR vs VR (routed computing companion, 2019 Q91)
**Front:** How do augmented reality and virtual reality differ?
**Definition (learn):** AR overlays digital information on the physical world; VR immerses the user in a computer-generated environment.
**Breakdown:**
- AR: physical world plus overlay.
- VR: fully computer-generated environment.
- Neither term proves interoperability or a specific hardware capability (✅ owner 08).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2019 Prelims GS-I Q91 (owner routing; key unavailable locally).
**Links:** SNT-2096, SNT-2097.
**Trap:** AR is not VR with a headset; the distinction is the physical scene.
**Mains hook:** Use it as a conceptual baseline only; the full computing mechanism is in Topic 25.
**Back:** *"AR adds to your world; VR replaces it."*

### Flashcard SNT-2096 — Wearable technology (2019 Q95)
**Front:** What do wearable devices do, and are they clinical diagnostic devices?
**Definition (learn):** Body-worn devices combining sensors, local processing and communication to track motion, location or physiological signals, or deliver notifications.
**Breakdown:**
- Sensors + processing + communication (✅ owner 08).
- Consumer wearables are not automatically clinically validated (✅ owner 08).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2019 Prelims GS-I Q95 (owner routing; key unavailable locally).
**Links:** SNT-2095, SNT-2101.
**Trap:** Treating a fitness wearable as a diagnostic device.
**Mains hook:** Validation matters for health claims; a consumer device is not a clinic.
**Back:** *"Wearables sense and notify; they do not diagnose by default."*

### Flashcard SNT-2097 — LTE vs VoLTE (2019 Q75)
**Front:** How do LTE and VoLTE differ?
**Definition (learn):** LTE is the 4G data-network standard; VoLTE (Voice over LTE) carries voice calls as packet data over LTE rather than over legacy circuit switching.
**Breakdown:**
- LTE: packet-data network standard (⚠️ general knowledge; owner routes detail to Topic 25).
- VoLTE: voice on LTE packet network (⚠️ general knowledge).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2019 Prelims GS-I Q75; key unavailable locally.
**Links:** SNT-2095, Topic 25 owner.
**Trap:** VoLTE is not a separate network or a different radio generation.
**Mains hook:** Use only if connectivity or telecom reform is asked.
**Back:** *"LTE carries data; VoLTE carries your voice on it."*

### Flashcard SNT-2098 — Software as a Service (2022 Q33)
**Front:** What defines Software as a Service?
**Definition (learn):** A cloud model in which the provider hosts and maintains the application and users access it over the network, usually by subscription.
**Breakdown:**
- SaaS: application-level service (⚠️ general knowledge; owner routes cloud-model detail to Topic 25).
- Contrast with infrastructure and platform service models (⚠️ general knowledge).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 Prelims GS-I Q33; key unavailable locally.
**Links:** SNT-2099, Topic 25 owner.
**Trap:** SaaS is not the same as owning the software on your premises.
**Mains hook:** Use for cloud-governance context only.
**Back:** *"SaaS rents the finished app over the cloud."*

### Flashcard SNT-2099 — Blockchain public ledger (2020 Q40)
**Front:** What features of a blockchain public ledger does the owner route?
**Definition (learn):** A distributed, shared record in which transactions are appended in linked blocks and copies are held across participants.
**Breakdown:**
- Distributed ledger, consensus, tamper-evidence (⚠️ general knowledge; owner routes to Topic 25).
- Applications in records and supply chains (⚠️ general knowledge).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2020 Prelims GS-I Q40; key unavailable locally.
**Links:** SNT-2098, Topic 25 owner.
**Trap:** A blockchain is not automatically a secure or private database.
**Mains hook:** Use it when asked about trust architectures in digital governance.
**Back:** *"Many copies, linked blocks, shared trust."*

### Flashcard SNT-2100 — Internet of Things (2018 Q66)
**Front:** What is the Internet of Things?
**Definition (learn):** Everyday physical devices with sensors and connectivity that exchange data over networks.
**Breakdown:**
- Sensor, connectivity, data (⚠️ general knowledge; owner routes to Topic 25).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018 Prelims GS-I Q66; key unavailable locally.
**Links:** SNT-2095, SNT-2101.
**Trap:** IoT is not a single network standard.
**Mains hook:** Use for smart-city or service-delivery examples.
**Back:** *"Things that sense and talk."*

### Flashcard SNT-2101 — Aadhaar Open APIs and biometric authentication (2018 Q17)
**Front:** What do Aadhaar Open APIs enable?
**Definition (learn):** Aadhaar's API layer lets authorised applications integrate identity verification and biometric authentication into their services.
**Breakdown:**
- Integration of authentication into services (✅ owner 08 routed PYQ, domain).
- Authentication, e-KYC and offline routes (SNT-2069).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018 Prelims GS-I Q17; key unavailable locally.
**Links:** SNT-2067, SNT-2069.
**Trap:** Open APIs do not mean Aadhaar data is public.
**Mains hook:** Pair APIs with consent and purpose limits.
**Back:** *"Open doors, consented keys."*

### GROUP — Artificial Intelligence, Governance and IndiaAI (basic 09 + advanced 09)

### Flashcard SNT-2102 — Core theme: AI capability needs compute, data, talent and governance together
**Front:** Why is India's AI policy an ecosystem mission rather than a model-building race?
**Definition (learn):** AI capability depends on compute, datasets, talent and applications; governance (safety, standards, accountability) must advance with capability.
**Breakdown:**
- Compute + datasets + talent → model development and evaluation → sectoral applications → governance (✅ owner 09 architecture, analytical).
- Mission logic: compute, datasets and skilling are state-capacity questions (⚠️ owner analytical).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** IndiaAI Mission (SNT-2113).
**Links:** SNT-2110, SNT-2113, SNT-2114, SNT-2121.
**Trap:** Treating AI as only a computer-science topic.
**Mains hook:** Answer thesis: an ecosystem mission with a calibrated, India-specific, innovation-friendly governance approach.
**Back:** *"Compute feeds models, data feeds models, governance steers them."*

### Flashcard SNT-2103 — AI, machine learning, deep learning and generative AI (nested)
**Front:** How are AI, ML, deep learning and generative AI related?
**Definition (learn):** Nested categories: AI is the umbrella (including rule-based expert systems); ML learns patterns from data; deep learning uses multi-layer neural networks; generative AI produces new content, typically using transformer-based foundation models.
**Breakdown:**
- Rule-based expert system = AI but not ML (✅ owner 09).
- ML classifier is not generative AI (✅ owner 09).
- Deep learning needs large data and compute (✅ owner 09).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2104, SNT-2105, SNT-2132, SNT-2138.
**Trap:** AI, ML and GenAI are not synonyms. Quantum computing is not a DL method (see SNT-2172).
**Mains hook:** Specify which layer a claim concerns before judging it.
**Back:** *"AI contains ML; ML contains deep nets; deep nets can generate."*

### Flashcard SNT-2104 — Learning paradigms
**Front:** What are supervised, unsupervised, reinforcement and self-supervised learning?
**Definition (learn):** Supervised: labelled data to prediction. Unsupervised: structure or clusters in unlabelled data. Reinforcement: agent learns from reward signals. Self-supervised: pre-training on unlabelled data, how modern foundation models are trained.
**Breakdown:**
- Labelled, unlabelled, reward, self-generated labels (✅ owner 09).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2103, SNT-2105.
**Trap:** Self-supervised is not the same as unsupervised clustering.
**Mains hook:** Foundation models use self-supervised pre-training; their risks propagate downstream.
**Back:** *"Labels, clusters, rewards, or self-made tasks."*

### Flashcard SNT-2105 — Foundation model and upstream risk
**Front:** What is a foundation model and why does it raise systemic governance risk?
**Definition (learn):** A broad-purpose model pre-trained on large unlabelled corpora and adapted to many downstream tasks.
**Breakdown:**
- One upstream model's flaws propagate to every application built on it (✅ owner 09).
- Hence "regulate the application, not the model" is contested (✅ owner 09 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2104, SNT-2106, SNT-2127.
**Trap:** Treating model risk as a product-level bug only.
**Mains hook:** Systemic risk needs upstream responsibility; the debate is about where duties attach.
**Back:** *"One base model, many children: the flaw travels."*

### Flashcard SNT-2106 — Hallucination
**Front:** What is AI hallucination and how is it reduced?
**Definition (learn):** Fluent but factually false or fabricated model output.
**Breakdown:**
- Models optimise for plausible continuation, not verified truth (✅ owner 09).
- Mitigations: retrieval grounding, citation, human review in public-service deployments (✅ owner 09).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2105, SNT-2138.
**Trap:** Better data alone does not remove hallucination.
**Mains hook:** Public-service AI needs human review, not only accuracy claims.
**Back:** *"Fluent is not factual: ground it, cite it, check it."*

### Flashcard SNT-2107 — Explainability and contestable decisions
**Front:** Why does explainability matter legally?
**Definition (learn):** Ability to state why a model gave an output; decisive wherever AI affects rights (credit, welfare, policing).
**Breakdown:**
- An unexplainable adverse decision is hard to contest (✅ owner 09).
- Core administrative-law problem of algorithmic governance (✅ owner 09).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2108, SNT-2109.
**Trap:** Accuracy is not the same as explainability.
**Mains hook:** Contestability is the test for public AI.
**Back:** *"No reason, no appeal."*

### Flashcard SNT-2108 — Algorithmic accountability in public administration
**Front:** Do administrative-law duties apply when the State uses AI?
**Definition (learn):** Yes: reasoned decisions, right to be heard and non-arbitrariness under Article 14 do not disappear when the State uses AI for targeting, eligibility or policing.
**Breakdown:**
- DPDP has no algorithmic-decision safeguard, so general constitutional principles fill the gap (✅ owner 09 advanced, analytical).
- Art. 14 non-arbitrariness (⚠️ owner analytical).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2107, SNT-2089, SNT-2121.
**Trap:** Assuming an algorithm's output is automatically lawful.
**Mains hook:** Demand reasoned, auditable public AI decisions.
**Back:** *"Art 14 follows the algorithm into the office."*

### Flashcard SNT-2109 — Model evaluation and red-teaming
**Front:** What are evaluation and red-teaming?
**Definition (learn):** Structured adversarial testing for safety, bias and misuse before deployment.
**Breakdown:**
- Governance without evaluation capacity is aspiration, not regulation (✅ owner 09).
- Relevant to Safe & Trusted AI pillar (SNT-2117).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2107, SNT-2117.
**Trap:** Benchmark scores are not deployment safety.
**Mains hook:** Build evaluation capacity as a precondition for regulation.
**Back:** *"Attack it before it attacks people."*

### Flashcard SNT-2110 — Compute capacity and compute sovereignty
**Front:** What is compute capacity and why is it a policy variable?
**Definition (learn):** High-performance infrastructure (GPU or accelerator clusters) for training and running AI. Compute sovereignty: a state's control over accelerator access, data-centre capacity and energy.
**Breakdown:**
- Compute is the infrastructure pillar of IndiaAI (✅ owner 09).
- GPU empanelment figures are policy variables (✅ owner 09 advanced).
- Link to semiconductors (Topic 11) and energy policy.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** See SNT-2111.
**Links:** SNT-2111, SNT-2113, SNT-2114.
**Trap:** Compute alone solves AI gaps — wrong.
**Mains hook:** AI sovereignty begins as a procurement and energy problem.
**Back:** *"Compute is power you rent or own."*

### Flashcard SNT-2111 — Compute unit vs GPU (measurement trap)
**Front:** What is the difference between the 10,000-GPU objective and the "18,000+ compute units" figure?
**Definition (learn):** The IndiaAI Mission's objective is 10,000 GPUs; the headline achievement is "18,000+ affordable AI compute units". These are different measures.
**Breakdown:**
- GPU empanelment RFE issued 16 Aug 2024; financial bids of ten technically qualified bidders opened 22 Jan 2025 (✅ owner 09).
- Official announcement of 18,000+ compute units (✅ owner 09; IndiaAI article, owner-cited).
- Do not convert units into GPUs; do not call empanelment deployed capacity (✅ owner 09).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 10,000 GPUs (objective); 18,000+ compute units (announced); 16 Aug 2024; 22 Jan 2025.
**Links:** SNT-2110, SNT-2113.
**Trap:** "18,000 GPUs have been deployed" — wrong.
**Mains hook:** Measurement discipline: distinguish objective, procurement, announcement and deployment.
**Back:** *"Ten thousand GPUs was the aim; eighteen thousand units was the announcement."*

### Flashcard SNT-2112 — AIKosh dataset platform
**Front:** What is AIKosh?
**Definition (learn):** The official IndiaAI datasets platform, an organised data-access or sharing layer for AI development and evaluation.
**Breakdown:**
- Dataset pillar of IndiaAI (✅ owner 09).
- Datasets reduce training and evaluation bottlenecks (✅ owner 09 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated in owner.
**Links:** SNT-2114, SNT-2116.
**Trap:** AIKosh is not a regulator or a model.
**Mains hook:** Multilingual data is India's bottleneck; AIKosh is one attempt.
**Back:** *"AIKosh stores the fuel for training."*

### Flashcard SNT-2113 — IndiaAI Mission: approval and outlay
**Front:** When was the IndiaAI Mission approved and with what outlay?
**Definition (learn):** Approved by the Union Cabinet on 7 Mar 2024 with an outlay of ₹10,371.92 crore.
**Breakdown:**
- 7 Mar 2024 approval (✅ owner 09; PIB PRID=2012375 cited; PIB fetch returned 403 so ✅ owner-cited).
- An outlay is an authorisation, not expenditure (✅ owner 09 advanced).
- Implemented through IndiaAI, an Independent Business Division of the Digital India Corporation (✅ owner 09).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 7 Mar 2024; ₹10,371.92 crore.
**Links:** SNT-2114, SNT-2115, SNT-2111.
**Trap:** Quoting the outlay as money already spent.
**Mains hook:** Mission-mode public financing for an ecosystem, not a startup subsidy.
**Back:** *"Seven March, ten thousand three hundred seventy-one crore ninety-two lakh, authorised."*

### Flashcard SNT-2114 — The seven IndiaAI pillars
**Front:** What are the seven official IndiaAI pillars?
**Definition (learn):** IndiaAI Innovation Centre; IndiaAI Application Development Initiative; AIKosh Platform; IndiaAI Compute Capacity; IndiaAI Startup Financing; IndiaAI FutureSkills; Safe & Trusted AI.
**Breakdown:**
- Compute: SNT-2110, SNT-2111.
- Data: SNT-2112.
- Skilling: FutureSkills.
- Startups: Startup Financing.
- Governance: SNT-2117.
(✅ owner 09; PIB and IndiaAI pillar list.)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Current official labels.
**Links:** SNT-2113, SNT-2117.
**Trap:** Naming "AI Mission has five pillars" or using older labels.
**Mains hook:** Map pillars to bottlenecks: compute, data, talent, applications, governance.
**Back:** *"Innovate, apply, pool data, compute, fund, skill, and trust."*

### Flashcard SNT-2115 — Institutional home: MeitY, IndiaAI and Digital India Corporation
**Front:** Which institution implements IndiaAI, and what is MeitY's role?
**Definition (learn):** MeitY is the central ministry-level anchor for AI policy and issues advisories and IT Rules amendments; IndiaAI is an Independent Business Division of the Digital India Corporation.
**Breakdown:**
- MeitY: policy and rule-making (✅ owner 09).
- IndiaAI implements the mission (✅ owner 09).
- Digital India Corporation hosts IndiaAI as an IBD (✅ owner 09).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2113, SNT-2116.
**Trap:** NITI Aayog runs the IndiaAI Mission — wrong.
**Mains hook:** Strategy authorship and implementation sit in different institutions.
**Back:** *"MeitY makes the rules; IndiaAI builds the mission."*

### Flashcard SNT-2116 — NITI Aayog and the 2018 AI strategy (#AIforAll)
**Front:** What did NITI Aayog author in AI policy?
**Definition (learn):** The National Strategy for Artificial Intelligence (#AIforAll, 2018) and the Responsible AI for All approach papers.
**Breakdown:**
- Strategy and think-tank role (✅ owner 09).
- Distinct from MeitY's implementation and rule-making (✅ owner 09).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018 (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** SNT-2115, SNT-2113.
**Trap:** Authors of a strategy are not necessarily the mission's implementers.
**Mains hook:** Use the 2018 strategy to show India's earlier policy base.
**Back:** *"Twenty-eighteen wrote the strategy; MeitY runs the rails."*

### Flashcard SNT-2117 — Safe & Trusted AI pillar
**Front:** What does the Safe & Trusted AI pillar do?
**Definition (learn):** IndiaAI pillar for governance tools, guidelines, frameworks, standards and indigenous tools for responsible AI.
**Breakdown:**
- Second Expression of Interest round invited on 20 Dec 2024 (✅ owner 09; PIB PRID=2086605).
- Seeks a balanced, technology-enabled and India-specific approach (✅ owner 09 advanced).
- A pillar, not an anti-technology veto (✅ owner 09 rapid revision).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** EoI round 20 Dec 2024.
**Links:** SNT-2109, SNT-2118, SNT-2121.
**Trap:** Treating Safe & Trusted AI as a ban.
**Mains hook:** Governance is operationalised through proposals and tools.
**Back:** *"Safe and trusted means tools, not vetoes."*

### Flashcard SNT-2118 — AI Governance Guidelines subcommittee report
**Front:** What is the status of MeitY's AI governance guidelines work?
**Definition (learn):** A subcommittee report on AI Governance Guidelines Development released for public consultation; consultation closed 27 Feb 2025.
**Breakdown:**
- A consultation report is advisory, not binding (✅ owner 09).
- Digital India governance-guidelines page is live (✅ owner 09, verified 16 Jul 2026).
- India chose consultation and guidance first, not an EU-style statute (✅ owner 09 advanced analytical).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Consultation closed 27 Feb 2025.
**Links:** SNT-2121, SNT-2119.
**Trap:** Calling the guidelines India's AI law.
**Mains hook:** Keep "articulated" and "enforceable" separate.
**Back:** *"Guidelines guide; they do not yet bind."*

### Flashcard SNT-2119 — IT Rules amendment on synthetically generated information (G.S.R. 120(E))
**Front:** What binding AI-related obligation did India notify in 2026?
**Definition (learn):** An amendment to the IT Rules, 2021 (G.S.R. 120(E), 10 Feb 2026) addressing synthetically generated information and deepfakes, reported effective 20 Feb 2026.
**Breakdown:**
- Subordinate legislation under the IT Act (✅ owner 09; eGazette PDF cited).
- India's only binding AI-specific obligation at verified date (✅ owner 09).
- Regulates distribution and labelling, not model creation (✅ owner 09 advanced analytical).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** G.S.R. 120(E), 10 Feb 2026; effective 20 Feb 2026 (owner-reported).
**Links:** SNT-2120, SNT-2121, SNT-2065.
**Trap:** "A new AI Act" — no; it is an IT Rules amendment.
**Mains hook:** Fast to deploy, but addresses the highest-salience harm at the distribution layer.
**Back:** *"Ten February: the deepfake rules, under the IT Act."*

### Flashcard SNT-2120 — Deepfake and synthetically generated information
**Front:** What counts as a deepfake under Indian rules?
**Definition (learn):** Synthetic media generated or altered through AI in a misleading or deceptive manner.
**Breakdown:**
- Addressed through IT Rules amendments, not an AI statute (✅ owner 09).
- Labelling and intermediary duties (⚠️ owner analytical).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** G.S.R. 120(E) (SNT-2119).
**Links:** SNT-2119, SNT-2122.
**Trap:** "All AI is deepfake" — wrong.
**Mains hook:** Deepfakes threaten elections and trust; labelling is one tool.
**Back:** *"Fake faces, real harm: label and trace."*

### Flashcard SNT-2121 — India has no AI Act: the instrument hierarchy
**Front:** What is the hierarchy from advisory to Act, and where does India's AI regulation sit?
**Definition (learn):** Advisory (persuasive) < guideline or report (non-binding) < Rules notified under a parent Act (binding) < Act (primary law). India has no AI Act; it regulates AI through the IT Act and IT Rules, consumer and criminal law, sectoral regulators and non-binding guidelines.
**Breakdown:**
- Guidelines: non-binding (SNT-2118).
- IT Rules amendment: binding subordinate legislation (SNT-2119).
- No AI-specific primary legislation (✅ owner 09; ⚠️ as at verification date).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As at 16 Jul 2026 per owner; re-check for later statute.
**Links:** SNT-2118, SNT-2119, SNT-2108.
**Trap:** Calling any MeitY guideline India's AI law.
**Mains hook:** Distinguish instrument types in any governance answer.
**Back:** *"Advisory, guideline, rule, act: India is at rule, not act."*

### Flashcard SNT-2122 — Bias and representational harm
**Front:** What is AI bias and why does Indian diversity raise the stakes?
**Definition (learn):** Systematic distortion in AI outputs due to data, model design or deployment context.
**Breakdown:**
- Indian-language and demographic diversity make data quality and fairness central (✅ owner 09 advanced analytical).
- Bias can persist after accuracy improves (⚠️ owner analytical).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2109, SNT-2120.
**Trap:** Accuracy can conceal bias.
**Mains hook:** Test by subgroup, not only overall accuracy.
**Back:** *"Average accuracy can hide who is wronged."*

### Flashcard SNT-2123 — Techno-legal regulation and the pro-innovation stance
**Front:** What do "techno-legal" and "pro-innovation" mean in India's AI approach?
**Definition (learn):** Techno-legal: embedding compliance in technical design (labelling, provenance metadata, consent artefacts, auditability) as well as legal prohibition. Pro-innovation: enabling innovation while building targeted safeguards rather than sweeping statutory restriction.
**Breakdown:**
- Cost of pro-innovation: harms must occur before they are corrected (⚠️ owner advanced).
- Techno-legal design depends on standards bodies and testing capacity still being built (⚠️ owner analytical).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2121, SNT-2119, SNT-2125.
**Trap:** "Pro-innovation means no governance" — wrong; it means calibrated governance.
**Mains hook:** Argue that calibrated, technical-plus-legal regulation scales better, provided testing capacity is built.
**Back:** *"Build the safety into the code, then write the law around it."*

### Flashcard SNT-2124 — India AI Impact Summit (19–20 Feb 2026)
**Front:** When and where was the India AI Impact Summit held?
**Definition (learn):** A summit held in New Delhi on 19–20 February 2026.
**Breakdown:**
- Date and place (✅ owner 09; ⚠️ no binding declaration recorded in owner).
- Used as evidence of India's agenda-setting and Global South positioning (✅ owner 09 advanced).
