- Do not invent a binding declaration (✅ owner 09 guardrail).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 19–20 Feb 2026, New Delhi.
**Links:** SNT-2127, SNT-2139.
**Trap:** Confusing the 2026 summit with the 2025 AI Action Summit in Paris.
**Mains hook:** India's diplomacy is in its convening role, not in a claimed treaty.
**Back:** *"New Delhi, February 2026: summit, not statute."*

### Flashcard SNT-2125 — OECD AI Principles
**Front:** When were the OECD AI Principles adopted and updated?
**Definition (learn):** Principles on trustworthy AI, adopted by the OECD in 2019 and updated in May 2024.
**Breakdown:**
- 2019 adoption; May 2024 update (✅ owner 09; OECD page verified 16 Jul 2026).
- Reference for comparison, not a binding instrument (⚠️ owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2019; May 2024.
**Links:** SNT-2126, SNT-2123.
**Trap:** OECD principles are not enforceable law.
**Mains hook:** Use as global reference for principles, not rules.
**Back:** *"Twenty-nineteen principles, twenty-twenty-four refresh."*

### Flashcard SNT-2126 — EU AI Act risk tiers
**Front:** What are the EU AI Act's risk tiers?
**Definition (learn):** A risk-based framework with unacceptable, high, limited and minimal risk tiers, each with different obligations (owner names the four tiers).
**Breakdown:**
- Unacceptable, high, limited, minimal (✅ owner 09 basic; ✅ EU page overview).
- India's approach is pro-innovation and techno-legal, not EU-style ex-ante licensing (✅ owner 09).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated in owner.
**Links:** SNT-2125, SNT-2123.
**Trap:** Copying the EU model wholesale is not the Indian position.
**Mains hook:** Compare risk-tiering with calibrated governance.
**Back:** *"Four tiers: unacceptable, high, limited, minimal."*

### Flashcard SNT-2127 — Global Partnership on AI (GPAI)
**Front:** What is GPAI and what did India do with it?
**Definition (learn):** The Global Partnership on AI, a multi-country forum; India chaired it (owner-stated).
**Breakdown:**
- India's chair role (⚠️ owner-stated; chair year not recorded in owner — do not quote a year).
- Used for comparison and diplomacy (✅ owner 09).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Year not recorded.
**Links:** SNT-2124, SNT-2105.
**Trap:** GPAI is not an Indian regulator.
**Mains hook:** Use as evidence of multilateral engagement.
**Back:** *"GPAI: a forum, chaired by India."*

### Flashcard SNT-2128 — AI safety summits: Bletchley Park and Seoul (advanced)
**Front:** What do Bletchley Park and Seoul represent in AI governance?
**Definition (learn):** International AI safety dialogues reflecting a turn toward frontier-safety discussion (owner analytical).
**Breakdown:**
- National regulatory styles still differ (⚠️ owner analytical).
- India's stance is participation without copying one external model (⚠️ owner analytical).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated in owner — ⚠️ do not invent years.
**Links:** SNT-2124, SNT-2126.
**Trap:** Summits are not binding treaties.
**Mains hook:** Frontier-safety dialogue is a global trend, not a single rulebook.
**Back:** *"Summits talk safety; statutes differ by country."*

### Flashcard SNT-2129 — Existential-risk debate (framing)
**Front:** How should a UPSC answer treat existential AI risk?
**Definition (learn):** A live global debate, not a settled empirical conclusion.
**Breakdown:**
- Present globally; safe framing is "debate exists" (✅ owner 09 advanced).
- Do not dismiss developmental promise because of it (✅ owner 09).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2128, SNT-2134.
**Trap:** "Existential risk is established fact" — wrong.
**Mains hook:** Balance developmental promise with governance concerns.
**Back:** *"A debate, not a verdict."*

### Flashcard SNT-2130 — Agentic AI: definition (2026 GS-III Q16)
**Front:** What is agentic AI?
**Definition (learn):** AI systems with "agency": they autonomously set sub-goals, plan action sequences, decide and act toward a high-level objective with minimal step-by-step human intervention.
**Breakdown:**
- Distinct from AI that only responds to a single prompt (✅ owner 09; OCR of 2026 GS-III Q16 paper).
- 2026 GS-III Q16: 15 marks, 250 words (✅ owner 09).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2026 GS-III Q16.
**Links:** SNT-2131, SNT-2132, SNT-2134.
**Trap:** Agentic AI is not just a chatbot with a longer prompt.
**Mains hook:** Start with the definition, then the working model, then risks.
**Back:** *"Sets its own sub-goals, acts, adapts."*

### Flashcard SNT-2131 — Agentic AI working model: five stages (+ memory)
**Front:** What are the five stages of an agentic AI loop?
**Definition (learn):** Perception → reasoning → planning → action → reflection, sustained by a persistent memory layer.
**Breakdown:**
- Perception: collects data from environment and systems (✅ owner 09; December 2025 magazine framing).
- Reasoning: LLM interprets context (✅ owner 09).
- Planning: decomposes goals (✅ owner 09).
- Action: invokes tools, APIs or software (✅ owner 09).
- Reflection: evaluates outcome, adapts next step (✅ owner 09).
- Memory: flagged as analytical addition (⚠️ owner, not a verbatim magazine claim).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Five sourced stages.
**Links:** SNT-2130, SNT-2132.
**Trap:** Presenting memory as a sourced stage of the magazine model.
**Mains hook:** Cite five sourced stages; flag memory as architectural inference.
**Back:** *"Perceive, reason, plan, act, reflect, remember."*

### Flashcard SNT-2132 — Generative AI vs agentic AI (tested distinction)
**Front:** What separates generative AI from agentic AI?
**Definition (learn):** Generative AI creates content in response to a prompt and stops; agentic AI uses generated or retrieved outputs as inputs to a goal-directed action loop with tools and multi-step execution.
**Breakdown:**
- Core function: creation vs autonomous action (✅ owner 09).
- Tool/API use: not inherent vs central (✅ owner 09).
- Adaptation: no feedback loop vs reflection (✅ owner 09).
- Example: a chatbot drafting an email vs an agent that drafts, sends, monitors and follows up (✅ owner 09).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2131, SNT-2133.
**Trap:** Agentic and generative AI are not the same thing "marketed differently."
**Mains hook:** The creation-vs-action distinction is the core of the answer.
**Back:** *"Generative writes; agentic acts."*

### Flashcard SNT-2133 — Manus AI as an agentic example
**Front:** What is Manus AI and why is it cited?
**Definition (learn):** An autonomous general-purpose agent that works in a sandbox, accesses the internet and installs software to complete tasks with limited supervision.
**Breakdown:**
- Cited as an operating agentic system (✅ owner 09; December 2025 magazine framing).
- China restricted foreign investment in it, indicating strategic sensitivity (✅ owner 09).
- Illustrative extensions (customer support, code deployment, research synthesis) are ⚠️ analytical, not separately sourced products.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Restriction on foreign investment reported (owner, December 2025 reporting).
**Links:** SNT-2132, SNT-2134.
**Trap:** Not a verified national-level regulation of all agentic AI.
**Mains hook:** A named investment restriction shows the risk is a present governance concern.
**Back:** *"Sandbox, internet, install: a larger attack surface."*

### Flashcard SNT-2134 — Agentic AI: advantages and risks
**Front:** What are the advantages and risks of agentic AI?
**Definition (learn):** Advantages: reduces micromanagement, runs continuously, coordinates tools. Risks: accountability gap, loss of per-step oversight, security and misuse surface, goal mis-specification and reward hacking, weaker explainability.
**Breakdown:**
- Accountability gap: developer, deployer, agent (⚠️ owner analytical).
- Loss of oversight: no per-step sign-off (⚠️ owner).
- Security: sandbox and software access (⚠️ owner).
- Goal mis-specification and reward-hacking (⚠️ owner).
- Explainability: multi-step chain harder to audit (⚠️ owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2130, SNT-2129, SNT-2107.
**Trap:** Listing advantages only; the 2026 question asks for risks too.
**Mains hook:** End with a conditional verdict: useful with oversight, logs and liability rules.
**Back:** *"Continuous helpers, unaccountable steps: govern the chain."*

### Flashcard SNT-2135 — Labour displacement and adaptation (advanced)
**Front:** How should AI-driven labour displacement be framed?
**Definition (learn):** Automation and augmentation affect sectors unevenly; policy should focus on adaptation and skilling, not simplistic doom or hype.
**Breakdown:**
- Adaptation and skilling (✅ owner 09 advanced analytical).
- FutureSkills pillar (SNT-2114).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2114, SNT-2136.
**Trap:** Assuming AI displaces all jobs uniformly.
**Mains hook:** Pair automation with skilling and transition support.
**Back:** *"Uneven waves: train for the shore."*

### Flashcard SNT-2136 — Liability and accountability for AI outputs (advanced)
**Front:** Who is responsible when an AI output misleads, discriminates or causes harm?
**Definition (learn):** A live policy problem with no settled answer in Indian law (owner).
**Breakdown:**
- Developer, deployer and user chains (⚠️ owner).
- Accountability gap is a central agentic-AI issue (SNT-2134).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2134, SNT-2108.
**Trap:** Saying the responsibility is clearly settled.
**Mains hook:** Name the unresolved duty and propose a remedy route.
**Back:** *"Who answers when the machine answers?"*

### Flashcard SNT-2137 — AI, drones and GIS/RS in planning (2025 GS-I Q15)
**Front:** How should an answer use AI with drones and GIS/RS for planning?
**Definition (learn):** Remote-sensing and drone observations feed GIS layers; AI classifies or predicts patterns; planners review and verify in the field.
**Breakdown:**
- Claim: AI extracts patterns from spatial data (✅ owner 09 routed PYQ).
- GIS layers: hazards, population, land use, networks, boundaries.
- Drones supplement, not replace; airspace and privacy limits apply.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 GS-I Q15, 15 marks, 250 words.
**Links:** SNT-2110, SNT-2131.
**Trap:** A classified image is not a fair or lawful plan.
**Mains hook:** AI is decision support; accountable public decisions remain human.
**Back:** *"Map, model, check on the ground, decide accountably."*

### Flashcard SNT-2138 — LLMs: probabilistic prediction and bias (2026 Prelims Q42)
**Front:** What must a candidate know about LLM outputs for the 2026 question?
**Definition (learn):** An LLM predicts likely token sequences from learned patterns; it can produce confident errors and biased output and is not a database of verified facts.
**Breakdown:**
- Probabilistic prediction (✅ owner 09 guardrail).
- Bias and hallucination (SNT-2106, SNT-2122).
- 2026 Prelims GS-I Q42; provisional Set-A key present; answer not recorded or inferred here.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2026 Q42.
**Links:** SNT-2103, SNT-2106, SNT-2122.
**Trap:** An LLM is not a causal engine or fact-checker.
**Mains hook:** Verification needs retrieval, citation and human review.
**Back:** *"Likely words, not verified facts."*

### Flashcard SNT-2139 — AI Action Summit, Paris (2025 Prelims Q95)
**Front:** What is the AI Action Summit and how is it distinct from India's summit?
**Definition (learn):** The AI Action Summit held at the Grand Palais, Paris, in February 2025 (owner route).
**Breakdown:**
- Identify by place and date only (✅ owner 09 audit route).
- Not the same as India's AI Impact Summit (SNT-2124).
- Attendance is not domestic AI regulation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Paris, February 2025 (2025 Prelims Q95; official Set-A key available locally; answer not recorded here).
**Links:** SNT-2124.
**Trap:** Conflating Paris 2025 with New Delhi 2026.
**Mains hook:** Diplomacy and domestic law are different registers.
**Back:** *"Paris 2025 is theirs; New Delhi 2026 is ours."*

### Flashcard SNT-2140 — AI in clinical diagnosis and privacy (2023 GS-III Q5)
**Front:** How does AI in clinical diagnosis threaten individual privacy?
**Definition (learn):** Clinical AI uses patient data to diagnose; it can expose sensitive data and raise consent and secondary-use concerns.
**Breakdown:**
- Benefits: diagnostic support (✅ owner 09 routed PYQ).
- Privacy threats: data use, re-identification, consent (⚠️ owner analytical).
- 2023 GS-III Q5, 10 marks, 150 words.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023 GS-III Q5.
**Links:** SNT-2107, SNT-2136, Topic 12 owner.
**Trap:** Treating diagnostic accuracy as privacy safety.
**Mains hook:** Balance the clinical gain against consent and purpose limits.
**Back:** *"Better diagnosis, careful data."*

### Flashcard SNT-2141 — AI capabilities in industry and society (2020 Prelims Q38)
**Front:** What does the 2020 AI question route test?
**Definition (learn):** Current capabilities of AI in industry and society; the owner routes it to the computing taxonomy and application core.
**Breakdown:**
- AI taxonomy (SNT-2103).
- Applications across sectors (✅ owner 09 routed PYQ).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2020 Prelims GS-I Q38; key unavailable locally.
**Links:** SNT-2103, SNT-2102.
**Trap:** Overclaiming general intelligence.
**Mains hook:** Separate narrow applications from general capability.
**Back:** *"Narrow tools in many sectors, not general minds."*

### Flashcard SNT-2142 — Digital India AI initiative (umbrella narrative)
**Front:** What does the Digital India AI page place under the AI umbrella?
**Definition (learn):** A public-facing explanation of MeitY's AI programme as an umbrella for inclusion, innovation and social impact.
**Breakdown:**
- Data, skilling, ethics and governance, compute, AI R&D and a National Centre on AI (✅ owner 09 advanced; verified 16 Jul 2026).
- Umbrella narrative, not a statute.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Page verified 16 Jul 2026.
**Links:** SNT-2066, SNT-2115.
**Trap:** Digital India AI page is not the legal source of AI obligations.
**Mains hook:** Use it to show official framing.
**Back:** *"AI sits under Digital India, not above the law."*

### GROUP — National Quantum Mission and Quantum Technology (basic 10 + advanced 10)

### Flashcard SNT-2143 — Core theme: quantum technology is four verticals, not one machine
**Front:** What are the four NQM verticals, and why must they be kept apart?
**Definition (learn):** Quantum Computing, Quantum Communication, Quantum Sensing & Metrology, and Quantum Materials & Devices: related but distinct verticals, each with its own hub.
**Breakdown:**
- Computing = processing; communication = secure transmission and key exchange; sensing and metrology = precision measurement; materials and devices = hardware substrate (✅ owner 10 analytical summary; DST NQM page cited).
- Mission design prevents reducing quantum tech to "how many qubits India has" (⚠️ owner analytical).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Four verticals; four T-Hubs (SNT-2160).
**Links:** SNT-2151, SNT-2155, SNT-2157, SNT-2158, SNT-2172.
**Trap:** NQM is only about a quantum computer — wrong.
**Mains hook:** Thesis: a four-vertical capability ecosystem, not a race for one headline machine.
**Back:** *"Compute, communicate, measure, make: four verticals, one mission."*

### Flashcard SNT-2144 — Qubit
**Front:** What is a qubit and how does it differ from a classical bit?
**Definition (learn):** The basic unit of quantum information: a two-level quantum system whose state is a weighted combination (amplitudes) of |0⟩ and |1⟩, collapsing to one classical value on measurement.
**Breakdown:**
- Measurement yields 0 or 1 with probabilities |a|² and |b|² (✅ owner 10 visual).
- Not "both values at once" in the classical sense (✅ owner 10).
- Not simply a faster classical bit (✅ owner 10 trap).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2145, SNT-2146, SNT-2165.
**Trap:** "A qubit is two classical bits" or "a faster bit" — both wrong.
**Mains hook:** The speed-up comes from interference, not clock speed.
**Back:** *"Amplitudes before measurement, one value after."*

### Flashcard SNT-2145 — Superposition
**Front:** What is superposition in quantum computing?
**Definition (learn):** A quantum property that lets a qubit exist in a combination of basis states until measurement.
**Breakdown:**
- A single qubit can be in superposition (✅ owner 10).
- Speed-up depends on interference across computational paths (✅ owner 10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2144, SNT-2146.
**Trap:** Superposition is not the same as entanglement.
**Mains hook:** Superposition is necessary but not sufficient for advantage.
**Back:** *"Many paths until you look."*

### Flashcard SNT-2146 — Entanglement
**Front:** What is entanglement, and does it allow faster-than-light communication?
**Definition (learn):** A joint state of two or more quantum systems that cannot be factorised into independent states.
**Breakdown:**
- Requires at least two systems; a single qubit cannot be entangled (✅ owner 10).
- Measuring one constrains the other's statistics but carries no faster-than-light signalling (✅ owner 10).
- Classical communication is still needed to make correlations useful (✅ owner 10 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2144, SNT-2145, SNT-2152.
**Trap:** "A single qubit is entangled" and "entanglement allows FTL" are both wrong.
**Mains hook:** Use entanglement as the resource for networks, not as a message channel.
**Back:** *"Two or more, linked, silent for messages."*

### Flashcard SNT-2147 — Decoherence and coherence time
**Front:** What is decoherence and why does it drive the engineering stack?
**Definition (learn):** Loss of quantum coherence through unwanted interaction with the environment (heat, vibration, stray fields). Coherence time is the window in which quantum information survives.
**Breakdown:**
- Needs dilution refrigerators, vacuum and shielding (✅ owner 10).
- Explains why quantum materials and devices are a vertical in their own right (✅ owner 10 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2148, SNT-2157.
**Trap:** Decoherence is not a one-off error; it is continuous loss.
**Mains hook:** The central engineering enemy of quantum tech is the environment.
**Back:** *"The world leaks quantum: keep it cold, quiet and shielded."*

### Flashcard SNT-2148 — Quantum error correction: logical vs physical qubits
**Front:** Why are physical qubit counts misleading?
**Definition (learn):** Error correction encodes one reliable logical qubit across many noisy physical qubits; useful computation needs logical, error-corrected qubits.
**Breakdown:**
- Adding noisy physical qubits adds error without correction (✅ owner 10 trap).
- NQM target of 50–1000 physical qubits is a hardware-scaling target, not fault tolerance (✅ owner 10 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** NQM physical-qubit target (SNT-2161).
**Links:** SNT-2161, SNT-2166.
**Trap:** Reading a physical-qubit target as fault-tolerant computing — the biggest analytical error on this topic.
**Mains hook:** Use the logical-physical distinction to qualify every qubit headline.
**Back:** *"Many noisy qubits make one honest qubit."*

### Flashcard SNT-2149 — Qubit modalities
**Front:** What are the main qubit modalities and their trade-offs?
**Definition (learn):** Superconducting (fast gates, millikelvin cryogenics); trapped ion (long coherence, slower gates); photonic (natural for communication, hard for memory); neutral atom; topological (theoretically robust, least mature).
**Breakdown:**
- No modality has won; NQM does not bet on one (✅ owner 10).
- Vertical structure hedges but disperses talent (✅ owner 10 advanced analytical).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2167, SNT-2163.
**Trap:** A company's modality claim is not a national milestone.
**Mains hook:** Hedging suits a follower country; dispersal of scarce talent is the cost.
**Back:** *"Five doors, no winner: superconducting, ion, photon, atom, topology."*

### Flashcard SNT-2150 — Quantum advantage vs quantum supremacy
**Front:** What is the difference between quantum supremacy and quantum advantage?
**Definition (learn):** Supremacy: a quantum device performs some task, however contrived, beyond practical classical reach. Advantage: a quantum device solves a useful problem better or cheaper than the best classical method.
**Breakdown:**
- Supremacy claims exist and are contested (✅ owner 10).
- Practical advantage on a useful problem is not established (✅ owner 10).
- Speed-up confined to factoring, unstructured search, simulation, some optimisation (✅ owner 10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2144, SNT-2148.
**Trap:** "Quantum supremacy means quantum is now better" — wrong.
**Mains hook:** Discipline in readiness claims.
**Back:** *"Supremacy is a stunt; advantage is a use."*

### Flashcard SNT-2151 — Quantum communication
**Front:** What is quantum communication?
**Definition (learn):** Use of quantum principles for secure communication, including QKD-type systems.
**Breakdown:**
- Vertical of NQM led by IIT Madras with C-DOT (SNT-2160) (✅ owner 10).
- Satellite and fibre links; trusted nodes and repeaters are active problems (✅ owner 10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Target: satellite-based secure communication over 2,000 km (SNT-2161).
**Links:** SNT-2152, SNT-2153, SNT-2161.
**Trap:** Quantum communication is not quantum computing.
**Mains hook:** Communication is strategic before fault-tolerant computing arrives.
**Back:** *"Communicate securely by physics, not only by math."*

### Flashcard SNT-2152 — QKD (quantum key distribution)
**Front:** How does QKD protect a key, and what does it need?
**Definition (learn):** Distributes encryption keys using quantum states, so eavesdropping disturbs them detectably.
**Breakdown:**
- Needs new hardware and physical links (✅ owner 10).
- Protects key exchange; does not authenticate endpoints (✅ owner 10 advanced).
- Belongs to communication, not computing (✅ owner 10 traps).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2153, SNT-2151.
**Trap:** QKD is not a computing algorithm.
**Mains hook:** QKD is the strategic, capital-intensive option; PQC is the cheap near-term one.
**Back:** *"Quantum keys reveal eavesdroppers; they need their own wires."*

### Flashcard SNT-2153 — Post-quantum cryptography (PQC)
**Front:** What is PQC and how does it differ from QKD?
**Definition (learn):** Classical mathematics believed hard for quantum computers, deployable as a software or standards upgrade on existing networks.
**Breakdown:**
- Software and standards route, not physics hardware (✅ owner 10).
- Complements QKD; PQC is the near-term practical migration (✅ owner 10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2152, SNT-2154.
**Trap:** QKD and PQC are not the same defence.
**Mains hook:** Sequence PQC first for near-term security; QKD for strategic networks.
**Back:** *"Classical math, new standards, existing wires."*

### Flashcard SNT-2154 — "Harvest now, decrypt later" (advanced)
**Front:** Why does the quantum threat matter today?
**Definition (learn):** Encrypted data stored now with RSA or ECC may be decrypted later when quantum computers exist.
**Breakdown:**
- Turns quantum security into a present-day data-retention and cryptographic-inventory problem (✅ owner 10 advanced analytical).
- PQC migration is the urgent, cheap action (✅ owner 10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2153, SNT-2152.
**Trap:** Saying quantum computers exist and break RSA today.
**Mains hook:** Start the cryptographic inventory now.
**Back:** *"Steal the ciphertext today, open it tomorrow."*

### Flashcard SNT-2155 — Quantum sensing
**Front:** What is quantum sensing and what are its near-term uses?
**Definition (learn):** Using quantum effects for highly precise measurement of physical quantities.
**Breakdown:**
- Most credible near-term deployments: atomic clocks, magnetometers, gravimeters, GNSS-denied navigation (✅ owner 10 advanced).
- Often the first vertical to yield usable output (✅ owner 10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2156, SNT-2160.
**Trap:** Quantum sensors are not smaller computers.
**Mains hook:** Sensing matters for navigation resilience even before fault-tolerant computing.
**Back:** *"Quantum sensors measure the world more precisely."*

### Flashcard SNT-2156 — Metrology and its spillovers
**Front:** What is metrology and why does it matter in quantum tech?
**Definition (learn):** The science of measurement; in quantum tech it includes ultra-precise clocks and sensors.
**Breakdown:**
- Spillovers to navigation resilient to GNSS denial, gravimetry and medical imaging (✅ owner 10).
- Metrology sits within the quantum sensing and metrology vertical (✅ owner 10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2155, SNT-2160.
**Trap:** Metrology is not only laboratory standards.
**Mains hook:** Use navigation resilience as a strategic example.
**Back:** *"Measure precisely, navigate without satellites."*

### Flashcard SNT-2157 — Quantum materials and devices
**Front:** What is the fourth NQM vertical and why is it a substrate?
**Definition (learn):** Hardware, materials and components that enable computing, communication and sensing; official name is "Quantum Materials & Devices".
**Breakdown:**
- Hardware substrate for the other three (✅ owner 10).
- Materials purity and fabrication are central (⚠️ owner analytical).
- Hosted by IIT Delhi (SNT-2160).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2147, SNT-2160, Topic 11 owner (semiconductors).
**Trap:** Generic "hardware" is not the official vertical name.
**Mains hook:** Hardware dependence can cripple mission ambition; materials are the bottleneck.
**Back:** *"Materials and devices: the floor under the other three."*

### Flashcard SNT-2158 — National Quantum Mission: approval and outlay
**Front:** When was NQM approved, with what outlay, and for which years?
**Definition (learn):** Approved by the Union Cabinet on 19 April 2023 with an outlay of ₹6,003.65 crore for 2023-24 to 2030-31.
**Breakdown:**
- 19 Apr 2023 approval (✅ owner 10; DST NQM page cited).
- ₹6,003.65 crore (✅ owner 10).
- Outlay is an authorisation, not expenditure (⚠️ analytical).
- DST NQM page fetch returned empty on 2 Aug 2026 check; owner-stated figures retained — ⚠️ re-check the DST page.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 19 Apr 2023; ₹6,003.65 crore; 2023-24 to 2030-31.
**Links:** SNT-2159, SNT-2160, SNT-2161.
**Trap:** Quoting the outlay as spent money.
**Mains hook:** Mission-mode financing of a pre-commercial technology.
**Back:** *"Nineteen April 2023: six thousand and three crore sixty-five lakh, for eight years."*

### Flashcard SNT-2159 — DST: a Department, not a ministry
**Front:** Which body implements NQM, and what is its status?
**Definition (learn):** The Department of Science and Technology, a Department under the Ministry of Science and Technology, which implements the mission.
**Breakdown:**
- DST is a Department (✅ owner 10).
- Implementing anchor for NQM (✅ owner 10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2158, SNT-2160.
**Trap:** "DST is a ministry" — wrong.
**Mains hook:** Know the administrative level for correct institutional attribution.
**Back:** *"DST is a department, not a ministry."*

### Flashcard SNT-2160 — The four T-Hubs and their hosts
**Front:** Which institutions host the four NQM T-Hubs?
**Definition (learn):** T-Hub 1: IISc Bengaluru (Quantum Computing; Foundation for QC Innovation). T-Hub 2: IIT Madras with C-DOT (Quantum Communication; Samgnya Technologies Foundation). T-Hub 3: IIT Bombay (Quantum Sensing & Metrology; Qmet Tech Foundation). T-Hub 4: IIT Delhi (Quantum Materials & Devices; QMD Foundation).
**Breakdown:**
- Hub names and hosts (✅ owner 10; DST T-Hub page, updated 01 Oct 2024).
- Hub-vertical mapping: computing = IISc, communication = IIT Madras/C-DOT, sensing = IIT Bombay, materials = IIT Delhi.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Announcement page last updated 1 Oct 2024 (✅ owner 10).
**Links:** SNT-2143, SNT-2158, SNT-2162.
**Trap:** Mixing hub-to-vertical pairs (common Prelims match).
**Mains hook:** Institutional distribution across IITs and IISc avoids single-centre risk.
**Back:** *"IISc computes, Madras communicates, Bombay measures, Delhi makes devices."*

### Flashcard SNT-2161 — NQM stated targets
**Front:** What are NQM's stated quantitative targets?
**Definition (learn):** Intermediate-scale quantum computers of 50–1000 physical qubits within eight years; satellite-based secure quantum communication over 2,000 km within India; long-distance and inter-city QKD; multi-node networks with quantum memories; sensors, atomic clocks, quantum materials, single-photon sources and detectors.
**Breakdown:**
- 50–1000 physical qubits (✅ owner 10; DST NQM page cited).
- 2,000 km satellite secure communication (✅ owner 10).
- Targets are not demonstrated capability (✅ owner 10 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Eight years (from 2023-24 start, owner-stated).
**Links:** SNT-2148, SNT-2151, SNT-2162.
**Trap:** Reading targets as achievements.
**Mains hook:** A physical-qubit target is not fault tolerance.
**Back:** *"Fifty to a thousand physical qubits: a target, not a machine."*

### Flashcard SNT-2162 — NQM progress metrics (as of 21 May 2026)
**Front:** What progress figures has DST reported, and what do they not prove?
**Definition (learn):** DST reported 152 researchers across 43 institutions in 17 States and 2 UTs, in 14 technical groups, with eight startups supported and rolling startup calls.
**Breakdown:**
- DST NQM page last updated 21 May 2026 (✅ owner 10).
- PIB parliamentary answer 31 Jul 2025: 14 technical groups across 17 States and 2 UTs (✅ owner 10; PIB PRID=2150820).
- Input and participation metrics, not demonstrated qubit counts or key-distribution distances (✅ owner 10 analytical).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 21 May 2026 (152 researchers; 43 institutions; 8 startups); 31 Jul 2025 (14 groups).
**Links:** SNT-2160, SNT-2161.
**Trap:** Upgrading participation numbers into capability claims.
**Mains hook:** India has built organisational capacity quickly; hardware milestones remain to be demonstrated.
**Back:** *"Researchers counted, qubits not yet."*

### Flashcard SNT-2163 — Majorana 1 and the claim test (2025 Prelims Q47)
**Front:** How should a candidate treat a Majorana or topological-hardware announcement?
**Definition (learn):** As a claim about a possible qubit architecture, not as proof of a useful error-corrected computer, a national network, or deployment in India.
**Breakdown:**
- 2025 Prelims GS-I Q47: Majorana 1 chip, quantum computing and deep learning; official Set-A key available locally; answer not recorded here (✅ owner 10).
- Status test: announcement vs demonstrated capability (✅ owner 10).
- Deep learning is a neural-network method, not a quantum-computing method (✅ owner 10 taxonomy).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 Q47.
**Links:** SNT-2149, SNT-2103, SNT-2172.
**Trap:** Treating a company announcement as a national milestone.
**Mains hook:** Use the announced-tested-deployed ladder.
**Back:** *"A chip announcement is a claim until it computes usefully."*

### Flashcard SNT-2164 — NQM targets and hubs (2026 Prelims Q49)
**Front:** What does the 2026 NQM question test?
**Definition (learn):** NQM, quantum-computing targets and thematic hubs.
**Breakdown:**
- Hubs (SNT-2160) and targets (SNT-2161).
- 2026 Prelims GS-I Q49; provisional Set-A key present locally; answer not recorded or inferred here (✅ owner 10 PYQ block).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2026 Q49.
**Links:** SNT-2160, SNT-2161.
**Trap:** Mixing targets with achievements.
**Mains hook:** Use mission structure rather than headlines.
**Back:** *"Targets and hubs: know which is which."*

### Flashcard SNT-2165 — Qubit concept in context (2022 Prelims Q35)
**Front:** What must a candidate know about a qubit for the 2022 question?
**Definition (learn):** The qubit concept in quantum computing context: a quantum state with amplitudes, not two classical bits.
**Breakdown:**
- Qubit definition (SNT-2144).
- Classical-versus-quantum computing distinction (✅ owner 10 routed PYQ).
- 2022 Prelims GS-I Q35; key unavailable locally.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 Q35.
**Links:** SNT-2144, SNT-2145.
**Trap:** Qubit ≠ two bits.
**Mains hook:** Start any quantum answer with the qubit.
**Back:** *"Amplitudes, not two bits."*

### Flashcard SNT-2166 — Physical vs logical qubits (advanced)
**Front:** What is the difference between a physical and a logical qubit?
**Definition (learn):** A physical qubit is a noisy hardware element; a logical qubit is an error-corrected unit encoded across many physical qubits.
**Breakdown:**
- Logical qubits are the meaningful unit (✅ owner 10).
- Few logical qubits exist anywhere (✅ owner 10 basic).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2148, SNT-2161.
**Trap:** Physical qubit count = capability.
**Mains hook:** Qualify every qubit claim with the logical-qubit count.
**Back:** *"Physical counts, logical capability."*

### Flashcard SNT-2167 — Modality hedging and talent dispersal (advanced)
**Front:** Why does NQM's multi-vertical approach both help and cost?
**Definition (learn):** The vertical structure lets India hedge across modalities rather than pick a winner; the cost is dispersal of scarce talent.
**Breakdown:**
- Defensible for a follower country (✅ owner 10 advanced analytical).
- Dispersal risk (⚠️ owner analytical).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2149, SNT-2160.
**Trap:** Assuming hedging is costless.
**Mains hook:** Hedge with concentration where it counts.
**Back:** *"Hedge the bets, mind the talent."*

### Flashcard SNT-2168 — Quantum threat timeline and cryptographic inventory (advanced)
**Front:** Why reframe quantum security as a present-day problem?
**Definition (learn):** The risk is not quantum computers tomorrow but data encrypted today that is stored and later decrypted.
**Breakdown:**
- Data-retention issue now (✅ owner 10 advanced).
- Cryptographic inventory for government systems (✅ owner 10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2154, SNT-2153.
**Trap:** Waiting for quantum computers before acting.
**Mains hook:** Inventory first; migrate second.
**Back:** *"Inventory the locks before the thieves learn physics."*

### Flashcard SNT-2169 — Quantum bottlenecks: hardware, talent, translation, standards (advanced)
**Front:** What are the four main constraints on India's quantum programme?
**Definition (learn):** Hardware (controlled environments, fabrication, instrumentation); talent (physics, engineering, materials and algorithms together); translation (lab to field); standards and interoperability.
**Breakdown:**
- Hardware bottleneck (✅ owner 10 advanced).
- Talent bottleneck (✅ owner 10 advanced).
- Translation bottleneck (✅ owner 10 advanced).
- Standards and interoperability (✅ owner 10 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2157, SNT-2167.
**Trap:** Believing that research funding alone solves translation.
**Mains hook:** Engineering and ecosystem constraints bind more than theory.
**Back:** *"Hardware, talent, translation, standards: four gates."*

### Flashcard SNT-2170 — Secrecy vs scientific openness (advanced)
**Front:** How does national security affect a frontier-technology mission?
**Definition (learn):** Collaboration accelerates science, but frontier technologies may carry national-security sensitivity, creating a tension with openness.
**Breakdown:**
- Dual-use potential (✅ owner 10 advanced analytical).
- Governance needs a balance of openness and secrecy (⚠️ owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2143, SNT-2169.
**Trap:** Assuming all quantum research is open or all classified.
**Mains hook:** Name the balance and the safeguard.
**Back:** *"Share the science, guard the sensitive."*

### Flashcard SNT-2171 — Quantum complements classical computing (advanced trap)
**Front:** Will quantum computers replace classical computers?
**Definition (learn):** No: quantum systems complement classical computing for specific problem classes.
**Breakdown:**
- Advantage is problem-specific (✅ owner 10).
- Most everyday computing gains nothing (✅ owner 10 traps).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2150, SNT-2172, Topic 25 owner (computing stack).
**Trap:** "Classical computing becomes irrelevant."
**Mains hook:** Complementarity, not replacement.
**Back:** *"Quantum helps some problems; classical runs the rest."*

### Flashcard SNT-2172 — Verticals matrix: computing vs communication vs sensing vs materials
**Front:** Which quantum vertical does each application belong to?
**Definition (learn):** Matrix: computing (processing, algorithms), communication (QKD, secure links), sensing and metrology (clocks, navigation sensors), materials and devices (hardware substrate).
**Breakdown:**
- QKD → communication (✅ owner 10).
- Quantum sensors → sensing and metrology (✅ owner 10).
- Quantum computing → computing (✅ owner 10).
- Materials → hardware substrate (✅ owner 10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** SNT-2143, SNT-2152, SNT-2155.
**Trap:** Mapping QKD to computing or sensing.
**Mains hook:** Use the matrix to avoid catch-all "quantum" claims.
**Back:** *"Process, transmit, measure, build."*

## SCIENCE AND TECHNOLOGY — Part 3: Semiconductors, Data Protection, Biotechnology, Genetic Engineering and Vaccines

### GROUP — Semiconductor Mission and Electronics Manufacturing (basic, owner 11)

### Flashcard SNT-3001 — Semiconductor value chain: design, fabrication, back-end
**Front:** Name the stages of the semiconductor value chain and say which one India's policy is trying to build first.
**Definition (learn):** A chip passes through three distinct stages: design/IP (architecture, logic, layout and verification), front-end fabrication (transistors and interconnects built on wafers), and back-end ATMP/OSAT (assembly, testing, marking, packaging). Each needs different capital, skills and infrastructure.
**Breakdown:**
- Design/IP and EDA tools -> fabless design firm -> tape-out -> foundry/fab
- Wafer fab -> dicing -> ATMP/OSAT -> PCB/module -> phones, servers, EVs, telecom gear
- India's early wins sit in back-end and compound semiconductors; leading-edge silicon fabs are the hardest layer
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Three-stage split and the design-fab-ATMP distinction (✅ owner 11 basic; §1 and §3).
**Links:** SNT-3002 (business models), SNT-3004 (ATMP/OSAT), SNT-3020 (value-capture ladder)
**Trap:** "Chip design, fabrication and ATMP are the same thing." Design makes the blueprint, fabrication makes the wafer, ATMP/OSAT packages and tests the diced dies.
**Mains hook:** Judge the semiconductor push stage by stage. Qualified verdict: approvals in one stage do not prove capability in another.
**Back:** *"Design draws it, fab etches it, ATMP packs and tests it."*

### Flashcard SNT-3002 — Fab, foundry, IDM and fabless
**Front:** Distinguish a fab, a foundry, an IDM and a fabless firm.
**Definition (learn):** A fab is a wafer-fabrication plant. A foundry is a fab that manufactures to other firms' designs. An IDM (integrated device manufacturer) designs and fabricates its own products. A fabless firm designs chips and outsources manufacturing.
**Breakdown:**
- Every foundry is a fab, but not every fab is a foundry
- The business model, not the building, defines the term
- India's ambition spans all four positions; each needs a different policy instrument
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Definitions (✅ owner 11 basic §2; ⚠️ analytical framing in owner 11 advanced §3).
**Links:** SNT-3001, SNT-3003, SNT-3009 (EDA)
**Trap:** A fabless success story does not mean fabrication self-reliance. Design strength and fab depth are separate capabilities.
**Mains hook:** Use the four-model grid to show why design-led and fab-led strategies need different tools. Qualified verdict: a fabless design base can be strategic even without a fab.
**Back:** *"Fab builds, foundry rents capacity, IDM owns both, fabless only designs."*

### Flashcard SNT-3003 — Front-end wafer fabrication steps
**Front:** What happens inside a wafer fab, and why is it capital and utility intensive?
**Definition (learn):** Front-end fabrication builds transistors and interconnects layer by layer on a wafer through hundreds of tightly controlled process steps in ultra-clean clean rooms.
**Breakdown:**
- Deposition -> lithography -> etching -> implantation/doping -> metallisation -> process control and testing
- Needs reliable power, ultra-pure water, specialty gases and chemicals
- Yield improves only through sustained production, which is why a fab can exist long before it is competitive
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Step list (✅ owner 11 basic §3; standard process description).
**Links:** SNT-3005 (nodes), SNT-3006 (lithography), SNT-3023 (yield)
**Trap:** Fabrication is not the same as packaging. Packaging happens after dicing, in ATMP/OSAT units.
**Mains hook:** Show that fab success depends on utilities, materials and learning curves, not only on the approval letter.
**Back:** *"Deposit, pattern, etch, dope, metallise, test: a wafer in six moves."*

### Flashcard SNT-3004 — ATMP and OSAT
**Front:** Expand ATMP and OSAT and explain how they differ.
**Definition (learn):** ATMP is Assembly, Testing, Marking and Packaging, the process steps after wafers are made. OSAT is Outsourced Semiconductor Assembly and Test, the outsourced service model that performs them.
**Breakdown:**
- ATMP describes what is done; OSAT describes who does it for others
- Steps: dicing, bonding, encapsulation, testing, marking, packaging
- India's early approvals include several ATMP/OSAT units, including Sanand and Morigaon
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ATMP/OSAT definitions (✅ owner 11 basic §2); Morigaon and Sanand OSAT units named in the PIB release of 12 Mar 2024 (✅ owner 11 basic §6).
**Links:** SNT-3001, SNT-3008 (advanced packaging), SNT-3016 (2024 units), SNT-3017 (Sanand)
**Trap:** OSAT is not a fab. ATMP/OSAT is easier than front-end fab but still needs quality control, test infrastructure and customer trust.
**Mains hook:** Back-end capacity builds engineering and customer relationships, but it does not prove indigenous wafer-process capability.
**Back:** *"ATMP is the process, OSAT is the outsourced service; neither makes wafers."*

### Flashcard SNT-3005 — Process node labels and mature nodes
**Front:** What does a process node such as 28 nm or 7 nm mean, and why do mature nodes matter?
**Definition (learn):** A process node is a generation label for transistor density and performance. Historically it was a physical gate length, but today it is mainly a marketing-cum-generation label.
**Breakdown:**
- Leading-edge nodes (e.g. 7 nm, 3 nm) cost much more and need EUV
- Mature nodes (28 nm and above) still serve automotive, industrial, power and defence demand
- A late entrant can be competitive in mature nodes
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Node labels and mature-node demand (⚠️ analytical inference in owner 11 basic §2 table; owner-stated).
**Links:** SNT-3006 (DUV vs EUV), SNT-3021 (node economics)
**Trap:** A smaller node is not always better for India. Do not assign a node size to an Indian fab unless an official source confirms it.
**Mains hook:** Argue for segment choice: serve mature and specialty demand first, leading edge later. Qualified verdict: strategic relevance does not require leading-edge capability.
**Back:** *"Smaller is not the only strategy; 28 nm and above still power cars and defence."*

### Flashcard SNT-3006 — DUV vs EUV lithography chokepoint
**Front:** Compare DUV and EUV lithography and name the tightest chokepoint in the chain.
**Definition (learn):** Lithography prints circuit patterns on wafers. DUV (deep ultraviolet, 193 nm, often immersion) serves mature and mid-range nodes. EUV (extreme ultraviolet, 13.5 nm) is needed for leading-edge nodes.
**Breakdown:**
- EUV is supplied by a single global vendor and is subject to export controls
- DUV is sufficient for mature nodes
- EUV is the binding chokepoint for leading-edge nodes
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** DUV 193 nm and EUV 13.5 nm (⚠️ standard technical knowledge, owner 11 basic §2; not fetched). Single-vendor and export-control point (⚠️ owner-stated analysis).
**Links:** SNT-3005, SNT-3003, SNT-3021
**Trap:** A country without EUV can still make mature chips. "No EUV" does not mean "no semiconductors".
**Mains hook:** Use EUV dependence to justify a staged, mature-node-first plus packaging strategy.
**Back:** *"DUV for mature, EUV for leading edge, and EUV is the single-vendor bottleneck."*

### Flashcard SNT-3007 — Compound semiconductors vs silicon photonics
**Front:** What is a compound semiconductor, and why is silicon photonics not one?
**Definition (learn):** A compound semiconductor is made of two or more elements, such as GaN, GaAs, InP or SiC, and suits power, RF and optoelectronics where silicon performs poorly.
**Breakdown:**
- Silicon photonics is a silicon platform for optical interconnects
- Indian scheme nomenclature groups them together for policy convenience only
- Physics and supply chains differ
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Compound-semiconductor examples and silicon photonics classification (✅ owner 11 basic §2; ⚠️ policy-grouping remark owner-stated).
**Links:** SNT-3011 (Semicon 1.0 scheme grouping), SNT-3001
**Trap:** "Silicon photonics is a compound semiconductor." It is a silicon-based platform grouped only in scheme nomenclature.
**Mains hook:** Compound and specialty materials suit India's early entry. Qualified verdict: name the material, not just the label.
**Back:** *"Compound = two or more elements (GaN, GaAs, InP, SiC); silicon photonics = silicon."*

### Flashcard SNT-3008 — Advanced packaging
**Front:** What is advanced packaging, and why is it a realistic route for a late entrant?
**Definition (learn):** Advanced packaging integrates dies through 2.5D/3D integration, chiplets, through-silicon vias (TSVs) and heterogeneous integration to gain system-level performance.
**Breakdown:**
- Chiplets and 2.5D/3D stacking can improve system performance without leading-edge lithography
- TSVs are vertical electrical connections through silicon
- Heterogeneous integration combines different chip types in one package
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Definition and strategic framing (⚠️ owner 11 basic §2 and advanced §3).
**Links:** SNT-3004, SNT-3024 (packaging-first debate), SNT-3022
**Trap:** Packaging is not low-value trivia. Advanced packaging is strategically important.
**Mains hook:** Packaging-first is a deliberate sequencing choice that builds capability ladders. Qualified verdict: it does not replace fab depth in the long run.
**Back:** *"Stack, bond, through-silicon: performance without the EUV queue."*

### Flashcard SNT-3009 — EDA tools
**Front:** What are EDA tools and why are they a strategic dependency?
**Definition (learn):** Electronic Design Automation software used to design, verify and tape out chips.
**Breakdown:**
- Used in logic design, layout and verification
- A concentrated global market
- A real dependency for a design-heavy country
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** EDA role (✅ owner 11 basic §2).
**Links:** SNT-3002, SNT-3013 (DLI includes EDA access)
**Trap:** Design talent is not the same as owning the EDA toolchain.
**Mains hook:** Design sovereignty needs tool access and IP, not only engineers.
**Back:** *"EDA designs the chip; whoever owns the tools shapes the design."*

### Flashcard SNT-3010 — India Semiconductor Mission (ISM)
**Front:** What is the India Semiconductor Mission, and under which ministry does it sit?
**Definition (learn):** ISM is the MeitY-led implementing agency for semiconductor and display ecosystem schemes in India.
**Breakdown:**
- Implementing agency, not the scheme itself
- Semicon India Programme is the scheme package
- Implements the Semicon India 1.0 and Semicon 2.0 framework
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ISM under MeitY (✅ owner 11 basic §4, verified against the ISM Semicon 2.0 page as of 2026-07-16 per owner).
**Links:** SNT-3011, SNT-3012, SNT-3014
**Trap:** ISM, Semicon India, SPECS and PLI are not one scheme. ISM implements; Semicon India is the package.
**Mains hook:** ISM's role is coordination across design, fab, ATMP and materials. Qualified verdict: coordination is only as good as project delivery.
**Back:** *"ISM implements; Semicon India funds; SPECS and PLI sit beside it."*

### Flashcard SNT-3011 — Semicon India 1.0 (₹76,000 crore, four schemes)
**Front:** What is the Semicon India Programme (1.0), its outlay and its four schemes?
**Definition (learn):** The Semicon India Programme is the umbrella scheme package with an outlay of ₹76,000 crore, covering four schemes.
**Breakdown:**
- Semiconductor Fab Scheme
- Display Fab Scheme
- Compound Semiconductor / Silicon Photonics / Sensors Fab and ATMP-OSAT Scheme
- Design Linked Incentive (DLI) Scheme
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ₹76,000 crore and four schemes (✅ owner 11 basic §4 and §6; owner-grounded in ISM pages).
**Links:** SNT-3010, SNT-3012 (2.0), SNT-3013 (DLI), SNT-3007
**Trap:** The ₹76,000 crore figure is the Semicon 1.0 outlay, not the Semicon 2.0 figure.
**Mains hook:** Fiscal support is a means; the test is capacity created and commissioned.
**Back:** *"Seventy-six thousand crore, four doors: fab, display, compound/ATMP, design."*

### Flashcard SNT-3012 — Semicon 2.0 (₹1,27,500 crore, approved 15 Jul 2026)
**Front:** When was Semicon 2.0 approved, with what outlay, and what are its six pillars?
**Definition (learn):** Semicon 2.0 is the next phase of the semiconductor programme, approved by the Union Cabinet on 15 Jul 2026 with a fiscal outlay of ₹1,27,500 crore.
**Breakdown:**
- Six pillars: design; machines and materials; more fabs; ATMP/OSAT; R&D; talent development
- Moves from attracting first movers to ecosystem depth
- An outlay is an authorisation, not disbursement
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 15 Jul 2026 and ₹1,27,500 crore (✅ owner 11 basic §4 and §8, from ISM Semicon 2.0 page verified 2026-07-16; re-verified 2 Aug 2026 per owner).
**Links:** SNT-3011, SNT-3028 (advanced pillars), SNT-3010
**Trap:** Approval is not disbursement or output. Do not state Semicon 2.0 has delivered fabs.
**Mains hook:** The shift to machines, materials and talent shows India treating semiconductors as an ecosystem. Qualified verdict: money authorised is not capability created.
**Back:** *"Fifteen July twenty twenty-six: 1,27,500 crore, six pillars, authorised not spent."*

### Flashcard SNT-3013 — Design Linked Incentive (DLI) scheme
**Front:** What does the DLI scheme do, and what does it not do?
**Definition (learn):** The Design Linked Incentive scheme supports chip design companies, startups and MSMEs with financial and design-infrastructure support, including design tools and EDA access through the DLI portal.
**Breakdown:**
- Targets domestic chip design products and productisation
- Operated through the DLI portal with C-DAC architecture support
- Part of the Semicon India four-scheme package
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** DLI as design-focused scheme (✅ owner 11 basic §4 and §6; DLI portal reference in owner grounding).
**Links:** SNT-3009, SNT-3011, SNT-3001
**Trap:** DLI is not India's wafer-fab subsidy. It supports design capability.
**Mains hook:** Design-linked support converts design-services talent into domestic IP. Qualified verdict: it builds the design layer, not the fab layer.
**Back:** *"DLI pays for designing chips, not for building fabs."*

### Flashcard SNT-3014 — ISM vs SPECS and PLI (electronics manufacturing)
**Front:** How do SPECS and PLI differ from ISM's semiconductor schemes?
**Definition (learn):** SPECS and the electronics PLI are separate MeitY instruments. SPECS supports components and sub-assemblies; PLI supports large-scale electronics and IT-hardware manufacturing. They complement semiconductor schemes but are not part of ISM's mandate.
**Breakdown:**
- ISM: semiconductor and display ecosystem
- SPECS/PLI: electronics manufacturing incentives
- PLI creates downstream demand pull for domestic chips
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Separate MeitY instruments (✅ owner 11 basic §4; MeitY electronics PLI page cited in owner grounding).
**Links:** SNT-3010, SNT-3011, SNT-3001
**Trap:** The PLI macro debate belongs in the Economy note. Keep the technology-capability question in S&T.
**Mains hook:** Downstream electronics demand gives semiconductor plants a market. Qualified verdict: demand pull helps, but does not build process know-how.
**Back:** *"ISM makes chips; SPECS and PLI make the gadgets that buy them."*

### Flashcard SNT-3015 — Approval timeline 2024 to 2026
**Front:** List the official ISM approval tranches and their dates.
**Definition (learn):** ISM approvals are recorded individually in its press-release register; no official aggregate total was located.
**Breakdown:**
- 29 Feb 2024: three units approved
- 02 Sep 2024: one further unit
- 14 May 2025: a "sixth unit"
- 12 Aug 2025: SiCSem, CDIL, 3D Glass Solutions and ASIP
- 05 May 2026: two more units, including Crystal Matrix (Dholera) and Suchi Semicon (Surat)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** All dates (✅ owner 11 basic §5, §8; ISM press-release register per owner, verified 2 Aug 2026).
**Links:** SNT-3016, SNT-3017, SNT-3019
**Trap:** Do not assert a single total number of approved projects; ISM does not publish one.
**Mains hook:** Use the approval ladder to show project pipeline depth. Qualified verdict: approval is the first rung, not production.
**Back:** *"Feb 24, Sep 24, May 25, Aug 25, May 26: approvals, not factories."*

### Flashcard SNT-3016 — Dholera fab and Morigaon/Sanand OSAT units (12 Mar 2024)
**Front:** Which three facilities did the 12 Mar 2024 PIB release identify, and at what stage?
**Definition (learn):** The PIB release of 12 Mar 2024 identified a fab at Dholera and two OSAT units, at Morigaon (Assam) and Sanand (Gujarat), with foundation stones laid.
**Breakdown:**
- Dholera: fab
- Morigaon and Sanand: OSAT/ATMP units
- Status at the time: approved and foundation-stage
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 12 Mar 2024 PIB release (✅ owner 11 basic §6 and §8).
**Links:** SNT-3015, SNT-3004, SNT-3017
**Trap:** A foundation stone is not commissioning. Pair each location with its verb: approved, groundbreaking, inaugurated.
**Mains hook:** Location plus status is the evidence unit for any semiconductor claim.
**Back:** *"Dholera fab; Morigaon and Sanand back-end; foundation stones, not output."*

### Flashcard SNT-3017 — Sanand inaugurations and related milestones (2025–2026)
**Front:** Which facilities were inaugurated at Sanand, on what dates, and what other milestones were recorded?
**Definition (learn):** Three facilities at Sanand, Gujarat were inaugurated in 2026, and two other milestones were recorded.
**Breakdown:**
- Micron ATMP facility, Sanand: inaugurated 28 Feb 2026
- Kaynes Semicon, Sanand: 31 Mar 2026
- CG Semi OSAT, Sanand: 04 Jul 2026
- Tata Electronics fab fiscal-support agreement: 12 Mar 2025
- HCL-Foxconn groundbreaking announced: 20 Feb 2026
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** All dates (✅ owner 11 basic §5 and §8; ISM register verified 2 Aug 2026).
**Links:** SNT-3015, SNT-3016, SNT-3018
**Trap:** An agreement is a financing milestone, not production. An inauguration is not commercial output.
**Mains hook:** Clustering three units at Sanand is itself a supply-chain strategy. Qualified verdict: physical materialisation is real, but qualification and yield remain open.
**Back:** *"Micron 28 Feb, Kaynes 31 Mar, CG Semi 4 Jul: Sanand is the back-end cluster."*

### Flashcard SNT-3018 — First Made-in-India chips and the status ladder
**Front:** What did the "first set of Made-in-India chips" milestone prove, and what does it not prove?
**Definition (learn):** On 02 Sep 2025 a first set of Made-in-India chips was presented to the Prime Minister. This is a demonstration, not a verified commercial rollout.
**Breakdown:**
- Status ladder: approval -> agreement -> groundbreaking -> inauguration -> pilot output -> qualified commercial supply
- A presentation shows a process line works
- It does not show customer qualification or market sale
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 02 Sep 2025 presentation (✅ owner 11 basic §5; ISM register per owner).
**Links:** SNT-3017, SNT-3019, SNT-3023
**Trap:** "India is now producing commercial chips at scale." No official source establishes commercial-scale production or sale.
**Mains hook:** Use precise verbs. Qualified verdict: demonstration proves process capability, not market maturity.
**Back:** *"Presented is not produced; approved is not inaugurated; inaugurated is not sold."*

### Flashcard SNT-3019 — 2025 GS-III semiconductor PYQ route
**Front:** How should a candidate answer the 2025 GS-III question on semiconductor challenges and ISM salient features?
**Definition (learn):** The 2025 GS-III question (Q16, 250 words) asked for the challenges faced by the semiconductor industry in India and the salient features of the India Semiconductor Mission.
**Breakdown:**
- Challenges: capital intensity, EUV/DUV dependence, ultra-pure water, power, specialty gases, materials import, process know-how, talent, small domestic demand, subsidised global incumbents
- ISM features: MeitY-led, four-scheme Semicon 1.0, Semicon 2.0 approval, DLI, project-cost-share support
- Conclusion: a staged strategy across design, back-end and mature nodes
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Q16, 2025, 250 words (✅ owner 11 basic §9 and owner PYQ integration block).
**Links:** SNT-3001, SNT-3010, SNT-3012, SNT-3020
**Trap:** Listing ISM schemes without date-stamping project status loses marks.
**Mains hook:** Argue for a staged capability ladder and date-stamp each claim.
**Back:** *"Name the bottlenecks, name ISM's four doors, end with a staged ladder."*

### GROUP — Semiconductor Mission and Electronics Manufacturing (advanced, owner 11)

### Flashcard SNT-3020 — Value-capture ladder
**Front:** What is the value-capture ladder for semiconductors, and what happens if India only scales final assembly?
**Definition (learn):** A layered model in which value rises from design IP and EDA, through front-end fabrication and back-end packaging/test, to module/board/system integration and domestic demand.
**Breakdown:**
- Design IP / EDA / verification -> wafer fabrication -> packaging/test/reliability -> module/system -> domestic demand, exports and resilience
- Only final assembly means shallow value capture
- Adding design, packaging and selected fabrication deepens capability
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Ladder model (⚠️ analytical, owner 11 advanced §2).
**Links:** SNT-3001, SNT-3021, SNT-3025
**Trap:** Assembly-heavy growth is not semiconductor capability.
**Mains hook:** Map each policy instrument to a rung on the ladder. Qualified verdict: rungs must be built together to reinforce each other.
**Back:** *"Design, fab, pack, integrate: each rung adds value, assembly alone does not."*

### Flashcard SNT-3021 — Node economics and choosing a segment
**Front:** What is the strategic question in node economics for India?
**Definition (learn):** Leading-edge nodes need EUV and steeply rising capital; mature nodes (≥28 nm) carry most automotive, industrial, power and defence demand. The question is which demand segment to serve, not how small a node to reach.
**Breakdown:**
- Leading edge: EUV, single supplier, high capex
- Mature: DUV, serves most strategic and industrial demand
- Specialty and compound niches are a third route
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Node demand split (⚠️ analytical, owner 11 advanced §3).
**Links:** SNT-3005, SNT-3006, SNT-3024
**Trap:** Leading-edge capability is not the only strategically meaningful capability.
**Mains hook:** Argue a segment strategy with a stated demand base. Qualified verdict: mature-node competitiveness can be strategic.
**Back:** *"Serve the demand segment, not the smallest node."*

### Flashcard SNT-3022 — Advanced packaging as a leapfrog
**Front:** Why is advanced packaging a deliberate strategy rather than a consolation prize?
**Definition (learn):** Chiplets, 2.5D/3D and heterogeneous integration deliver system-level gains without leading-edge lithography, making them the strongest technical argument for back-end-first sequencing.
**Breakdown:**
- No EUV needed for system gains
- Builds customer relationships and test capability
- Supports downstream electronics integration
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Strategic framing (⚠️ owner 11 advanced §3 and §5).
**Links:** SNT-3008, SNT-3004, SNT-3024
**Trap:** "Packaging is low-value trivia compared with fabs." It can be an entry point with real performance value.
**Mains hook:** Packaging-first builds a capability ladder. Qualified verdict: it must link to design and fab later.
**Back:** *"Packaging leapfrogs the EUV queue."*

### Flashcard SNT-3023 — Yield, learning curves and customer qualification
**Front:** Why can a fab exist without being competitive?
**Definition (learn):** Yield is the share of good dies, and it improves only through sustained production. Customers also need proof of reliability and consistency before moving orders.
**Breakdown:**
- Yield learning cannot be bought outright
- Customer qualification cycles can outlast construction
- Inauguration dates matter less than qualification
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Yield and qualification (⚠️ owner 11 advanced §3 and §7).
**Links:** SNT-3003, SNT-3018, SNT-3026
**Trap:** Inauguration equals commercial production. It does not.
**Mains hook:** Measure ecosystems by qualified output, not by announcements. Qualified verdict: learning curves are the binding constraint.
**Back:** *"Yield is learned, not inaugurated."*

### Flashcard SNT-3024 — Packaging-first versus fab-first debate
**Front:** State the packaging-first versus fab-first debate and the best-supported middle position.
**Definition (learn):** Packaging-first argues that ATMP and compound niches give faster learning and export integration. Fab-first argues that without front-end depth, value capture stays shallow.
**Breakdown:**
- Packaging-first: faster learning, customer links, export integration
- Fab-first: deeper control of process and materials
- Middle: stage-specific sequencing
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Debate framing (⚠️ owner 11 advanced §5).
**Links:** SNT-3022, SNT-3020, SNT-3021
**Trap:** Presenting either side as obviously right.
**Mains hook:** Use the 2025 PYQ and the critical examine style. Qualified verdict: packaging-first with a fab pathway.
**Back:** *"Pack first, fab later, sequence by stage."*

### Flashcard SNT-3025 — Subsidy versus ecosystem debate
**Front:** Why does a large subsidy not guarantee a viable plant?
**Definition (learn):** Fiscal support attracts proposals, but process engineers, utilities, materials and anchor customers decide viability.
**Breakdown:**
- Subsidy attracts capital
- Viability needs utilities, suppliers and skills
- Anchor customers reduce risk
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Debate framing (⚠️ owner 11 advanced §5).
**Links:** SNT-3011, SNT-3012, SNT-3026
**Trap:** Equating subsidy approval with ecosystem maturity.
**Mains hook:** Judge incentives by spillover into suppliers and skills. Qualified verdict: ecosystem spillover matters more than headline outlay.
**Back:** *"Money brings the proposal; suppliers and skills bring the plant."*

### Flashcard SNT-3026 — Strategic autonomy versus selective resilience
**Front:** Why is full semiconductor autarky unrealistic, and what is the realistic goal?
**Definition (learn):** Semiconductors are strategic infrastructure, but global supply is concentrated in equipment, chemicals and tools. The realistic goal is selective resilience in critical layers.
**Breakdown:**
- Critical layers: design, packaging, power electronics, sensors, compound semiconductors
- Import dependence remains for equipment and wafers
- Security and export-control constraints apply to partnerships
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Framing (⚠️ owner 11 advanced §5).
**Links:** SNT-3024, SNT-3027, SNT-3029
**Trap:** Autarky as a policy goal is not realistic; selective resilience is.
**Mains hook:** Select layers where domestic capability reduces vulnerability. Qualified verdict: resilience through layers, not total self-sufficiency.
**Back:** *"Not autarky, but resilience in the layers that matter."*

### Flashcard SNT-3027 — Implementation constraints for fabs
**Front:** List the implementation constraints that can stall a fab project.
**Definition (learn):** Fabs need dependable power, ultra-pure water, gases and high-reliability environmental controls, plus talent and long gestation.
**Breakdown:**
- Utility intensity: power, ultra-pure water, gases
- Imported tool-chain dependence
- Long gestation and obsolescence risk
- Talent gap: design talent is not process talent
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Constraints (⚠️ owner 11 basic §7 and advanced §7).
**Links:** SNT-3003, SNT-3023, SNT-3025
**Trap:** Design talent is not equivalent to fab-process or packaging-reliability expertise.
**Mains hook:** Name utilities and talent as the bottlenecks. Qualified verdict: policy must address utilities and skills together.
**Back:** *"Power, water, gases, tools, talent: the five stalls."*

### Flashcard SNT-3028 — Semicon 2.0 six pillars (advanced framing)
**Front:** What six pillars does Semicon 2.0 officially identify?
**Definition (learn):** Design; machines and materials; more fabs; ATMP/OSAT; R&D; talent development.
**Breakdown:**
- Design: chip design capability
- Machines and materials: equipment, gases, chemicals
- More fabs and ATMP/OSAT
- R&D and talent
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Six pillars (✅ owner 11 advanced §11, from ISM Semicon 2.0 page verified 2026-07-16).
**Links:** SNT-3012, SNT-3011, SNT-3026
**Trap:** Six pillars are not six approved projects.
**Mains hook:** Pillars show that the policy language treats semiconductors as an ecosystem. Qualified verdict: pillars need delivery metrics.
**Back:** *"Design, machines-materials, fabs, ATMP, R&D, talent: six pillars."*

### Flashcard SNT-3029 — DHRUV64 processor
**Front:** What is DHRUV64, and how does it differ from chip fabrication?
**Definition (learn):** DHRUV64 is a homegrown 1.0 GHz, 64-bit dual-core microprocessor identified in C-DAC public material and linked to India's RISC-V/processor IP effort.
**Breakdown:**
- A processor design headline
- Separate from fabrication, volume production, software ecosystem and deployed capability
- Relates to the computing-architecture owner (owner 25, cross-link only)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1.0 GHz, 64-bit, dual-core (✅ owner 11 basic §10 routed PYQ evidence, C-DAC source; PDF not machine-readable).
**Links:** SNT-3030, SNT-3026
**Trap:** A processor design is not a fabricated, commercially volume-produced chip.
**Mains hook:** Separate design from fabrication and deployment. Qualified verdict: design milestones are inputs.
**Back:** *"DHRUV64: a design headline, not a fab."*

### Flashcard SNT-3030 — 2026 Prelims PYQ on DHRUV64 and DIR-V (provisional key)
**Front:** What does the 2026 Prelims GS-I question on DHRUV64 and DIR-V test, and what is the key status?
**Definition (learn):** The 2026 Prelims GS-I Q79 covers DHRUV64, the DIR-V programme and indigenous computing capability. The 2026 Set-A key held locally is provisional, so no answer letter is recorded here.
**Breakdown:**
- Test point: the processor and its programme
- DIR-V ordinal claim could not be verified from a primary C-DAC page
- Do not convert a question statement into a stored fact
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2026 Prelims GS-I Q79 (✅ owner 11 advanced PYQ integration; provisional key ⚠️).
**Links:** SNT-3029, SNT-3031
**Trap:** Treating a provisional key as confirmed.
**Mains hook:** Not applicable beyond concept recall.
**Back:** *"Q79: DHRUV64 and DIR-V, key provisional, claim unchecked."*

### Flashcard SNT-3031 — 2026 Prelims PYQ on state-level semiconductor plants (provisional key)
**Front:** What does the 2026 Prelims GS-I Q83 test?
**Definition (learn):** Q83 asks about Indian semiconductor plants and their announced state-level manufacturing locations.
**Breakdown:**
- Locations: Dholera (Gujarat), Morigaon (Assam), Sanand (Gujarat), Surat (Gujarat) per owner-listed projects
- Pair each location with its status verb
- Answer key is provisional; none recorded here
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2026 Prelims GS-I Q83 (✅ owner 11 advanced PYQ integration; ✅ owner 11 basic §6 for locations).
**Links:** SNT-3016, SNT-3015, SNT-3030
**Trap:** Matching a plant to the wrong state, or a location to its status.
**Mains hook:** Not applicable beyond concept recall.
**Back:** *"Q83: where each plant sits, paired with its status."*

### Flashcard SNT-3032 — Clustering as a supply-chain strategy
**Front:** Why is the three-unit cluster at Sanand strategically important?
**Definition (learn):** Clustering back-end units in one place builds shared suppliers, skills and logistics, which is itself a supply-chain strategy.
**Breakdown:**
- Micron, Kaynes and CG Semi at Sanand
- Shared utilities and suppliers
- Shows back-end physical materialisation
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Sanand inaugurations (✅ owner 11 advanced §13).
**Links:** SNT-3017, SNT-3020
**Trap:** Clustering is not evidence of yield maturity.
**Mains hook:** Clusters are the physical form of ecosystem depth. Qualified verdict: clusters need customers and yield.
**Back:** *"Cluster the back-end; share the suppliers."*

### GROUP — Data Protection, DPDP Act and Cybersecurity (basic, owner 12)

### Flashcard SNT-3033 — Privacy law versus cybersecurity: two different questions
**Front:** Why are the DPDP Act and cybersecurity law related but distinct?
**Definition (learn):** The DPDP Act governs lawful processing of digital personal data. Cybersecurity protects networks, systems and critical infrastructure from attack, breach or disruption. A single incident can touch both, but the law, institution and remedy differ.
**Breakdown:**
- DPDP asks: was personal data collected and used lawfully?
- Cybersecurity asks: was the system protected, and was the incident handled?
- DPDP actors: Data Principal, Fiduciary, Processor, Consent Manager, Board
- Cyber actors: CERT-In, NCIIPC, sectoral mechanisms, I4C for cybercrime
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Two-domain framing (✅ owner 12 basic §1 and §2).
**Links:** SNT-3034, SNT-3050, SNT-3051, SNT-3062
**Trap:** "DPDP is a cyber law" or "CERT-In regulates consent." Neither is true.
**Mains hook:** Distinguish legal test, institution and remedy in every digital-governance answer. Qualified verdict: complementary but legally distinct.
**Back:** *"DPDP guards data use; cyber guards systems."*

### Flashcard SNT-3034 — DPDP Act 2023: enactment and scope
**Front:** When was the DPDP Act enacted, under which Act number, and what does it cover?
**Definition (learn):** The Digital Personal Data Protection Act, 2023 was assented to on 11 August 2023 as Act No. 22 of 2023. It covers digital personal data, including data digitised later, with reach to processing outside India where goods or services are offered in India.
**Breakdown:**
- Digital personal data only
- Offline and anonymised data fall outside
- Extraterritorial reach where goods or services are offered in India (✅ owner 12 basic §11 answer skeleton)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Assent 11 Aug 2023 and Act No. 22 of 2023 (✅ Wikipedia fetch on the Act; ✅ owner 12 basic §5).
**Links:** SNT-3033, SNT-3044, SNT-3047
**Trap:** The Act is not in force as one block. Enactment is not commencement.
**Mains hook:** Use the 2024 PYQ context: Puttaswamy (2017) led to the Srikrishna draft, the 2019 Bill and the 2022 draft. Qualified verdict: a rights law whose core duties are phased.
**Back:** *"Eleven August 2023, Act 22: digital personal data, phased in."*

### Flashcard SNT-3035 — Data Principal, Data Fiduciary and Data Processor
**Front:** Define Data Principal, Data Fiduciary and Data Processor.
**Definition (learn):** The Data Principal is the individual to whom the data relates (including a parent or guardian in certain cases). A Data Fiduciary determines the purpose and means of processing. A Data Processor processes on behalf of a Data Fiduciary.
**Breakdown:**
- Principal: rights-bearing individual
- Fiduciary: decides why and how
- Processor: acts on the fiduciary's instructions
- Fiduciary must make processors stop processing when consent is withdrawn, unless another lawful basis applies
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Definitions (✅ owner 12 basic §2; ✅ owner 12 basic §3 on withdrawal).
**Links:** SNT-3036, SNT-3037, SNT-3038
**Trap:** The processor is not the fiduciary. Liability and purpose-setting sit with the fiduciary.
**Mains hook:** Map the chain Principal -> Fiduciary -> Processor. Qualified verdict: accountability flows to the fiduciary.
**Back:** *"Principal owns the data, Fiduciary decides, Processor obeys."*

### Flashcard SNT-3036 — Consent Manager
**Front:** What is a Consent Manager, and what must it do?
**Definition (learn):** A Consent Manager is a Board-registered entity that acts as a single point through which a Data Principal can give, manage, review and withdraw consent.
**Breakdown:**
- Must be registered with the Data Protection Board
- Interoperable consent intermediary
- Commencement of its framework: one year after 13 Nov 2025 (s.6(9) and rule 4)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Registration with the Board (✅ owner 12 basic §2 and §6); one-year tranche (✅ owner 12 basic §3; ✅ Wikipedia commencement table).
**Links:** SNT-3035, SNT-3044, SNT-3045
**Trap:** Consent Managers are not in force on 13 Nov 2025; they follow the one-year tranche.
**Mains hook:** Consent infrastructure becomes a regulated market. Qualified verdict: registration is the control point.
**Back:** *"One window to give, manage and withdraw consent: registered, not optional."*

### Flashcard SNT-3037 — Consent and "certain legitimate uses" (s.7)
**Front:** On what grounds may a Data Fiduciary process personal data under the DPDP Act?
**Definition (learn):** Consent, or one of the certain legitimate uses listed in s.7. The s.7 list is closed and replaces the 2022 draft's "deemed consent".
**Breakdown:**
- Voluntary provision of data for the stated purpose
- State provision of subsidy, benefit or service
- Medical emergency
- Employment purposes
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** s.7 list and replacement of "deemed consent" (✅ owner 12 basic §3 and §7).
**Links:** SNT-3035, SNT-3038, SNT-3042
**Trap:** "Deemed consent" is a 2022-draft concept, not the enacted Act's term.
**Mains hook:** A closed legitimate-use list limits state-led processing. Qualified verdict: the state exemption in s.17 weakens that limit.
**Back:** *"Consent first; otherwise only the closed s.7 list of legitimate uses."*

### Flashcard SNT-3038 — Data Principal rights and the s.15 duty
**Front:** What rights does a Data Principal have, and what duty does s.15 impose?
**Definition (learn):** The Data Principal can access information, seek correction or erasure, nominate, raise grievance and withdraw consent. Section 15 imposes a duty not to file false or frivolous grievances.
**Breakdown:**
- Rights: access, correction, erasure, nomination, grievance, withdrawal
- Duty (s.15): no false or frivolous complaints
- The Act lacks an explicit data-portability right
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Rights and s.15 (✅ owner 12 basic §3 and advanced §5 on omissions).
**Links:** SNT-3035, SNT-3037, SNT-3058
**Trap:** Assuming DPDP mirrors GDPR's rights list. Duties on principals and no explicit portability are departures.
**Mains hook:** Rights exist only if grievance redress works. Qualified verdict: enforcement capacity decides usability.
**Back:** *"Access, correct, erase, nominate, grieve, withdraw: and no false complaints."*

### Flashcard SNT-3039 — Significant Data Fiduciary (SDF)
**Front:** What makes a Data Fiduciary a Significant Data Fiduciary, and what extra duties follow?
**Definition (learn):** An SDF is a fiduciary or class of fiduciaries notified by the Central Government based on factors such as volume and sensitivity of data, risk to rights, sovereignty, electoral democracy, State security and public order.
**Breakdown:**
- Status is notified, not automatic
- Extra duties: India-based Data Protection Officer answerable to the board, independent data auditor, periodic Data Protection Impact Assessments and audits
- Duties commence at eighteen months
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** SDF factors and duties (✅ owner 12 basic §2, §3, §6; eighteen-month tranche per owner and Wikipedia commencement table).
**Links:** SNT-3035, SNT-3044, SNT-3040
**Trap:** Saying SDFs must appoint a DPO today. As at the verification date, SDF duties are scheduled, not operative.
**Mains hook:** Risk-based status shows calibrated regulation. Qualified verdict: notification power gives the executive discretion.
**Back:** *"Notified, not automatic: big data, big duties."*

### Flashcard SNT-3040 — Children's personal data
**Front:** How does the DPDP Act treat children's personal data?
**Definition (learn):** Children's data attracts heightened protection, including a bar on processing detrimental to well-being and on tracking, behavioural monitoring or targeted advertising directed at children. A minor is under 18.
**Breakdown:**
- Minor: under 18
- Parental or lawful guardian consent route
- Penalty for children's-data breach: up to ₹200 crore (⚠️ owner-only figure; not confirmed by the fetched Wikipedia page)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Under 18 and bars (✅ Wikipedia fetch; ✅ owner 12 basic §2 on guardian). ₹200 crore penalty figure ⚠️ owner-only.
**Links:** SNT-3039, SNT-3043
**Trap:** The fiduciary is not allowed to track children for ads. Do not assume a children's-data ban on all processing.
**Mains hook:** Children's data is an enhanced-fairness issue. Qualified verdict: protection depends on age assurance and enforcement.
**Back:** *"Under eighteen: no tracking, no targeting, guardian in the loop."*

### Flashcard SNT-3041 — Cross-border transfer under s.16 (negative list)
**Front:** How does s.16 regulate transfer of personal data outside India?
**Definition (learn):** Transfer outside India is permitted except to countries or territories that the Central Government notifies as restricted. It is a negative-list, notification-based model, not an adequacy whitelist and not a blanket localisation mandate.
**Breakdown:**
- Default: permitted
- Restriction: by notified country or territory
- Sectoral regulators (e.g. RBI's payment-data direction) can impose stricter localisation
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** s.16 negative list (✅ owner 12 basic §3 and §6; ✅ Wikipedia GDPR comparison: restriction-based).
**Links:** SNT-3042, SNT-3044, SNT-3059
**Trap:** "DPDP creates blanket data localisation." It does not. Also "s.16 is GDPR-style adequacy." It is not.
**Mains hook:** Negative-list design gives flexibility but creates regulatory uncertainty because the list can change by notification. Qualified verdict: predictability needs published criteria.
**Back:** *"Permitted unless notified: a negative list, not a wall."*

### Flashcard SNT-3042 — State exemption under s.17(2)(a)
**Front:** What does s.17(2)(a) allow, and why do critics focus on it?
**Definition (learn):** Section 17(2)(a) lets the State exempt any of its instrumentalities by notification, on grounds such as sovereignty, security, public order or foreign relations.
**Breakdown:**
