- Exemptions also cover enforcement of legal rights, judicial functions, offence prevention, and foreign-contract processing of non-residents' data (s.17)
- No independent oversight of State processing is written into the statute
- Proportionality (Puttaswamy) is not codified
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** s.17(2)(a) (✅ owner 12 basic §3 and §6).
**Links:** SNT-3041, SNT-3056, SNT-3037
**Trap:** Assuming that the State is bound by the same duties as private fiduciaries.
**Mains hook:** The State is the largest processor and can exempt itself. Qualified verdict: independent oversight must be added.
**Back:** *"The State can notify itself out of the Act."*

### Flashcard SNT-3043 — Penalties under the Schedule
**Front:** Are DPDP penalties criminal, and what is the maximum for a security-safeguard failure?
**Definition (learn):** Penalties are set out in the Schedule, are financial and civil only, and are adjudicated by the Board. There is no imprisonment under the Act.
**Breakdown:**
- Maximum for failure to take reasonable security safeguards: ₹250 crore (⚠️ owner-only; the fetched Wikipedia page did not confirm the Schedule, and it lists a minimum-penalty figure of ₹50 crore that was not reconciled)
- Children's-data breach: up to ₹200 crore (⚠️ owner-only)
- No compensation to the affected individual
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Schedule, no imprisonment (✅ owner 12 basic §3 and §6). ₹250 crore and ₹200 crore ⚠️ verify against the Act's Schedule before quoting.
**Links:** SNT-3040, SNT-3045, SNT-3062
**Trap:** Saying the DPDP Act has criminal penalties, or that the individual gets compensation.
**Mains hook:** Financial-only penalties with no individual compensation are a key critique. Qualified verdict: remedy design matters.
**Back:** *"Money from the Schedule, no jail, no compensation to the individual."*

### Flashcard SNT-3044 — DPDP commencement tranches
**Front:** Which DPDP provisions commenced on 13 Nov 2025, and when do the others commence?
**Definition (learn):** Commencement is phased by G.S.R. 843(E). Board establishment, definitions, rule-making and the RTI s.8(1)(j) amendment commenced on 13 Nov 2025. Consent Manager provisions follow at one year. The compliance core follows at eighteen months.
**Breakdown:**
- 13 Nov 2025: ss.1(2), 2, 18-26, 35, 38-43, 44(1), 44(3); rules 1, 2, 17-21
- 13 Nov 2026: s.6(9) and s.27(1)(d); rule 4
- 13 May 2027: ss.3-5, most of s.6, ss.7-17, s.27 (except 27(1)(d)), ss.28-34, 36-37, s.44(2); rules 3, 5-16, 22, 23
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Tranche dates (✅ owner 12 basic §3; ✅ Wikipedia commencement list, which matches; one-year date 13 Nov 2026 and eighteen-month date 13 May 2027 are consistent).
**Links:** SNT-3034, SNT-3036, SNT-3039, SNT-3043, SNT-3054
**Trap:** The Wikipedia status box says "In force", which conflicts with the tranche structure. Trust the tranche list and G.S.R. 843(E), not the status label.
**Mains hook:** Date-stamp which duties are live. Qualified verdict: enacted does not mean enforceable.
**Back:** *"Thirteen Nov 2025 for the Board; 2026 for consent managers; May 2027 for the core."*

### Flashcard SNT-3045 — Data Protection Board of India
**Front:** What is the Data Protection Board of India, how is it constituted, and where do appeals go?
**Definition (learn):** A statutory digital adjudicatory body established under the DPDP Act with effect from 13 Nov 2025 by G.S.R. 844(E), with a Chairperson and four Members fixed by G.S.R. 845(E). Its orders are appealable to TDSAT.
**Breakdown:**
- Designed as a digital office
- Adjudicates compliance matters; does not make binding regulations
- Appeals: Telecom Disputes Settlement and Appellate Tribunal (TDSAT) (✅ owner 12 basic §4)
- Note: the Wikipedia page describes it as an adjudicator and did not confirm TDSAT
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 13 Nov 2025; G.S.R. 844(E) and 845(E) (✅ owner 12 basic §3, §4 and §8).
**Links:** SNT-3046, SNT-3036, SNT-3043
**Trap:** The Board is not a full-spectrum regulator, and it does not handle cyber incidents.
**Mains hook:** A legally established body without staff is not an enforcement outcome. Qualified verdict: capacity and independence decide impact.
**Back:** *"Established on paper in November 2025; appeals to TDSAT."*

### Flashcard SNT-3046 — Board staffing: advertisement of 6 May 2026
**Front:** What does the MeitY advertisement of 6 May 2026 tell us about the Data Protection Board?
**Definition (learn):** MeitY advertised for the Chairperson and four Members of the Board on 6 May 2026, showing the Board existed in law but was not yet staffed.
**Breakdown:**
- Legal establishment: 13 Nov 2025
- Recruitment: 6 May 2026
- Status at verification (2 Aug 2026): constitution under way
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 6 May 2026 advertisement (✅ owner 12 basic §8 and advanced §13, MeitY PDF cited).
**Links:** SNT-3045, SNT-3044
**Trap:** Stating the Board is adjudicating cases in 2026 without evidence.
**Mains hook:** Institutional capacity lags legal design. Qualified verdict: staffing is the first enforcement test.
**Back:** *"Created in November, advertised in May: still being staffed."*

### Flashcard SNT-3047 — DPDP Rules 2025 and the draft of January 2025
**Front:** When were the DPDP Rules notified, and what was the draft stage?
**Definition (learn):** The DPDP Rules, 2025 were notified as G.S.R. 846(E) on 13 Nov 2025, after PIB released the draft Rules on 05 Jan 2025.
**Breakdown:**
- Draft: 05 Jan 2025 (PIB)
- Final Rules: G.S.R. 846(E), 13 Nov 2025
- Rules are phased: rules 3, 5-16, 22, 23 follow the eighteen-month tranche
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 05 Jan 2025 draft and 13 Nov 2025 notification (✅ owner 12 basic §4 and §8; ✅ owner references to MeitY G.S.R. 846(E) PDF).
**Links:** SNT-3044, SNT-3034
**Trap:** Notifying the Rules is not commencing the substantive provisions.
**Mains hook:** Subordinate rules are where rights become operational. Qualified verdict: rules do not equal compliance culture.
**Back:** *"Draft January, Rules November: notified is not operative."*

### Flashcard SNT-3048 — RTI amendment through s.44(3)
**Front:** What does s.44(3) do to the RTI Act, and why is it contested?
**Definition (learn):** Section 44(3) substitutes RTI s.8(1)(j) so that "information which relates to personal information" is exempt from disclosure. It took effect on 13 Nov 2025.
**Breakdown:**
- Earlier public-interest override and Parliament/Legislature proviso are removed (owner advanced §5)
- Pits privacy against transparency
- The most politically contested part of the Act
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** s.44(3), 13 Nov 2025 (✅ owner 12 basic §3, §6; ✅ owner 12 advanced §5 on removed proviso).
**Links:** SNT-3044, SNT-3057
**Trap:** Thinking the RTI change is a commencement-date item that waited eighteen months. It commenced in the first tranche.
**Mains hook:** A genuine rights-versus-rights conflict. Qualified verdict: a narrower public-interest test is needed.
**Back:** *"Section 44(3): personal information exempt from RTI, from day one."*

### Flashcard SNT-3049 — Omission of IT Act s.43A
**Front:** Which provision of the IT Act is omitted, by which section of the DPDP Act, and when?
**Definition (learn):** IT Act s.43A, which provides compensation for failure to protect sensitive personal data, is omitted by DPDP s.44(2). Section 44(2) commences at eighteen months.
**Breakdown:**
- s.43A: compensation for failure to protect sensitive personal data
- Omitted by s.44(2)
- Commencement: eighteen-month tranche (13 May 2027)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** s.43A and s.44(2) (✅ owner 12 basic §3, §4).
**Links:** SNT-3044, SNT-3048, SNT-3062
**Trap:** Saying s.43A is already gone. It remains until the eighteen-month tranche.
**Mains hook:** Compensation shifts from the IT Act to a penalty-only regime, a point for critique. Qualified verdict: individuals lose a compensation route.
**Back:** *"43A stays until May 2027, then goes."*

### Flashcard SNT-3050 — CERT-In and its 2022 directions
**Front:** What is CERT-In, under which section, and what do its 2022 directions require?
**Definition (learn):** CERT-In is the national nodal agency for responding to computer security incidents, operating under s.70B of the IT Act and administered by MeitY.
**Breakdown:**
- 28 Apr 2022 directions under s.70B
- Report specified cyber incidents within 6 hours
- Synchronise ICT clocks to NIC/NPL
- Retain logs for 180 days within India
- KYC and record-retention duties for VPN, cloud and VPS providers
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** s.70B; 28 Apr 2022; 6 hours; 180 days (✅ owner 12 basic §4 and §6; CERT-In directions page cited in owner).
**Links:** SNT-3033, SNT-3051, SNT-3052
**Trap:** CERT-In is under MeitY, not the Ministry of Home Affairs. The MHA body is I4C.
**Mains hook:** Cyber obligations run on faster timelines than privacy obligations. Qualified verdict: cyber regime is operationally stricter.
**Back:** *"Seventy-B, six hours, one-eighty days: CERT-In's clock."*

### Flashcard SNT-3051 — NCIIPC and Critical Information Infrastructure
**Front:** What is NCIIPC, under which section, and when does an asset become CII?
**Definition (learn):** NCIIPC is the national nodal agency for protection of Critical Information Infrastructure, created under s.70A of the IT Act and functioning as a unit of NTRO. CII is a critical digital system whose incapacitation would have debilitating impact on national security, economy, public health or safety.
**Breakdown:**
- s.70A IT Act
- Unit of NTRO
- An asset becomes CII only when declared or notified as a protected system
- Sector membership alone does not confer CII status
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** s.70A; NTRO unit (✅ owner 12 basic §2, §4).
**Links:** SNT-3050, SNT-3033
**Trap:** NCIIPC is not CERT-In, and it does not cover all digital platforms.
**Mains hook:** CII protection needs declared assets and sectoral depth. Qualified verdict: designation drives coverage.
**Back:** *"Seventy-A: declared CII, under NTRO."*

### Flashcard SNT-3052 — I4C, the 1930 helpline and IT Rules 2021 (2026 synthetic-content amendment)
**Front:** Which body handles cybercrime coordination, and what did the 2026 IT Rules amendment cover?
**Definition (learn):** I4C (Indian Cyber Crime Coordination Centre, MHA) coordinates cybercrime reporting, including the National Cybercrime Reporting Portal and helpline 1930. The IT (Intermediary Guidelines and Digital Media Ethics Code) Rules, 2021 were amended in 2022, 2023 and 2026. The 2026 amendment, G.S.R. 120(E) dated 10 Feb 2026, covers synthetically generated information.
**Breakdown:**
- I4C: cybercrime, MHA
- CERT-In: incident response, MeitY
- IT Rules 2021: platform law under the IT Act, not DPDP
- 2026 amendment: deepfake and synthetic content (reported effective 20 Feb 2026)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1930, G.S.R. 120(E), 10 Feb 2026 (✅ owner 12 basic §4 and §8; ✅ eGazette link cited in owner).
**Links:** SNT-3050, SNT-3059, SNT-3033
**Trap:** I4C is not CERT-In. The IT Rules are not a standalone AI statute.
**Mains hook:** India regulates AI-era harms through existing intermediary law. Qualified verdict: effective intermediary duties matter more than new labels.
**Back:** *"Cybercrime to I4C and 1930; deepfakes to the IT Rules, not DPDP."*

### GROUP — Data Protection, DPDP Act and Cybersecurity (advanced, owner 12)

### Flashcard SNT-3053 — Analytical frame: privacy and cybersecurity are not one bucket
**Front:** Why is it analytically weak to merge privacy and cybersecurity into one governance bucket?
**Definition (learn):** Privacy asks whether personal data is lawfully collected, processed, stored and shared. Cybersecurity asks whether networks, platforms and critical systems resist compromise, disruption or exfiltration. One incident may touch both, but the governing law, institutions and remedies differ.
**Breakdown:**
- Privacy chain: Principal -> notice/consent -> Fiduciary -> Processor -> grievance -> Board
- Cyber chain: operator -> detection/logging/response -> CERT-In advisories -> NCIIPC where CII
- Same breach, two questions: lawful handling? adequate protection and incident response?
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Two-chain frame (✅ owner 12 advanced §1 and §2).
**Links:** SNT-3033, SNT-3054, SNT-3062
**Trap:** A digital lending app can be non-compliant under privacy law while never under cyberattack.
**Mains hook:** Separate legal test, institution and remedy in the first paragraph. Qualified verdict: complementary but separate.
**Back:** *"Two chains, same breach: was data used lawfully, and was the system defended?"*

### Flashcard SNT-3054 — Commencement discipline: enactment, notification, commencement, enforcement
**Front:** Why must a candidate distinguish enactment, notification, commencement and enforcement under the DPDP Act?
**Definition (learn):** Enactment is passage of the Act (11 Aug 2023). Notification is publication of rules or notifications (13 Nov 2025). Commencement is when a provision legally takes effect (tranches). Enforcement is when a functioning body applies it. The compliance core was scheduled, not operative, as at 2 Aug 2026.
**Breakdown:**
- Live at 13 Nov 2025: Board establishment, definitions, rule-making, RTI s.44(3)
- Deferred: consent manager (one year), core compliance duties (eighteen months)
- Write deferred duties in the future or conditional tense
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Tranche list (✅ owner 12 advanced §4 and §13; ✅ Wikipedia commencement table).
**Links:** SNT-3044, SNT-3046, SNT-3047
**Trap:** Writing "SDFs must now appoint a DPO" is factually wrong at this date.
**Mains hook:** A current-affairs answer that date-stamps status shows factual discipline. Qualified verdict: legal establishment is not enforcement.
**Back:** *"Enacted, notified, commenced, enforced: four different stages."*

### Flashcard SNT-3055 — Board design: adjudicator, not regulator
**Front:** Why is the Data Protection Board described as an adjudicator rather than a full regulator, and what is the structural critique?
**Definition (learn):** The Board functions as a digital office that adjudicates compliance matters. It does not make binding regulations or conduct suo motu rule-making, and its members are appointed by the Central Government.
**Breakdown:**
- Adjudicates; imposes monetary penalties from the Schedule
- No binding regulations; no suo motu rule-making
- Appointment by the Central Government: the strongest structural critique (composition, tenure, appointment)
- Appeals to TDSAT
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Board design (✅ owner 12 advanced §5 and §4; ✅ Wikipedia describes it as adjudicator).
**Links:** SNT-3045, SNT-3046, SNT-3056
**Trap:** Calling the Board a regulator on the level of CDSCO or SEBI.
**Mains hook:** Independence in appointment is a design question. Qualified verdict: structure matters more than capacity alone.
**Back:** *"A digital office that adjudicates; the Government appoints its members."*

### Flashcard SNT-3056 — Enforcement asymmetry: the State can exempt itself
**Front:** What is the enforcement asymmetry in the DPDP Act?
**Definition (learn):** The State is the largest processor of personal data in India and is also empowered to exempt its own instrumentalities by notification under s.17(2)(a). There is no built-in independent authorisation or oversight of State processing.
**Breakdown:**
- Largest processor: State
- Exemption power: s.17(2)(a) by notification
- No codified Puttaswamy proportionality test in the statute
- Private-sector provisions alone cannot make the regime complete
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** s.17(2)(a) (✅ owner 12 advanced §4 and §5).
**Links:** SNT-3042, SNT-3055, SNT-3037
**Trap:** Assuming the State is bound equally with private fiduciaries.
**Mains hook:** A privacy regime whose main risk-holder can opt out is incomplete. Qualified verdict: add independent oversight of State processing.
**Back:** *"The biggest processor can exempt itself by notification."*

### Flashcard SNT-3057 — RTI amendment: privacy versus transparency
**Front:** What exactly did the RTI amendment remove, and why is it a rights-versus-rights conflict?
**Definition (learn):** Section 44(3) substitutes RTI s.8(1)(j) with an exemption for "information which relates to personal information". It removed the earlier public-interest override and the proviso on information available to Parliament or a State Legislature.
**Breakdown:**
- Earlier public-interest override: removed
- Proviso for information available to Parliament/State Legislature: removed
- Pits privacy against transparency
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** s.44(3), 13 Nov 2025 (✅ owner 12 advanced §5).
**Links:** SNT-3048, SNT-3044
**Trap:** Calling the RTI change a technical or clerical amendment.
**Mains hook:** The most likely nuanced Mains question on the Act. Qualified verdict: a public-interest test should be restored.
**Back:** *"Privacy won the RTI fight by losing the public-interest override."*

### Flashcard SNT-3058 — What the DPDP Act omits
**Front:** List the features the DPDP Act does not contain.
**Definition (learn):** The Act has no separate class of sensitive personal data, no explicit right to data portability or erasure-by-design, no statutory data-breach compensation to individuals, no framework for non-personal data, and no algorithmic-decision safeguard.
**Breakdown:**
- No sensitive-data category
- No explicit portability right
- No compensation to individuals
- No non-personal data framework; no AI or algorithmic safeguard (the seam with Topic 09 on AI governance)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Omissions (✅ owner 12 advanced §5 and owner 12 basic §5 Limitation 4).
**Links:** SNT-3038, SNT-3062, SNT-3049
**Trap:** Assuming the GDPR-style right to be forgotten or portability is in DPDP.
**Mains hook:** Omissions define the gaps to critique. Qualified verdict: add algorithmic accountability and non-personal data rules through a later law.
**Back:** *"No sensitive class, no portability, no breach compensation, no algorithm rules."*

### Flashcard SNT-3059 — Regulatory overlap: DPDP, IT Act and IT Rules
**Front:** Which laws overlap with DPDP in operational compliance?
**Definition (learn):** DPDP compliance, IT Act obligations (ss.69, 69A, 79), the IT Rules 2021 as amended in 2022, 2023 and 2026, and sectoral cyber norms can apply to the same organisation at once.
**Breakdown:**
- s.69: interception, monitoring, decryption
- s.69A: blocking public access
- s.79: intermediary safe harbour, conditional on due diligence
- IT Rules 2021 (amended 2022, 2023, 2026): platform obligations
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Provisions (✅ owner 12 advanced §5 and owner 12 basic §4).
**Links:** SNT-3052, SNT-3041, SNT-3050
**Trap:** Treating the IT Rules as part of DPDP. They sit under the IT Act.
**Mains hook:** Map every overlapping instrument before concluding. Qualified verdict: layered governance needs clear lead agencies.
**Back:** *"69 intercepts, 69A blocks, 79 shields, IT Rules govern platforms."*

### Flashcard SNT-3060 — National cyber strategy gap
**Front:** What is the status of India's national cyber security strategy?
**Definition (learn):** Official sources reviewed show active institutions (CERT-In, NCIIPC, I4C), but no later formally notified National Cyber Security Strategy replacing the 2013 policy document was located at the verification date.
**Breakdown:**
- 2013 policy document: the older frame
- Institutions active; strategy document status unclear
- Verification date: 2026-07-16 (owner search)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Strategy gap (✅ owner 12 advanced preamble and §5; the 2013 document reference is owner-stated, ⚠️ not separately fetched).
**Links:** SNT-3050, SNT-3051, SNT-3052
**Trap:** Describing a 2013 document as the current strategy without a date.
**Mains hook:** Institutions without a current strategy text make coordination harder to judge. Qualified verdict: publish and update the strategy.
**Back:** *"Institutions active; the strategy document was not located in final form."*

### Flashcard SNT-3061 — Encryption and traceability tension
**Front:** Why does traceability of the first originator sit uneasily with end-to-end encryption?
**Definition (learn):** IT Rules obligations to identify the first originator of a message can conflict with end-to-end encryption, which prevents intermediaries reading content.
**Breakdown:**
- Traceability: IT Rules
- End-to-end encryption: privacy and security design
- The 2024 GS-III question on social media and encrypted messaging touches this intersection
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Tension and 2024 PYQ link (✅ owner 12 advanced §5 and §14; the traceability provision is owner-stated, ⚠️ not separately verified).
**Links:** SNT-3059, SNT-3034
**Trap:** Assuming encryption and traceability are simply compatible.
**Mains hook:** Rights trade-offs are central. Qualified verdict: targeted, accountable access over blanket surveillance.
**Back:** *"Trace the first sender, but keep the message encrypted: the tension."*

### Flashcard SNT-3062 — DPDP versus GDPR: key departures
**Front:** List the key departures of the DPDP Act from the GDPR.
**Definition (learn):** The DPDP Act is not a GDPR copy. It has no separate sensitive-data category, no explicit portability or right to be forgotten, duties on Data Principals (s.15), wide State exemptions (s.17), financial penalties with no compensation to the individual, and an adjudicating Board rather than a full-spectrum regulator.
**Breakdown:**
- Duties on principals (s.15): no GDPR analogue
- Adequacy whitelist absent; s.16 is negative-list
- Penalties civil and financial; no compensation
- Board adjudicates rather than regulates
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Departures (✅ owner 12 basic §7; ✅ Wikipedia GDPR comparison notes restriction-based transfers).
**Links:** SNT-3041, SNT-3043, SNT-3058
**Trap:** "India has implemented GDPR." Use GDPR only as a comparator.
**Mains hook:** Comparison shows the design choices. Qualified verdict: GDPR is a benchmark, not the template.
**Back:** *"No sensitive class, duties on principals, no compensation, negative-list transfers."*

### Flashcard SNT-3063 — Digital signatures, PKI and Web3 PYQ boundaries
**Front:** What do the 2019, 2020 and 2022 routed PYQs on digital signatures, PKI and Web 3.0 test?
**Definition (learn):** A digital signature verified with the corresponding public key supports origin and integrity within a certificate and trust framework. It is not whole-message encryption for confidentiality, and a scanned signature is not a digital signature. PKI supports digital security. Web 3.0 features such as blockchain and user-data control are design aspirations, not automatic privacy outcomes.
**Breakdown:**
- 2019 GS-I Q94: digital signature characteristics and electronic authentication (key unavailable locally)
- 2020 GS-I Q46: PKI digital security context in India (key unavailable locally)
- 2022 GS-I Q32: Web 3.0 features and user data control (key unavailable locally)
- 2019 GS-I Q88: GDPR adoption (comparator only)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PYQ numbers and years (✅ owner 12 basic PYQ integration and owner 12 advanced routed evidence; keys not held locally).
**Links:** SNT-3062, SNT-3034
**Trap:** Treating a digital signature as encryption of the whole message.
**Mains hook:** Not applicable beyond concept recall; use for Prelims statement checks.
**Back:** *"Signature proves who and what; encryption hides; Web3 promises need proof."*

### Flashcard SNT-3064 — 2018 GS-III PYQ on personal data protection in cyberspace
**Front:** What did the 2018 GS-III Q19 ask, and how should it be answered?
**Definition (learn):** The 2018 GS-III Q19 (15 marks, 250 words) asked for a report on the strengths and weaknesses of personal data protection in cyberspace.
**Breakdown:**
- Strengths: rights recognition after Puttaswamy, a dedicated Act, institutional clarity
- Weaknesses: phased commencement, state exemption, limited remedies, enforcement capacity
- Conclusion: pair privacy law with cyber resilience
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018, Q19, 15 marks, 250 words (✅ owner 12 advanced PYQ integration).
**Links:** SNT-3034, SNT-3056, SNT-3053
**Trap:** Answering only the 2023 Act and ignoring cyber institutions.
**Mains hook:** Strength-and-weakness structure with a qualified verdict.
**Back:** *"Strengths under Puttaswamy, weaknesses in exemptions, verdict in capacity."*

### GROUP — Biotechnology Fundamentals and DBT Missions (basic, owner 13)

### Flashcard SNT-3065 — Biotechnology as a platform field
**Front:** Why is biotechnology a platform field rather than a single technique?
**Definition (learn):** Biotechnology links molecular biology, cell systems, industrial processing and regulated product development. It applies living systems or their components to make products or services.
**Breakdown:**
- Four application baskets: agriculture, health, industry, environment
- Tools: recombinant DNA, PCR, tissue culture, fermentation, diagnostics
- Lab-to-product chain: basic research -> pilot scale -> translation support -> products
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Platform framing (✅ owner 13 basic §1 and §5).
**Links:** SNT-3066, SNT-3083, SNT-3069
**Trap:** Reducing biotechnology to genetic engineering or GM crops. Fermentation and diagnostics are biotechnology too.
**Mains hook:** Start with the platform definition, then map applications. Qualified verdict: platform strength depends on translation.
**Back:** *"Living systems plus engineering: a platform, not one technique."*

### Flashcard SNT-3066 — The central dogma and the three intervention points
**Front:** State the central dogma and the three steps that biotechnology techniques target.
**Definition (learn):** DNA is transcribed to RNA, and RNA is translated to protein. Most biotechnology techniques intervene at one of these three steps.
**Breakdown:**
- DNA -> (transcription) -> RNA -> (translation) -> protein
- Gene manipulation acts on DNA; RNA tools act on transcripts; protein tools act on products
- Useful as the opening line of a biotechnology answer
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Central dogma (✅ owner 13 basic §3 item 2).
**Links:** SNT-3067, SNT-3068, SNT-3079
**Trap:** Treating RNA interference as DNA cutting. RNAi acts on messenger RNA, not on DNA.
**Mains hook:** Use the dogma to classify a technique in one line.
**Back:** *"DNA to RNA to protein: intervene at one of three steps."*

### Flashcard SNT-3067 — Recombinant DNA technology
**Front:** What are the tools and steps of recombinant DNA technology, and what is the classic first product?
**Definition (learn):** Recombinant DNA technology joins DNA from different sources into a desired construct. A target gene is cut by restriction endonucleases, joined to a vector by DNA ligase, introduced into a host, and selected with marker genes.
**Breakdown:**
- Restriction endonucleases recognise palindromic sequences
- Vectors: plasmid, bacteriophage or Ti plasmid
- DNA ligase joins the fragments
- Host transformation and selection by marker genes
- Classic first product: recombinant human insulin, expressed in E. coli
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Insulin first major rDNA drug (✅ owner 13 basic §3 and §6).
**Links:** SNT-3066, SNT-3068, SNT-3097
**Trap:** Restriction enzymes cut; ligase joins. Do not swap the roles.
**Mains hook:** rDNA turns a gene into a product only after process engineering. Qualified verdict: lab success is not market success.
**Back:** *"Cut with restriction enzymes, paste with ligase, select with markers."*

### Flashcard SNT-3068 — PCR and RT-PCR
**Front:** What do PCR and RT-PCR do, and why is RT-PCR the standard for RNA viruses?
**Definition (learn):** PCR amplifies a target DNA sequence through repeated cycles of denaturation, primer annealing and extension by a thermostable DNA polymerase (Taq), doubling copies each cycle. RT-PCR first converts RNA to cDNA.
**Breakdown:**
- Denaturation -> annealing of primers -> extension by Taq
- Doubling each cycle
- RT-PCR: RNA -> cDNA -> PCR
- Standard for RNA viruses because RNA is the template
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PCR steps and Taq (✅ owner 13 basic §3 item 4).
**Links:** SNT-3066, SNT-3073, SNT-3072
**Trap:** PCR does not detect antibodies; it amplifies nucleic acid.
**Mains hook:** Diagnostics depends on amplification plus specificity. Qualified verdict: a positive signal needs clinical correlation.
**Back:** *"Denature, anneal, extend: copies double each cycle; RT first for RNA."*

### Flashcard SNT-3069 — Fermentation and bioprocessing
**Front:** What is fermentation or bioprocessing, and why is it the bottleneck that BioE3 targets?
**Definition (learn):** Controlled use of microbes or cells in bioreactors to make useful substances such as enzymes, vaccines, biologics and industrial metabolites. Nutrients, temperature, pH and oxygen are managed to maximise output.
**Breakdown:**
- Converts a lab construct into an industrial product
- Scale-up behaves differently from small batches
- Fermentation is not only alcohol production
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Bioprocessing role (✅ owner 13 basic §3 item 5 and §7; BioE3 bottleneck owner-stated).
**Links:** SNT-3077, SNT-3091, SNT-3084
**Trap:** "Fermentation is only about alcohol production."
**Mains hook:** Scale-up is the commercial filter. Qualified verdict: pilot plants decide viability.
**Back:** *"Microbes in controlled vessels: the bridge from lab to factory."*

### Flashcard SNT-3070 — Tissue culture, totipotency and micropropagation
**Front:** What is tissue culture, and why does plant totipotency make micropropagation possible?
**Definition (learn):** Plant or animal cells and tissues are grown aseptically on nutrient media. Plant hormones (auxin and cytokinin balance) drive regeneration. Totipotency lets one somatic cell regenerate a whole plant, giving micropropagation of uniform, disease-free planting material.
**Breakdown:**
- Aseptic, controlled growth
- Auxin/cytokinin balance steers shoots and roots
- Micropropagation: genetically uniform, disease-free planting material
- Banana and potato planting stock examples (owner routes)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Totipotency and micropropagation (✅ owner 13 basic §3 item 6 and §6).
**Links:** SNT-3065, SNT-3084, SNT-3062
**Trap:** Tissue culture and recombinant DNA are not the same. Tissue culture is a growth technique; rDNA changes genes.
**Mains hook:** Tissue culture improves raw perishable crop quality. Qualified verdict: good planting material still needs markets and extension.
**Back:** *"One cell, one plant: totipotency, sterility, uniform clones."*

### Flashcard SNT-3071 — Stem cells: embryonic, adult and iPSC
**Front:** Classify stem cells and explain why iPSCs avoid the embryo objection.
**Definition (learn):** Stem cells are undifferentiated cells that can self-renew and differentiate. Embryonic stem cells are pluripotent. Adult or tissue-specific stem cells are multipotent. Induced pluripotent stem cells (iPSCs) are adult cells reprogrammed to pluripotency.
**Breakdown:**
- Embryonic: pluripotent
- Adult/tissue-specific: multipotent
- iPSC: adult cells reprogrammed, avoiding embryo use
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Classification (✅ owner 13 basic §3 item 7 and §6).
**Links:** SNT-3072, SNT-3070, SNT-3090
**Trap:** iPSCs are not embryonic stem cells, and they do not come from an embryo.
**Mains hook:** The ethics question shifts when reprogramming replaces embryo sourcing. Qualified verdict: safety and tumour risk still need study.
**Back:** *"Embryo: pluripotent. Adult: multipotent. iPSC: adult reprogrammed."*

### Flashcard SNT-3072 — Gene therapy: somatic versus germline
**Front:** What is gene therapy, and why is germline modification not permitted in India?
**Definition (learn):** Gene therapy delivers a functional gene or corrects a defective one, usually with a viral vector. Somatic therapy affects only the treated person; germline modification is heritable and is not permitted in India.
**Breakdown:**
- Somatic: treated individual only
- Germline: heritable, not permitted in India
- Viral vectors are common delivery carriers
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Somatic/germline distinction and India's bar (✅ owner 13 basic §3 item 8).
**Links:** SNT-3071, SNT-3109, SNT-3124
**Trap:** Gene therapy does not rewrite a whole person's DNA sequence.
**Mains hook:** Somatic therapy and germline editing have different ethics and law. Qualified verdict: lab feasibility is not permission.
**Back:** *"Somatic heals one person; germline passes on, and India bars it."*

### Flashcard SNT-3073 — Molecular diagnostics
**Front:** What do molecular diagnostics detect, and where does PCR fit?
**Definition (learn):** Molecular diagnostics detect a disease or biological state using molecular markers such as nucleic acids or specific proteins.
**Breakdown:**
- Targets: pathogens, mutations, biomarkers
- Methods: amplification (PCR), detection of DNA, RNA or protein signatures
- Bridge from molecular detection to clinical use
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Definition (✅ owner 13 basic §2 and §3 item 9).
**Links:** SNT-3068, SNT-3076, SNT-3091
**Trap:** Diagnostics is a core biotech tool even with no transgenic crop involved.
**Mains hook:** Diagnostics builds public-health capacity. Qualified verdict: validation and quality control decide reliability.
**Back:** *"Find the marker: DNA, RNA or protein says the disease."*

### Flashcard SNT-3074 — DBT: a central department under MoST
**Front:** What is DBT, under which ministry does it sit, and which autonomous institutes does it run?
**Definition (learn):** The Department of Biotechnology is a central department under the Ministry of Science and Technology. It drives research, mission design and policy support, and administers autonomous institutes.
**Breakdown:**
- Autonomous institutes: NII, NIBMG, THSTI, inStem, NABI, RCB
- Mission design and funding
- It is a Union department, not a State body or a ministry
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** MoST parent and institute list (✅ owner 13 basic §4).
**Links:** SNT-3075, SNT-3077, SNT-3076
**Trap:** DBT is not a ministry, and not a State-government body.
**Mains hook:** DBT designs missions; BIRAC operationalises translation. Qualified verdict: design and execution are separate roles.
**Back:** *"DBT: central department, Ministry of Science and Technology, mission designer."*

### Flashcard SNT-3075 — BIRAC: an interface and funding agency
**Front:** What is BIRAC, what legal form does it take, and what is it not?
**Definition (learn):** The Biotechnology Industry Research Assistance Council is a DBT-established not-for-profit Section 8 public sector enterprise. It funds start-ups, incubators, translational innovation, technology transfer and IP management.
**Breakdown:**
- Section 8 public sector enterprise
- Industry-academia interface
- BioNEST incubation ecosystem (owner-listed)
- It is not a biosafety regulator
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Section 8 PSE and mandate (✅ owner 13 basic §4; BIRAC mandate page cited in owner).
**Links:** SNT-3074, SNT-3082, SNT-3085
**Trap:** BIRAC is the statutory biosafety authority. It is not. GEAC and RCGM handle biosafety.
**Mains hook:** Translation support is a missing rung in many biotech pipelines. Qualified verdict: funding alone does not make product markets.
**Back:** *"BIRAC funds and incubates; it never regulates biosafety."*

### Flashcard SNT-3076 — Research funders versus regulators in biotechnology
**Front:** Match DBT, ICMR, CSIR, ICAR, CDSCO and GEAC to their functions.
**Definition (learn):** Research funding, research performance and regulatory approval are three different functions. DBT is biotechnology policy and missions. ICMR is biomedical research under MoHFW. CSIR is industrial and multidisciplinary R&D under DSIR. ICAR is agricultural research under DARE. CDSCO approves drugs and vaccines. GEAC appraises environmental release of GMOs under MoEF&CC.
**Breakdown:**
- Research bodies: DBT, ICMR, CSIR, ICAR
- Regulators: CDSCO (drugs and vaccines), GEAC (GMO environmental release)
- Keep research funding and product approval apart
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Institutional mapping (✅ owner 13 basic §4).
**Links:** SNT-3074, SNT-3075, SNT-3101, SNT-3085
**Trap:** Using "DBT" as shorthand for the regulator. DBT does not approve products; CDSCO and GEAC do.
**Mains hook:** Use the functional split to avoid the one-regulator error. Qualified verdict: clarity prevents blame-shifting.
**Back:** *"Fund, research, approve: DBT, ICMR, CSIR, ICAR fund; CDSCO and GEAC approve."*

### Flashcard SNT-3077 — BioE3 policy (Cabinet approval 24 Aug 2024)
**Front:** What is BioE3, when was it approved, and what is its orientation?
**Definition (learn):** BioE3 (Biotechnology for Economy, Environment and Employment) is a policy approved by the Union Cabinet on 24 Aug 2024 to foster high-performance biomanufacturing.
**Breakdown:**
- Economy, environment and employment as three goals
- Shift from research support to biomanufacturing scale-up
- Six thematic sectors (see SNT-3078)
- Official elaboration: PIB 27 Nov 2024
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 24 Aug 2024; PIB 27 Nov 2024 (✅ owner 13 basic §4, §5, §6).
**Links:** SNT-3078, SNT-3069, SNT-3082, SNT-3094
**Trap:** BioE3 is a policy, not a scheme with a fixed outlay in these sources. Do not invent a figure.
**Mains hook:** BioE3 signals biomanufacturing as an economic strategy. Qualified verdict: outcomes need measured output.
**Back:** *"Twenty-four August 2024: economy, environment, employment through biomanufacturing."*

### Flashcard SNT-3078 — BioE3's six thematic sectors
**Front:** List the six thematic sectors of BioE3.
**Definition (learn):** BioE3 is organised around six thematic sectors that structure its biomanufacturing agenda.
**Breakdown:**
- Bio-based chemicals and enzymes
- Functional food and smart proteins
- Precision biotherapeutics
- Climate-resilient agriculture
- Carbon capture and its utilisation
- Futuristic marine and space research
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Six sectors (✅ owner 13 basic §4, §6; BIRAC and bmi.dbt.gov.in pages cited in owner).
**Links:** SNT-3077, SNT-3076, SNT-3095
**Trap:** Listing five and forgetting marine and space, or merging carbon capture with agriculture.
**Mains hook:** Each sector ties biotech to an energy, health or climate problem. Qualified verdict: sector targets need funded output.
**Back:** *"Chemicals, food, therapeutics, crops, carbon, marine-space."*

### Flashcard SNT-3079 — Bio-RIDE (19 Sep 2024)
**Front:** What is Bio-RIDE, and when was it approved?
**Definition (learn):** Bio-RIDE (Biotechnology Research Innovation and Entrepreneurship Development) is DBT's umbrella scheme, approved on 19 Sep 2024, consolidating research, industry-academia translation and bio-manufacturing support.
**Breakdown:**
- Umbrella for research, innovation and entrepreneurship
- Industry-academia translation
- Bio-manufacturing support
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 19 Sep 2024 (✅ owner 13 basic §4, §8; DBT press-release index per owner).
**Links:** SNT-3077, SNT-3074, SNT-3075
**Trap:** Bio-RIDE is not an independent regulator and does not replace BIRAC's role.
**Mains hook:** Consolidation of schemes shows mission-mode governance. Qualified verdict: consolidation must show outputs.
**Back:** *"Bio-RIDE: one umbrella, nineteenth of September 2024."*

### Flashcard SNT-3080 — GenomeIndia: 10,000 genomes (28 Feb 2024)
**Front:** What did DBT announce about GenomeIndia on 28 Feb 2024?
**Definition (learn):** DBT announced completion of sequencing of 10,000 genomes from the Indian population under GenomeIndia.
**Breakdown:**
- Reference dataset for population-specific genomics
- Uses: pharmacogenomics, rare-disease research, variant interpretation
- Genome data is sensitive personal data
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 10,000 genomes, 28 Feb 2024 (✅ owner 13 basic §4, §6; DBT press-release index per owner).
**Links:** SNT-3081, SNT-3059, SNT-3096
**Trap:** Research value does not erase consent, equity or data-security needs.
**Mains hook:** Genomic data is a national asset and a privacy risk at once. Qualified verdict: governed access, not open release.
**Back:** *"Ten thousand Indian genomes, February 2024: a reference, not a diagnosis."*

### Flashcard SNT-3081 — National Biotechnology Development Strategy 2021-2025 has expired
**Front:** Is the National Biotechnology Development Strategy 2021-2025 current policy?
**Definition (learn):** It was the earlier medium-term strategic frame for translation, capability building and bioeconomy growth. Its stated period has elapsed, and no official successor was located at the verification date.
**Breakdown:**
- Cite as historical policy
- Current anchors: BioE3 (24 Aug 2024) and Bio-RIDE (19 Sep 2024)
- Verification: 2 Aug 2026
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Period 2021-2025 and no successor located (✅ owner 13 basic §4, §8; ⚠️ the strategy's own text was not fetched).
**Links:** SNT-3077, SNT-3079
**Trap:** Calling 2021-2025 India's current biotech strategy.
**Mains hook:** Policy continuity matters; a lapsed frame must be flagged. Qualified verdict: a successor framework is needed.
**Back:** *"Expired on paper: BioE3 and Bio-RIDE are the current anchors."*

### Flashcard SNT-3082 — 2,000 proposals (21 Jul 2025): inputs versus outputs
**Front:** What did PIB report on the first DBT-BIRAC joint calls, and why is it an input metric?
**Definition (learn):** PIB's 21 Jul 2025 review said the first round of DBT-BIRAC joint calls under BioE3 drew over 2,000 proposals. Proposals are inputs; funded projects, pilot plants and marketed products are outputs.
**Breakdown:**
- Input: proposals received
- Output: funded projects, pilot plants, marketed products
- Do not upgrade one to the other
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Over 2,000 proposals; 21 Jul 2025 (✅ owner 13 basic §6, §8).
**Links:** SNT-3077, SNT-3075, SNT-3087
**Trap:** "2,000 proposals proves BioE3 is delivering."
**Mains hook:** Use input-output distinction to test any scheme claim. Qualified verdict: proposal volume is evidence of demand, not capacity.
**Back:** *"Two thousand proposals are input, not output."*

### Flashcard SNT-3083 — Four application baskets of biotechnology
**Front:** Name the four application baskets and one example for each.
**Definition (learn):** Biotechnology supports four major baskets: agriculture, health, industry and environment.
**Breakdown:**
- Agriculture: improved traits, tissue-culture planting material, crop diagnostics
- Health: vaccines, molecular diagnostics, biologics, cell systems
- Industry: enzymes, bio-based chemicals, fermentation products
- Environment: waste treatment, remediation, sustainability solutions
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Four baskets (✅ owner 13 basic §5, §10).
**Links:** SNT-3065, SNT-3084, SNT-3078
**Trap:** Reducing biotechnology to GM crops or to health alone.
**Mains hook:** Build the answer on the four baskets. Qualified verdict: each basket has different regulators and risks.
**Back:** *"Farm, clinic, factory, environment: four baskets."*

### Flashcard SNT-3084 — 2026 GS-III Q5: biotechnology for processing perishable crops
**Front:** What did the 2026 GS-III Q5 ask, and what two examples fit its processing directive?
**Definition (learn):** The 2026 GS-III Q5 (10 marks, 150 words) asks how biotechnology has helped Indian farmers process perishable crops, by giving two examples.
**Breakdown:**
- Example 1: fermentation-produced pectinase and cellulase for juice and pulp extraction and clarification (mango, guava, citrus)
- Example 2: lactic-acid bacteria or yeasts fermenting surplus fruit and vegetables into pickles, beverages or vinegar
- Secondary: tissue-culture planting material; bio-coatings; tannase and naringinase (extension only)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2026 GS-III Q5; 10 marks; 150 words (✅ owner 13 basic §13, OCR of question paper).
**Links:** SNT-3069, SNT-3070, SNT-3083
**Trap:** Citing only GM cultivation traits such as Bt cotton does not answer a processing directive.
**Mains hook:** Frame, two examples, conclusion on cold chain and market links. Qualified verdict: hygienic processing and market linkage are needed.
**Back:** *"Enzymes extract the juice; microbes preserve the surplus."*

### GROUP — Biotechnology Fundamentals and DBT Missions (advanced, owner 13)

### Flashcard SNT-3085 — Biotechnology as a general-purpose platform
**Front:** Why is biotechnology best read as a general-purpose platform technology in strategic terms?
**Definition (learn):** Biotechnology translates biology into agriculture, health products, industrial processes, environmental services and high-value manufacturing. The strategic question is whether DBT-led mission architecture can convert research depth into scalable biomanufacturing.
**Breakdown:**
- Discovery science -> prototype -> pilot -> regulatory support -> deployment
- DBT: mission design and policy
- BIRAC: funding, incubation, translation, industry interface
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Platform frame (⚠️ analytical, owner 13 advanced §1 and §2).
**Links:** SNT-3065, SNT-3086, SNT-3076
**Trap:** Treating biotechnology as a purely laboratory science.
**Mains hook:** The platform argument is the entry to any biotech answer. Qualified verdict: platform value depends on translation.
**Back:** *"A platform: biology in, products out, through missions and bridges."*

### Flashcard SNT-3086 — The bioeconomy translation chain
**Front:** Lay out the translation chain from discovery science to market deployment.
**Definition (learn):** Discovery science identifies a gene, enzyme, cell or pathway; prototype proves a concept; pilot fermentation scales it; regulatory and product support follows; manufacturing and market deployment complete it.
**Breakdown:**
- Discovery -> prototype -> pilot scale-up -> regulatory support -> manufacturing and market
- DBT designs; BIRAC bridges
- Each step has a distinct failure mode
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Chain (✅ owner 13 advanced §2 and §4).
**Links:** SNT-3085, SNT-3069, SNT-3091
**Trap:** Lab validation proves feasibility, not commercial viability.
**Mains hook:** Use the chain to show where budgets are failing. Qualified verdict: map the stage before prescribing funding.
**Back:** *"Discover, prototype, scale, approve, sell: five gates."*

### Flashcard SNT-3087 — Science funding versus translation funding
**Front:** Why does pure research funding not create biomanufacturing capability?
**Definition (learn):** Research grants support discovery, but biomanufacturing needs pilot infrastructure, scale-up, validation and translation finance.
**Breakdown:**
- Discovery is funded by research grants
- Translation needs incubators, pilot facilities and regulatory readiness
- Mission-mode challenge calls fill the gap
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Debate framing (⚠️ owner 13 advanced §5).
**Links:** SNT-3082, SNT-3075, SNT-3086
**Trap:** Counting publications as industrial capacity.
**Mains hook:** Funding must follow the translation gap. Qualified verdict: translation money, not only research money.
**Back:** *"Grants make papers; translation makes plants."*

### Flashcard SNT-3088 — Public-good science versus commercial IP
**Front:** How should biotechnology policy balance open science and product incentives?
**Definition (learn):** Open scientific progress benefits everyone, but product developers need IP incentives to justify translation costs.
**Breakdown:**
- Public good: open research and data
- Product incentive: patents and exclusive rights
- Balance: licensing, public-sector access and open platforms
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Debate framing (⚠️ owner 13 advanced §5). Patent-specific detail belongs to the Intellectual Property Rights owner (file 17), not this part.
**Links:** SNT-3087, SNT-3089
**Trap:** Assuming IP always blocks access; licensing often balances it.
**Mains hook:** Policy must balance access and reward. Qualified verdict: public-sector licensing helps.
**Back:** *"Share the science, reward the product, license the difference."*

### Flashcard SNT-3089 — Biosafety and quality oversight
**Front:** Why can fast innovation not bypass biosafety, validation and ethics?
**Definition (learn):** Biotechnology raises biosafety, dual-use risk, equity and ecological concerns. Fast innovation must still pass validation, safety and ethical scrutiny.
**Breakdown:**
- Biosafety and dual-use risk
- Validation and GMP-quality production
- Ethics applies beyond GM crops (diagnostics, cell systems, industrial microbes)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Oversight framing (⚠️ owner 13 advanced §5, §8).
**Links:** SNT-3101, SNT-3072, SNT-3090
**Trap:** Biosafety is only a GM-crop issue. It is not.
**Mains hook:** Safety and speed are both required. Qualified verdict: credible regulation sustains public trust.
**Back:** *"Speed without safety is a recall, not a product."*

### Flashcard SNT-3090 — Biofoundries and shared infrastructure
**Front:** What is a biofoundry, and why does shared infrastructure matter?
**Definition (learn):** A biofoundry is shared infrastructure for rapid design-build-test-learn cycles and scale-up in biotechnology. Biomanufacturing initiatives on BIRAC and PIB sources point toward shared translation infrastructure.
**Breakdown:**
- Design-build-test-learn cycles
- Shared pilot and scale-up facilities
- Lowers entry cost for start-ups
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Concept and BIRAC biomanufacturing reference (✅ owner 13 basic §2, advanced §9).
**Links:** SNT-3086, SNT-3091, SNT-3075
**Trap:** A biofoundry is shared infrastructure, not a regulator.
**Mains hook:** Shared facilities convert research into product. Qualified verdict: utilisation rates decide value.
**Back:** *"Biofoundry: a shared lab that builds, tests and scales biology."*

### Flashcard SNT-3091 — Infrastructure and financing gaps
**Front:** Name the main implementation gaps in translating biotechnology.
**Definition (learn):** Scale-up, infrastructure, regulatory uncertainty and financing gaps slow translation.
**Breakdown:**
- Scale-up gap: benchtop vs pilot plant
- Infrastructure gap: biofoundries, test facilities, GMP plants
- Regulatory uncertainty: several regulators per product
- Financing gap: long gestation and technical failure risk
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Constraints (✅ owner 13 basic §5 limitations; ⚠️ owner 13 advanced §7).
**Links:** SNT-3069, SNT-3086, SNT-3090
**Trap:** Assuming an approved scheme removes the scale-up risk.
**Mains hook:** Name the gap, then the instrument. Qualified verdict: finance and pilot capacity first.
**Back:** *"Scale, infrastructure, rules, money: four gaps."*

### Flashcard SNT-3092 — 2023 Prelims PYQs: biofilters, Wolbachia and aerial metagenomics
**Front:** What do the 2023 Prelims GS-I questions on biofilters, Wolbachia and aerial metagenomics test?
**Definition (learn):** Q55 tests biofilters in recirculating aquaculture water treatment. Q67 tests the Wolbachia method for mosquito-borne viral disease control. Q69 tests collecting DNA from air (aerial metagenomics). Official keys are not held locally.
**Breakdown:**
- Biofilter: attached microbes convert nitrogenous waste; depends on oxygen, flow, temperature
- Wolbachia: alters vector-pathogen dynamics, not a conventional insecticide
- Aerial metagenomics: DNA in air shows biological material, not proof of a live or harmful organism
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023 GS-I Q55, Q67, Q69 (✅ owner 13 basic PYQ integration and routed evidence; keys unavailable).
**Links:** SNT-3069, SNT-3073
**Trap:** Aerial DNA proves presence of living disease agents.
**Mains hook:** Not applicable beyond concept recall.
**Back:** *"Biofilter cleans water, Wolbachia changes vectors, air DNA shows presence only."*

### Flashcard SNT-3093 — 2022 Prelims PYQ: DNA barcoding
**Front:** What does DNA barcoding do, and what is the trap in the 2022 Q99?
**Definition (learn):** DNA barcoding uses a short standard DNA marker to identify species or food material. It is not a full genome diagnosis, and it needs reference databases and controls.
**Breakdown:**
- Short standard marker
- Species and food identification
- Reference database needed for identification
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 GS-I Q99 (✅ owner 13 basic PYQ integration; key unavailable).
**Links:** SNT-3073, SNT-3080
**Trap:** Barcoding is not proof of origin without reference data.
**Mains hook:** Not applicable beyond concept recall.
**Back:** *"Short marker, species name, reference database required."*

### Flashcard SNT-3094 — 2018, 2021 GS-III PYQs on biotech and biopharma
**Front:** What did the 2018 and 2021 GS-III biotechnology questions ask?
**Definition (learn):** The 2018 GS-III Q15 asked about biotechnology activity in India and biopharma sector benefits (15 marks, 250 words). The 2021 GS-III Q15 asked about applied biotechnology R&D achievements and social upliftment potential (15 marks, 250 words).
**Breakdown:**
- 2018: activity and benefits, including biopharma
- 2021: R&D achievements, social upliftment
- Structure: context, dimensions, evidence, counterpoint, conclusion
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018 Q15 and 2021 Q15 (✅ owner 13 advanced PYQ integration).
**Links:** SNT-3077, SNT-3083, SNT-3113
**Trap:** Answering only with research counts rather than translated products.
**Mains hook:** Qualified verdict: scientific talent is not a marketed product.
**Back:** *"Activity and benefits (2018), achievements and upliftment (2021)."*

### Flashcard SNT-3095 — 2023 GS-III PYQ: microorganisms and the fuel shortage
**Front:** What does the 2023 GS-III Q6 ask about microorganisms and fuel shortage?
**Definition (learn):** The 2023 GS-III Q6 (10 marks, 150 words) asks how microorganisms can help address the fuel shortage in various ways.
**Breakdown:**
- Anaerobic digestion to biogas
- Fermentation-based biofuels
- Enzyme and bioprocess routes from biomass or waste
- Qualification: feedstock, water and land use, lifecycle emissions, scale
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023 GS-III Q6; 10 marks; 150 words (✅ owner 13 advanced PYQ integration).
**Links:** SNT-3069, SNT-3078, SNT-3094
**Trap:** Biofuel is not automatically clean; feedstock competition matters.
**Mains hook:** Biology lowers fossil dependence only with feedstock and lifecycle discipline. Qualified verdict: complement, not substitute, for other clean-energy routes.
**Back:** *"Microbes make biogas and biofuels; feedstock decides whether it helps."*

### GROUP — Genetic Engineering, GM Crops and CRISPR (basic, owner 14)

### Flashcard SNT-3096 — Transgenic GM and gene-edited crops are separate regulatory categories
**Front:** Why must transgenic GM crops and gene-edited crops be kept separate in India?
**Definition (learn):** Since 2022, India treats certain gene-edited plants without exogenous foreign DNA (SDN-1 and SDN-2) on a lighter track, while transgenic GMOs stay on the classic GMO route under the Rules, 1989.
**Breakdown:**
- Transgenic: foreign gene inserted -> GMO biosafety regime -> GEAC-centred appraisal
- Gene-edited SDN-1/SDN-2: no foreign DNA -> exempt from Rules 7-11 (2022)
- SDN-3 (gene-sized insertion): full GMO-type scrutiny
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Two-track framing (✅ owner 14 basic §1 and §3).
**Links:** SNT-3097, SNT-3111, SNT-3112, SNT-3117
**Trap:** "GM crop and gene-edited crop are always the same thing."
**Mains hook:** Separate the regulatory track from the tool. Qualified verdict: regulation should follow the final product's characteristics.
**Back:** *"Foreign gene: GMO route. Native-genome edit: lighter 2022 track."*

### Flashcard SNT-3097 — Genetic engineering and transgenic, cisgenic, intragenic
**Front:** Define genetic engineering and distinguish transgenic, cisgenic and intragenic constructs.
**Definition (learn):** Genetic engineering is deliberate modification of genetic material to create a desired trait. Transgenic DNA comes from an unrelated species. Cisgenic DNA comes from the same or a sexually compatible species. Intragenic constructs are rearranged elements from the same species' gene pool.
**Breakdown:**
- Transgenic: unrelated species
- Cisgenic: same or compatible species, transferred intact
- Intragenic: rearranged same-species elements
- All three are GM in Indian law; the source of DNA differs
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Definitions (✅ owner 14 basic §2).
**Links:** SNT-3096, SNT-3117, SNT-3067
**Trap:** Assuming cisgenic is outside GM law in India. It sits inside the GMO framework.
**Mains hook:** Biological novelty and legal category can diverge. Qualified verdict: the reform debate centres on this mismatch.
**Back:** *"Other species, sister species, same species: three flavours of GM."*

### Flashcard SNT-3098 — GM versus hybrid: DMH-11 is both
**Front:** Why is a hybrid not a GM crop, and why is DMH-11 confusing?
**Definition (learn):** A hybrid is produced by controlled cross-pollination between parent lines. It involves no laboratory gene transfer and is not regulated as a GMO. DMH-11 is a GM mustard hybrid, so it is both a hybrid and transgenic.
**Breakdown:**
- Conventional hybrid: cross-pollination
- Transgenic: recombinant DNA gene transfer
- DMH-11: hybrid plus transgenic construct
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Definitions (✅ owner 14 basic §2).
**Links:** SNT-3113, SNT-3096
**Trap:** "A hybrid crop is a GM crop." Only DMH-11-type constructs are both.
**Mains hook:** Not applicable beyond concept recall; use for Prelims.
**Back:** *"Hybrid by cross, GM by gene transfer; DMH-11 has both."*

### Flashcard SNT-3099 — GEAC under MoEF&CC
**Front:** What is GEAC, which ministry houses it, and what does it appraise?
**Definition (learn):** The Genetic Engineering Appraisal Committee is the apex environmental appraisal body for release of genetically engineered organisms and products. It sits under MoEF&CC.
**Breakdown:**
- Appraises environmental release and large-scale use
- Also appraises experimental field-trial proposals
- Not part of DBT
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** MoEF&CC placement (✅ owner 14 basic §4, §6; GEAC about page cited in owner).
**Links:** SNT-3100, SNT-3101, SNT-3113
**Trap:** "GEAC is under DBT." It is under MoEF&CC.
**Mains hook:** GEAC's environmental remit distinguishes it from research approvals. Qualified verdict: environmental appraisal must be independent and transparent.
**Back:** *"GEAC: environment ministry, environmental release."*

### Flashcard SNT-3100 — RCGM (DBT) versus GEAC (MoEF&CC)
**Front:** Which body approves research and contained trials, and which approves environmental release?
**Definition (learn):** RCGM (Review Committee on Genetic Manipulation) under DBT clears research and contained trials. GEAC under MoEF&CC appraises environmental release and large-scale use.
**Breakdown:**
- RCGM: DBT, research and contained trials
- GEAC: MoEF&CC, environmental release
- The sharpest institutional distinction in this topic
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Split (✅ owner 14 basic §4 and advanced §9).
**Links:** SNT-3099, SNT-3101
**Trap:** "RCGM and GEAC are the same body."
**Mains hook:** Name the stage before naming the approver. Qualified verdict: laboratory clearance is not environmental clearance.
**Back:** *"RCGM: lab, DBT. GEAC: field, environment ministry."*

### Flashcard SNT-3101 — The six-tier biosafety structure under the Rules, 1989
**Front:** List the six-tier structure of Indian biosafety bodies.
**Definition (learn):** The Rules, 1989 create a tiered structure: RDAC (advisory, DBT), IBSC (at each institution), RCGM (DBT), GEAC (MoEF&CC), SBCC (State Biotechnology Coordination Committee) and DLC (District Level Committee).
**Breakdown:**
- RDAC: Recombinant DNA Advisory Committee, DBT, advisory
- IBSC: Institutional Biosafety Committee, day-to-day oversight
- RCGM: research and contained trials
- GEAC: environmental release
- SBCC and DLC: State and district monitoring
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Six tiers (✅ owner 14 basic §4 and advanced §9).
**Links:** SNT-3099, SNT-3100, SNT-3102, SNT-3089
**Trap:** Confusing the advisory RDAC with the approving RCGM.
**Mains hook:** Map the tiers when asked who regulates what. Qualified verdict: monitoring needs SBCC and DLC resources.
**Back:** *"RDAC advises, IBSC watches, RCGM labs, GEAC fields, SBCC and DLC monitor."*

### Flashcard SNT-3102 — Rules, 1989 under the Environment (Protection) Act, 1986
**Front:** Under which statute are the Rules, 1989 made, and what do they cover?
**Definition (learn):** The Rules, 1989 govern manufacture, use, import, export and storage of hazardous micro-organisms and genetically engineered organisms or cells. They are made under the Environment (Protection) Act, 1986.
**Breakdown:**
- Parent Act: Environment (Protection) Act, 1986
- Scope: hazardous micro-organisms and GEOs
- Core Indian GMO biosafety framework
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** EP Act 1986 and Rules 1989 (✅ owner 14 basic §4).
**Links:** SNT-3101, SNT-3112
**Trap:** The Rules are not a DBT statute; they sit under the environment law.
**Mains hook:** Biosafety law is environmental law. Qualified verdict: environment law gives the GEAC route its legal basis.
**Back:** *"Environment Act 1986 gives birth to the 1989 Rules."*

### Flashcard SNT-3103 — FSSAI and GM food
**Front:** Which authority regulates GM food safety and labelling, and under which Act?
**Definition (learn):** FSSAI is the statutory food regulator for GM food safety and labelling under the Food Safety and Standards Act, 2006. It is a food regulator, not a biosafety regulator.
**Breakdown:**
- FSS Act 2006, s.23 on GM-food labelling (as invoked in the 2024 Supreme Court order)
- Separate track from biosafety
- Relevant to GM mustard litigation
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** FSSAI and FSS Act 2006 (✅ owner 14 basic §4, advanced §9 and §10; s.23 as cited in the 23 Jul 2024 order per owner).
**Links:** SNT-3120, SNT-3101
**Trap:** FSSAI approves environmental release. It does not.
**Mains hook:** Food safety and environmental release are separate gates. Qualified verdict: both gates must be passed.
**Back:** *"FSSAI labels the food; GEAC clears the field."*

### Flashcard SNT-3104 — Bt cotton: cry genes and Bollgard II
**Front:** What is Bt cotton, which gene family does it carry, and why does Bollgard II stack two genes?
**Definition (learn):** Bt cotton expresses cry genes (e.g. cry1Ac, cry2Ab) from Bacillus thuringiensis. Its crystal protein is toxic to specific lepidopteran larvae such as bollworms but not to mammals. Bollgard II stacks two cry genes to delay resistance.
**Breakdown:**
- Insect resistance trait, not herbicide tolerance
- Target: bollworm larvae (lepidopterans)
- Gene stacking delays resistance; refuge and monitoring still needed
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** cry1Ac and cry2Ab; Bollgard II (✅ owner 14 basic §2 and §7).
**Links:** SNT-3106, SNT-3105, SNT-3124
**Trap:** Bt cotton is not herbicide tolerant.
**Mains hook:** Resistance management is an ongoing duty. Qualified verdict: stacking helps, but does not end the need for refuges.
**Back:** *"Bt cry proteins kill bollworm larvae; Bollgard II stacks two."*

### Flashcard SNT-3105 — Bt cotton is the only GM crop approved for commercial cultivation
**Front:** Which GM crop is officially approved for commercial cultivation in India, and what is the date of the last official restatement located?
**Definition (learn):** Bt cotton is the only GM crop approved for commercial cultivation, per PIB's 06 Aug 2024 release.
**Breakdown:**
- Commercial GM crop: Bt cotton
- Last official restatement located: 06 Aug 2024
- No 2026 restatement was located
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 06 Aug 2024 (✅ owner 14 basic §6 and §8; ⚠️ 2026 restatement not located).
**Links:** SNT-3104, SNT-3113, SNT-3122
**Trap:** Describing GM mustard as commercially cultivated. It is not, per the sources reviewed.
**Mains hook:** Use the date-stamped status. Qualified verdict: current status must be rechecked before use.
**Back:** *"Bt cotton: the only commercial GM, as of August 2024."*

### Flashcard SNT-3106 — Insect resistance (Bt) versus herbicide tolerance (HT)
**Front:** How do Bt traits and herbicide-tolerance traits differ, and what is DMH-11's bar gene for?
**Definition (learn):** Bt traits give insect resistance through cry proteins. HT traits let a crop survive a broad-spectrum herbicide. DMH-11 carries the bar gene for glufosinate tolerance, used as a selection marker within the barnase-barstar hybridisation system, not as an agronomic HT product.
**Breakdown:**
- Bt: insect resistance
- HT: herbicide survival
- HT is the most contested category in India: weed resistance, labour, illegal HT cotton
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Definitions (✅ owner 14 basic §2 and §7).
**Links:** SNT-3104, SNT-3113, SNT-3122
**Trap:** "Bt crops are herbicide-tolerant."
**Mains hook:** HT and Bt raise different ecological concerns. Qualified verdict: evaluate each trait separately.
**Back:** *"Bt kills insects, HT survives herbicide, DMH-11's bar is a marker."*

### Flashcard SNT-3107 — Bt brinjal moratorium (9 Feb 2010)
**Front:** What was the Bt brinjal moratorium, and what does it not mean?
**Definition (learn):** A moratorium imposed on 09 Feb 2010 suspended release of Bt brinjal pending independent long-term human-health and environmental studies. The government said a moratorium implies rejection for the time being.
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 09 Feb 2010
- Purpose: independent long-term safety studies
- Continuation or withdrawal: no later official source located
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 09 Feb 2010 (✅ owner 14 basic §8; PIB release cited in owner).
**Links:** SNT-3105, SNT-3122
**Trap:** Bt brinjal was not permanently banned, nor conditionally approved.
**Mains hook:** A moratorium is a suspension, not a verdict. Qualified verdict: a decision still needs a studied outcome.
**Back:** *"Feb 2010: paused for safety studies, not formally killed."*

### Flashcard SNT-3108 — CRISPR-Cas9 mechanism
**Front:** Explain CRISPR-Cas9 in four steps.
**Definition (learn):** A guide RNA matches a target sequence (about 20 bases). Cas9 binds and cuts DNA when a PAM sequence sits next to the target. The cut creates a double-strand break, and cell repair decides the outcome.
**Breakdown:**
- Guide RNA targets DNA
- Cas9 nuclease binds and cuts at a PAM (NGG per owner; PAM NGG not confirmed by the fetched Wikipedia page)
- Double-strand break created
- Repair decides the edit: NHEJ, HDR, or no break in base/prime editing
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Mechanism (✅ owner 14 basic §2 and §3); PAM NGG ⚠️ owner-stated, not confirmed by fetch. Charpentier and Doudna shared the 2020 Nobel in Chemistry (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SNT-3109, SNT-3110, SNT-3111
**Trap:** "CRISPR cuts and the cell makes whatever change we want."
**Mains hook:** The tool is not the product. Qualified verdict: targeted does not mean consequence-free.
**Back:** *"Guide finds, Cas cuts, repair edits: the three-act play."*

### Flashcard SNT-3109 — NHEJ versus HDR
**Front:** What do NHEJ and HDR produce after a CRISPR cut?
**Definition (learn):** NHEJ (non-homologous end joining) is the error-prone default repair that produces small insertions or deletions, which can knock out a gene. HDR (homology-directed repair) uses a supplied template for a precise substitution or insertion, but is much less efficient.
**Breakdown:**
- NHEJ: random small indels; gene knockout (SDN-1)
- HDR with short template: defined small change (SDN-2)
- HDR with gene-sized donor: targeted insertion (SDN-3)
- A targeted substitution does not arise spontaneously from NHEJ
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** NHEJ and HDR (✅ owner 14 basic §2; ✅ Wikipedia genome-editing page: NHEJ error-prone small indels; HDR accurate template-based).
**Links:** SNT-3108, SNT-3111, SNT-3119
**Trap:** NHEJ cannot make a designed substitution.
**Mains hook:** Repair pathway decides regulatory category. Qualified verdict: category follows the edit, not the tool.
**Back:** *"NHEJ knocks out, HDR writes with a template."*

### Flashcard SNT-3110 — Base editing and prime editing
**Front:** How do base and prime editing differ from nuclease cutting?
**Definition (learn):** Base editing chemically converts one base into another, and prime editing writes short new sequences. Both work without relying on a double-strand break. Base editing suits single-nucleotide changes.
**Breakdown:**
- Base editing: nucleobase-modifying enzymes guided by nucleic acid; no full cut
- Prime editing: pegRNA-based writing (⚠️ details beyond owner not fetched)
- Both avoid random indels from DSB repair
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Definitions (✅ owner 14 basic §2; ✅ Wikipedia genome-editing page on base editing, Komor et al., Nature 2016).
**Links:** SNT-3108, SNT-3109, SNT-3123
**Trap:** Base and prime editing are not the same as CRISPR-Cas9 nuclease editing.
**Mains hook:** Precision tools challenge a framework written around nuclease cuts. Qualified verdict: regulators must adapt categories.
**Back:** *"Base swaps a letter; prime writes a short line; neither cuts."*

### Flashcard SNT-3111 — SDN-1, SDN-2 and SDN-3 categories
**Front:** Define SDN-1, SDN-2 and SDN-3, and state which two get the lighter treatment.
**Definition (learn):** SDN-1 is a site-directed nuclease edit with no repair template; NHEJ repairs the break. SDN-2 uses a short homologous template for a small defined change. SDN-3 inserts a substantial gene-sized sequence at a targeted site. SDN-1 and SDN-2 with no foreign DNA receive lighter treatment; SDN-3 does not.
**Breakdown:**
- SDN-1: no template, small indels
- SDN-2: short template, small defined change
- SDN-3: gene-sized insertion, transgenic or cisgenic
- Lighter track: SDN-1 and SDN-2 without exogenous DNA
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Categories (✅ owner 14 basic §2, §3 item 5).
**Links:** SNT-3112, SNT-3109, SNT-3118
**Trap:** "Any genome-edited plant escapes regulation." Only specified categories do.
**Mains hook:** The category, not the tool, sets the regulatory route. Qualified verdict: lighter track needs traceability.
**Back:** *"One: no template. Two: short template. Three: gene-sized insert, not lighter."*

### Flashcard SNT-3112 — 30 Mar 2022 MoEF&CC office memorandum and DBT's 17 May 2022 guidelines
**Front:** What did the MoEF&CC office memorandum of 30 Mar 2022 do, and what did DBT notify on 17 May 2022?
**Definition (learn):** MoEF&CC's office memorandum of 30 Mar 2022 exempted SDN-1 and SDN-2 plants free of exogenous DNA from Rules 7-11 of the 1989 Rules. DBT notified the Guidelines for Safety Assessment of Genome Edited Plants on 17 May 2022.
**Breakdown:**
- OM: 30 Mar 2022, exemption from Rules 7-11
- Guidelines: DBT, 17 May 2022
- PIB explained the exemption on 19 Jul 2022
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 30 Mar 2022; 17 May 2022; 19 Jul 2022 PIB (✅ owner 14 basic §4, §6, §8).
**Links:** SNT-3111, SNT-3102, SNT-3121
**Trap:** The exemption is not for all gene edits. It covers SDN-1 and SDN-2 with no foreign DNA.
**Mains hook:** India's 2022 move is a product-based carve-out in a process-based law. Qualified verdict: its boundaries are contested.
**Back:** *"Thirty March exempts, seventeen May guides: SDN-1 and SDN-2 only."*

### Flashcard SNT-3113 — GM mustard DMH-11: construct and status
**Front:** What is DMH-11, what system does it use, and what status is documented?
**Definition (learn):** DMH-11 is a transgenic mustard hybrid built on the barnase-barstar system. Barnase induces male sterility in one parent, barstar restores fertility in the hybrid, and bar provides glufosinate tolerance as a selection marker. GEAC considered environmental release on 18 Oct 2022, and the Government approved environmental release for seed production and testing on 07 Feb 2023 under conditions.
**Breakdown:**
- Purpose: hybridisation in a self-pollinating crop
- Barnase: male sterility; barstar: restorer
- bar: selection marker
- Status: approval for seed production and testing, not commercial cultivation
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 18 Oct 2022 (147th GEAC meeting); 07 Feb 2023 (PIB) (✅ owner 14 basic §2, §6, §8).
**Links:** SNT-3098, SNT-3120, SNT-3105
**Trap:** "GM mustard was approved for commercial cultivation." It was not.
**Mains hook:** DMH-11 shows the contested-legality problem. Qualified verdict: status is unsettled, not settled in favour of cultivation.
**Back:** *"Barnase sterile, barstar restores, bar marks; approved for testing, not sowing."*

### Flashcard SNT-3114 — Cartagena Protocol, Nagoya Protocol and the Biological Diversity Act, 2002
**Front:** What do the Cartagena and Nagoya Protocols cover, and how does India implement the Nagoya access and benefit-sharing?
**Definition (learn):** The Cartagena Protocol on Biosafety governs transboundary movement of living modified organisms, using advance informed agreement and precaution. The Nagoya Protocol governs access to genetic resources and benefit-sharing. India implements the latter through the Biological Diversity Act, 2002 (amended 2023) and the National Biodiversity Authority.
**Breakdown:**
- Cartagena: LMO movement, advance informed agreement, precaution
- Nagoya: access and benefit-sharing
- Domestic: Biological Diversity Act, 2002 (amended 2023); NBA
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Instruments and Act (✅ owner 14 basic §4 and advanced §9).
**Links:** SNT-3101, SNT-3102, SNT-3080
**Trap:** Cartagena and Nagoya are not the same protocol. Do not confuse LMO movement with benefit-sharing.
**Mains hook:** International biosafety meets domestic benefit-sharing. Qualified verdict: both obligations apply to GM research.
**Back:** *"Cartagena moves LMOs carefully; Nagoya shares benefits; India uses the 2002 Act."*

### Flashcard SNT-3115 — The terminator technology myth
**Front:** Is terminator technology used in Indian GM seeds?
**Definition (learn):** Genetic Use Restriction Technology (terminator) has never been commercialised anywhere, and India's Seeds and PPV&FR framework does not permit it. It is a persistent myth, not a fact.
**Breakdown:**
- Terminator: sterile-seed technology
- Never commercialised per owner
- Not permitted under Indian seed law (owner-stated)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Myth status (✅ owner 14 basic §7).
**Links:** SNT-3105, SNT-3114
**Trap:** Describing an Indian GM seed as terminator-based.
**Mains hook:** Seed sovereignty fears need factual grounding. Qualified verdict: name the law that forbids it.
**Back:** *"Terminator: a myth in Indian seed fields."*

### GROUP — Genetic Engineering, GM Crops and CRISPR (advanced, owner 14)

### Flashcard SNT-3116 — Product-based versus process-based regulation
**Front:** What is the difference between product-based and process-based GMO regulation, and where does India sit?
**Definition (learn):** Process-based regulation asks how an organism was made. Product-based regulation asks what the final organism is. India and the EU default to process-based law. India's 2022 exemption is a partial product-based carve-out inside a process-based statute.
**Breakdown:**
- Process-based: India's default and the EU's
- Product-based: the US approach (owner-stated)
- India's 2022 carve-out for SDN-1 and SDN-2 without foreign DNA
- Boundaries of the carve-out are contested
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Framing (⚠️ owner 14 advanced §3; US and EU positioning owner-stated, not fetched).
**Links:** SNT-3112, SNT-3111, SNT-3096
**Trap:** Saying India's gene-editing regime is fully product-based. It is partial.
**Mains hook:** Regulatory design decides innovation speed. Qualified verdict: a product-based carve-out needs clear, auditable boundaries.
**Back:** *"Process asks how; product asks what; India carves out a product exception."*

### Flashcard SNT-3117 — Cisgenic constructs and the legal mismatch
**Front:** Why is the cisgenic category a legal-biological mismatch in India?
**Definition (learn):** A cisgenic construct uses genes from the same or a sexually compatible species and can be biologically close to a conventional product. In India it still falls inside the GMO framework, creating a mismatch between biological novelty and legal category.
**Breakdown:**
- Cisgenic: same or compatible species, intact gene
- Legal result: GMO route
- Central to the reform debate
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Mismatch (✅ owner 14 advanced §3).
**Links:** SNT-3097, SNT-3116
**Trap:** "Cisgenic means non-GM in law." Not in India.
**Mains hook:** Use the mismatch to argue for reform. Qualified verdict: category should follow risk.
**Back:** *"Close-relative gene, still GM on paper."*

### Flashcard SNT-3118 — SDN-3 is not "transgene-like" by definition
**Front:** How should SDN-3 be defined, and why is "transgene-like" imprecise?
**Definition (learn):** SDN-3 is defined by targeted insertion of a gene-sized sequence at a cut site. The inserted DNA may be transgenic or cisgenic, so the defining point is the gene-sized insertion, not transgenic identity.
**Breakdown:**
- Insertion of a gene-sized construct
- Keeps SDN-3 outside the lighter track
- Transgenic or cisgenic inserts both possible
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** SDN-3 definition (✅ owner 14 advanced §3 and §12).
**Links:** SNT-3111, SNT-3097
**Trap:** "SDN-3 means transgenic."
**Mains hook:** Precise definitions avoid false equivalence. Qualified verdict: the insertion size is the regulatory hinge.
**Back:** *"SDN-3: gene-sized insert, transgenic or cisgenic, full scrutiny."*

### Flashcard SNT-3119 — The repair pathway determines the category
**Front:** How does the repair pathway map to the Indian regulatory category?
**Definition (learn):** NHEJ gives indels, which map to SDN-1. HDR with a short template gives a defined small change, which maps to SDN-2. HDR with a gene-sized donor maps to SDN-3. Base and prime editing sit awkwardly in a framework written around nuclease cuts.
**Breakdown:**
- NHEJ -> SDN-1
- Short-template HDR -> SDN-2
- Gene-sized donor -> SDN-3
- Base/prime editing -> not cleanly classified in a nuclease framework
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Mapping (✅ owner 14 advanced §3 and §4).
**Links:** SNT-3109, SNT-3110, SNT-3111
**Trap:** Assuming the tool name settles the category.
**Mains hook:** Regulators must update categories as tools change. Qualified verdict: classify by the edit.
**Back:** *"Indel is SDN-1, short template is SDN-2, gene-sized donor is SDN-3."*

### Flashcard SNT-3120 — DMH-11 Supreme Court split verdict (23 Jul 2024)
**Front:** What did the Supreme Court decide in Gene Campaign v Union of India on GM mustard, and what is its status?
**Definition (learn):** In Gene Campaign & Anr. v. Union of India & Ors. (2024 INSC 545, 23 Jul 2024), Justice B.V. Nagarathna held the GEAC recommendation of 18 Oct 2022 and the Union decision of 25 Oct 2022 legally vitiated and quashed them, requiring fresh consideration. Justice Sanjay Karol held the conditional approval not vitiated and allowed the process to continue subject to directions. The divided question went to the Chief Justice for an appropriate Bench.
**Breakdown:**
- Common directions: national GM-crop policy consultation; conflict-of-interest safeguards; FSS Act s.23 labelling compliance
- Divided question: unresolved
- Do not describe it as referred to a larger bench (the wording is "appropriate Bench")
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 23 Jul 2024; 2024 INSC 545; 18 Oct 2022; 25 Oct 2022 (✅ owner 14 basic §8 and advanced §10; judgment URL cited in owner grounding, ✅ owner).
**Links:** SNT-3113, SNT-3103, SNT-3121
**Trap:** A split verdict settles nothing on DMH-11; but the common directions do bind.
**Mains hook:** Judicial dissent shows the process problem. Qualified verdict: recheck status before any current-affairs answer.
**Back:** *"Nagarathna quashed, Karol upheld, Bench pending: unsettled."*
