### Flashcard SNT-4042 — TRIPS Agreement and the Doha Declaration (2001)
**Front:** What is TRIPS, and what did the Doha Declaration affirm?
**Definition (learn):** TRIPS is the WTO agreement setting minimum standards for IP protection with flexibilities. The Doha Declaration on TRIPS and Public Health (2001) affirmed members' right to grant compulsory licences and determine the grounds (✅ owner 17 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- TRIPS: WTO minimum standards and flexibilities.
- Doha (2001): public health and compulsory licensing right affirmed.
- Enforced through WTO dispute settlement (✅ owner 17 §4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Doha Declaration 2001 (✅ owner 17).
**Links:** SNT-4041, SNT-4054, SNT-4036.
**Trap:** TRIPS is a WTO agreement; it is not administered by WIPO.
**Mains hook:** Use TRIPS and Doha to argue that flexibilities are legitimate, not exceptions to be avoided.
**Back:** *"Minimum rules, public-health flexibility, WTO enforces."*

### Flashcard SNT-4043 — Patent Co-operation Treaty (PCT)
**Front:** What does the PCT provide, and what does it not provide?
**Definition (learn):** A WIPO-administered filing system for international patent applications. It is a filing route, not a global patent grant (✅ owner 17 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Provides: a common international filing route.
- Does not provide: a single world patent. National or regional phase decisions determine enforceable rights.
- India is a PCT contracting state (✅ owner 17 §6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** WIPO PCT contracting states page as cited by owner (verified 16 Jul 2026 per owner).
**Links:** SNT-4054, SNT-4063, SNT-4064.
**Trap:** "PCT gives a global patent." It does not.
**Mains hook:** Explain PCT as a procedural convenience that still depends on national examination.
**Back:** *"Filing route, not a world patent."*

### Flashcard SNT-4044 — Trademark: definition and 10-year renewable term
**Front:** What is a trademark, under which Act, and how long does registration last?
**Definition (learn):** A sign capable of graphic representation that distinguishes the goods or services of one person from those of others. Governed by the Trade Marks Act, 1999 (✅ owner 17 §2–3).
**Breakdown:**
- Distinguishes commercial source.
- Term: 10 years, renewable indefinitely in 10-year blocks (✅ owner 17 §6).
- Registrations are administered by the Trade Marks Registry under CGPDTM.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 10 years, renewable (✅ IP India Basics of Trademarks page, cited by owner).
**Links:** SNT-4029, SNT-4045, SNT-4063.
**Trap:** A trademark can be renewed indefinitely; a patent cannot.
**Mains hook:** Use trademarks to show market signalling and consumer trust.
**Back:** *"Brand sign: ten years, renew forever."*

### Flashcard SNT-4045 — Geographical Indication (GI): collective, place-based right
**Front:** What is a GI, under which Act, and why is it not a private trademark?
**Definition (learn):** An indication identifying goods as originating in a territory, region or locality where a given quality, reputation or characteristic is essentially attributable to that origin. Governed by the GI of Goods (Registration and Protection) Act, 1999 (✅ owner 17 §2, §3).
**Breakdown:**
- Collective right of producers in the region.
- Term: 10 years, renewable in 10-year blocks (✅ owner 17 §6).
- Authorised-user registration lets individual producers enforce it (✅ owner 17 §3).
- Nodal registry: GI Registry, Chennai (✅ owner 17 §4).
- Examples: Darjeeling Tea, Kanchipuram Silk (✅ owner 17 §5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Registration runs 10 years and is renewable.
**Links:** SNT-4029, SNT-4044, SNT-4071.
**Trap:** A GI cannot be assigned or licensed to an outsider, unlike a trademark. Do not describe it as one company's brand.
**Mains hook:** Present GIs as rural-development and origin-protection tools, then note that registration alone does not guarantee income.
**Back:** *"Place-made, collective, ten-year renewal."*

### Flashcard SNT-4046 — Copyright: automatic, expression-only, life plus 60 years
**Front:** What does copyright protect, under which Act, and how long does it last?
**Definition (learn):** Original literary, artistic, musical and related works, protected automatically on creation. Copyright protects expression, not ideas (✅ owner 17 §2, ⚠️ term as recorded by owner).
**Breakdown:**
- Automatic on creation; no registration needed for protection.
- Governed by the Copyright Act, 1957.
- Term: generally author's life plus 60 years (⚠️ standard, owner summary).
- Copyright Office sits under DPIIT, not CGPDTM (✅ owner 17 §4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Berne Convention is the international reference (SNT-4056).
**Links:** SNT-4029, SNT-4051, SNT-4056.
**Trap:** Protecting an idea versus the expression of it. Copyright protects expression only.
**Mains hook:** Discuss copyright's role in the digital economy, with exceptions for research and access (⚠️ standard).
**Back:** *"Expression, not idea: life plus sixty."*

### Flashcard SNT-4047 — Industrial design: Designs Act, 2000
**Front:** What do industrial designs protect, and what term does the owner record?
**Definition (learn):** The visual appearance of shape, pattern, ornament or composition, not the function. Governed by the Designs Act, 2000 (⚠️ owner 17 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- Protects appearance, not function.
- Term recorded by owner: 10 years, extendable by 5 years (⚠️ owner summary; not independently verified).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Designs Act, 2000.
**Links:** SNT-4029, SNT-4046.
**Trap:** A design right does not protect technical function; a patent does.
**Mains hook:** Rarely central; use for the design-versus-patent distinction.
**Back:** *"Look, not function: ten plus five."*

### Flashcard SNT-4048 — Semiconductor integrated-circuit layout-design right
**Front:** What is a layout-design right, under which Act, and for how long?
**Definition (learn):** A sui generis right over the layout-design of integrated circuits, under the Semiconductor Integrated Circuits Layout-Design Act, 2000 (⚠️ owner 17 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- Sui generis right, not a patent.
- Term recorded by owner: 10 years.
- Links IPR to semiconductor policy (Part 4 cross-topic link).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Act of 2000; 10-year term (⚠️ owner summary).
**Links:** SNT-4029, SNT-4043.
**Trap:** A chip's layout-design is protected differently from the chip's functional invention.
**Mains hook:** Link IPR to semiconductor self-reliance and design capability.
**Back:** *"Chip layout: its own law, ten years."*

### Flashcard SNT-4049 — PPV&FR Act, 2001: plant varieties and farmers' rights
**Front:** How does India protect plant varieties, and what farmer right does the Act preserve?
**Definition (learn):** The Protection of Plant Varieties and Farmers' Rights Act, 2001 is a sui generis, TRIPS-compliant alternative to plant patents. It expressly preserves the farmer's right to save, use, sow, exchange and sell farm-saved seed, but not branded seed of a protected variety (✅ owner 17 §2, §4).
**Breakdown:**
- Authority: PPV&FR Authority, under the Ministry of Agriculture (✅ owner 17 §4).
- Protects breeders and farmers' rights, including farmers' varieties.
- Benefit sharing is part of the framework (✅ owner 17 §4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Act of 2001; term crop-dependent (⚠️ owner summary).
**Links:** SNT-4034, SNT-4065, SNT-4068.
**Trap:** Farm-saved seed right does not extend to branded seed of a protected variety.
**Mains hook:** Use PPV&FR to show how India protects both breeders and farmers.
**Back:** *"Save, sow, exchange, sell: not the branded seed."*

### Flashcard SNT-4050 — Trade secrets: no fixed term and no standalone statute
**Front:** How is a trade secret protected in India, and what is the gap?
**Definition (learn):** Confidential business information with competitive value, protected through secrecy and legal confidentiality mechanisms rather than registration (✅ owner 17 §2; ⚠️ owner advanced §6 on the statute gap).
**Breakdown:**
- No fixed statutory term; protection lasts while secrecy holds.
- Owner records that India lacks a standalone comprehensive trade-secret statute (⚠️ owner advanced §6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** No statutory term.
**Links:** SNT-4029, SNT-4041.
**Trap:** A trade secret is not registered and not time-limited by statute; disclosure ends it.
**Mains hook:** Argue for a clearer trade-secret framework for MSMEs and start-ups.
**Back:** *"Secret kept, right kept; secret leaked, right lost."*

### Flashcard SNT-4051 — CGPDTM: administrative apex and what it does not administer
**Front:** Which IP rights does CGPDTM administer, and which does it not?
**Definition (learn):** The Office of the Controller General of Patents, Designs and Trade Marks administers patents, designs, trade marks and geographical indications (✅ owner 17 §4).
**Breakdown:**
- Administers: patents, designs, trade marks, GIs.
- Does not administer: copyright (Copyright Office under DPIIT) and plant varieties (PPV&FR Authority).
- Includes Patent Office, Trade Marks Registry and GI Registry (✅ owner 17 §4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level institutional mapping.
**Links:** SNT-4052, SNT-4046, SNT-4049.
**Trap:** CGPDTM is not the umbrella authority for all IP. Copyright and plant varieties sit elsewhere.
**Mains hook:** Use the institutional map to show why IPR needs coordination across ministries.
**Back:** *"Patents, designs, marks, places: not copyright, not plants."*

### Flashcard SNT-4052 — DPIIT, CIPAM and the National IPR Policy 2016
**Front:** What is the National IPR Policy 2016, and which bodies coordinate it?
**Definition (learn):** The National IPR Policy 2016 emphasises awareness, stronger administration, commercialisation and balance with public interest. DPIIT is the policy ministry; CIPAM supports implementation (✅ owner 17 §4, §5).
**Breakdown:**
- DPIIT: policy coordination.
- CIPAM: implementation support.
- CGPDTM: administrative and adjudicatory function.
- Policy pillars: awareness, administration, commercialisation, public interest.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Policy year 2016 (✅ owner 17).
**Links:** SNT-4051, SNT-4071, SNT-4073.
**Trap:** The National IPR Policy is not only about stronger monopolies; it also speaks of public interest.
**Mains hook:** Use the four pillars to structure a balanced IPR policy answer.
**Back:** *"Awareness, administration, commerce, public interest."*

### Flashcard SNT-4053 — TKDL and biopiracy: turmeric, neem, basmati
**Front:** What is the Traditional Knowledge Digital Library (TKDL), and which biopiracy cases did it respond to?
**Definition (learn):** A defensive database built by CSIR with the Ministry of Ayush. It makes traditional knowledge searchable by foreign patent offices as prior art, to prevent wrongful patent grants (✅ owner 17 §2, §4).
**Breakdown:**
- Built by CSIR with the Ministry of Ayush.
- Function: defensive prior-art documentation.
- Cases cited in the owner: turmeric, neem and basmati (✅ owner 17 §2).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level institutional and case mapping; no year attached.
**Links:** SNT-4054, SNT-4065, SNT-4066.
**Trap:** TKDL is a defensive knowledge library, not a patent-granting body.
**Mains hook:** Argue that defensive documentation protects community knowledge without expanding monopolies.
**Back:** *"Prior art in a library stops wrongful patents."*

### Flashcard SNT-4054 — WIPO versus WTO
**Front:** How do WIPO and the WTO differ in IP governance?
**Definition (learn):** WIPO is a UN specialised agency that administers IP treaties and registries, including the PCT, Madrid, Paris, Berne and Budapest. The WTO sets and enforces TRIPS through dispute settlement (✅ owner 17 §4).
**Breakdown:**
- WIPO: treaties and registries; a UN specialised agency.
- WTO: TRIPS rules and dispute settlement.
- Not the same organisation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4042, SNT-4043, SNT-4055.
**Trap:** Treating WIPO and WTO as interchangeable. WIPO is not a WTO organ.
**Mains hook:** Use the two-body map when discussing global IP negotiation.
**Back:** *"WIPO registers; WTO enforces TRIPS."*

### Flashcard SNT-4055 — Paris Convention: priority and national treatment
**Front:** What does the Paris Convention provide?
**Definition (learn):** The Paris Convention for the Protection of Industrial Property, covering patents and trademarks, with national treatment and right of priority (⚠️ owner 17 §2 list; standard knowledge).
**Breakdown:**
- National treatment: foreign applicants get domestic treatment.
- Priority: a filing in one member gives a time window to file elsewhere.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Convention administered through WIPO (⚠️ standard).
**Links:** SNT-4054, SNT-4043.
**Trap:** Priority is a filing-date advantage, not a grant.
**Mains hook:** Rarely central; use to contrast filing rights with substantive grant.
**Back:** *"Paris: same treatment, first-filing priority."*

### Flashcard SNT-4056 — Berne Convention for copyright
**Front:** Which treaty is the international reference for copyright?
**Definition (learn):** The Berne Convention for the Protection of Literary and Artistic Works (⚠️ owner 17 §2 list; standard knowledge).
**Breakdown:**
- Copyright protection applies across member countries without formal registration.
- Administered through WIPO.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level reference.
**Links:** SNT-4046, SNT-4054.
**Trap:** Berne does not replace national copyright law; India's Copyright Act, 1957 applies domestically.
**Mains hook:** Use to discuss copyright in digital markets.
**Back:** *"Berne: copyright across borders."*

### Flashcard SNT-4057 — Madrid Protocol for international trademarks
**Front:** What is the Madrid Protocol used for?
**Definition (learn):** A WIPO system for international registration of trademarks across member countries (⚠️ owner 17 §2 list; standard knowledge).
**Breakdown:**
- One application, several member designations.
- Still subject to each country's examination.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level reference.
**Links:** SNT-4044, SNT-4054.
**Trap:** Madrid is for trademarks; PCT is for patents. Do not mix them.
**Mains hook:** Use to contrast filing systems across IP types.
**Back:** *"Madrid for marks, PCT for patents."*

### Flashcard SNT-4058 — Budapest Treaty for micro-organism deposits
**Front:** Why does the Budapest Treaty matter for patenting micro-organisms?
**Definition (learn):** The Budapest Treaty allows deposit of micro-organisms with a recognised depository for patent purposes (⚠️ owner 17 §2 list; standard knowledge).
**Breakdown:**
- Micro-organisms remain patentable under the Indian framework (✅ owner 17 advanced §10).
- Deposit supports disclosure and reproducibility.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level reference.
**Links:** SNT-4034, SNT-4054.
**Trap:** Micro-organisms are not excluded by s.3(j), unlike plants and animals.
**Mains hook:** Use to explain why the life-materials regime is not uniform.
**Back:** *"Micro-organism: deposit, then patent."*

### Flashcard SNT-4059 — Patents (Amendment) Rules, 2024
**Front:** When were the Patents (Amendment) Rules, 2024 notified?
**Definition (learn):** IP India records the Patents (Amendment) Rules, 2024 dated 15 March 2024, with a second amendment dated 16 March 2024 (✅ owner 17 §8, IP India PDF as cited).
**Breakdown:**
- Dates: 15 Mar 2024 and 16 Mar 2024.
- Detailed changes were not extracted in the owner's verification; do not assert specific changes.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 15 and 16 Mar 2024 (✅ owner 17).
**Links:** SNT-4030, SNT-4040.
**Trap:** Asserting specific changes (inventorship certificate, Form 27 frequency, opposition procedure) without reading the official text.
**Mains hook:** Cite the dates and say the content needs the official Gazette text.
**Back:** *"Fifteen and sixteen March: read the text."*

### Flashcard SNT-4060 — Form 27: working statement and annual renewal
**Front:** What is Form 27 in patent administration?
**Definition (learn):** The working statement that patentees file to show how a patented invention is worked in India (✅ owner 17 §3, owner's mechanism step 3).
**Breakdown:**
- Filed on the patent-working statement cycle.
- Linked to s.84 concerns about non-working.
- Annual renewal fees also apply.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Form 27 frequency was not verified in this build (⚠️).
**Links:** SNT-4036, SNT-4030.
**Trap:** Do not state a specific Form 27 frequency without checking the official rule text.
**Mains hook:** Link working statements to the "not worked in India" compulsory-licence ground.
**Back:** *"Show the working, or face the licence."*

### Flashcard SNT-4061 — Global Innovation Index: India 38th in 2025
**Front:** Where did India rank in WIPO's Global Innovation Index 2025?
**Definition (learn):** India ranked 38th in WIPO's Global Innovation Index 2025 (✅ owner 17 §8, WIPO GII 2025 results page as cited).
**Breakdown:**
- Always cite the edition year with the rank.
- GII is a composite index; the rank is not a patent-filing count.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 38th (GII 2025).
**Links:** SNT-4062, SNT-4065.
**Trap:** Quoting a GII rank without the year, or treating it as a patent count.
**Mains hook:** Use the rank with caution and link to commercialisation, not to filing volume.
**Back:** *"GII 2025: thirty-eighth, year first."*

### Flashcard SNT-4062 — CGPDTM Annual Report 2024-25: no statistic asserted
**Front:** What filing or grant statistic can be quoted from the CGPDTM Annual Report 2024-25?
**Definition (learn):** The report exists on ipindia.gov.in, but its tables were not machine-readable at verification, so no filing, grant or resident-share figure is asserted (✅ owner 17 §8).
**Breakdown:**
- Owner records continued filing growth and administrative modernisation as reported (✅ owner 17 §9).
- Cite the report directly for numbers.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** FY 2024-25 report.
**Links:** SNT-4061, SNT-4052.
**Trap:** The 2025 PYQ premise "India is second in the world to file patents" is not a verified statistic (✅ owner 17 advanced PYQ note).
**Mains hook:** Answer commercialisation questions without relying on unverified filing ranks.
**Back:** *"Report exists; numbers unverified."*

### Flashcard SNT-4063 — Trap pairs: patent, GI and trademark
**Front:** How do patent, GI and trademark differ in what they protect, who holds them and how they work?
**Definition (learn):** A patent protects an invention for a fixed term. A trademark protects a brand identifier and can be renewed indefinitely. A GI protects origin-linked goods as a collective, place-based right (✅ owner 17 §6, §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Patent: invention; holder: inventor or assignee; term: 20 years from filing.
- Trademark: brand; holder: proprietor; term: 10 years, renewable.
- GI: place; holder: producer collective via authorised-user route; term: 10 years, renewable.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Terms as in owner tables.
**Links:** SNT-4029, SNT-4030, SNT-4044, SNT-4045.
**Trap:** Interchangeable labels; GI as a company brand; patent renewable indefinitely.
**Mains hook:** Use the three-way table to structure any IPR definitional answer.
**Back:** *"Invention fixed, brand forever, place collective."*

### Flashcard SNT-4064 — Trap set: s.3(d), PCT, compulsory licence and renewal
**Front:** Which four IP statements are most often wrong in Prelims?
**Definition (learn):** (a) s.3(d) bans all pharma patents; (b) PCT gives a single global patent; (c) compulsory licensing abolishes patent rights; (d) patents renew indefinitely like trademarks. All four are false (✅ owner 17 §7).
**Breakdown:**
- (a) s.3(d) targets evergreening without enhanced efficacy.
- (b) PCT is a filing route.
- (c) Compulsory licence is conditional use, not cancellation.
- (d) Patent term is fixed at 20 years from filing.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner trap list (✅ owner 17 §7).
**Links:** SNT-4033, SNT-4043, SNT-4036, SNT-4030.
**Trap:** Each false statement sounds like a "pro-public" or "pro-innovation" slogan; test the legal mechanism.
**Mains hook:** Use the four corrections to frame a nuanced patent-policy answer.
**Back:** *"Not all pharma, not a world patent, not abolition, not forever."*

### Flashcard SNT-4065 — 2024 GS-III PYQ: IPR, life materials and low commercialisation
**Front:** What is the present world scenario of IPR with respect to life materials, and why are few Indian patents commercialised? (2024 GS-III Q5, 10 marks, 150 words)
**Definition (learn):** A world patchwork: TRIPS Art. 27.3(b) allows exclusion of plants and animals but requires some protection of plant varieties. India uses s.3(j) plus the PPV&FR Act, 2001 (✅ owner 17 §9 routed PYQ, ⚠️ analysis).
**Breakdown:**
- Life-materials world scenario: patchwork across jurisdictions.
- Indian route: s.3(j) exclusion + sui generis plant-variety law + micro-organisms patentable.
- Low commercialisation: research, institutional, industry, process and ecosystem causes (SNT-4068).
- Premise check: the "second in filing" premise is not a verified current statistic.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Paper GS-III, 2024, Q5.
**Links:** SNT-4034, SNT-4049, SNT-4053, SNT-4068.
**Trap:** Answering the filing-rank premise as a verified fact.
**Mains hook:** Answer the commercialisation question directly, with the causal chain, then conclude with translation support.
**Back:** *"Patchwork law, sui generis plants, few products: fix translation."*

### Flashcard SNT-4066 — 2019 GS-III PYQ: traditional knowledge and pharmaceutical patents
**Front:** How can traditional knowledge of medicine be protected from pharmaceutical patenting? (2019 GS-III Q15, 15 marks, 250 words)
**Definition (learn):** Protection works through defensive documentation (TKDL), prior-art opposition, and public-interest filters such as s.3(d) and opposition (✅ owner 17 advanced routed PYQ, ⚠️ analysis).
**Breakdown:**
- Context: biopiracy cases and wrongful patents.
- Tools: TKDL as prior art; s.25 opposition; s.3(d) filter.
- Counterpoint: patents still reward genuine innovation built on traditional knowledge.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Paper GS-III, 2019, Q15.
**Links:** SNT-4053, SNT-4040, SNT-4033.
**Trap:** Declaring all traditional knowledge un-patentable. The tool is prior-art documentation and benefit-sharing discipline.
**Mains hook:** Conclude that defensive documentation plus transparent benefit-sharing protects community knowledge while keeping innovation possible.
**Back:** *"Document the old, oppose the copy, keep real novelty."*

### Flashcard SNT-4067 — 2019 Prelims PYQ: Patents Act and the Intellectual Property Board
**Front:** What does the 2019 Prelims item on the Patents Act and the Intellectual Property Board test?
**Definition (learn):** It tests the statutory appellate-body status of the IP Board under the Patents Act (✅ owner 17 PYQ routing). The owner notes the status is date-sensitive.
**Breakdown:**
- Official key not held locally.
- Owner's cross-routing flags a date-sensitive appellate-body status.
- ⚠️ Standard knowledge: the Intellectual Property Appellate Board was abolished in 2021 through tribunal reforms, so the current position must be checked.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Prelims GS-I 2019 Q57 (✅ owner 17 PYQ table).
**Links:** SNT-4040, SNT-4051.
**Trap:** Describing IPAB as currently operational without checking the date.
**Mains hook:** Rarely central; use to show why appellate bodies must be re-dated.
**Back:** *"Board's status: check the date."*

### Flashcard SNT-4068 — Why Indian patents are poorly commercialised
**Front:** What causes low commercialisation of Indian patents, organised by stage?
**Definition (learn):** Commercialisation breaks at several links: research, institution, industry, process and ecosystem (✅ owner 17 advanced core architecture, ⚠️ analysis).
**Breakdown:**
- Research: defensive or academic filing, weak market validation, thin proof-of-concept funding.
- Institutions: under-resourced Technology Transfer Offices, unclear ownership and revenue-sharing rules.
- Industry: limited absorption, low corporate demand for university IP.
- Process: examination capacity, maintenance costs for individuals and MSMEs, enforcement cost.
- Ecosystem: quantity-driven patent incentives, weak pilot facilities, scarce deep-tech risk capital.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level analysis; no commercialisation percentage asserted.
**Links:** SNT-4065, SNT-4052, SNT-4061.
**Trap:** Counting patents as commercialisation. Filing is an input, not market adoption.
**Mains hook:** Structure the causes by stage and close with a realistic bridge: TTOs, pilot funding and industry linkage.
**Back:** *"Research, institute, industry, process, ecosystem: each leaks."*

### Flashcard SNT-4069 — Advanced: evergreening, s.3(d) and the access debate
**Front:** How does s.3(d) balance innovation and access in pharmaceuticals?
**Definition (learn):** S.3(d) filters evergreening claims over known substances while preserving protection for genuine inventions (✅ owner 17 advanced §6).
**Breakdown:**
- Filter: weak extension claims without enhanced therapeutic efficacy.
- Preserves: genuine invention protection.
- Compulsory licensing and opposition are companion correctives.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Novartis (2013) reading of efficacy (SNT-4033).
**Links:** SNT-4033, SNT-4040, SNT-4036.
**Trap:** Treating s.3(d) as anti-patent rather than as a balancing device within patent law.
**Mains hook:** Argue that a well-designed filter protects both innovation and access.
**Back:** *"Filter the extension, keep the invention."*

### Flashcard SNT-4070 — Advanced: sui generis versus patents for life forms
**Front:** Why does India protect plant varieties outside patent law while keeping micro-organisms patentable?
**Definition (learn):** TRIPS Art. 27.3(b) allows exclusion of plants and animals but requires protection of plant varieties by patents, an effective sui generis system, or a combination. India chose sui generis for plants (✅ owner 17 advanced §2, §9).
**Breakdown:**
- Plants, animals, essentially biological processes: excluded (s.3(j)).
- Plant varieties: PPV&FR Act, 2001, with farmers' rights.
- Micro-organisms: patentable, with Budapest deposit.
- Benefit sharing and biodiversity overlay: Biological Diversity Act and Nagoya Protocol (⚠️ standard).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** TRIPS Art. 27.3(b) (✅ owner 17 §9).
**Links:** SNT-4034, SNT-4049, SNT-4058.
**Trap:** Saying that all life forms are patentable under Indian law, or none are.
**Mains hook:** Use the sui generis choice to show policy space that TRIPS allows.
**Back:** *"Plants sui generis, microbes patentable."*

### Flashcard SNT-4071 — Advanced: GI commercialisation depends on more than registration
**Front:** Why does GI registration not automatically raise producer incomes?
**Definition (learn):** Registration creates a legal collective right, but income depends on branding, quality control and market access (✅ owner 17 advanced §6).
**Breakdown:**
- Legal right: collective, origin-linked.
- Income drivers: branding, quality control, market access, producer organisation.
- GI is a rural-development instrument, not only a legal badge.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level analysis.
**Links:** SNT-4045, SNT-4052.
**Trap:** A GI is not a rural trademark; it cannot be licensed to outsiders.
**Mains hook:** Argue that GI success requires producer organisations, quality control and export-market linkage.
**Back:** *"Registration opens the door; branding walks through it."*

### Flashcard SNT-4072 — Advanced: compulsory licensing as calibrated flexibility
**Front:** Why is compulsory licensing a calibrated flexibility, not a routine policy tool?
**Definition (learn):** It is a statutory, conditional exception to patent exclusivity, with grounds and procedure set in the Act (✅ owner 17 advanced §6).
**Breakdown:**
- Conditions: s.84 grounds and three-year bar; s.92 emergency route.
- Not anti-innovation by definition.
- Not a substitute for industrial capacity; affordability still needs manufacturing.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Natco (2012) as the only recorded s.84 case (SNT-4037).
**Links:** SNT-4036, SNT-4037, SNT-4038.
**Trap:** "Compulsory licence means cheap medicine automatically." It also needs a manufacturer and supply.
**Mains hook:** Argue that flexibilities work best when paired with manufacturing and transparent pricing.
**Back:** *"Conditions, not cancellation; supply still needs factories."*

### Flashcard SNT-4073 — NIPAM outreach and IP awareness
**Front:** What is the National Intellectual Property Awareness Mission (NIPAM), and what does its outreach show?
**Definition (learn):** An IP awareness programme whose sessions continued through 2024, recorded on the official awareness portal (✅ owner 17 §8).
**Breakdown:**
- Event listings: 18 Jul 2024 and 07 Dec 2024 (✅ owner 17 §8).
- Purpose: broaden IP literacy beyond elite filers.
- Outreach is activity, not outcome.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Event dates as cited by owner.
**Links:** SNT-4052, SNT-4062.
**Trap:** Awareness events do not prove filing or commercialisation gains.
**Mains hook:** Use NIPAM to argue that IP literacy is a precondition for MSME and farmer benefit.
**Back:** *"Awareness events, not outcome numbers."*

### GROUP — Electric Vehicles, Batteries and Alternative Fuels

### Flashcard SNT-4074 — Four electric-drive architectures: where the electricity comes from
**Front:** What distinguishes BEV, HEV, PHEV and FCEV by the source of their electricity?
**Definition (learn):** BEV stores electricity in a battery; HEV is charged only by its engine and regenerative braking; PHEV has a larger battery charged externally; FCEV generates electricity onboard from hydrogen (✅ owner 18 §1).
**Breakdown:**
- BEV: battery plus electric motor; zero tailpipe emission.
- HEV: engine plus small battery; cannot be plugged in.
- PHEV: engine plus larger battery; plugged in; real electric-only range.
- FCEV: hydrogen fuel cell plus motor; tailpipe product is water.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level architecture table.
**Links:** SNT-4075, SNT-4076, SNT-4077, SNT-4078.
**Trap:** "Every electric-looking vehicle is a BEV." Hybrids and FCEVs are distinct architectures.
**Mains hook:** Start the clean-mobility answer with the three-way powertrain distinction.
**Back:** *"Stored, engine-charged, both, or made from hydrogen."*

### Flashcard SNT-4075 — Battery electric vehicle (BEV)
**Front:** How does a BEV work, and what does it need to operate?
**Definition (learn):** A vehicle propelled only by an electric motor drawing on a battery pack. It has no combustion engine (✅ owner 18 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Energy source: stored electricity.
- Needs charging infrastructure.
- Zero tailpipe emission, but lifecycle emissions depend on grid mix and battery manufacturing (✅ owner 18 §7).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level definition.
**Links:** SNT-4074, SNT-4080, SNT-4106.
**Trap:** "BEVs are automatically zero-emission." They are zero tailpipe emission only.
**Mains hook:** Use the BEV to show the trade-off between urban tailpipe gains and grid and battery lifecycle questions.
**Back:** *"Battery only: zero tailpipe, not zero lifecycle."*

### Flashcard SNT-4076 — Hybrid electric vehicle (HEV)
**Front:** Why can a HEV not be charged from a wall socket, and what does it improve?
**Definition (learn):** An HEV has an engine plus a small battery and motor. The battery is charged only by the engine and regenerative braking. It improves fuel efficiency but has no meaningful electric-only range (✅ owner 18 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Charging source: engine and regenerative braking.
- Benefit: fuel efficiency.
- Limit: no external plug, no meaningful electric-only range.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4074, SNT-4077.
**Trap:** Treating HEV and PHEV as the same "hybrid" category.
**Mains hook:** Use HEVs as a transitional efficiency technology that still uses liquid fuel.
**Back:** *"Engine charges it; no plug."*

### Flashcard SNT-4077 — Plug-in hybrid electric vehicle (PHEV)
**Front:** What distinguishes a PHEV from an HEV?
**Definition (learn):** A hybrid with a larger battery that can be charged externally and provides real electric-only range for short trips, with the engine for longer trips (✅ owner 18 §1).
**Breakdown:**
- External charging: yes.
- Electric-only range: meaningful.
- Engine: retained for long trips.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4076, SNT-4074.
**Trap:** Only the PHEV can be plugged in. The 2025 PYQ on alternative powertrains tests this distinction (SNT-4112).
**Mains hook:** Position PHEVs as a bridge for users without full charging access.
**Back:** *"Plug in, drive short, engine for long."*

### Flashcard SNT-4078 — Fuel-cell electric vehicle (FCEV)
**Front:** How does an FCEV generate electricity, and what is its tailpipe output?
**Definition (learn):** An FCEV generates electricity onboard from hydrogen in a proton-exchange-membrane (PEM) fuel cell and drives an electric motor. Tailpipe output is water (✅ owner 18 §2, §6).
**Breakdown:**
- Electrochemical reaction of hydrogen and oxygen generates electricity.
- Tailpipe output: water and heat.
- Still an electric-drive vehicle.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024 Prelims GS-I Q37 tested FCEV exhaust as water vapour (SNT-4111).
**Links:** SNT-4074, SNT-4079, SNT-4091, SNT-4111.
**Trap:** "Fuel-cell vehicles are non-electric." They are electric-drive vehicles.
**Mains hook:** Qualify hydrogen mobility by production route, storage and refuelling, not tailpipe alone.
**Back:** *"Hydrogen in, electricity made, water out."*

### Flashcard SNT-4079 — PEM versus SOFC fuel cells
**Front:** Why does a PEM fuel cell suit vehicles while a solid-oxide fuel cell (SOFC) suits stationary power?
**Definition (learn):** A PEM fuel cell operates at low temperature and suits vehicles. An SOFC operates at high temperature and suits stationary power (✅ owner 18 §2, advanced §2).
**Breakdown:**
- PEM: low temperature; vehicle use.
- SOFC: high temperature; stationary power.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4078.
**Trap:** Assuming all fuel cells are used in cars.
**Mains hook:** Use the temperature difference to explain why cars and power plants use different fuel-cell types.
**Back:** *"PEM for cars, SOFC for hot stationary power."*

### Flashcard SNT-4080 — Lithium-ion cell basics: cathode, anode, electrolyte
**Front:** What are the core parts of a lithium-ion cell, and how does discharge deliver power?
**Definition (learn):** Cathode, anode and electrolyte. During discharge, lithium ions move through the electrolyte and electrons flow through the external circuit to power the motor (✅ owner 18 §1–3).
**Breakdown:**
- Cathode: positive electrode material.
- Anode: negative electrode, usually graphite.
- Electrolyte: medium for ion movement.
- Discharge: electrons power the motor externally.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level cell description.
**Links:** SNT-4081, SNT-4082, SNT-4085, SNT-4087.
**Trap:** Battery chemistry is not only the lithium mineral; cathode choice decides mineral needs (SNT-4081).
**Mains hook:** Explain the cell briefly, then connect to energy density and mineral supply.
**Back:** *"Cathode, anode, electrolyte: ions move, electrons power."*

### Flashcard SNT-4081 — Cathode chemistry: NMC/NCA versus LFP
**Front:** How do NMC/NCA cathodes differ from LFP cathodes in mineral use and performance?
**Definition (learn):** NMC/NCA have higher energy density and longer range but use cobalt and nickel. LFP has lower energy density but is cheaper, more thermally stable and free of cobalt and nickel (✅ owner 18 §2, ⚠️ owner analysis on supply risk).
**Breakdown:**
- NMC/NCA: nickel, manganese or aluminium, cobalt; high density; supply and cost risk.
- LFP: iron phosphate; lower density; cheaper; thermally stable.
- LFP dominates Indian two-wheeler, three-wheeler and bus applications (⚠️ owner analysis).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level comparison.
**Links:** SNT-4080, SNT-4173, SNT-4182.
**Trap:** "EV batteries need cobalt and nickel." It depends on chemistry; LFP uses neither.
**Mains hook:** State the chemistry before naming the mineral in any critical-minerals argument.
**Back:** *"NMC needs nickel and cobalt; LFP needs neither."*

### Flashcard SNT-4082 — Anode: graphite and silicon blending
**Front:** What is the usual anode material in a lithium-ion cell, and what is changing?
**Definition (learn):** The negative electrode, usually graphite, which stores lithium during charging. Silicon blending is increasing to raise capacity (✅ owner 18 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Graphite: common anode.
- Silicon blends: higher capacity.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4080, SNT-4081.
**Trap:** Confusing anode and cathode. The anode is negative and usually graphite.
**Mains hook:** Use anode materials to show that graphite supply is also a strategic input (⚠️ owner analysis).
**Back:** *"Anode stores; graphite usually, silicon rising."*

### Flashcard SNT-4083 — Sodium-ion battery
**Front:** Why is sodium-ion strategically interesting for India?
**Definition (learn):** A battery that uses abundant sodium instead of lithium. It has lower energy density but better resource security and cold-weather behaviour (⚠️ owner 18 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Resource: sodium is abundant.
- Trade-off: lower energy density.
- Strategic logic: reduces lithium import dependence.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level; no deployment figure.
**Links:** SNT-4081, SNT-4173.
**Trap:** Sodium-ion does not use lithium at all; do not count it in lithium-supply estimates.
**Mains hook:** Present sodium-ion as a resource-diversification option, not a current replacement.
**Back:** *"Sodium instead of lithium: less dense, more secure."*

### Flashcard SNT-4084 — Solid-state battery: promise and pre-commercial status
**Front:** What does a solid-state battery promise, and is it deployed in Indian EVs?
**Definition (learn):** Replaces the liquid or gel electrolyte with a solid one, promising higher energy density and lower fire risk. It remains pre-commercial at automotive scale (⚠️ owner 18 §2, §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Promise: energy density and fire safety.
- Status: pre-commercial at automotive scale.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level status as of build.
**Links:** SNT-4080, SNT-4110.
**Trap:** "Solid-state batteries are already in Indian EVs." Not supported by the owner's sources.
**Mains hook:** Present solid-state as a future option; base current policy on deployable chemistries.
**Back:** *"Solid electrolyte: promising, not yet on the road."*

### Flashcard SNT-4085 — Battery Management System (BMS)
**Front:** What does a BMS do, and why do most EV fire incidents trace to it?
**Definition (learn):** Electronics that monitor cell voltage, current and temperature, balance cells, and prevent overcharge and over-discharge (⚠️ owner 18 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Monitors: voltage, current, temperature.
- Balances cells.
- Protects against overcharge and over-discharge.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner analysis: incidents trace to thermal management and BMS quality rather than lithium as such (⚠️).
**Links:** SNT-4080, SNT-4106.
**Trap:** Blaming lithium chemistry alone for fire safety. Standards enforcement and thermal design matter more.
**Mains hook:** Argue that battery safety is a standards and quality problem, not only a technology choice.
**Back:** *"Watch volts, amps, heat: prevent the cascade."*

### Flashcard SNT-4086 — Battery swapping
**Front:** What is battery swapping, and what does it require to work at scale?
**Definition (learn):** Exchanging a depleted pack for a charged one at a station. It removes charging time and decouples battery cost from vehicle cost (⚠️ owner 18 §2, advanced §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Attractive for two- and three-wheelers.
- Requires standardised pack form factors and interoperability.
- Failure mode: fragmentation into proprietary networks.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level analysis; no programme figure.
**Links:** SNT-4087, SNT-4102.
**Trap:** Battery swapping without standard packs creates lock-in, not a national network.
**Mains hook:** Argue for interoperability standards and battery-as-a-service financing.
**Back:** *"Swap works only if the packs match."*

### Flashcard SNT-4087 — Energy density
**Front:** Why does energy density matter for EV design?
**Definition (learn):** Energy stored per unit mass or volume. It determines range and the weight trade-off (✅ owner 18 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Higher density: longer range or lighter pack.
- Chemistry choice trades density against cost and mineral risk.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level definition.
**Links:** SNT-4081, SNT-4080.
**Trap:** Confusing energy density with power density or with capacity alone.
**Mains hook:** Use density to explain why LFP is preferred in some segments despite lower density.
**Back:** *"Energy per kilogram: range and weight."*

### Flashcard SNT-4088 — E20 and flex-fuel vehicles
**Front:** What is E20, and what is a flex-fuel vehicle?
**Definition (learn):** E20 is 20 percent ethanol blended with petrol, India's blending target under the ethanol-blending programme. A flex-fuel vehicle can run on a wide range of ethanol-petrol blends (⚠️ owner 18 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- E20: 20% ethanol.
- Blending programme: run by MoPNG (✅ owner 18 §4).
- Flex-fuel: engine and fuel system adapted to a wide ethanol range.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** E20 target (⚠️ owner summary, no date verified here).
**Links:** SNT-4089, SNT-4090, SNT-4100.
**Trap:** E20 is a fuel-transition measure, not a battery technology.
**Mains hook:** Use E20 to argue for fuel diversification alongside electrification.
**Back:** *"Twenty percent ethanol, flex to more."*

### Flashcard SNT-4089 — Compressed Bio-Gas (CBG) and SATAT
**Front:** What is CBG, and which initiative promotes it?
**Definition (learn):** Purified biogas from agricultural residue, press mud or municipal waste. It is promoted through the SATAT initiative (⚠️ owner 18 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Feedstock: agricultural residue, press mud, municipal waste.
- Product: purified biogas (compressed).
- Initiative: SATAT.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level; SATAT under MoPNG.
**Links:** SNT-4088, SNT-4090.
**Trap:** CBG is not hydrogen; it is a biogas product.
**Mains hook:** Link CBG to farm-waste value and rural income.
**Back:** *"Farm waste, purified, compressed: SATAT."*

### Flashcard SNT-4090 — Biodiesel and transesterification
**Front:** How is biodiesel made, and how is it used?
**Definition (learn):** Biodiesel is produced by transesterification of vegetable oils or used cooking oil and blended with diesel (⚠️ owner 18 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Process: transesterification.
- Feedstock: vegetable oils or used cooking oil.
- Use: blend with diesel.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4088, SNT-4089.
**Trap:** Biodiesel is not ethanol. Different feedstock and process.
**Mains hook:** Use biodiesel to show feedstock-versus-food trade-offs (⚠️ standard).
**Back:** *"Oil reacts, becomes diesel blend."*

### Flashcard SNT-4091 — Hydrogen colours: production route, not the gas
**Front:** What do green, blue and grey hydrogen mean?
**Definition (learn):** Colours describe how hydrogen is produced. Green uses electrolysis with renewable electricity; blue is fossil-based with carbon capture; grey is fossil-based without capture. The molecule is identical (⚠️ owner 18 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Green: renewable electrolysis.
- Blue: fossil plus carbon capture.
- Grey: fossil without capture.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level labels.
**Links:** SNT-4078, SNT-4103.
**Trap:** Thinking colours change the gas's chemistry.
**Mains hook:** Qualify any hydrogen claim by production route and upstream emissions.
**Back:** *"Colour is the route, not the molecule."*

### Flashcard SNT-4092 — Scheme chronology: FAME-I, FAME-II, EMPS-2024, PM E-DRIVE
**Front:** What is the sequence of India's EV demand-incentive schemes?
**Definition (learn):** FAME-I, then FAME-II (ended 31 Mar 2024), then EMPS-2024 as a bridge (April to September 2024), then PM E-DRIVE from 1 Oct 2024 (✅ owner 18 §6).
**Breakdown:**
- FAME-II: ended 31 Mar 2024.
- EMPS-2024: bridge, Apr–Sep 2024.
- PM E-DRIVE: from 1 Oct 2024.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Dates as recorded by owner.
**Links:** SNT-4093, SNT-4094.
**Trap:** PM E-DRIVE is not the old FAME with a new title; it is a successor with its own notification.
**Mains hook:** Use the chronology to show policy shift from broad subsidy to segment-wise design.
**Back:** *"FAME, FAME-II ends, EMPS bridges, E-DRIVE leads."*

### Flashcard SNT-4093 — PM E-DRIVE: approval, outlay and original notification
**Front:** When was PM E-DRIVE approved, what is its outlay, and what was the original period?
**Definition (learn):** A demand-incentive scheme approved by the Union Cabinet on 11 Sep 2024 with an outlay of ₹10,900 crore. Notified by S.O. 4259(E) dated 29 Sep 2024 for 1 Oct 2024 to 31 Mar 2026 (✅ owner 18 §6, §8; PMO and gazette as cited by owner).
**Breakdown:**
- Approval: 11 Sep 2024.
- Outlay: ₹10,900 crore.
- Notification: S.O. 4259(E), 29 Sep 2024.
- Original period: 1 Oct 2024 to 31 Mar 2026.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** SNT-4092, SNT-4094, SNT-4095.
**Trap:** Approval is not expenditure; the outlay is a ceiling.
**Mains hook:** Use the approval-notification-extension trail to show policy reviewability.
**Back:** *"Eleven September approves; S.O. 4259 notifies."*

### Flashcard SNT-4094 — PM E-DRIVE extension to 31 March 2028
**Front:** To what date was PM E-DRIVE extended, under which notification, and what happened to EMPS-2024?
**Definition (learn):** Extended to 31 March 2028 by S.O. 3626(E) dated 7 Aug 2025, within the same ₹10,900 crore ceiling, expressly subsuming EMPS-2024 (✅ owner 18 §6, §8).
**Breakdown:**
- New end date: 31 Mar 2028.
- Notification: S.O. 3626(E), 7 Aug 2025.
- Outlay unchanged: ₹10,900 crore.
- EMPS-2024 subsumed.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** SNT-4093, SNT-4095, SNT-4108.
**Trap:** "PM E-DRIVE ended on 31 March 2026." It was extended; segment dates differ.
**Mains hook:** Argue that taper-by-segment is a policy signal of which markets are considered self-sustaining.
**Back:** *"Extended to 2028, same ceiling, EMPS folded in."*

### Flashcard SNT-4095 — PM E-DRIVE segment-wise end dates
**Front:** What are the segment-wise end dates under PM E-DRIVE as recorded by the owner?
**Definition (learn):** S.O. 1617(E), 27 Mar 2026, fixed segment dates. The e-3W (L5) segment closed on 26 Dec 2025 after reaching its target. Registered e-2W incentives run to 31 Jul 2026. E-rickshaw and e-cart run to 31 Mar 2028 (✅ owner 18 §8).
**Breakdown:**
- e-3W (L5): closed 26 Dec 2025, target reached.
- e-2W: to 31 Jul 2026.
- e-rickshaw and e-cart: to 31 Mar 2028.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Segment dates as at owner's build; time-varying, re-check the portal before quoting.
**Links:** SNT-4094, SNT-4108.
**Trap:** Headline end date and segment end dates differ.
**Mains hook:** Use segment tapering to argue for differentiated subsidy exit.
**Back:** *"Headline 2028; segments end sooner."*

### Flashcard SNT-4096 — MHI Lok Sabha reply (28 Jul 2026): incentivised EVs and reimbursement
**Front:** What performance figures did MHI report in its 28 Jul 2026 Lok Sabha reply?
**Definition (learn):** MHI reported 23,22,878 EVs incentivised and ₹2,281.94 crore reimbursed to OEMs as of 22 Jul 2026 (✅ owner 18 §8, Lok Sabha reply as cited).
**Breakdown:**
- EVs incentivised: 23,22,878.
- Reimbursement: ₹2,281.94 crore.
- As-of date: 22 Jul 2026.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Figures are dated; re-check for later updates.
**Links:** SNT-4093, SNT-4094.
**Trap:** Reimbursement against a ceiling measures disbursal pace, not market size.
**Mains hook:** Use the figures to judge disbursal pace and implementation, not market maturity.
**Back:** *"Twenty-three lakh, two thousand crore: dated figures."*

### Flashcard SNT-4097 — PM-eBus Sewa and its payment-security mechanism
**Front:** What are PM-eBus Sewa and its payment-security mechanism, and who runs each?
**Definition (learn):** PM-eBus Sewa is a MoHUA scheme for city e-buses, targeting 10,000 e-buses. Its payment-security mechanism (PSM) is an MHI instrument worth ₹3,435.33 crore (✅ owner 18 §6, §8).
**Breakdown:**
- PM-eBus Sewa: MoHUA; 10,000 e-buses; owner cites ₹57,613 crore estimate including ₹20,000 crore central support.
- PSM: MHI; ₹3,435.33 crore.
- Separate ministry and separate scheme from PM E-DRIVE.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level figures.
**Links:** SNT-4093, SNT-4100.
**Trap:** Treating PM-eBus Sewa as part of PM E-DRIVE.
**Mains hook:** Use it to show public-transport procurement risk as a distinct market failure.
**Back:** *"Buses by MoHUA, payment security by MHI."*

### Flashcard SNT-4098 — PLI for Advanced Chemistry Cell (ACC) battery storage
**Front:** What does the ACC battery PLI fund, and what is its outlay and capacity target?
**Definition (learn):** A manufacturing-side incentive for domestic advanced-chemistry-cell capacity, with ₹18,100 crore for 50 GWh (✅ owner 18 §4, §8).
**Breakdown:**
- Target: domestic cell manufacturing.
- Outlay: ₹18,100 crore.
- Capacity: 50 GWh.
- Supply-side, not a buyer subsidy.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level; ongoing.
**Links:** SNT-4100, SNT-4099, SNT-4080.
**Trap:** An announced GWh target is not operating capacity.
**Mains hook:** Argue that cell manufacturing is the strategic bottleneck, not only vehicle sales.
**Back:** *"Eighteen thousand crore for fifty gigawatt-hours of cells."*

### Flashcard SNT-4099 — PLI-Auto (automobile and auto components)
**Front:** What does PLI-Auto fund, and what are its outlay and period?
**Definition (learn):** A manufacturing incentive for the automobile and auto-component industry, with outlay ₹25,938 crore and incentive period FY2022-23 to FY2026-27 (✅ owner 18 §8).
**Breakdown:**
- Supply-side incentive.
- Outlay: ₹25,938 crore.
- Period: FY2022-23 to FY2026-27.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4098, SNT-4100.
**Trap:** PLI-Auto is not the EV demand subsidy; PM E-DRIVE is.
**Mains hook:** Use PLI-Auto with PLI-ACC to show the supply-side layer of mobility policy.
**Back:** *"Auto PLI for makers, not buyers."*

### Flashcard SNT-4100 — Ministry of Heavy Industries: the scheme anchor
**Front:** Which ministry anchors PM E-DRIVE, the ACC PLI and PLI-Auto?
**Definition (learn):** The Ministry of Heavy Industries (MHI) anchors PM E-DRIVE demand incentives and the PLI-Auto and PLI-ACC manufacturing schemes (✅ owner 18 §4).
**Breakdown:**
- Demand: PM E-DRIVE.
- Supply: PLI-Auto and PLI-ACC.
- PM e-Bus Sewa PSM: also MHI.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level institutional mapping.
**Links:** SNT-4093, SNT-4098, SNT-4099.
**Trap:** Confusing MHI with MoRTH (vehicle approval) or MoP (charging guidelines).
**Mains hook:** Use the ministry map to show that EV policy is spread across several ministries.
**Back:** *"Heavy Industries: demand and manufacturing schemes."*

### Flashcard SNT-4101 — MoRTH and vehicle type approval
**Front:** What does MoRTH regulate for EVs?
**Definition (learn):** Vehicle type approval, safety and emission norms under the Motor Vehicles Act and the Central Motor Vehicles Rules (CMVR). MoRTH decides what may legally be sold and driven (✅ owner 18 §4).
**Breakdown:**
- Type approval.
- Safety norms.
- Emission norms.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4100, SNT-4102.
**Trap:** MoRTH is not the charging-standard authority.
**Mains hook:** Use to show the regulator of vehicles versus regulator of charging.
**Back:** *"MoRTH approves the vehicle."*

### Flashcard SNT-4102 — Charging governance: Ministry of Power, BIS, CEA, BEE
**Front:** Which four bodies have distinct roles in EV charging governance?
**Definition (learn):** Ministry of Power issues charging guidelines and standards; BIS notifies connector and safety standards; CEA governs electricity-system and safety rules; BEE handles efficiency labelling (✅ owner 18 §4).
**Breakdown:**
- MoP: guidelines.
- BIS: standards.
- CEA: power-system and electrical safety.
- BEE: efficiency labelling.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Bharat AC-001 and DC-001 belong to India's charging vocabulary (✅ owner 18 §6).
**Links:** SNT-4086, SNT-4101.
**Trap:** Treating the charging ecosystem as one standards body.
**Mains hook:** Argue that charging rollout needs coordination across four roles, not one.
**Back:** *"Power guides, BIS standards, CEA safety, BEE labels."*

### Flashcard SNT-4103 — MNRE: green hydrogen mission and transport pilots
**Front:** What did MNRE issue for hydrogen in transport, and when?
**Definition (learn):** Scheme guidelines for green-hydrogen pilot projects in the transport sector under the National Green Hydrogen Mission, issued 14 Feb 2024 (✅ owner 18 §8, MNRE notice as cited).
**Breakdown:**
- Mission: National Green Hydrogen Mission (MNRE).
- Transport pilots: guidelines, 14 Feb 2024.
- Relevance: heavy-duty and long-haul pathways.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 14 Feb 2024.
**Links:** SNT-4091, SNT-4078.
**Trap:** Hydrogen generation belongs to the Environment cross-link; this card covers mobility use.
**Mains hook:** Argue that hydrogen suits hard-to-electrify segments.
**Back:** *"Green hydrogen pilots for transport: February 2024."*

### Flashcard SNT-4104 — Battery Waste Management Rules, 2022: EPR
**Front:** What do the Battery Waste Management Rules, 2022 require, and who administers them?
**Definition (learn):** Extended Producer Responsibility for producers over collection, recycling and recovery of battery materials, for all battery types with targets. Administered by MoEFCC (✅ owner 18 §4, §6).
**Breakdown:**
- EPR: producer responsibility for end-of-life.
- Covers all battery types.
- Recycling and recovery targets.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 Rules.
**Links:** SNT-4119, SNT-4176.
**Trap:** Electrification without recycling relocates the environmental problem.
**Mains hook:** Use EPR as the legal hook for end-of-life responsibility.
**Back:** *"Producers own the battery's end of life."*

### Flashcard SNT-4105 — NITI Aayog: advisory, not implementing
**Front:** What is NITI Aayog's role in the EV transition?
**Definition (learn):** Policy analysis and roadmap work, such as studies on battery swapping and mobility transition. It is advisory, not an implementing ministry (✅ owner 18 §4).
**Breakdown:**
- Role: advisory and roadmap.
- Not an implementer.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4086, SNT-4100.
**Trap:** Treating NITI Aayog as the scheme-implementing ministry.
**Mains hook:** Use to show the separation between advice and implementation.
**Back:** *"Aayog advises; ministries implement."*

### Flashcard SNT-4106 — Tailpipe versus lifecycle emissions
**Front:** Why is a BEV not automatically zero-emission across its lifecycle?
**Definition (learn):** BEVs eliminate tailpipe emissions but shift the burden to grid mix, cell manufacturing and end-of-life handling (✅ owner 18 §7, ⚠️ owner analysis).
**Breakdown:**
- Tailpipe: zero.
- Grid mix: determines electricity emissions.
- Manufacturing and end-of-life: add burden.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level analysis, no figure.
**Links:** SNT-4075, SNT-4104, SNT-4109.
**Trap:** Zero tailpipe is not zero lifecycle.
**Mains hook:** Pair electrification with grid greening and recycling.
**Back:** *"Zero at the pipe, not in the grid."*

### Flashcard SNT-4107 — Trap: every electric-looking vehicle is a BEV
**Front:** Which vehicle-classification statements are wrong in Prelims?
**Definition (learn):** HEVs, PHEVs and FCEVs are distinct architectures. Only the PHEV can be externally charged. A fuel-cell vehicle is electric-drive (✅ owner 18 §7).
**Breakdown:**
- HEV and PHEV are not the same.
- FCEV is not non-electric.
- BEV stores all driving energy in the battery.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner trap list.
**Links:** SNT-4074, SNT-4076, SNT-4077, SNT-4078.
**Trap:** Statement elimination: check the energy source first.
**Mains hook:** Start with the architecture before any policy claim.
**Back:** *"Ask where the electricity comes from."*

### Flashcard SNT-4108 — Trap: PM E-DRIVE ended on 31 March 2026
**Front:** Did PM E-DRIVE end on 31 March 2026?
**Definition (learn):** No. It was extended to 31 March 2028 by S.O. 3626(E). Segment dates differ, with the e-2W segment ending 31 Jul 2026 and e-rickshaw and e-cart ending 31 Mar 2028 (✅ owner 18 §7, §8).
**Breakdown:**
- Original end: 31 Mar 2026.
- Extension: 31 Mar 2028.
- Segment dates: differ.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** SNT-4094, SNT-4095.
**Trap:** Headline end date versus segment end date.
**Mains hook:** Use the example to teach dated-source discipline.
**Back:** *"Extended, but segments close in their own time."*

### Flashcard SNT-4109 — Trap: clean mobility means only renewable generation
**Front:** Why is clean mobility not reducible to renewable electricity generation?
**Definition (learn):** Vehicle technology, storage, charging infrastructure and alternative transport fuels are separate analytical layers (✅ owner 18 §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Vehicle layer: powertrain.
- Storage layer: battery manufacturing and recycling.
- Infrastructure: charging and standards.
- Fuel layer: ethanol, CNG/LNG, hydrogen.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4106, SNT-4088, SNT-4091.
**Trap:** Treating a clean grid as the whole answer.
**Mains hook:** Structure clean-mobility answers into vehicle, storage, infrastructure and fuel layers.
**Back:** *"Vehicle, battery, charger, fuel: four layers."*

### Flashcard SNT-4110 — Trap: solid-state already in Indian EVs and lithium-only battery thinking
**Front:** Which two battery statements are wrong in Prelims or Mains?
**Definition (learn):** (a) Solid-state batteries are already in Indian EVs: they remain pre-commercial at automotive scale. (b) Battery chemistry is only about lithium: cathode chemistry decides which minerals matter (✅ owner 18 §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Solid-state: pre-commercial.
- Chemistry: LFP needs neither cobalt nor nickel.
- Sodium-ion avoids lithium.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner trap list.
**Links:** SNT-4084, SNT-4081, SNT-4083.
**Trap:** Mineral statements need the chemistry first.
**Mains hook:** Use chemistry to make mineral-security arguments precise.
**Back:** *"Solid not yet, lithium not alone."*

### Flashcard SNT-4111 — 2024 Prelims PYQ: FCEV exhaust is water vapour
**Front:** What is the exhaust of a fuel-cell electric vehicle? (2024 Prelims GS-I Q37)
**Definition (learn):** Water vapour. The onboard fuel cell combines hydrogen and oxygen electrochemically; the direct products are water and heat (✅ owner 18 PYQ integration, key available locally, answer not recorded here).
**Breakdown:**
- Reaction: hydrogen plus oxygen.
- Products: water and heat.
- Lifecycle caveat: hydrogen production and compression matter.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Prelims GS-I 2024 Q37.
**Links:** SNT-4078, SNT-4091.
**Trap:** "Tailpipe zero means lifecycle zero."
**Mains hook:** Qualify with production route.
**Back:** *"Hydrogen in, water out."*

### Flashcard SNT-4112 — 2025 Prelims PYQ: alternative-powertrain vehicles
**Front:** Which alternative-powertrain vehicles does the 2025 Prelims GS-I Q41 test (battery EV, hydrogen fuel-cell, hybrid)?
**Definition (learn):** The item compares battery EVs, hydrogen fuel-cell vehicles and hybrids (✅ owner 18 PYQ integration; answer not recorded here).
**Breakdown:**
- Battery EV: stored electricity.
- Fuel-cell: onboard electricity from hydrogen.
- Hybrid: engine plus battery, no full plug-in.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Prelims GS-I 2025 Q41.
**Links:** SNT-4074, SNT-4077.
**Trap:** Mixing up the energy source of each.
**Mains hook:** Use the three-way distinction in any powertrain answer.
**Back:** *"Battery, fuel-cell, hybrid: know the source."*

### Flashcard SNT-4113 — 2019 Prelims PYQ: hydrogen-enriched CNG
**Front:** What advantages of hydrogen-enriched CNG for public transport does the 2019 Prelims item test?
**Definition (learn):** Adding hydrogen to CNG can change combustion and may reduce selected local pollutants in an appropriate engine and calibration. It is not a fuel-cell vehicle (✅ owner 18 advanced routed evidence; key not held locally).
**Breakdown:**
- Hydrogen-enriched CNG: combustion change.
- Not an FCEV.
- Benefit depends on blend, engine and measurement.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Prelims GS-I 2019 Q43.
**Links:** SNT-4116, SNT-4078.
**Trap:** Calling H-CNG "universally cleaner."
**Mains hook:** Qualify with blend and upstream source.
**Back:** *"Blend helps locally, not universally."*

### Flashcard SNT-4114 — 2023 GS-III PYQ: EV contribution to emission reduction
**Front:** How do electric vehicles contribute to carbon-emission reduction, and what are their benefits? (2023 GS-III Q15, 15 marks, 250 words)
**Definition (learn):** The answer should separate tailpipe, grid, manufacturing and recycling effects and then state benefits and conditions (✅ owner 18 advanced PYQ routing, ⚠️ analysis).
**Breakdown:**
- Tailpipe benefit in urban areas.
- Grid-mix dependence.
- Battery manufacturing and recycling.
- Benefits: urban air quality, fuel-import reduction in selected segments.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Paper GS-III, 2023, Q15.
**Links:** SNT-4106, SNT-4109, SNT-4119.
**Trap:** Claiming EVs are emission-free overall.
**Mains hook:** Conclude that EVs are part of a broader system, with conditions.
**Back:** *"Cleaner streets, cleaner grid, cleaner cycle."*

### Flashcard SNT-4115 — Advanced: chemistry-conditional mineral demand
**Front:** Why must any EV critical-mineral argument start with battery chemistry?
**Definition (learn):** NMC/NCA need nickel and cobalt; LFP needs neither; sodium-ion avoids lithium; graphite is common to most lithium-ion anodes (✅ owner 18 advanced §2, ⚠️ analysis).
**Breakdown:**
- Chemistry first.
- Then mineral.
- Then supply-chain location.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4081, SNT-4173, SNT-4083.
**Trap:** "EVs need cobalt." Only some chemistries.
**Mains hook:** Use chemistry to make mineral-security claims precise.
**Back:** *"Chemistry sets the mineral bill."*

### Flashcard SNT-4116 — Advanced: hydrogen-enriched CNG is not a fuel-cell vehicle
**Front:** Why must hydrogen-enriched CNG not be confused with a hydrogen fuel-cell vehicle?
**Definition (learn):** H-CNG burns a hydrogen-methane mix in an engine; an FCEV generates electricity in a fuel cell (✅ owner 18 advanced routed evidence).
**Breakdown:**
- H-CNG: engine combustion.
- FCEV: electrochemical conversion.
- Different engines, different emissions profiles.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4113, SNT-4078.
**Trap:** "Hydrogen in both means the same vehicle."
**Mains hook:** Separate combustion and fuel-cell pathways in any alternative-fuel answer.
**Back:** *"Burn the mix, or make electricity: different roads."*

### Flashcard SNT-4117 — Advanced: charging, grid and standards debate
**Front:** What constraints does large-scale EV charging place on the grid and on standards?
**Definition (learn):** Charging rollout needs land, transformer capacity, tariff clarity, interoperability and safety discipline. Large charging increases electricity demand (✅ owner 18 advanced §5, §6, ⚠️ analysis).
**Breakdown:**
- Land and transformers.
- Tariff clarity.
- Interoperability and safety standards.
- Grid readiness.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4102, SNT-4106.
**Trap:** "One connector standard is the whole charging debate."
**Mains hook:** Argue that charging is a power-system and standards problem.
**Back:** *"Land, grid, tariff, standard, safety."*

### Flashcard SNT-4118 — Advanced: announced capacity is not operating or safe fleet stock
**Front:** Why should announced battery capacity not be read as operating capacity or deployed fleet?
**Definition (learn):** An announced GWh target is an authorised plan. Operating output, yield and safe deployed fleet stock are separate measures (✅ owner 18 advanced core architecture, ⚠️ analysis).
**Breakdown:**
- Announced capacity.
- Operating capacity.
- Deployed fleet stock.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level analysis.
**Links:** SNT-4098, SNT-4094.
**Trap:** Quoting the 50 GWh PLI target as produced capacity.
**Mains hook:** Use the three measures to evaluate any battery-manufacturing claim.
**Back:** *"Announced, operating, deployed: three numbers."*

### Flashcard SNT-4119 — Advanced: battery recycling as materials security
**Front:** Why is battery recycling a materials-security instrument, not only a waste rule?
**Definition (learn):** Recycling is the only domestic "mine" India can expand. It recovers materials from end-of-life cells and packs under the 2022 Rules and EPR (✅ owner 18 advanced §2, ⚠️ analysis).
**Breakdown:**
- Domestic source of material.
- EPR hook.
- Reduces import dependence.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4104, SNT-4176, SNT-4106.
**Trap:** Recycling treated as a downstream afterthought.
**Mains hook:** Argue that recycling is central to materials strategy and resource efficiency.
**Back:** *"Recycle is the domestic mine."*

### GROUP — Drones, UAVs and Robotics Policy

### Flashcard SNT-4120 — Drone governance: aircraft, software, airspace and human control
**Front:** Why is drone policy more than a manufacturing question?
**Definition (learn):** Drone capability and risk come from the whole system: airframe, propulsion, sensors, communication links, software, payload, airspace rules and human control, not from the airframe alone (✅ owner 19 advanced §1, §5).
**Breakdown:**
- Hardware: airframe, propulsion, power.
- Mission layer: sensors, software, communications, payload.
- Governance layer: registration, permission, airspace, pilot competence.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level architecture.
**Links:** SNT-4121, SNT-4122, SNT-4124, SNT-4125.
**Trap:** "Indigenous drones means assembling airframes." Software and payload matter equally.
**Mains hook:** Frame drone policy as capability plus governance, not one without the other.
**Back:** *"Airframe is the body; software and rules are the brain and the road."*

### Flashcard SNT-4121 — UAV, UAS, RPAS and drone
**Front:** What is the difference between a UAV, a UAS, an RPAS and a drone?
**Definition (learn):** A UAV is the aircraft. A UAS is the aircraft plus the remote pilot station, command-and-control links and associated elements, the term used in Indian rules. RPAS is ICAO's term for a UAS with a remote pilot in command, so non-autonomous. Drone is colloquial (✅ owner 19 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- UAV: aircraft.
- UAS: aircraft plus ground and command elements.
- RPAS: UAS with remote pilot in command.
- Drone: informal umbrella.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level definitions.
**Links:** SNT-4120, SNT-4138.
**Trap:** Treating the four as synonyms is a definitional error.
**Mains hook:** Open the answer with precise terms, since the regulatory term matters.
**Back:** *"Aircraft, system, pilot-commanded, colloquial."*

### Flashcard SNT-4122 — DGCA weight categories: nano, micro, small, medium, large
**Front:** What are the all-up-weight thresholds for drone categories in India?
**Definition (learn):** Nano up to 250 g; micro more than 250 g up to 2 kg; small more than 2 kg up to 25 kg; medium more than 25 kg up to 150 kg; large more than 150 kg (✅ owner 19 §1, Drone Rules 2021 as cited).
**Breakdown:**
- Nano: up to 250 g.
- Micro: >250 g to 2 kg.
- Small: >2 kg to 25 kg.
- Medium: >25 kg to 150 kg.
- Large: >150 kg.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Thresholds 250 g, 2 kg, 25 kg, 150 kg.
**Links:** SNT-4123, SNT-4146.
**Trap:** Using "more than" versus "up to" boundaries wrongly.
**Mains hook:** Explain that regulatory burden rises with category.
**Back:** *"Two-fifty grams, two kilos, twenty-five, one-fifty."*

### Flashcard SNT-4123 — Remote pilot certificate exemptions: nano and non-commercial micro
**Front:** Which drone operations are exempt from the remote pilot certificate requirement?
**Definition (learn):** Nano drones and micro drones used non-commercially are exempt from the RPC requirement (⚠️ owner 19 §2 summary of Drone Rules 2021).
**Breakdown:**
- Nano: exempt.
- Micro for non-commercial use: exempt.
- Most other categories: RPC from a DGCA-authorised RPTO.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner summary; consolidated current text not verified.
**Links:** SNT-4126, SNT-4122.
**Trap:** Nano exemption does not exempt airspace rules.
**Mains hook:** Show how light-touch rules for small drones balance innovation and safety.
**Back:** *"Nano free; micro free if not for money."*

### Flashcard SNT-4124 — Airspace zones: green, yellow and red
**Front:** What do the green, yellow and red airspace zones require?
**Definition (learn):** Green: no prior permission needed up to the prescribed altitude. Yellow: permission from the concerned air-traffic authority. Red: flight only with Central Government permission (⚠️ owner 19 §2; the zone map is on Digital Sky).
**Breakdown:**
- Green: no prior permission up to prescribed altitude.
- Yellow: ATC permission.
- Red: Central Government permission.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Altitude limit not stated in owner; not asserted.
**Links:** SNT-4125, SNT-4132.
**Trap:** Yellow permission comes from air-traffic authority, not DGCA licensing.
**Mains hook:** Use zone logic to show how airspace safety and security interact.
**Back:** *"Green go, yellow ask ATC, red needs Centre."*

### Flashcard SNT-4125 — Digital Sky: compliance platform, not air-traffic management
**Front:** What does Digital Sky do, and what does it not do?
**Definition (learn):** The official online platform for drone registration, unique identification, permissions and compliance workflows. It is not an air-traffic management system; airspace services remain with AAI and ATC (✅ owner 19 §2, §4).
**Breakdown:**
- Does: registration, unique identification, permissions, compliance, zone map.
- Does not: air-traffic control, airspace management.
- Access: publicly accessible per owner (16 Jul 2026 access).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Portal accessibility is not proof of current rule text (✅ owner 19 §8).
**Links:** SNT-4120, SNT-4124, SNT-4132.
**Trap:** "Digital Sky is only a map app" or "it manages airspace."
**Mains hook:** Use Digital Sky as platformised compliance, then add the privacy and security gaps.
**Back:** *"Permissions and IDs, not traffic control."*

### Flashcard SNT-4126 — Remote Pilot Certificate and Remote Pilot Training Organisations
**Front:** Who issues the remote pilot certificate, and through which bodies must training be obtained?
**Definition (learn):** A remote pilot certificate is required for most categories and is obtained through a DGCA-authorised Remote Pilot Training Organisation (RPTO) (⚠️ owner 19 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- RPC: required for most categories.
- RPTO: DGCA-authorised training.
- Exemptions: see SNT-4123.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner summary.
**Links:** SNT-4123, SNT-4132.
**Trap:** Thinking DGCA itself runs the training.
**Mains hook:** Argue that pilot skills and training ecosystems determine whether drone services scale safely.
**Back:** *"RPC from an authorised RPTO."*

### Flashcard SNT-4127 — BVLOS: sandbox authorisation versus routine permission
**Front:** What is BVLOS, and how has India permitted it?
**Definition (learn):** Beyond Visual Line of Sight operation, the threshold for drone delivery and long-range logistics. India has permitted it through experimental or sandbox authorisations rather than as a general right (⚠️ owner 19 §2, advanced §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Definition: beyond visual line of sight.
- Status: experimental or sandbox authorisation.
- Not: a routine general permission (per owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level; no list of permits asserted.
**Links:** SNT-4120, SNT-4150.
**Trap:** "BVLOS is allowed" versus "BVLOS trials were permitted."
**Mains hook:** Argue that BVLOS needs safety, airspace and liability frameworks before routine use.
**Back:** *"Trial permits, not general right."*

### Flashcard SNT-4128 — Counter-UAS: detection and mitigation under security agencies
**Front:** What is counter-UAS, and which institutions own it?
**Definition (learn):** Detection (radar, RF, acoustic, optical) plus mitigation (jamming, spoofing, capture, kinetic). It is a security function coordinated by security agencies and the armed forces, not civil-aviation licensing (⚠️ owner 19 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Detection: radar, RF, acoustic, optical.
- Mitigation: jamming, spoofing, capture, kinetic.
- Owner: security agencies and armed forces.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level; no 2025-26 framework verified.
**Links:** SNT-4132, SNT-4142.
**Trap:** Counter-UAS is not a DGCA function.
**Mains hook:** Explain why rogue-drone incidents fall between regulatory institutions.
**Back:** *"Detect, then mitigate, by security forces."*

### Flashcard SNT-4129 — Loitering munition
**Front:** What is a loitering munition, and which regime governs it?
**Definition (learn):** A one-way attack system that loiters over an area, searches for a target and strikes it. It sits between an armed UAV and a guided missile (⚠️ owner 19 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Behaviour: loiter, search, strike.
- Regime: defence procurement, not civil drone rules.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4128, SNT-4149.
**Trap:** A loitering munition is a weapon, not a civilian drone category.
**Mains hook:** Separate civil, commercial and military tracks in any drone-policy answer.
**Back:** *"Loiters, finds, strikes: a weapon."*

### Flashcard SNT-4130 — Payload
**Front:** What is a drone payload?
**Definition (learn):** The device or material carried by a drone in addition to the flying platform (✅ owner 19 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Examples: cameras, sensors, spray tanks, parcels (⚠️ owner 19 §5 applications).
- Payload limits constrain endurance.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4120, SNT-4122.
**Trap:** Payload is not the aircraft weight class alone; it is an added load.
**Mains hook:** Use payload to explain why spraying or delivery drones need higher categories.
**Back:** *"Carried extra: the load."*

### Flashcard SNT-4131 — Import policy: complete drones restricted, components free
**Front:** What is India's import policy for drones and drone components?
**Definition (learn):** Import of complete foreign drones is prohibited except for R&D, defence and security with approval. Drone components may be imported freely (✅ owner 19 §4).
**Breakdown:**
- Complete drones: prohibited, with exceptions.
- Components: free import.
- Pairing: restrict finished goods, liberalise inputs.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** DGFT and MoCA import policy as cited by owner.
**Links:** SNT-4130, SNT-4134.
**Trap:** "Drone imports are fully banned." Exceptions exist and components are free.
**Mains hook:** Use the import pair as a model of industrial policy for manufacturing ecosystems.
**Back:** *"Finished drones restricted; parts free."*

### Flashcard SNT-4132 — Institutional split: MoCA, DGCA, AAI and BCAS
**Front:** Which body does policy, regulation, airspace and aviation security for drones?
**Definition (learn):** MoCA makes policy and notifies rules. DGCA regulates: certification, RPC and airworthiness. AAI manages airspace and air-traffic services. BCAS handles aviation security (✅ owner 19 §4).
**Breakdown:**
- MoCA: policy and rules.
- DGCA: civil-aviation regulator.
- AAI: airspace and ATC.
- BCAS: aviation security.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level institutional mapping.
**Links:** SNT-4125, SNT-4124, SNT-4128.
**Trap:** Keep the four distinct; DGCA is not the air-traffic manager.
**Mains hook:** Argue that drone governance needs coordination across four bodies.
**Back:** *"Policy, regulate, airspace, security."*

### Flashcard SNT-4133 — Drone Rules, 2021: gazetted 25 August 2021
**Front:** When were the Drone Rules, 2021 notified, and what did they change?
**Definition (learn):** Notified in the Gazette on 25 Aug 2021, as a lighter regime with fewer forms, no pilot licence for nano drones, a colour-coded airspace map and higher payload limits (✅ owner 19 §8; Gazette as cited).
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 25 Aug 2021.
- Shift: liberalisation with digital compliance.
- Features: airspace map, categories, lighter forms.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 25 Aug 2021.
**Links:** SNT-4134, SNT-4122.
**Trap:** Citing the rules as unchanged since 2021; amendments exist.
**Mains hook:** Use as a case of regulatory liberalisation in a dual-use technology.
**Back:** *"Twenty-fifth August twenty-twenty-one: lighter and digital."*

### Flashcard SNT-4134 — Drone (Amendment) Rules, 2023
**Front:** When were the Drone (Amendment) Rules, 2023 notified, and what is unverified?
**Definition (learn):** Notified 3 Oct 2023, showing the framework evolves after 2021 (✅ owner 19 §8).
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 3 Oct 2023.
- Consolidated text with all amendments: not verified in this build.
- Do not quote specific thresholds without checking the Gazette.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 03 Oct 2023.
**Links:** SNT-4133, SNT-4131.
**Trap:** Treating the 2021 text as the current law without checking amendments.
**Mains hook:** Argue that drone regulation is a living framework needing version control.
**Back:** *"Three October twenty-three: check the current text."*

### Flashcard SNT-4135 — Drone PLI scheme and operational guidelines
**Front:** What is the drone PLI scheme, and what does the owner verify about it?
**Definition (learn):** A production-linked incentive for domestic drones and drone components. Operational guidelines followed on 29 Nov 2022 (✅ owner 19 §4, §8).
**Breakdown:**
- Purpose: domestic manufacturing ecosystem.
- Guidelines: 29 Nov 2022.
- Outlay and completion status: not verified.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 29 Nov 2022.
**Links:** SNT-4131, SNT-4136.
**Trap:** Quoting an outlay for the drone PLI without an official source.
**Mains hook:** Pair manufacturing support with airspace and training capacity.
**Back:** *"Drone PLI: guidelines in 2022, outlay unchecked."*

### Flashcard SNT-4136 — Namo Drone Didi: ₹1,261 crore, 15,000 women SHGs
**Front:** What is Namo Drone Didi, and what was its outlay, target and period?
**Definition (learn):** A Central Sector Scheme under the Ministry of Rural Development through DAY-NRLM. It equips 15,000 selected women SHGs with drones for agricultural rental services (✅ owner 19 §4, §8, PIB and scheme page as cited).
**Breakdown:**
- Outlay: ₹1,261 crore.
- Target: 15,000 women SHGs.
- Period: FY 2023-24 to FY 2025-26.
- Purpose: agricultural rental services and livelihood.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Period has ended; continuation needs fresh verification.
**Links:** SNT-4135, SNT-4137, SNT-4146.
**Trap:** Claiming the scheme continues in FY 2026-27 without a source.
**Mains hook:** Use it as technology-as-livelihood, then examine utilisation, repair ecosystems and rental demand.
**Back:** *"Fifteen thousand SHGs, twelve sixty-one crore, ended."*

### Flashcard SNT-4137 — SVAMITVA drone surveys
**Front:** What is SVAMITVA, and how do drones serve it?
**Definition (learn):** A Ministry of Panchayati Raj scheme using drone-based surveys of inhabited rural land to issue property cards (✅ owner 19 §4).
