**Breakdown:**
- Ministry: Panchayati Raj.
- Method: drone survey of inhabited rural land.
- Output: property cards.
- Completion statistics: not verified in this build.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level; no completion figure asserted.
**Links:** SNT-4136, SNT-4120.
**Trap:** Calling SVAMITVA a drone-manufacturing scheme.
**Mains hook:** Present it as the largest civilian governance use, with data-privacy questions.
**Back:** *"Drone surveys for rural property cards."*

### Flashcard SNT-4138 — Robotics control loop: sensor, controller, actuator, feedback
**Front:** What is the basic robotics loop?
**Definition (learn):** Sensors collect data, a controller processes it, actuators execute the response, and feedback corrects performance (✅ owner 19 §1, §3).
**Breakdown:**
- Sensor: detects position, motion or environment.
- Controller or software: decides.
- Actuator: converts control into movement.
- Feedback: loop closes.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4139, SNT-4140, SNT-4121.
**Trap:** Treating the sensor as the actuator, or omitting feedback.
**Mains hook:** Use the loop to explain why drones are robotics applications.
**Back:** *"Sense, decide, act, check again."*

### Flashcard SNT-4139 — Teleoperated versus autonomous systems
**Front:** How do teleoperated and autonomous systems differ in human role?
**Definition (learn):** Teleoperated systems are controlled remotely by humans. Autonomous systems perform tasks using onboard logic and feedback with limited direct control (✅ owner 19 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Teleoperated: human control.
- Autonomous: onboard logic and limited direct control.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4140, SNT-4148.
**Trap:** Treating autonomous and remotely piloted systems as identical.
**Mains hook:** Argue that liability rises as human control falls.
**Back:** *"Human hand, or onboard mind."*

### Flashcard SNT-4140 — Automation versus autonomy
**Front:** What distinguishes automation from autonomy, and why does it matter for law?
**Definition (learn):** Automation repeats a pre-programmed sequence in a structured setting. Autonomy senses an unstructured environment and chooses actions to meet a goal. Liability and meaningful human control attach to autonomy (⚠️ owner 19 §2, advanced §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Automation: fixed sequence, structured.
- Autonomy: unstructured, chooses actions.
- Governance: liability and human control.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4139, SNT-4151.
**Trap:** Regulating autonomy with rules designed for automation under-governs it.
**Mains hook:** Use the distinction to argue for new accountability rules.
**Back:** *"Automation repeats; autonomy chooses."*

### Flashcard SNT-4141 — 2026 Prelims PYQ: drone swarm communication and countermeasures
**Front:** What does the 2026 Prelims GS-I Q47 test on swarms and countermeasures?
**Definition (learn):** Drone swarm communication, autonomous coordination and electronic countermeasure techniques (✅ owner 19 PYQ integration; provisional 2026 key present, answer not recorded here).
**Breakdown:**
- Swarm: coordinated through communication, localisation and distributed or autonomous control.
- Countermeasures: detection, identification, jamming and spoofing.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Prelims GS-I 2026 Q47; provisional key.
**Links:** SNT-4142, SNT-4140.
**Trap:** A swarm is not many drones flying nearby.
**Mains hook:** Argue that swarm resilience depends on network design and control rules.
**Back:** *"Swarm talks, autonomy decides, jamming interferes."*

### Flashcard SNT-4142 — Countermeasure boundary: detection, identification, neutralisation
**Front:** Why is no single electronic technique a universal counter-drone answer?
**Definition (learn):** Detection, identification, command-link protection, jamming or spoofing resistance and safe neutralisation are separate functions. Interference can also disrupt legitimate communications (✅ owner 19 advanced routed evidence, ⚠️ analysis).
**Breakdown:**
- Detect.
- Identify.
- Protect command link.
- Neutralise safely.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4128, SNT-4141.
**Trap:** Treating jamming as a universal fix.
**Mains hook:** Argue for layered counter-drone capacity.
**Back:** *"Detect, identify, protect, neutralise: four steps."*

### Flashcard SNT-4143 — 2020 Prelims PYQ: drone applications in agriculture, volcano and wildlife
**Front:** What do drones offer in agriculture, volcano observation and wildlife research? (2020 Prelims GS-I Q42)
**Definition (learn):** Drones can collect imagery and sensor data where human access is risky. They do not replace ground verification, permits, animal-welfare safeguards or trained interpretation (✅ owner 19 advanced routed evidence; key not held locally).
**Breakdown:**
- Agriculture: crop assessment.
- Volcano: observation where access is risky.
- Wildlife: research.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Prelims GS-I 2020 Q42.
**Links:** SNT-4120, SNT-4141.
**Trap:** Drones replace ground verification.
**Mains hook:** Qualify drone data with ground truth.
**Back:** *"Eyes where people cannot safely go."*

### Flashcard SNT-4144 — 2025 Prelims PYQ: UAV capabilities by type
**Front:** What does the 2025 Prelims GS-I Q42 test on UAVs?
**Definition (learn):** The capabilities of different types of unmanned aerial vehicles (✅ owner 19 PYQ integration; answer not recorded here).
**Breakdown:**
- Capability depends on type and category.
- Distinguish civil, commercial and military types.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Prelims GS-I 2025 Q42.
**Links:** SNT-4121, SNT-4122.
**Trap:** Assuming all UAVs have the same endurance or payload.
**Mains hook:** Use type-based capability to argue for category-based regulation.
**Back:** *"Type decides capability."*

### Flashcard SNT-4145 — 2026 Prelims PYQ: provisional key status
**Front:** Why is the 2026 drone PYQ treated as provisional in this card set?
**Definition (learn):** The 2026 Set-A key held locally is provisional, so no answer letter is recorded or inferred (✅ owner 19 PYQ integration).
**Breakdown:**
- Key status: provisional.
- Action: confirm key before using as practice answer.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2026 Prelims GS-I Q47.
**Links:** SNT-4141.
**Trap:** Quoting a provisional key as final.
**Mains hook:** Not applicable; a verification note.
**Back:** *"Provisional key: confirm before use."*

### Flashcard SNT-4146 — Trap: a "nano drone" is any tiny aircraft
**Front:** Which drone-category statement is wrong in Prelims?
**Definition (learn):** A "nano drone" is defined by weight, up to 250 g all-up weight, not by appearance. UPSC may test the exact threshold (✅ owner 19 §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Test: all-up weight.
- Boundary: 250 g.
- Appearance is irrelevant.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 250 g.
**Links:** SNT-4122, SNT-4136.
**Trap:** Weight, not size, sets the category.
**Mains hook:** Rarely central; use for exact-threshold recall.
**Back:** *"Weight decides, two-fifty grams."*

### Flashcard SNT-4147 — Trap: Digital Sky is a map app or air-traffic control
**Front:** Which Digital Sky statements are wrong?
**Definition (learn):** Digital Sky is a registration, permission and compliance platform. It is not only a map, and it does not manage air traffic (✅ owner 19 §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Wrong: map-only.
- Wrong: air-traffic management.
- Right: permissions and compliance.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4125, SNT-4132.
**Trap:** Mixing platform functions with airspace services.
**Mains hook:** Use to explain institutional roles.
**Back:** *"Permissions, not traffic control."*

### Flashcard SNT-4148 — Trap: teleoperation and autonomy are the same; drones unrelated to robots
**Front:** Which two robotics-drone statements are wrong?
**Definition (learn):** Teleoperated and autonomous systems differ in human role. Drones are robotics applications combining sensors, control and actuation (✅ owner 19 §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Teleoperation: human control.
- Autonomy: onboard logic.
- Drones: a robotics application.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4138, SNT-4139.
**Trap:** Saying drones and robots are unrelated.
**Mains hook:** Link drone policy to robotics and AI governance.
**Back:** *"Remote is not autonomous; drones are robots."*

### Flashcard SNT-4149 — Trap: civilian drone policy covers military UAVs
**Front:** Does civil drone policy cover military UAVs and loitering munitions?
**Definition (learn):** No. Defence UAV issues have a separate operational and procurement context (✅ owner 19 §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Civil: DGCA and Digital Sky.
- Military: defence procurement and operations.
- Overlap: some technologies.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4129, SNT-4128.
**Trap:** Treating defence UAVs as covered by Drone Rules 2021.
**Mains hook:** Separate civil and strategic tracks.
**Back:** *"Civil rules, defence track."*

### Flashcard SNT-4150 — Advanced: privacy and surveillance debate
**Front:** Why does persistent aerial observation raise privacy concerns?
**Definition (learn):** Persistent observation can conflict with civil liberties unless governed by clear data, retention and use rules (⚠️ owner 19 advanced §6).
**Breakdown:**
- Observation: continuous.
- Governance: data retention, purpose limits.
- Digital Sky does not solve privacy alone.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4125, SNT-4127.
**Trap:** Claiming registration solves privacy.
**Mains hook:** Argue for purpose-bound data rules for drone imagery.
**Back:** *"Watching needs limits."*

### Flashcard SNT-4151 — Advanced: autonomy, liability and accountability
**Front:** Why does greater autonomy make liability harder to assign?
**Definition (learn):** As human control falls, responsibility spreads across software, operator, manufacturer and deployer (⚠️ owner 19 advanced §6).
**Breakdown:**
- Human control falls.
- Software reliability, geofencing, failsafes matter.
- Liability frameworks must follow autonomy.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4140, SNT-4139.
**Trap:** Autonomy removing the need for regulation.
**Mains hook:** Argue for failsafe and accountability standards.
**Back:** *"More autonomy, more accountability."*

### Flashcard SNT-4152 — Advanced: self-certification plus digital compliance as liberalisation
**Front:** What regulatory model did the Drone Rules, 2021 adopt?
**Definition (learn):** A move from restrictive licensing toward self-certification plus digital compliance, an instructive case of liberalisation in a dual-use technology (✅ owner 19 advanced §9).
**Breakdown:**
- Lighter forms.
- Digital permissions.
- Category-based burden.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 25 Aug 2021.
**Links:** SNT-4133, SNT-4125.
**Trap:** Treating liberalisation as absence of safety rules.
**Mains hook:** Evaluate whether digital compliance is enough for dual-use safety.
**Back:** *"Lighter rules, digital checks."*

### Flashcard SNT-4153 — Advanced: dual-use drones and import liberalisation as industrial policy
**Front:** Why is the drone import policy a good comparative example of industrial policy?
**Definition (learn):** It restricts finished imports while freeing component imports, the same logic used in electronics manufacturing (✅ owner 19 advanced §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Restrict finished goods.
- Free components.
- Build domestic assembly and component capacity.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4131, SNT-4135.
**Trap:** Calling import restriction protectionism only.
**Mains hook:** Use as a model of input liberalisation with output protection.
**Back:** *"Finished goods out, parts in."*

### GROUP — Emerging Materials, Rare Earths and Critical Minerals

### Flashcard SNT-4154 — Resource-to-technology value chain and supply security
**Front:** What are the stages of the critical-mineral value chain?
**Definition (learn):** Exploration, mining, beneficiation or refining, component manufacture and strategic use. Supply security depends on the whole chain (✅ owner 20 §1, advanced §1).
**Breakdown:**
- Explore → mine → beneficiate and refine → manufacture components → use.
- Recycling and overseas assets supplement domestic resources.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4155, SNT-4160, SNT-4170.
**Trap:** A mine alone is not security.
**Mains hook:** Argue for a full-chain strategy.
**Back:** *"Explore, mine, refine, build, recycle."*

### Flashcard SNT-4155 — Critical minerals as a policy category
**Front:** What is a critical mineral, and how does it differ from the statutory list?
**Definition (learn):** A mineral essential for economic, strategic or technological reasons and vulnerable to supply disruption. It is a periodically reassessed policy category, distinct from the statutory Part D list (✅ owner 20 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Criterion: economic or strategic importance plus supply vulnerability.
- Reassessed periodically.
- Distinct from Part D and the Seventh Schedule lists.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** See SNT-4166 to SNT-4168.
**Links:** SNT-4156, SNT-4166, SNT-4167, SNT-4168.
**Trap:** Treating the policy category as a fixed chemical class.
**Mains hook:** Define criticality by function and vulnerability.
**Back:** *"Policy category, not a fixed list."*

### Flashcard SNT-4156 — Rare earth elements: 17 elements
**Front:** Which elements make up the rare-earth group?
**Definition (learn):** A specific group of 17 elements: the 15 lanthanides plus scandium and yttrium (✅ owner 20 §1–2, ⚠️ standard composition).
**Breakdown:**
- Lanthanides: 15.
- Plus scandium and yttrium.
- Total: 17.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 17 elements.
**Links:** SNT-4155, SNT-4157, SNT-4174.
**Trap:** REEs are only one subset of critical minerals.
**Mains hook:** Use the exact group to avoid category errors in both Prelims and Mains.
**Back:** *"Fifteen lanthanides, scandium, yttrium: seventeen."*

### Flashcard SNT-4157 — Light versus heavy rare earths
**Front:** Which rare earths are light and abundant, and which are heavy and scarce?
**Definition (learn):** Light REEs (lanthanum, cerium, praseodymium, neodymium) are relatively abundant. Heavy REEs (dysprosium, terbium, yttrium and others) are scarcer, harder to separate and concentrated in a few countries (⚠️ owner 20 §2, §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Light: La, Ce, Pr, Nd.
- Heavy: Dy, Tb, Y and others.
- High-temperature magnets need Dy and Tb.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4156, SNT-4187.
**Trap:** Light-REE reserves do not translate into magnet security.
**Mains hook:** Use the light-heavy split to explain why magnet supply is constrained.
**Back:** *"Light for abundance, heavy for magnets."*

### Flashcard SNT-4158 — Resources versus reserves
**Front:** What separates a resource from a reserve?
**Definition (learn):** A resource is a geologically identified occurrence. A reserve is the economically and legally extractable subset at current prices and technology (⚠️ owner 20 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Resource: geological.
- Reserve: economic and legal.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4154, SNT-4187.
**Trap:** Quoting a resource figure as a reserve figure overstates security.
**Mains hook:** Use the distinction to qualify any reserve claim.
**Back:** *"Found is resource; mineable is reserve."*

### Flashcard SNT-4159 — Beneficiation
**Front:** What is beneficiation?
**Definition (learn):** Processing that upgrades ore quality before later refining and value addition (✅ owner 20 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Upgrades ore grade.
- Precedes refining.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4154, SNT-4160.
**Trap:** Beneficiation is not final refining.
**Mains hook:** Count beneficiation as a value-chain step.
**Back:** *"Upgrade the ore before refining."*

### Flashcard SNT-4160 — Midstream chokepoint: separation, refining and magnets
**Front:** Where does the rare-earth dependence actually sit in the value chain?
**Definition (learn):** China's dominance lies mainly in separation and magnet manufacture, not in ore. The midstream is the chokepoint (⚠️ owner 20 §2, advanced §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Mining: ore.
- Midstream: separation and refining.
- Downstream: magnets and components.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level analysis.
**Links:** SNT-4154, SNT-4162, SNT-4189.
**Trap:** "Owning a mine secures magnets."
**Mains hook:** Argue that India's constraint is midstream capacity.
**Back:** *"Separate and magnet: the midstream bottleneck."*

### Flashcard SNT-4161 — KABIL: overseas acquisition of strategic minerals
**Front:** What is KABIL, who owns it, and what is its mandate?
**Definition (learn):** Khanij Bidesh India Limited, a joint venture of NALCO, HCL and MECL under the Ministry of Mines, for overseas acquisition and exploration of strategic minerals (✅ owner 20 §2, §4).
**Breakdown:**
- Promoters: NALCO, HCL, MECL (per owner).
- Mandate: identify, explore, acquire, develop, process and procure overseas.
- Ministry: Mines.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Shareholding is as recorded by owner; re-check current structure (⚠️).
**Links:** SNT-4177, SNT-4178, SNT-4172.
**Trap:** KABIL is not a regulator or a domestic mine developer.
**Mains hook:** Use KABIL as the commercial overseas route, then note MoUs are not supply.
**Back:** *"KABIL buys abroad: NALCO, HCL, MECL."*

### Flashcard SNT-4162 — GSI: survey and exploration, not mining
**Front:** What does the Geological Survey of India do, and what does it not do?
**Definition (learn):** GSI is the national geoscientific survey and exploration agency. It is not the mine developer, lease-granting authority or sectoral regulator (✅ owner 20 §4).
**Breakdown:**
- Does: survey and explore.
- Does not: develop mines, auction leases, regulate.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4163, SNT-4164, SNT-4160.
**Trap:** "GSI operates mines."
**Mains hook:** Use the role split for institutional clarity.
**Back:** *"GSI surveys; it does not mine."*

### Flashcard SNT-4163 — Indian Bureau of Mines: regulatory and technical role
**Front:** What does the Indian Bureau of Mines (IBM) do?
**Definition (learn):** Regulates scientific mining, mineral conservation, approval of mining plans and mineral statistics (⚠️ owner 20 §2, §4).
**Breakdown:**
- Mining plans.
- Conservation.
- Statistics.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4162, SNT-4164.
**Trap:** IBM is not the explorer; GSI is.
**Mains hook:** Use IBM as the regulatory-technical counterpart of GSI.
**Back:** *"Bureau approves the mining plan."*

### Flashcard SNT-4164 — State concessions versus Central s.11D auction for Part D minerals
**Front:** Who grants mineral concessions, and who auctions Part D minerals under s.11D?
**Definition (learn):** State governments grant concessions for most minerals. For Part D critical and strategic minerals, the Centre conducts the auction under s.11D, while the State grants the concession and receives the statutory proceeds (✅ owner 20 §4).
**Breakdown:**
- Most minerals: State concession.
- Part D: Centre auction (s.11D).
- State: grants and receives proceeds.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** MMDR (Amendment) Act, 2023.
**Links:** SNT-4165, SNT-4167, SNT-4169.
**Trap:** Thinking the Centre grants all mining leases.
**Mains hook:** Use s.11D as a recentralisation for strategic minerals within a State-subject framework.
**Back:** *"States grant; Centre auctions the strategic."*

### Flashcard SNT-4165 — IREL (India) Ltd and monazite
**Front:** Why does India's rare-earth story sit partly under the atomic-energy establishment?
**Definition (learn):** IREL (India) Ltd, a DAE undertaking, processes beach-sand monazite, India's principal domestic rare-earth source, which also carries thorium (⚠️ owner 20 §2, §4).
**Breakdown:**
- Source: beach-sand monazite.
- Outputs: rare earths and thorium.
- Ministry: DAE.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4169, SNT-4180, SNT-4179.
**Trap:** Monazite reserves do not mean magnet self-sufficiency.
**Mains hook:** Connect monazite to nuclear and critical-mineral policy.
**Back:** *"Monazite: rare earths, and thorium with it."*

### Flashcard SNT-4166 — Ministry of Mines 2023 report: 30 critical minerals
**Front:** What did the Ministry of Mines 2023 report identify?
**Definition (learn):** An expert report, Critical Minerals for India, identifying 30 minerals most critical for India. It lists rare-earth elements within the category (✅ owner 20 §6, report as cited).
**Breakdown:**
- Count: 30.
- Includes REE as a category.
- Expert-report list, distinct from statutory lists.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023 report.
**Links:** SNT-4155, SNT-4167, SNT-4168.
**Trap:** Merging the 30-mineral list with the Part D or Seventh Schedule list.
**Mains hook:** Cite the report as the policy basis, then distinguish legal instruments.
**Back:** *"Thirty experts' list, not the law."*

### Flashcard SNT-4167 — Part D of the First Schedule: critical and strategic minerals
**Front:** How many entries does the Part D list hold, and what changed with coking coal?
**Definition (learn):** The MMDR (Amendment) Act, 2023 inserted Part D for critical and strategic minerals, centrally auctioned under s.11D. It held 24 minerals as inserted. Coking coal was added as entry 3A by G.S.R. 64(E) dated 27 Jan 2026, taking the current consolidation to 25 (✅ owner 20 §4, §6, §8).
**Breakdown:**
- As inserted in 2023: 24.
- Current consolidation: 25.
- Coking coal entry: 3A, G.S.R. 64(E), 27 Jan 2026.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 27 Jan 2026 for coking coal.
**Links:** SNT-4164, SNT-4166, SNT-4168.
**Trap:** Quoting 24 as the current count.
**Mains hook:** Show criticality is legally dynamic and industry-driven.
**Back:** *"Twenty-four, then twenty-five with coal."*

### Flashcard SNT-4168 — Seventh Schedule and the s.10BA Exploration Licence
**Front:** What is the Exploration Licence under s.10BA, and which minerals does it cover?
**Definition (learn):** A competitively auctioned, State-granted right to reconnoitre and prospect, not mine, for the 29 minerals in the Seventh Schedule. Valid five years, extendable by up to two (✅ owner 20 §4, advanced §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Scope: 29 Seventh Schedule minerals.
- Term: 5 years plus up to 2.
- Share: in the applicable amount of the later mining-lease auction if discovery is proved.
- Purpose: attract junior exploration firms.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 29 minerals; 5 + 2 years.
**Links:** SNT-4155, SNT-4166, SNT-4167.
**Trap:** An exploration licence is not a mining lease.
**Mains hook:** Argue that shifting exploration risk to private firms needs transparent auctions.
**Back:** *"Prospect, don't mine: five plus two."*

### Flashcard SNT-4169 — MMDR (Amendment) Act, 2023: six minerals removed from the atomic list
**Front:** Which six minerals were removed from the atomic-minerals list?
**Definition (learn):** Lithium-bearing, titanium-bearing (ilmenite, rutile, leucoxene), beryl and beryllium-bearing, niobium-bearing, tantalum-bearing, and zirconium-bearing minerals (zircon). The removal opened them to private exploration and mining (✅ owner 20 §4).
**Breakdown:**
- Lithium; titanium; beryllium; niobium; tantalum; zirconium.
- Effect: private capital access.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** MMDR (Amendment) Act, 2023.
**Links:** SNT-4165, SNT-4164.
**Trap:** Lithium is not a rare earth; it was removed from the atomic list.
**Mains hook:** Analyse as liberalising a nuclear-adjacent resource base.
**Back:** *"Lithium, titanium, beryl, niobium, tantalum, zirconium: free."*

### Flashcard SNT-4170 — National Critical Mineral Mission (NCMM)
**Front:** When was the NCMM approved, what is its outlay, and what period does it cover?
**Definition (learn):** Approved by the Union Cabinet on 29 Jan 2025 with ₹34,300 crore total: ₹16,300 crore Government expenditure plus ₹18,000 crore expected PSU and stakeholder investment, for FY 2024-25 to FY 2030-31 (✅ owner 20 §4, §7, §8, PMO and PIB as cited).
**Breakdown:**
- Approval: 29 Jan 2025.
- Outlay: ₹34,300 crore.
- Split: ₹16,300 crore Government; ₹18,000 crore PSU/stakeholder.
- Period: FY 2024-25 to FY 2030-31.
- Scope: exploration, mining, beneficiation, processing, recycling, stockpiling.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** SNT-4154, SNT-4171, SNT-4176.
**Trap:** An approved outlay is authorisation, not expenditure incurred.
**Mains hook:** Argue that a mission outlay must be judged by exploration throughput and processing capacity.
**Back:** *"Thirty-four thousand three hundred crore, seven years."*

### Flashcard SNT-4171 — Deep Ocean Mission and polymetallic nodules
**Front:** What is India's seabed mineral route, and under which regime?
**Definition (learn):** MoES and NIOT's Deep Ocean Mission explores polymetallic nodules in India's allotted area in the Central Indian Ocean Basin, under an International Seabed Authority contract (✅ owner 20 §4, advanced §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Ministry: MoES; institute: NIOT.
- Resource: polymetallic nodules.
- Regime: ISA contract, separate from land mining.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4154, SNT-4172.
**Trap:** Seabed mining uses land-mining law.
**Mains hook:** Argue for a separate legal and environmental regime for seabed resources.
**Back:** *"Nodules on the seabed, under the ISA."*

### Flashcard SNT-4172 — Mineral Security Partnership (MSP)
**Front:** What is the Mineral Security Partnership, and what does it do?
**Definition (learn):** A plurilateral grouping to mobilise investment in critical-mineral supply chains, which India has joined (⚠️ owner 20 §4; year of joining not verified here).
**Breakdown:**
- Type: plurilateral.
- Function: investment and offtake coordination.
- Complement to KABIL's commercial route.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Joining year not verified.
**Links:** SNT-4161, SNT-4170, SNT-4183.
**Trap:** MSP does not change geology.
**Mains hook:** Present MSP as de-risking diplomacy.
**Back:** *"Partners invest; geology stays put."*

### Flashcard SNT-4173 — EV battery minerals are chemistry-specific
**Front:** Which minerals does an EV battery need, and does the answer depend on chemistry?
**Definition (learn):** It depends on chemistry. NMC and NCA use nickel and cobalt; LFP uses neither; sodium-ion avoids lithium; graphite is common to most lithium-ion anodes (✅ owner 20 §5, ⚠️ analysis).
**Breakdown:**
- NMC/NCA: nickel, cobalt.
- LFP: neither.
- Sodium-ion: no lithium.
- Graphite: common anode.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4081, SNT-4115, SNT-4110.
**Trap:** "EV batteries need cobalt and nickel."
**Mains hook:** State chemistry before minerals.
**Back:** *"Chemistry first, then the metal list."*

### Flashcard SNT-4174 — Permanent magnets: neodymium and dysprosium
**Front:** Why do rare earths matter for permanent magnets?
**Definition (learn):** Rare earths such as neodymium and dysprosium are important for advanced motors and strategic systems (✅ owner 20 §5).
**Breakdown:**
- Neodymium: magnets.
- Dysprosium: high-temperature magnets.
- Uses: motors, electronics, aerospace, defence.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4156, SNT-4157.
**Trap:** Heavy REEs are the binding constraint for high-temperature magnets.
**Mains hook:** Link magnet supply to EV motors and defence.
**Back:** *"Neodymium for magnets; dysprosium for heat."*

### Flashcard SNT-4175 — Gallium, germanium and electronics critical inputs
**Front:** Which critical inputs matter for electronics and semiconductors?
**Definition (learn):** Gallium, germanium and other critical inputs matter in high-tech manufacturing (✅ owner 20 §5).
**Breakdown:**
- Gallium and germanium: electronics inputs.
- Semiconductors need a wider set of inputs than rare earths.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level; no quantity asserted.
**Links:** SNT-4154, SNT-4176.
**Trap:** Thinking semiconductor supply depends only on rare earths.
**Mains hook:** Link critical minerals to semiconductor self-reliance.
**Back:** *"Gallium and germanium: chip inputs."*

### Flashcard SNT-4176 — Recycling and urban mining
**Front:** What is urban mining, and why is it India's only expandable deposit?
**Definition (learn):** Recovery of valuable minerals from end-of-life products and waste streams. It links to the Battery Waste Management Rules, 2022 and e-waste rules (✅ owner 20 §2, §4, §8).
**Breakdown:**
- Source: end-of-life products.
- Link: Battery Waste Management Rules, 2022.
- Role: domestic material supply.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4104, SNT-4119, SNT-4170.
**Trap:** Recycling is a downstream afterthought.
**Mains hook:** Argue that recycling is part of materials security.
**Back:** *"Urban mine: the domestic deposit."*

### Flashcard SNT-4177 — KABIL Argentina lithium exploration (4 Oct 2024)
**Front:** What did KABIL begin in Argentina, and what does it not prove?
**Definition (learn):** KABIL began exploration in the Cortadera lithium blocks in Argentina on 4 Oct 2024 (✅ owner 20 §8).
**Breakdown:**
- Action: exploration commenced.
- Date: 4 Oct 2024 as at owner source.
- No later official update on drilling, resource declaration or production located at owner's date.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 04 Oct 2024.
**Links:** SNT-4161, SNT-4178.
**Trap:** "India has secured lithium."
**Mains hook:** Use as a live example of why exploration is not supply.
**Back:** *"Exploration began; supply is not secured."*

### Flashcard SNT-4178 — KABIL, OIL and OVL MoU with IRH, UAE (10 Sep 2024)
**Front:** What did the 10 Sep 2024 MoU establish, and what kind of instrument is it?
**Definition (learn):** KABIL, OIL and ONGC Videsh signed an MoU with IRH, UAE, for global critical-mineral supply-chain collaboration. An MoU is an intent instrument, not an offtake contract (✅ owner 20 §8).
**Breakdown:**
- Parties: KABIL, OIL, OVL; partner IRH (UAE).
- Instrument: MoU.
- Not: contract for physical supply.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 10 Sep 2024.
**Links:** SNT-4161, SNT-4177.
**Trap:** Treating an MoU as secured supply.
**Mains hook:** Use to distinguish diplomatic intent from physical security.
**Back:** *"MoU signs intent, not ore."*

### Flashcard SNT-4179 — 2022 Prelims PYQ: monazite, rare earths and thorium
**Front:** What does the 2022 Prelims GS-I Q28 test on monazite?
**Definition (learn):** Monazite-bearing beach sands contain rare-earth values and thorium. Geological occurrence, extraction policy, radiation controls, separation and supply are distinct (✅ owner 20 PYQ integration; key not held locally).
**Breakdown:**
- Occurrence: beach sands.
- Products: rare earths and thorium.
- Controls: radiation and policy.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Prelims GS-I 2022 Q28.
**Links:** SNT-4165, SNT-4180.
**Trap:** Occurrence does not equal supply.
**Mains hook:** Use monazite to show nuclear-adjacent policy.
**Back:** *"Monazite holds rare earths and thorium."*

### Flashcard SNT-4180 — 2023 Prelims PYQ: carbon-fibre applications and recyclability
**Front:** What does the 2023 Prelims GS-I Q53 test on carbon fibre?
**Definition (learn):** Carbon-fibre composites give high strength and low weight, used in aerospace and performance automobiles. Recycling is hard because fibres are embedded in polymer matrix (✅ owner 20 PYQ integration; key not held locally).
**Breakdown:**
- Strength to weight.
- Uses: aerospace, performance cars.
- Recycling: difficult.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Prelims GS-I 2023 Q53.
**Links:** SNT-4185, SNT-4176.
**Trap:** "Lightweight means sustainable."
**Mains hook:** Qualify carbon-fibre claims by recovery quality and energy use.
**Back:** *"Light and strong, hard to recycle."*

### Flashcard SNT-4181 — 2025 Prelims PYQ: MSP, critical minerals and the MMDR 2023 amendment
**Front:** What does the 2025 Prelims GS-I Q6 test?
**Definition (learn):** The Minerals Security Partnership, critical minerals and the MMDR Act 2023 amendment (✅ owner 20 PYQ integration; key present locally, answer not recorded here).
**Breakdown:**
- MSP: plurilateral.
- Critical minerals: policy category.
- MMDR 2023: Part D, s.10BA, atomic-list removals.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Prelims GS-I 2025 Q6.
**Links:** SNT-4172, SNT-4167, SNT-4168, SNT-4169.
**Trap:** Mixing the three legal lists.
**Mains hook:** Use to structure a critical-mineral legal answer.
**Back:** *"MSP, critical list, MMDR twenty-three."*

### Flashcard SNT-4182 — 2025 Prelims PYQ: elements in EV battery cathodes
**Front:** Which elements make up EV battery cathodes in the 2025 Prelims GS-I Q43 options (cobalt, graphite, lithium, nickel)?
**Definition (learn):** Cathode materials depend on chemistry; cobalt and nickel are in NMC/NCA, lithium is common to many lithium-ion cells, and graphite is typically an anode material (✅ owner 20 PYQ integration; answer not recorded here; ⚠️ chemistry analysis).
**Breakdown:**
- Cathode minerals: vary by chemistry.
- Graphite: anode.
- Lithium: common lithium-ion element.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Prelims GS-I 2025 Q43.
**Links:** SNT-4081, SNT-4173.
**Trap:** Graphite is an anode material in common cells, not the main cathode metal.
**Mains hook:** Use to tie chemistry to minerals.
**Back:** *"Chemistry sets the cathode metals."*

### Flashcard SNT-4183 — 2025 Prelims PYQ: uses and properties of rare earths
**Front:** What does the 2025 Prelims GS-I Q81 test on rare earths?
**Definition (learn):** The uses and properties of rare-earth elements, such as magnets, phosphors, catalysts and specialised optics (✅ owner 20 PYQ integration; answer not recorded here).
**Breakdown:**
- Magnets, phosphors, catalysts, optics.
- Match use to element.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Prelims GS-I 2025 Q81.
**Links:** SNT-4156, SNT-4174.
**Trap:** Treating every critical mineral as a rare earth.
**Mains hook:** Match each use to the element's function.
**Back:** *"Each rare earth, its own job."*

### Flashcard SNT-4184 — 2026 Prelims PYQ: rare earths and self-reliance
**Front:** What does the 2026 Prelims GS-I Q95 test?
**Definition (learn):** Rare earth elements, critical minerals and India's self-reliance mission (✅ owner 20 PYQ integration; provisional key, not recorded).
**Breakdown:**
- Rare earths: 17 elements.
- Critical minerals: wider policy category.
- Self-reliance: full-chain strategy.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Prelims GS-I 2026 Q95; provisional key.
**Links:** SNT-4155, SNT-4156, SNT-4170.
**Trap:** Confusing REEs with critical minerals.
**Mains hook:** Use the full-chain frame.
**Back:** *"Rare earths in the critical basket, not equal to it."*

### Flashcard SNT-4185 — Carbon-fibre composites and lifecycle
**Front:** Why does a lightweight material not automatically mean sustainable?
**Definition (learn):** Carbon-fibre composites are strong and light, but fibres are bound in a polymer matrix, which makes recovery hard (✅ owner 20 PYQ routed evidence).
**Breakdown:**
- Strength and stiffness at low weight.
- Matrix makes recycling hard.
- Recovery quality and energy decide.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4180, SNT-4176.
**Trap:** "Lightweight equals green."
**Mains hook:** Qualify with end-of-life economics.
**Back:** *"Light is not the same as circular."*

### Flashcard SNT-4186 — Trap: lithium is a rare earth element
**Front:** Is lithium a rare earth element?
**Definition (learn):** No. Lithium is a critical mineral but not a rare earth element. REEs are the 17-element group (✅ owner 20 §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Lithium: critical mineral.
- REE: 17-element group.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner trap list.
**Links:** SNT-4156, SNT-4155.
**Trap:** Naming lithium as a REE.
**Mains hook:** Use the distinction to open a definitional answer.
**Back:** *"Lithium is critical, not rare-earth."*

### Flashcard SNT-4187 — Trap: monazite gives magnet self-sufficiency
**Front:** Does India's monazite endowment mean magnet self-sufficiency?
**Definition (learn):** No. Monazite is light-REE rich. High-temperature magnets need dysprosium and terbium, which are scarce (✅ owner 20 §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Light REEs: abundant.
- Heavy REEs: scarce.
- Magnets: need heavy REEs.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner trap list.
**Links:** SNT-4157, SNT-4165.
**Trap:** Light-REE abundance is not magnet security.
**Mains hook:** Argue that heavy-REE processing and recycling are the binding constraints.
**Back:** *"Light rich, heavy poor: magnets stay exposed."*

### Flashcard SNT-4188 — Trap: owning a mine or GSI running mines
**Front:** Why does owning a mine not guarantee autonomy, and who runs what?
**Definition (learn):** Separation, refining, alloying and component manufacture are where dependence lies. GSI surveys, IBM regulates, States grant concessions, companies mine (✅ owner 20 §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Mine ownership is not enough.
- Processing is the chokepoint.
- GSI does not operate mines.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner trap list.
**Links:** SNT-4160, SNT-4162, SNT-4163.
**Trap:** The mine-equals-security error.
**Mains hook:** Use the value chain to argue for processing investment.
**Back:** *"Mine is not midstream."*

### Flashcard SNT-4189 — Trap: three different lists
**Front:** Which three lists must not be merged?
**Definition (learn):** The 30-mineral expert report (2023), the statutory Part D list for central auction, and the Seventh Schedule list of 29 minerals eligible for exploration licences (✅ owner 20 §4, §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Expert report: 30.
- Part D: 24 as inserted; 25 now.
- Seventh Schedule: 29.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** SNT-4166, SNT-4167, SNT-4168.
**Trap:** Treating them as one list.
**Mains hook:** Name the legal effect of each list.
**Back:** *"Thirty report, twenty-five auction, twenty-nine explore."*

### Flashcard SNT-4190 — Advanced: criticality as a legally dynamic category
**Front:** Why does the statutory critical list change over time?
**Definition (learn):** Coking coal entered Part D on 27 Jan 2026, showing that criticality depends on industrial priorities as well as geology (✅ owner 20 advanced §9).
**Breakdown:**
- Criticality: policy-negotiated.
- Industry priorities: steel and coking coal.
- Geology: not the only driver.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** G.S.R. 64(E), 27 Jan 2026.
**Links:** SNT-4167, SNT-4155.
**Trap:** Treating criticality as fixed by geology.
**Mains hook:** Argue that criticality lists need periodic review.
**Back:** *"Criticality moves with industry."*

### Flashcard SNT-4191 — Advanced: geopolitics and resource nationalism
**Front:** How do processing concentration and export controls create strategic vulnerability?
**Definition (learn):** Concentration of processing capacity abroad creates vulnerability even when mining is geographically diverse. Countries may tighten export controls or renegotiate terms (⚠️ owner 20 advanced §6).
**Breakdown:**
- Processing concentration.
- Export controls.
- Renegotiation risk.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level analysis.
**Links:** SNT-4160, SNT-4170.
**Trap:** Diversified mining equals diversified supply.
**Mains hook:** Argue for midstream capacity and partnerships.
**Back:** *"Diverse mines, one refinery: exposed."*

### Flashcard SNT-4192 — Advanced: liberalising a nuclear-adjacent resource base
**Front:** Why is the atomic-list removal better analysed as liberalising a nuclear-adjacent base?
**Definition (learn):** Monazite carries thorium and historically sat under the atomic-energy establishment. Removing titanium and zirconium minerals from the atomic list opens them to private capital (✅ owner 20 advanced §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Monazite: thorium.
- Atomic list: historically reserved.
- Removal: private access.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** MMDR (Amendment) Act, 2023.
**Links:** SNT-4165, SNT-4169.
**Trap:** Treating the removal as only a mining reform.
**Mains hook:** Argue for clear radiation and safety controls in liberalised resources.
**Back:** *"Atomic list out, nuclear care stays."*

### Flashcard SNT-4193 — Advanced: mining trade-offs: water, land and consent
**Front:** What environmental and community trade-offs accompany mining expansion?
**Definition (learn):** Mining, tailings, water use and land disturbance create ecological and social costs. Community consent, rehabilitation and closure plans matter (⚠️ owner 20 advanced §6, §9).
**Breakdown:**
- Water and tailings.
- Land and livelihoods.
- Consent, rehabilitation, closure.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level analysis.
**Links:** SNT-4170, SNT-4154.
**Trap:** Treating mining as purely economic.
**Mains hook:** Argue that credible consent and closure plans are part of material security.
**Back:** *"Extract with consent, close with care."*

### GROUP — General Science: Physics Fundamentals

### Flashcard SNT-4194 — Physics as hidden infrastructure: principle to device to national capability
**Front:** How do physics fundamentals become GS-III material?
**Definition (learn):** A principle becomes a device, the device a system, the system an industry, and the industry a national capability. Policy answers should show this chain and its physical boundary (✅ owner 21 §2, advanced §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Principle: force, energy, waves, electricity, semiconductors.
- Device: transistor, lens, laser, transformer.
- System: chip, grid, instrument.
- Capability: electronics, energy, diagnostics, defence.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4198, SNT-4235, SNT-4231, SNT-4263.
**Trap:** Memorising formulas without the application or constraint.
**Mains hook:** Use the chain to link physics to semiconductor, energy and health policy.
**Back:** *"Principle, device, system, industry, nation."*

### Flashcard SNT-4195 — Newton's first law and inertia
**Front:** What does Newton's first law state?
**Definition (learn):** A body remains at rest or in uniform straight-line motion unless an external unbalanced force acts on it (✅ owner 21 §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Inertia: resistance to change in motion.
- Unbalanced force needed for change.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4196, SNT-4197.
**Trap:** Thinking motion needs a continuous force.
**Mains hook:** Rarely central; use for vehicle-safety reasoning.
**Back:** *"Rest or steady motion stays unless pushed."*

### Flashcard SNT-4196 — Newton's second law, momentum and impulse
**Front:** How do force, momentum and impulse relate, and why do airbags work?
**Definition (learn):** Force equals rate of change of momentum; for constant mass F = ma. Impulse equals force times time and equals change in momentum (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- F = rate of change of momentum.
- Impulse = F × t = change in momentum.
- Longer stopping time lowers force (airbags, crumple zones).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4195, SNT-4199.
**Trap:** Ignoring time in impulse; airbags reduce force by extending time.
**Mains hook:** Use impulse to explain vehicle safety design.
**Back:** *"Stretch the time, cut the force."*

### Flashcard SNT-4197 — Newton's third law: action and reaction on different bodies
**Front:** Why do action-reaction pairs not cancel?
**Definition (learn):** Equal and opposite forces act on different bodies, so they do not cancel (✅ owner 21 §6).
**Breakdown:**
- Pairs act on different bodies.
- Explains recoil and propulsion.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4199, SNT-4195.
**Trap:** Thinking action and reaction cancel out.
**Mains hook:** Use for rocket propulsion explanation.
**Back:** *"Equal, opposite, on different bodies."*

### Flashcard SNT-4198 — Work, energy and power
**Front:** How do work, energy and power differ?
**Definition (learn):** Work is force times displacement in the direction of force. Energy is capacity to do work. Power is the rate of doing work (✅ owner 21 §2, §6).
**Breakdown:**
- Work: F × displacement along F.
- Energy: capacity.
- Power: rate.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4196, SNT-4243.
**Trap:** Work is zero with no displacement along force, even when force is applied.
**Mains hook:** Use power as a rate in efficiency and energy-demand arguments.
**Back:** *"Work moves; energy stores; power rates."*

### Flashcard SNT-4199 — Conservation of linear momentum: recoil and rockets
**Front:** What does conservation of linear momentum explain?
**Definition (learn):** In an isolated system, total linear momentum stays constant. It explains recoil, rocket propulsion and collisions (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- Recoil: gun or launcher.
- Rocket: expelled exhaust.
- Collisions: total momentum conserved.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4197, SNT-4196.
**Trap:** Momentum is not conserved if external forces act.
**Mains hook:** Use for space-launch concepts.
**Back:** *"Push out, move on."*

### Flashcard SNT-4200 — Escape velocity is independent of the projectile's mass
**Front:** What is Earth's escape velocity, and what does it depend on?
**Definition (learn):** About 11.2 km/s from Earth. It depends on the mass and radius of the body being escaped, not on the projectile's mass (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table, standard value).
**Breakdown:**
- Value: about 11.2 km/s for Earth.
- Depends on: mass and radius of the body.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** About 11.2 km/s.
**Links:** SNT-4201, SNT-4202.
**Trap:** "Heavier projectile needs more escape speed."
**Mains hook:** Use for launch-vehicle energy arguments.
**Back:** *"Eleven point two, independent of the rocket's mass."*

### Flashcard SNT-4201 — Geostationary and polar orbits
**Front:** What altitude and period define a geostationary orbit, and where is a polar or sun-synchronous orbit?
**Definition (learn):** A geostationary satellite orbits above the equator at about 35,786 km, with a period of one sidereal day. Polar and sun-synchronous orbits are low and near-polar (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- Geostationary: about 35,786 km; equator; one sidereal day.
- Polar: low; near-polar.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 35,786 km.
**Links:** SNT-4200, SNT-4202.
**Trap:** Mixing geostationary with polar orbit altitude or inclination.
**Mains hook:** Link orbit choice to communication and earth-observation missions.
**Back:** *"Thirty-five thousand seven hundred eighty-six, over the equator."*

### Flashcard SNT-4202 — Weightlessness is free fall
**Front:** Why are astronauts weightless in orbit?
**Definition (learn):** They are in continuous free fall around the Earth. Gravity is present (✅ owner 21 §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Free fall: falling around the Earth.
- Gravity: still acting.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4200, SNT-4201.
**Trap:** "No gravity in orbit."
**Mains hook:** Rarely central; use to fix a concept error.
**Back:** *"Orbit is falling and missing."*

### Flashcard SNT-4203 — Archimedes' principle: upthrust equals weight of displaced fluid
**Front:** What does Archimedes' principle state, and where is it used?
**Definition (learn):** Upthrust equals the weight of fluid displaced. It explains floating and hydrometers (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- Upthrust = weight of displaced fluid.
- Floating, hydrometers.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4204, SNT-4205.
**Trap:** Upthrust depends on displaced fluid, not the body's total weight.
**Mains hook:** Use as basic physics for ship and hydrometer examples.
**Back:** *"Displaced weight lifts."*

### Flashcard SNT-4204 — Pascal's law: hydraulic brakes and presses
**Front:** What does Pascal's law explain?
**Definition (learn):** Pressure applied to a confined fluid transmits equally in all directions. It explains hydraulic brakes and presses (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- Transmission of pressure in confined fluid.
- Hydraulic brakes and presses.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4203, SNT-4205.
**Trap:** Confusing Pascal's law with Bernoulli's principle.
**Mains hook:** Use for everyday engineering examples.
**Back:** *"Confined pressure pushes everywhere."*

### Flashcard SNT-4205 — Bernoulli's principle: aerofoil lift and atomisers
**Front:** What does Bernoulli's principle explain?
**Definition (learn):** Higher fluid speed corresponds to lower pressure. It explains aerofoil lift and atomisers (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- Faster flow: lower pressure.
- Aerofoil lift.
- Atomisers.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4204, SNT-4203.
**Trap:** Bernoulli is about flow speed, not about force from pressure alone.
**Mains hook:** Use for aviation or spray technology examples.
**Back:** *"Fast flow, low pressure, lift."*

### Flashcard SNT-4206 — Atmospheric pressure and altitude: boiling and pressure cookers
**Front:** Why does water boil at a lower temperature on mountains, and why does a pressure cooker cook faster?
**Definition (learn):** Atmospheric pressure falls with altitude, lowering the boiling point. A pressure cooker raises pressure inside, which raises the boiling point and cooks faster (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table, standard principle).
**Breakdown:**
- Altitude: lower pressure, lower boiling point.
- Cooker: higher pressure, higher boiling point.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4250, SNT-4209.
**Trap:** Thinking pressure cookers boil at a lower temperature.
**Mains hook:** Use for everyday energy-saving examples.
**Back:** *"Higher pressure, hotter boil."*

### Flashcard SNT-4207 — Surface tension and capillarity
**Front:** What do surface tension and capillarity explain?
**Definition (learn):** Surface tension gives droplet shape. Capillarity explains water rise in soil and plants (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- Surface tension: droplet shape.
- Capillarity: water rise in soil and plants.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4206.
**Trap:** Capillarity is not caused by gravity alone.
**Mains hook:** Use for soil-moisture and irrigation examples.
**Back:** *"Surface pulls; capillary climbs."*

### Flashcard SNT-4208 — Heat versus temperature
**Front:** How do heat and temperature differ?
**Definition (learn):** Heat is energy in transfer. Temperature is a measure of thermal state, related to average kinetic energy (✅ owner 21 §2, §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Heat: energy transfer.
- Temperature: thermal state.
- Heat flows because of a temperature difference.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4209, SNT-4211.
**Trap:** Heat and temperature are not interchangeable.
**Mains hook:** Use to correct everyday thermal reasoning.
**Back:** *"Heat moves; temperature measures."*

### Flashcard SNT-4209 — Specific heat capacity and water's high value
**Front:** Why does water's high specific heat matter for climate and cooling?
**Definition (learn):** Specific heat is heat required to raise the temperature of unit mass by one unit. Water's is unusually high, which moderates coastal climates and makes water a good coolant (✅ owner 21 §2 table, §3).
**Breakdown:**
- Specific heat: unit mass, unit rise.
- Water: high value.
- Effects: coastal moderation, coolant use.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4210, SNT-4208.
**Trap:** Confusing specific heat with latent heat.
**Mains hook:** Use to explain coastal climate and industrial cooling.
**Back:** *"Water holds heat well: cool coasts, good coolant."*

### Flashcard SNT-4210 — Latent heat: phase change without temperature change
**Front:** What is latent heat, and why are steam burns worse than boiling-water burns?
**Definition (learn):** Energy absorbed or released at a phase change without temperature change. Steam releases latent heat on condensing, which is why steam burns are worse. Sweating cools by latent heat (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- Phase change: energy with no temperature rise.
- Steam burns: latent heat released on condensation.
- Sweat cooling: evaporation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4209, SNT-4208.
**Trap:** Latent heat is not temperature.
**Mains hook:** Use for thermal and cooling examples.
**Back:** *"Phase change hides heat."*

### Flashcard SNT-4211 — Heat transfer: conduction, convection, radiation
**Front:** What are the three modes of heat transfer, and which needs no medium?
**Definition (learn):** Conduction (solids), convection (fluids, such as land and sea breeze) and radiation, which needs no medium (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- Conduction: solids.
- Convection: fluids.
- Radiation: no medium.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4208, SNT-4213.
**Trap:** Radiation is not conduction through air.
**Mains hook:** Use to explain insulation and building design.
**Back:** *"Solid conducts, fluid convects, all radiate."*

### Flashcard SNT-4212 — First and second laws of thermodynamics
**Front:** What do the first and second laws state?
**Definition (learn):** The first law is energy conservation. The second law: heat flows spontaneously from hot to cold, and no engine is 100 percent efficient (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- First: conservation.
- Second: direction of heat flow; efficiency limit.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4211, SNT-4213.
**Trap:** Thinking engines can be 100 percent efficient.
**Mains hook:** Use to explain efficiency limits in energy policy.
**Back:** *"Energy kept; heat flows down; no perfect engine."*

### Flashcard SNT-4213 — Black body and peak wavelength
**Front:** What is a black body, and how does its peak wavelength change with temperature?
**Definition (learn):** A black body absorbs and emits all wavelengths. Hotter bodies emit at shorter peak wavelengths, the basis of infrared thermometry and the greenhouse effect (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- Absorbs and emits all wavelengths.
- Hotter: shorter peak.
- Applications: infrared thermometry, greenhouse effect.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4211, SNT-4225.
**Trap:** A hotter body does not emit only short wavelengths; the peak shifts.
**Mains hook:** Use to explain Earth's energy balance.
**Back:** *"Hotter peaks shift shorter."*

### Flashcard SNT-4214 — Wave speed equals frequency times wavelength
**Front:** What is the wave equation, and what does frequency control?
**Definition (learn):** Speed equals frequency times wavelength. Frequency determines pitch for sound (✅ owner 21 §2, §3).
**Breakdown:**
- v = fλ.
- Frequency: pitch.
- Medium: sets speed.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4215, SNT-4220.
**Trap:** Frequency does not set speed alone; the medium matters.
**Mains hook:** Rarely central; use for sound and radio.
**Back:** *"Speed is frequency times wavelength."*

### Flashcard SNT-4215 — Sound needs a medium; fastest in solids
**Front:** Why can sound not travel through a vacuum, and where does it travel fastest?
**Definition (learn):** Sound is a mechanical wave and needs a material medium. It travels fastest in solids and slowest in gases (⚠️ owner 21 §2–3).
**Breakdown:**
- Mechanical wave: medium needed.
- Solids: fastest.
- Gases: slowest.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4214, SNT-4216, SNT-4273.
**Trap:** Light needs no medium; sound does.
**Mains hook:** Use to contrast sound and EM waves in communication.
**Back:** *"Sound needs a medium; light does not."*

### Flashcard SNT-4216 — Infrasound, audible and ultrasound bands
**Front:** What are the three frequency bands of sound, and what uses are named?
**Definition (learn):** Infrasound is below 20 Hz (elephants, earthquakes, volcanic activity). Audible is 20 Hz to 20 kHz. Ultrasound is above 20 kHz (sonography, SONAR, non-destructive testing, cleaning) (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- Infrasound: below 20 Hz.
- Audible: 20 Hz to 20 kHz.
- Ultrasound: above 20 kHz.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 20 Hz and 20 kHz.
**Links:** SNT-4215, SNT-4220.
**Trap:** Ultrasound is a frequency band; supersonic is a speed relative to sound.
**Mains hook:** Use to explain diagnostic imaging.
**Back:** *"Below twenty, audible, above twenty kilohertz."*

### Flashcard SNT-4217 — Doppler effect: apparent frequency change with relative motion
**Front:** What is the Doppler effect, and where is it used?
**Definition (learn):** Apparent frequency changes with relative motion. Used in radar speed guns, Doppler weather radar and in the redshift of receding galaxies (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- Motion changes observed frequency.
- Radar speed guns.
- Weather radar.
- Galaxy redshift.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4214, SNT-4252.
**Trap:** Doppler is not the same as a change in actual source frequency.
**Mains hook:** Use for weather and astronomy examples.
**Back:** *"Motion shifts the pitch you hear."*

### Flashcard SNT-4218 — Resonance and marching across bridges
**Front:** What is resonance, and why do soldiers break step on bridges?
**Definition (learn):** Forced oscillation at the natural frequency gives large amplitude. Marching in step can drive a bridge to resonance (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- Natural frequency match.
- Large amplitude.
- Bridge risk.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4214, SNT-4219.
**Trap:** Resonance is a matching frequency, not any high frequency.
**Mains hook:** Use for structural safety examples.
**Back:** *"Match the natural beat, amplitude climbs."*

### Flashcard SNT-4219 — Echo versus reverberation
**Front:** How do echo and reverberation differ?
**Definition (learn):** They differ by time separation between original and reflected sound (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- Echo: distinct reflection.
- Reverberation: overlapping reflections.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4218, SNT-4216.
**Trap:** Calling every reflection an echo.
**Mains hook:** Use for acoustic design examples.
**Back:** *"Echo is distinct; reverb blurs."*

### Flashcard SNT-4220 — Loudness versus pitch
**Front:** Does louder sound mean higher pitch?
**Definition (learn):** No. Loudness depends mainly on amplitude; pitch depends on frequency (✅ owner 21 §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Amplitude: loudness.
- Frequency: pitch.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4214, SNT-4216.
**Trap:** Mixing amplitude with frequency.
**Mains hook:** Rarely central; concept correction.
**Back:** *"Amplitude is loud; frequency is high or low."*

### Flashcard SNT-4221 — Total internal reflection: fibre optics and endoscopy
**Front:** What is total internal reflection, and what does it explain?
**Definition (learn):** Beyond the critical angle, light moving from a denser to a rarer medium is totally reflected. It explains optical fibres, mirages, diamond sparkle and endoscopy (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- Denser to rarer.
- Beyond critical angle.
- Fibre optics, endoscopy, mirage.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4222, SNT-4224.
**Trap:** TIR requires the denser-to-rarer direction and angle beyond critical.
**Mains hook:** Link fibre optics to digital infrastructure.
**Back:** *"Denser to rarer, past critical: light stays in."*

### Flashcard SNT-4222 — Dispersion: white light splits into colours
**Front:** What is dispersion, and what natural phenomenon combines it?
**Definition (learn):** Dispersion splits white light into its component colours, as in a prism. A rainbow combines refraction, internal reflection and dispersion (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- Prism: separation of colours.
- Rainbow: combined effects.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4221, SNT-4225.
**Trap:** Dispersion is not total internal reflection alone.
**Mains hook:** Rarely central.
**Back:** *"Prism splits; rain bows."*

### Flashcard SNT-4223 — Lens defects of vision and their corrections
**Front:** Which lens corrects myopia, hypermetropia, presbyopia and astigmatism?
**Definition (learn):** Myopia is corrected by a concave lens; hypermetropia by a convex lens; presbyopia by bifocals; astigmatism by cylindrical lenses (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- Myopia: concave.
- Hypermetropia: convex.
- Presbyopia: bifocals.
- Astigmatism: cylindrical.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4245.
**Trap:** Myopia and hypermetropia lens types are reversed in common errors.
**Mains hook:** Rarely central; health-optics example.
**Back:** *"Near-sighted concave, far-sighted convex."*

### Flashcard SNT-4224 — Scattering: Rayleigh, Mie and Tyndall
**Front:** What do Rayleigh scattering, Mie scattering and the Tyndall effect explain?
**Definition (learn):** Rayleigh scattering (proportional to 1/λ⁴) explains the blue sky and red sunsets. Mie scattering by larger particles explains white clouds and haze. The Tyndall effect reveals colloids (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- Rayleigh: 1/λ⁴, small particles.
- Mie: larger particles.
- Tyndall: colloids.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4245, SNT-4225.
**Trap:** The sky is not blue because it reflects the sea.
**Mains hook:** Rarely central; use for atmospheric examples.
**Back:** *"Rayleigh blues the sky; Mie whitens clouds."*

### Flashcard SNT-4225 — Electromagnetic spectrum: order by frequency
**Front:** What is the order of the EM spectrum by increasing frequency?
**Definition (learn):** Radio, microwave, infrared, visible (VIBGYOR, red longest wavelength), ultraviolet, X-rays, gamma rays. All travel at light speed in vacuum (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- Radio → microwave → IR → visible → UV → X-ray → gamma.
- All at light speed in vacuum.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4226, SNT-4213.
**Trap:** Longer wavelength means lower frequency.
**Mains hook:** Use the spectrum to explain communication band choice.
**Back:** *"Radio, micro, infra, visible, ultra, X, gamma."*

### Flashcard SNT-4226 — EM bands: uses and ionising radiation
**Front:** Which EM bands are ionising, and what are their main uses?
**Definition (learn):** Microwaves heat by exciting water molecules; infrared gives thermal imaging; UV causes ozone-related and skin effects; X-rays and gamma are ionising (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- Microwave: heating.
- Infrared: thermal imaging.
- UV: skin and ozone effects.
- X-ray and gamma: ionising.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4225, SNT-4242.
**Trap:** Ionising is not the same as high-power.
**Mains hook:** Separate use cases by ionising and non-ionising.
**Back:** *"Heat, image, burn skin, ionise: X and gamma."*

### Flashcard SNT-4227 — Ohm's law and series versus parallel circuits
**Front:** What is Ohm's law, and how do series and parallel circuits share current and voltage?
**Definition (learn):** V = IR for ohmic conductors. Series circuits share current; parallel circuits share voltage. Domestic wiring is parallel (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- Ohm's law: V = IR.
- Series: same current.
- Parallel: same voltage.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4228, SNT-4244.
**Trap:** Ohm's law applies to ohmic conductors, not all devices.
**Mains hook:** Rarely central; use for household-circuit examples.
**Back:** *"Series shares current; parallel shares voltage."*

### Flashcard SNT-4228 — AC versus DC transmission and I²R losses
**Front:** Why is AC used for long-distance transmission?
**Definition (learn):** AC can be stepped to high voltage, lowering current and so losses, which scale as I²R (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- High voltage: lower current.
- Losses: I²R.
- Transformer: enables step-up.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4229, SNT-4235.
**Trap:** Thinking DC lines have no losses.
**Mains hook:** Link to grid losses and electrification.
**Back:** *"High voltage, low current, less loss."*

### Flashcard SNT-4229 — Transformers work only with alternating current
**Front:** Can a transformer step a steady DC voltage up or down?
**Definition (learn):** No. A transformer needs a changing magnetic flux, which only alternating current supplies (✅ owner 21 §3, §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Changing flux: needed.
- AC: yes. DC: no.
- Reason grids transmit AC.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4228, SNT-4235.
**Trap:** A transformer can step DC voltage up or down.
**Mains hook:** Use to explain grid architecture.
**Back:** *"No changing flux, no transformer."*

### Flashcard SNT-4230 — Superconductivity and the Meissner effect
**Front:** What are superconductors, and what does the Meissner effect mean?
**Definition (learn):** Superconductors show zero resistance and expel magnetic fields below a critical temperature (Meissner effect). Used in MRI, maglev and fusion or accelerator magnets (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- Zero resistance.
- Expels magnetic field.
- Uses: MRI, maglev, magnets.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4228, SNT-4240.
**Trap:** Superconductors are not perfect conductors only; the Meissner effect is expulsion.
**Mains hook:** Link to health imaging and fusion research.
**Back:** *"Zero resistance, magnetic exile."*

### Flashcard SNT-4231 — n-type and p-type doping
**Front:** What do pentavalent and trivalent impurities do in silicon?
**Definition (learn):** Doping silicon with a pentavalent impurity gives n-type, with electrons as majority carriers. A trivalent impurity gives p-type, with holes as majority carriers (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- Pentavalent: n-type.
- Trivalent: p-type.
- Doping controls carrier type.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4232, SNT-4194.
**Trap:** Doping does not create electricity; it controls carriers.
**Mains hook:** Use for semiconductor fabrication policy.
**Back:** *"Five gives electrons; three gives holes."*

### Flashcard SNT-4232 — p-n junction diode and rectification
**Front:** How does a p-n junction diode behave in forward and reverse bias?
**Definition (learn):** It conducts in forward bias and blocks in reverse bias, enabling rectification (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- Forward: conducts.
- Reverse: blocks.
- Rectification: AC to DC.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4231, SNT-4233.
**Trap:** Diode is not a resistor.
**Mains hook:** Use for power electronics.
**Back:** *"Forward passes, reverse blocks."*

### Flashcard SNT-4233 — LED and photodiode or solar cell: opposite energy flows
**Front:** How do an LED and a photodiode or solar cell differ in energy conversion?
**Definition (learn):** An LED emits light on recombination of carriers. A photodiode or solar cell generates current from light (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- LED: electricity to light.
- Photodiode or solar cell: light to electricity.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4232, SNT-4258.
**Trap:** LEDs do not generate electricity.
**Mains hook:** Use for lighting and solar examples.
**Back:** *"LED emits; solar cell harvests."*

### Flashcard SNT-4234 — Bipolar transistor versus MOSFET
**Front:** How does a bipolar transistor differ from a MOSFET?
**Definition (learn):** In a bipolar transistor a small base current controls a large collector current. In a MOSFET an insulated gate's electric field controls the channel, drawing almost no gate current (⚠️ owner 21 §2, advanced §4).
**Breakdown:**
- BJT: base current controls collector current.
- MOSFET: gate field controls channel.
- MOSFET underlies modern integrated circuits.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4271, SNT-4232.
**Trap:** "A transistor is just a smaller switch."
**Mains hook:** Explain why MOSFET-based logic enabled chip scaling.
**Back:** *"Current drives BJT; field drives MOSFET."*

### Flashcard SNT-4235 — Electromagnetic induction and generators
**Front:** How does a changing magnetic flux produce an emf, and what do generators do with it?
**Definition (learn):** A conductor in a changing magnetic flux has an induced emf. If the circuit is closed, current flows. Generators use rotation to keep flux changing (✅ owner 21 §3).
**Breakdown:**
- Changing flux: emf.
- Closed circuit: current.
- Generator: continuous rotation.
- Transformer: mutual induction.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4229, SNT-4228.
**Trap:** No changing flux, no induction.
**Mains hook:** Explain the physics behind electricity generation.
**Back:** *"Change the flux, make the emf."*

### Flashcard SNT-4236 — Photoelectric effect: frequency threshold, not intensity
**Front:** When are electrons ejected in the photoelectric effect?
**Definition (learn):** Light above a threshold frequency ejects electrons instantly. The effect was evidence for photons and earned Einstein his Nobel (⚠️ owner 21 §2, §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Threshold frequency.
- Instant emission.
- Intensity: only the number of electrons.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4241, SNT-4246.
**Trap:** Brighter light does not eject electrons if frequency is below threshold.
**Mains hook:** Use to show quantum evidence in devices.
**Back:** *"Frequency decides; brightness counts electrons."*

### Flashcard SNT-4237 — Radioactivity: alpha, beta and gamma
**Front:** How do alpha, beta and gamma radiation differ in nature, penetration and ionisation?
**Definition (learn):** Alpha is a helium nucleus: heavy, strongly ionising, stopped by paper. Beta is a fast electron or positron: stopped by aluminium. Gamma is electromagnetic: needs lead or concrete (⚠️ owner 21 §2, §3, §6).
**Breakdown:**
- Alpha: helium nucleus; strongly ionising; weak penetration.
- Beta: electron or positron; medium penetration.
- Gamma: electromagnetic; highly penetrating; weakly ionising relative to alpha.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4238, SNT-4242, SNT-4246.
**Trap:** Beta is not electromagnetic; gamma is.
**Mains hook:** Use for radiation safety and medical use.
**Back:** *"Alpha paper, beta aluminium, gamma lead."*

### Flashcard SNT-4238 — Half-life and carbon-14 dating
**Front:** What is half-life, and does temperature change it?
**Definition (learn):** The time for half the nuclei in a sample to decay. It is unaffected by ordinary temperature and pressure. Carbon-14 dating rests on it (⚠️ owner 21 §2, §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Half-life: constant for each isotope.
- Carbon-14: dating.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4237, SNT-4246.
**Trap:** Heating or compressing does not change half-life.
**Mains hook:** Use for radiometric dating examples.
**Back:** *"Half-life holds; heat does not move it."*

### Flashcard SNT-4239 — Fission and fusion
**Front:** How do fission and fusion differ?
**Definition (learn):** Fission splits heavy nuclei. Fusion joins light nuclei. Both are covered in the nuclear owners (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table; cross-topic to Part 4 nuclear owner).
**Breakdown:**
- Fission: heavy nuclei split.
- Fusion: light nuclei join.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4237, SNT-4240.
**Trap:** Fusion and fission are not the same reaction.
**Mains hook:** Use for energy-option comparisons.
**Back:** *"Split heavy, join light."*

### Flashcard SNT-4240 — Frontier physics: Higgs, gravitational waves, relativity, E = mc²
**Front:** What are the standard frontier-physics items?
**Definition (learn):** Antimatter, neutrinos, the Higgs boson (mass-giving field, LHC 2012), gravitational waves (first detected 2015) and special relativity: nothing carrying information exceeds light speed; E = mc² (⚠️ owner 21 §2 table).
**Breakdown:**
- Higgs: LHC 2012.
- Gravitational waves: first detected 2015.
- Relativity: E = mc².
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2012 and 2015 as recorded.
**Links:** SNT-4256, SNT-4239, SNT-4230.
**Trap:** Confusing Higgs with the source of all mass.
**Mains hook:** Use to show research-to-technology pipelines.
**Back:** *"Higgs in 2012, waves in 2015, E equals mc squared."*

### Flashcard SNT-4241 — Laser: stimulated emission, coherence and directionality
**Front:** What defines a laser, and what distinguishes it from bright light?
**Definition (learn):** An intense, coherent, nearly monochromatic beam produced by stimulated emission. Brightness alone does not define a laser (✅ owner 21 §2, §8).
**Breakdown:**
- Stimulated emission.
- Coherence.
- Monochromatic.
- Directional.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4236, SNT-4226.
**Trap:** "Bright light is a laser."
**Mains hook:** Use for surgery, cutting and surveying examples.
**Back:** *"Coherent, monochromatic, directional: stimulated light."*

### Flashcard SNT-4242 — Ionising versus non-ionising radiation use
**Front:** Why must ionising and non-ionising radiation be discussed separately?
**Definition (learn):** Ionising radiation (X-rays, gamma, alpha, beta) removes electrons; non-ionising (radio, microwave, IR, visible) does not. Penetration and use case differ (⚠️ owner 21 §5, §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Ionising: X, gamma, alpha, beta.
- Non-ionising: radio to visible.
- Use cases differ.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4226, SNT-4237.
**Trap:** All radiation fears the same.
**Mains hook:** Separate safety by ionisation and dose.
**Back:** *"Ionise or not: separate the fears."*

### Flashcard SNT-4243 — Trap: mass versus weight
**Front:** Are mass and weight the same?
**Definition (learn):** No. Mass is the amount of matter; weight is the gravitational force on that mass (✅ owner 21 §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Mass: amount of matter.
- Weight: force.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4198, SNT-4202.
**Trap:** Using kg for weight.
**Mains hook:** Rarely central.
**Back:** *"Mass stays; weight changes with gravity."*

### Flashcard SNT-4244 — Trap: current, voltage and power
**Front:** Is current "used up" in a circuit, and how do current, voltage and power differ?
**Definition (learn):** Current is the rate of charge flow. Voltage is work per unit charge. Power is the rate of energy transfer. Current is not used up; energy is transformed (✅ owner 21 §5, §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Current: charge flow.
- Voltage: energy per charge.
- Power: energy rate.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4227, SNT-4198.
**Trap:** Current disappears inside a bulb.
**Mains hook:** Rarely central.
**Back:** *"Current flows, voltage pushes, power counts."*

### Flashcard SNT-4245 — Trap: mirror versus lens; why the sky is blue
**Front:** Do mirrors and lenses work by the same principle, and why is the sky blue?
**Definition (learn):** Mirrors mainly reflect; lenses mainly refract. The sky is blue because of Rayleigh scattering of shorter wavelengths by air molecules (✅ owner 21 §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Mirror: reflection.
- Lens: refraction.
- Sky: Rayleigh scattering.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level.
**Links:** SNT-4223, SNT-4224.
**Trap:** The sky reflects the sea.
**Mains hook:** Rarely central.
**Back:** *"Mirror bounces; lens bends; sky scatters blue."*

### Flashcard SNT-4246 — Trap: beta as electromagnetic; half-life altered by heat; intensity sets photoelectrons
**Front:** Which three physics statements are wrong?
**Definition (learn):** (a) Beta radiation is electromagnetic: wrong, beta is electrons or positrons. (b) Heating or compressing changes half-life: wrong. (c) Photoelectric emission depends on brightness: wrong, it depends on frequency (✅ owner 21 §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Beta: particle.
- Half-life: nuclear constant.
- Photoelectric: frequency threshold.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner trap list.
**Links:** SNT-4236, SNT-4237, SNT-4238.
**Trap:** Each error sounds plausible.
**Mains hook:** Rarely central.
**Back:** *"Beta is a particle; half-life stays; frequency rules."*

### Flashcard SNT-4247 — Light-year is a unit of distance
**Front:** What is a light-year?
**Definition (learn):** The distance light travels in one year. It is a unit of distance, not time, brightness or speed (✅ owner 21 advanced routed PYQ, 2021 Q76).
**Breakdown:**
- Distance: light-speed times one year.
- Not: time, brightness or speed.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2021 Prelims GS-I Q76 (route; key not held locally).
**Links:** SNT-4248.
**Trap:** Calling a light-year a time unit.
**Mains hook:** Rarely central.
**Back:** *"Light's year is distance."*

### Flashcard SNT-4248 — Cepheids, nebulae and pulsars
**Front:** How do Cepheids, nebulae and pulsars differ?
**Definition (learn):** Cepheids are pulsating variable stars whose period-luminosity relation helps measure distance. A nebula is an interstellar gas or dust cloud. A pulsar is a rapidly rotating magnetised neutron star whose beam appears as pulses (✅ owner 21 advanced routed PYQ 2023 Q56).
**Breakdown:**
- Cepheid: variable star; distance.
- Nebula: gas or dust cloud.
- Pulsar: rotating neutron star.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023 Prelims GS-I Q56 (route; key not held locally).
**Links:** SNT-4247, SNT-4251.
**Trap:** Mixing object types.
**Mains hook:** Rarely central.
**Back:** *"Cepheid measures, nebula glows, pulsar pulses."*

### Flashcard SNT-4249 — Sodium-vapour lamp versus LED
**Front:** How does a sodium-vapour lamp differ from an LED for street lighting?
**Definition (learn):** A sodium-vapour lamp emits light through excited sodium atoms. An LED is a semiconductor junction device. Compare mechanism, spectrum, controllability, efficiency and lifetime only with a cited product context (✅ owner 21 advanced routed PYQ 2021 Q72).
**Breakdown:**
- Sodium: excited atoms.
- LED: semiconductor junction.
- Compare: spectrum, efficiency, lifetime.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2021 Prelims GS-I Q72.
**Links:** SNT-4233, SNT-4258.
**Trap:** Declaring one universally better without a product context.
**Mains hook:** Use to show lighting-policy trade-offs.
**Back:** *"Sodium excites atoms; LED junctions glow."*

### Flashcard SNT-4250 — 2021 Prelims PYQ: pressure cooker and boiling point
**Front:** Why does a pressure cooker cook faster? (2021 Prelims GS-I Q68)
**Definition (learn):** Higher pressure inside raises the boiling point, so food cooks at a higher temperature (⚠️ standard principle; owner route).
**Breakdown:**
- Higher pressure.
- Higher boiling point.
- Faster cooking.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2021 Prelims GS-I Q68.
**Links:** SNT-4206.
**Trap:** Pressure cooker boils at lower temperature.
**Mains hook:** Rarely central.
**Back:** *"Pressure up, boil hotter."*

### Flashcard SNT-4251 — 2023 Prelims PYQ: accelerometer functions
**Front:** What can an accelerometer detect? (2023 Prelims GS-I Q54)
**Definition (learn):** It senses acceleration and can support tilt, motion, free-fall or crash detection (✅ owner 21 advanced routed PYQ).
**Breakdown:**
- Acceleration sensing.
- Tilt, free-fall, crash.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023 Q54.
**Links:** SNT-4248, SNT-4259.
**Trap:** An accelerometer is not a substitute for recovery or investigation.
**Mains hook:** Rarely central.
**Back:** *"Accelerometer senses change in motion."*

### Flashcard SNT-4252 — 2024 Prelims PYQ: radar uses
**Front:** Which activities use radar? (2024 Prelims GS-I Q34)
**Definition (learn):** Radar uses radio waves for detection and ranging. Its uses include air-traffic, weather and navigation (⚠️ standard; owner route).
**Breakdown:**
- Radio waves.
- Detection and ranging.
- Examples: aviation, weather, navigation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024 Q34.
**Links:** SNT-4217, SNT-4225.
**Trap:** Radar is not a passive camera.
**Mains hook:** Rarely central.
**Back:** *"Radio echoes find range."*

### Flashcard SNT-4253 — 2020 Prelims PYQ: visible light communication
