- Pancreatic and intestinal enzymes: alkaline
- Bile: emulsification of fat
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table (⚠️ owner-stated).
**Links:** SNT-5078
**Trap:** Bile is not an enzyme; it emulsifies, it does not digest.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Amylase in mouth, pepsin in acid, pancreas in alkali, bile emulsifies."*

### Flashcard SNT-5078 — Endocrine glands and their hormones
**Front:** Which hormones do pituitary, thyroid, parathyroid, adrenal, pancreas and gonads produce?
**Definition (learn):** The pituitary is the master gland (growth hormone); thyroid makes thyroxine; parathyroid regulates calcium; adrenal makes adrenaline and cortisol; pancreas makes insulin and glucagon; gonads produce sex hormones (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Pituitary: master gland, growth hormone
- Thyroid: thyroxine
- Parathyroid: calcium
- Adrenal: adrenaline, cortisol
- Pancreas: insulin, glucagon
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table (⚠️ owner-stated).
**Links:** SNT-5061, SNT-5079, SNT-5112
**Trap:** Endocrine glands are ductless; hormones travel in blood.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Pituitary commands; thyroid burns; pancreas balances sugar."*

### Flashcard SNT-5079 — Insulin, glucagon and diabetes mellitus
**Front:** How do insulin and glucagon act, and what happens in diabetes mellitus?
**Definition (learn):** Insulin lowers blood glucose; glucagon raises it; diabetes mellitus is a disorder of this regulation (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Insulin: glucose uptake and storage
- Glucagon: glucose release
- Diabetes: imbalance in blood glucose control
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table (⚠️ owner-stated).
**Links:** SNT-5078, SNT-5100
**Trap:** Insulin and glucagon are opposite actions; do not give them the same function.
**Mains hook:** Diabetes is a non-communicable disease driven by diet and inactivity (SNT-5100).
**Back:** *"Insulin lowers sugar; glucagon raises it."*

### Flashcard SNT-5080 — Vitamin A and night blindness
**Front:** What deficiency causes night blindness and xerophthalmia?
**Definition (learn):** Vitamin A is fat-soluble; deficiency causes night blindness and xerophthalmia (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Fat-soluble vitamin
- Deficiency: night blindness, xerophthalmia
- Stored in liver
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table (⚠️ owner-stated).
**Links:** SNT-5081, SNT-5082
**Trap:** Fat-soluble vitamins are stored in the body; water-soluble ones are not.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"A for dark eyes."*

### Flashcard SNT-5081 — Vitamin D: rickets and osteomalacia
**Front:** What deficiency causes rickets and osteomalacia?
**Definition (learn):** Vitamin D deficiency causes rickets in children and osteomalacia in adults (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Fat-soluble vitamin
- Bone mineralisation affected
- Rickets: children; osteomalacia: adults
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table (⚠️ owner-stated).
**Links:** SNT-5080, SNT-5082
**Trap:** Vitamin D is fat-soluble; it is not water-soluble B or C.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"D for bones: rickets, osteomalacia."*

### Flashcard SNT-5082 — Vitamin K and clotting
**Front:** What deficiency of vitamin K causes clotting defects?
**Definition (learn):** Vitamin K is fat-soluble; deficiency causes clotting defects (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Fat-soluble vitamin
- Clotting defects
- Gut bacteria synthesise some vitamin K
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table (⚠️ owner-stated).
**Links:** SNT-5072, SNT-5104
**Trap:** Vitamin K is not for bone health; it is for clotting.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"K for clotting."*

### Flashcard SNT-5083 — Vitamin C and scurvy
**Front:** What deficiency causes scurvy?
**Definition (learn):** Vitamin C is water-soluble; deficiency causes scurvy (✅ owner 23 table; ⚠️ owner-stated).
**Breakdown:**
- Water-soluble vitamin
- Scurvy: bleeding gums, wound healing
- Not stored like fat-soluble vitamins
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table.
**Links:** SNT-5080, SNT-5084
**Trap:** Water-soluble vitamins are not stored in quantity; daily intake matters.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"C for scurvy."*

### Flashcard SNT-5084 — Vitamin B1 and beriberi
**Front:** What deficiency causes beriberi?
**Definition (learn):** Vitamin B1 (thiamine) deficiency causes beriberi (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- B-complex group, water-soluble
- Beriberi
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table.
**Links:** SNT-5085, SNT-5086
**Trap:** Beriberi is a B1 disease, not a B3 disease.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"B1 for beriberi."*

### Flashcard SNT-5085 — Vitamin B3 and pellagra
**Front:** What deficiency causes pellagra?
**Definition (learn):** Vitamin B3 (niacin) deficiency causes pellagra (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- B-complex group
- Pellagra
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table.
**Links:** SNT-5084, SNT-5086
**Trap:** Pellagra is not a B1 or B12 disease.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"B3 for pellagra."*

### Flashcard SNT-5086 — Vitamin B12 and folate: anaemia
**Front:** Which vitamins are linked to anaemia?
**Definition (learn):** Vitamin B12 and folate deficiency cause anaemia (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- B12 and folate
- Anaemia
- Distinct from iron-deficiency anaemia (SNT-5089)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table.
**Links:** SNT-5089, SNT-5084
**Trap:** Iron deficiency is not the only cause of anaemia.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"B12 and folate: anaemia."*

### Flashcard SNT-5087 — Kwashiorkor
**Front:** What is kwashiorkor?
**Definition (learn):** Protein-energy malnutrition with protein deficit and oedema (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Protein deficit
- Oedema
- Protein-energy malnutrition
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table.
**Links:** SNT-5088
**Trap:** Kwashiorkor is protein-led; marasmus is energy-led.
**Mains hook:** Nutrition programmes must address protein and total energy.
**Back:** *"Protein short, swollen."*

### Flashcard SNT-5088 — Marasmus
**Front:** What is marasmus?
**Definition (learn):** Protein-energy malnutrition with overall energy deficit and wasting (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Overall energy deficit
- Wasting
- Protein-energy malnutrition
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table.
**Links:** SNT-5087
**Trap:** Oedema is typical of kwashiorkor, not marasmus.
**Mains hook:** Nutrition programmes must address total energy.
**Back:** *"Energy short, wasted."*

### Flashcard SNT-5089 — Iron deficiency and anaemia
**Front:** What deficiency causes anaemia through haemoglobin shortfall?
**Definition (learn):** Iron deficiency reduces haemoglobin synthesis and causes anaemia (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Iron needed for haemoglobin
- Anaemia
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table.
**Links:** SNT-5086, SNT-5074
**Trap:** Iron deficiency does not cause goitre; iodine does.
**Mains hook:** Anaemia policy targets iron and folate supplementation.
**Back:** *"Iron short, haemoglobin low."*

### Flashcard SNT-5090 — Bacterial diseases
**Front:** Which diseases are bacterial in the owner list?
**Definition (learn):** TB, cholera, typhoid, tetanus and leprosy are bacterial diseases (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- TB, cholera, typhoid, tetanus, leprosy
- Bacteria are cellular, independently living
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table.
**Links:** SNT-5091, SNT-5101
**Trap:** Bacterial infections are treated with antibiotics, not antivirals.
**Mains hook:** Antibiotic stewardship (SNT-5103).
**Back:** *"TB, cholera, typhoid, tetanus, leprosy."*

### Flashcard SNT-5091 — Viral diseases
**Front:** Which diseases are viral in the owner list?
**Definition (learn):** Measles, polio, dengue, chikungunya, rabies, hepatitis, COVID-19 and influenza are viral (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Measles, polio, dengue, chikungunya
- Rabies, hepatitis, COVID-19, influenza
- Viruses are acellular and need host cells
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table.
**Links:** SNT-5090, SNT-5101
**Trap:** Antibiotics do nothing against viruses.
**Mains hook:** Vaccination is the main preventive tool for many viral diseases.
**Back:** *"Measles, polio, dengue, rabies, hepatitis, flu."*

### Flashcard SNT-5092 — Malaria and Plasmodium
**Front:** Which organism causes malaria, and which vector transmits it?
**Definition (learn):** Malaria is caused by Plasmodium, a protozoan, transmitted by female Anopheles (✅ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Pathogen: Plasmodium (protozoan)
- Vector: female Anopheles
- Protozoan disease
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table.
**Links:** SNT-5097, SNT-5093
**Trap:** Male mosquitoes do not transmit malaria.
**Mains hook:** Vector control is the main lever.
**Back:** *"Plasmodium, female Anopheles."*

### Flashcard SNT-5093 — Kala-azar and amoebiasis
**Front:** Which protozoans cause kala-azar and amoebiasis, and what is the vector?
**Definition (learn):** Kala-azar is caused by Leishmania, transmitted by sandfly; amoebiasis is a protozoan disease (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Kala-azar: Leishmania, sandfly
- Amoebiasis: protozoan
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table.
**Links:** SNT-5092
**Trap:** Kala-azar is not transmitted by Anopheles.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Leishmania, sandfly."*

### Flashcard SNT-5094 — Fungal diseases
**Front:** Which diseases are fungal in the owner list?
**Definition (learn):** Ringworm and candidiasis are fungal diseases (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Ringworm
- Candidiasis
- Fungi are eukaryotes
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table.
**Links:** SNT-5060
**Trap:** Fungi are not bacteria; antibiotics do not treat fungal infections.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Ringworm and candida: fungi."*

### Flashcard SNT-5095 — Helminthic diseases: filariasis and ascariasis
**Front:** Which worm diseases are named, and what vector spreads filariasis?
**Definition (learn):** Filariasis and ascariasis are helminthic (worm) diseases; filariasis is spread by Culex (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Filariasis: Culex
- Ascariasis: helminth
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table.
**Links:** SNT-5098
**Trap:** Helminths are multicellular parasites, not microbes like bacteria.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Worms: filariasis by Culex."*

### Flashcard SNT-5096 — Aedes aegypti
**Front:** Which diseases does Aedes aegypti transmit?
**Definition (learn):** Aedes aegypti is the vector for dengue, chikungunya and Zika (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Dengue, chikungunya, Zika
- Vector mosquito
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table.
**Links:** SNT-5091, SNT-5097, SNT-5098
**Trap:** Aedes is not the vector for malaria.
**Mains hook:** Vector control is an urban-health issue.
**Back:** *"Aedes: dengue, chikungunya, Zika."*

### Flashcard SNT-5097 — Anopheles mosquito
**Front:** Which disease does Anopheles transmit?
**Definition (learn):** Anopheles is the vector for malaria (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Malaria
- Female mosquito
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table.
**Links:** SNT-5092, SNT-5096
**Trap:** Anopheles is not the vector for filariasis; Culex is.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Anopheles: malaria."*

### Flashcard SNT-5098 — Culex mosquito
**Front:** Which diseases does Culex transmit?
**Definition (learn):** Culex transmits filariasis and Japanese encephalitis (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Filariasis
- Japanese encephalitis
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table.
**Links:** SNT-5095, SNT-5096, SNT-5097
**Trap:** Culex is not the malaria vector.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Culex: filariasis, JE."*

### Flashcard SNT-5099 — Zoonoses and One Health
**Front:** Which zoonoses does the owner name, and why does it justify One Health?
**Definition (learn):** Zoonoses such as rabies, Nipah, avian influenza and brucellosis pass between animals and humans; they justify the One Health approach (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Animal-human transmission
- One Health: human, animal, environment
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table.
**Links:** SNT-5091
**Trap:** Not every disease is zoonotic.
**Mains hook:** Zoonotic risk requires joint human and animal health surveillance.
**Back:** *"Rabies, Nipah, avian flu, brucella: One Health."*

### Flashcard SNT-5100 — Non-communicable diseases and the double burden
**Front:** What are the main NCDs and what is India's double burden?
**Definition (learn):** NCDs include cardiovascular disease, diabetes, cancers, chronic respiratory disease and mental illness; India's double burden is persisting infections plus rising NCDs (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Drivers: tobacco, alcohol, diet, inactivity, air pollution
- Double burden: infectious and NCD together
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table (⚠️ owner-stated).
**Links:** SNT-5079, SNT-5099
**Trap:** NCDs are not caused by infections.
**Mains hook:** Prevention of NCDs needs tobacco, diet and pollution policy.
**Back:** *"Infections persist; NCDs rise."*

### Flashcard SNT-5101 — Antibiotics versus antivirals
**Front:** Do antibiotics treat viral infections?
**Definition (learn):** Antibiotics act on bacteria only and do nothing against viruses (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Antibiotic: bacteria
- Antiviral: viruses
- Mixing them up causes misuse
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table.
**Links:** SNT-5090, SNT-5091, SNT-5102
**Trap:** Antibiotics for viral illness is misuse, not treatment.
**Mains hook:** Misuse drives AMR (SNT-5102).
**Back:** *"Antibiotics: bacteria only."*

### Flashcard SNT-5102 — Antimicrobial resistance as selection
**Front:** How does antimicrobial resistance arise?
**Definition (learn):** AMR is a selection process, not "the body becoming resistant"; over-prescription, incomplete courses, OTC sales, animal use and environmental residues select resistant microbes (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Selection under exposure
- Sources: over-prescription, OTC, animals, residues
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table.
**Links:** SNT-5101, SNT-5103, SNT-5129
**Trap:** The patient does not become resistant; the microbe population does.
**Mains hook:** AMR control needs stewardship, sanitation and veterinary use controls.
**Back:** *"Microbes selected, not bodies."*

### Flashcard SNT-5103 — National Action Plan on AMR and Schedule H1
**Front:** What are India's policy responses to AMR named in the owner?
**Definition (learn):** The National Action Plan on AMR and Schedule H1 prescription controls are the named responses (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- NAP-AMR
- Schedule H1: prescription control
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table (⚠️ owner-stated; years not given in the owner).
**Links:** SNT-5102
**Trap:** Schedule H1 is a drug-control schedule, not an AMR plan.
**Mains hook:** Stewardship is the policy lever.
**Back:** *"NAP-AMR plus H1 controls."*

### Flashcard SNT-5104 — Human microbiome, probiotics and prebiotics
**Front:** What do gut microbes do, and how do probiotics and prebiotics differ?
**Definition (learn):** Gut and skin microbes aid digestion, vitamin synthesis (K and some B), immune development and pathogen exclusion; probiotics add live microbes; prebiotics feed them (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Microbiome: digestion, vitamins, immunity
- Probiotic: live microbe
- Prebiotic: food for microbes
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table (⚠️ owner-stated).
**Links:** SNT-5082, SNT-5102
**Trap:** Probiotics are not the same as prebiotics.
**Mains hook:** Antibiotics disturb the microbiome.
**Back:** *"Probiotic adds, prebiotic feeds."*

### Flashcard SNT-5105 — Photosynthesis, transpiration and transport
**Front:** How do xylem and phloem move water and food, and what is transpiration?
**Definition (learn):** Transpiration pulls water up xylem; phloem translocates food; photosynthesis uses light, chlorophyll and stomata (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Photosynthesis: light and dark reactions
- Xylem: water upward
- Phloem: food translocation
- Stomata: gas exchange and transpiration
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table (⚠️ owner-stated).
**Links:** SNT-5063, SNT-5107
**Trap:** Xylem carries water; phloem carries food, not the reverse.
**Mains hook:** Drought adaptation relates to stomatal control.
**Back:** *"Xylem carries water up; phloem carries food."*

### Flashcard SNT-5106 — Plant hormones
**Front:** Which plant hormones control elongation, division, stress and ripening?
**Definition (learn):** Auxin (elongation, apical dominance), gibberellin (stem growth), cytokinin (cell division), abscisic acid (stress and stomatal closure), ethylene (fruit ripening) (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Auxin, gibberellin, cytokinin
- Abscisic acid, ethylene
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 table (⚠️ owner-stated).
**Links:** SNT-5105
**Trap:** Ethylene is a gas; it is not an enzyme.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Auxin grows, gibberellin stretches, cytokinin divides, ABA shuts, ethylene ripens."*

### Flashcard SNT-5107 — C3, C4 and CAM pathways
**Front:** What do C3, C4 and CAM pathways explain?
**Definition (learn):** C3, C4 and CAM are photosynthetic pathways; C4 and CAM show drought adaptation (⚠️ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- C3: standard pathway
- C4: adapted to heat and drought
- CAM: stomata open at night
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 (⚠️ owner-stated).
**Links:** SNT-5105
**Trap:** C4 is not a type of soil; it is a pathway.
**Mains hook:** Crop productivity under drought.
**Back:** *"C3 standard, C4 heat, CAM night."*

### Flashcard SNT-5108 — Innate immunity
**Front:** What are the components of innate immunity?
**Definition (learn):** Innate immunity is inborn, immediate and non-specific: barriers (skin, mucus, stomach acid, lysozyme), phagocytes, natural killer cells, complement, interferons, inflammation and fever (✅ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Barriers
- Phagocytes
- Natural killer cells
- Complement, interferons, inflammation
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 Must-Know Facts (✅ owner 23).
**Links:** SNT-5109, SNT-5111
**Trap:** Innate is non-specific; adaptive is specific and has memory.
**Mains hook:** Innate barriers protect public health before vaccines act.
**Back:** *"Barriers, cells, complement: fast and generic."*

### Flashcard SNT-5109 — Adaptive immunity
**Front:** What makes adaptive immunity specific and memory-capable?
**Definition (learn):** Adaptive immunity is acquired and specific; B cells and T cells provide memory (✅ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- B cells: antibodies
- T cells: coordination and cell-mediated action
- Memory cells for faster response
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 (✅ owner 23).
**Links:** SNT-5108, SNT-5110, SNT-5111
**Trap:** Adaptive immunity is slower on first exposure.
**Mains hook:** Vaccination trains adaptive immunity (Topic 15).
**Back:** *"Specific, slower, remembers."*

### Flashcard SNT-5110 — Antigen, antibody, immunogen and self-antigens
**Front:** What is an antigen, how does it differ from an immunogen, and what is immune tolerance?
**Definition (learn):** An antigen is recognised by an antibody or T-cell receptor; an immunogen actually provokes a response; self-antigens are normally tolerated (✅ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Antigen: recognised
- Immunogen: provokes response
- Immune tolerance: prevents response to self
- Tolerance failure: autoimmune disease
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 essential definitions (✅ owner 23).
**Links:** SNT-5109, SNT-5133
**Trap:** Antibody is the product, not the antigen.
**Mains hook:** Autoimmune disease is tolerance failure.
**Back:** *"Antigen is seen; immunogen triggers; tolerance spares self."*

### Flashcard SNT-5111 — B cells, T cells and memory in the immune sequence
**Front:** Put the adaptive immune response in order.
**Definition (learn):** Antigen is recognised as non-self; antigen-presenting cells activate lymphocytes; B cells become plasma cells making antibodies; T cells coordinate or attack; some cells become memory cells (✅ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Recognition
- Activation
- Antibody and T-cell action
- Memory
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 mechanism (✅ owner 23).
**Links:** SNT-5109, SNT-5110
**Trap:** Vaccine platforms belong to Topic 15, not to this immune sequence.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Recognise, activate, produce, remember."*

### Flashcard SNT-5112 — Nervous versus endocrine control
**Front:** How do nervous and endocrine control differ?
**Definition (learn):** Nervous control is rapid, wired and short-lived; endocrine control is slower, chemical and often longer-acting (✅ owner 23 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Nerves: electrical impulses
- Hormones: chemical messengers in blood
- Both interact in homeostasis
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 advanced (✅ owner 23).
**Links:** SNT-5076, SNT-5078
**Trap:** Nervous and endocrine systems are not isolated silos.
**Mains hook:** Homeostasis needs both systems.
**Back:** *"Nerves fast, hormones slow."*

### Flashcard SNT-5113 — Department of Biotechnology (DBT)
**Front:** What role does DBT play in India's life-science policy?
**Definition (learn):** DBT is a Department that drives biotechnology policy and programmes linking basic biology to applied missions (✅ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Department, not a ministry
- Applied life-science missions
- Links to Topic 13 biotechnology
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 (✅ owner 23).
**Links:** SNT-5114, SNT-5116
**Trap:** DBT is not ICMR; ICMR is under MoHFW.
**Mains hook:** Biotech missions depend on basic biology capacity.
**Back:** *"DBT: biotech policy department."*

### Flashcard SNT-5114 — CSIR-CCMB and CSIR-IGIB
**Front:** What do CSIR-CCMB and CSIR-IGIB research?
**Definition (learn):** CSIR-CCMB covers cellular and molecular biology; CSIR-IGIB covers genomics and integrative biology (✅ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- CCMB: cellular and molecular biology
- IGIB: genomics and integrative biology
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 (✅ owner 23).
**Links:** SNT-5113, SNT-5049
**Trap:** Both are CSIR labs, not DBT departments.
**Mains hook:** Genomics capacity links to public health.
**Back:** *"CCMB cells, IGIB genomes."*

### Flashcard SNT-5115 — ICMR: biomedical research, not surveillance
**Front:** What is ICMR's role, and what is it not?
**Definition (learn):** ICMR is India's apex body for biomedical research: it sets research policy, runs institutes and issues ethical guidelines; it is not the operational surveillance agency (✅ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Research policy and funding
- Ethical guidelines
- Not operational surveillance
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 (✅ owner 23).
**Links:** SNT-5116, SNT-5117
**Trap:** Collapsing ICMR, NCDC, MoHFW and CDSCO into one public-health ecosystem is an institutional error (owner 23).
**Mains hook:** Research, surveillance and regulation fail in different ways.
**Back:** *"ICMR researches; it does not surveil."*

### Flashcard SNT-5116 — NCDC, IDSP/IHIP and State health departments
**Front:** Which body runs operational disease surveillance in India?
**Definition (learn):** NCDC and the IDSP/IHIP network under MoHFW perform surveillance and outbreak response with State health departments (✅ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- NCDC under MoHFW
- IDSP and IHIP networks
- State health departments deliver programmes
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 (✅ owner 23).
**Links:** SNT-5115, SNT-5117
**Trap:** NCDC is not the drug regulator; CDSCO is.
**Mains hook:** Surveillance capacity is the bottleneck in outbreak response.
**Back:** *"NCDC and IDSP watch; states respond."*

### Flashcard SNT-5117 — CDSCO: regulatory approval for drugs and vaccines
**Front:** What does CDSCO approve?
**Definition (learn):** CDSCO grants regulatory approvals for drugs and vaccines (✅ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Drug and vaccine approval
- Regulator, not surveillance
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 (✅ owner 23).
**Links:** SNT-5115, SNT-5116
**Trap:** CDSCO is not ICMR.
**Mains hook:** Regulatory capacity is a separate failure mode.
**Back:** *"CDSCO approves."*

### Flashcard SNT-5118 — WHO validation of India's trachoma elimination (2024)
**Front:** What did WHO validate in October 2024?
**Definition (learn):** WHO validated India as having eliminated trachoma as a public-health problem on 8 Oct 2024 (✅ owner 23 cites WHO page; ✅ owner 23 date).
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 8 Oct 2024
- Public-health problem elimination
- Hygiene and microbiology link
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 08 Oct 2024 (📰 owner 23; status as of 16 Jul 2026 per owner).
**Links:** SNT-5090, SNT-5119
**Trap:** Trachoma elimination as a public-health problem is not the same as eradication of the pathogen.
**Mains hook:** Sustained hygiene, surveillance and treatment matter after validation.
**Back:** *"Trachoma: public-health elimination, Oct 2024."*

### Flashcard SNT-5119 — WHO India polio transition snapshot (April 2024)
**Front:** What does the April 2024 WHO polio transition snapshot emphasise?
**Definition (learn):** After eradication success, India must sustain immunisation, surveillance and emergency response (📰 owner 23, Apr 2024).
**Breakdown:**
- Immunisation sustained
- Surveillance continued
- Emergency response systems
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Apr 2024 (📰 owner 23).
**Links:** SNT-5091, SNT-5120
**Trap:** Eradication success does not end the need for surveillance.
**Mains hook:** Post-eradication systems are a public-health sustainability test.
**Back:** *"Polio gone, surveillance stays."*

### Flashcard SNT-5120 — WHO/UNICEF immunisation coverage estimates
**Front:** What do the WHO/UNICEF annual coverage estimates measure?
**Definition (learn):** WHO/UNICEF national immunisation coverage estimates are annual monitoring outputs; the owner cites data reported till 21 Jun 2026 (📰 owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Annual estimates
- Monitoring public health
- Biology enters public-health monitoring
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Data reported till 21 Jun 2026 (📰 owner 23; time-varying).
**Links:** SNT-5119, SNT-5108
**Trap:** Coverage estimates are not vaccine efficacy data.
**Mains hook:** Immunisation coverage is a monitored public-health metric.
**Back:** *"Coverage is tracked annually."*

### Flashcard SNT-5121 — PYQ: cicada, froghopper and pond skater (2024 Q21)
**Front:** Which insects are cicada, froghopper and pond skater? (PYQ: 2024 Prelims GS-I Q21)
**Definition (learn):** Cicada, froghopper and pond skater are insects; do not infer class from habitat, sound or surface skating (✅ owner 23 routed evidence).
**Breakdown:**
- Insect class from body plan
- Habitat is not a classification criterion
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024 Prelims GS-I Q21; official key available locally, option not recorded here (owner rule).
**Links:** SNT-5060
**Trap:** A pond skater's surface skating does not make it a non-insect.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Cicada, froghopper, pond skater: all insects."*

### Flashcard SNT-5122 — PYQ: plant-form pairs (2025 Q39)
**Front:** What are the plant-form classifications in the 2025 PYQ? (PYQ: 2025 Prelims GS-I Q39)
**Definition (learn):** Cassava is a woody shrub; ginger is a rhizomatous herb; Malabar spinach a climbing vine; mint a herb; papaya a large herb rather than a woody tree (✅ owner 23 routed evidence).
**Breakdown:**
- Cassava: woody shrub
- Ginger: rhizomatous herb
- Malabar spinach: climbing vine
- Mint: herb
- Papaya: large herb
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 Prelims GS-I Q39; key available locally, option not recorded here.
**Links:** SNT-5105
**Trap:** An edible part or common name is not a plant-form classification.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Cassava shrub, ginger herb, Malabar vine, papaya herb."*

### Flashcard SNT-5123 — PYQ: nitric oxide dilates blood vessels (2024 Q33)
**Front:** Which body-synthesised substance dilates blood vessels? (PYQ: 2024 Prelims GS-I Q33)
**Definition (learn):** Nitric oxide is a signalling molecule that dilates blood vessels (✅ owner 23 routed evidence).
**Breakdown:**
- Signalling molecule
- Dilates vessels
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024 Prelims GS-I Q33; official key available locally, option not recorded.
**Links:** SNT-5105
**Trap:** Observed biological role does not make a universal therapeutic claim (owner 23).
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Nitric oxide dilates."*

### Flashcard SNT-5124 — PYQ: mushrooms and fungi properties (2023 Q16)
**Front:** What properties do mushrooms have? (PYQ: 2023 Prelims GS-I Q16)
**Definition (learn):** Different fungi can be edible, medicinal, psychoactive, insect-pathogenic or bioluminescent; properties are species-specific (✅ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Species-specific properties
- "Mushroom" is not a safety label
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023 Prelims GS-I Q16; key unavailable locally.
**Links:** SNT-5094
**Trap:** "Mushroom" does not mean safe or medicinal.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Mushroom is not a label."*

### Flashcard SNT-5125 — PYQ: extremophiles (2023 Q18)
**Front:** Which microorganisms live in extreme environments? (PYQ: 2023 Prelims GS-I Q18)
**Definition (learn):** Thermophiles (heat), psychrophiles (cold) and acidophiles (acid) are extreme-environment microorganisms (✅ owner 23).
**Breakdown:**
- Thermophiles: heat
- Psychrophiles: cold
- Acidophiles: acid
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023 Prelims GS-I Q18; key unavailable locally.
**Links:** SNT-5104
**Trap:** Extremophiles are not all harmful.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Heat, cold, acid lovers."*

### Flashcard SNT-5126 — PYQ: viruses (2025 Q49)
**Front:** What is the viral fact in the 2025 PYQ? (PYQ: 2025 Prelims GS-I Q49)
**Definition (learn):** Viruses require host-cell machinery for replication; they can infect bacteria (✅ owner 23 routed evidence).
**Breakdown:**
- Host-cell dependence
- Bacteriophage: virus infecting bacteria
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 Prelims GS-I Q49; key available locally, option not recorded here.
**Links:** SNT-5091, SNT-5134
**Trap:** Viruses are not cellular.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Viruses borrow host machinery."*

### Flashcard SNT-5127 — PYQ: hepatitis B and C versus HIV (2019 Q100)
**Front:** How do hepatitis B and C virus characteristics differ from HIV? (PYQ: 2019 Prelims GS-I Q100)
**Definition (learn):** Hepatitis B and C and HIV are viruses with different genome and disease profiles; compare them by genome type and target (⚠️ owner 23 routed evidence; key unavailable locally).
**Breakdown:**
- Compare by genome type
- Compare by disease target
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2019 Prelims GS-I Q100; key unavailable locally.
**Links:** SNT-5091
**Trap:** Do not assume all viruses share the same genome structure.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Hepatitis and HIV: compare genome and target."*

### Flashcard SNT-5128 — Cellulose decomposition (2022 GS-III Mains Q6)
**Front:** How does natural decomposition process cellulose on Earth's surface? (PYQ: 2022 GS-III Q6, 10 marks)
**Definition (learn):** Decomposer fungi and bacteria secrete cellulases that hydrolyse cellulose into smaller sugars; microbial metabolism then returns carbon to soil and food webs (✅ owner 23 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Cellulase: enzyme
- Hydrolysis: cellulose to sugars
- Carbon return via soil and food webs
- Constraints: lignin, moisture, temperature, oxygen, microbial community
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 GS-III Q6, 10 marks, 150 words (✅ owner 23 advanced).
**Links:** SNT-5105, SNT-5124
**Trap:** Natural decomposition is not an industrially viable biofuel process.
**Mains hook:** Use it to link decomposition to composting and biomass-to-bioprocess, with the constraints stated.
**Back:** *"Cellulase cuts, microbes return carbon."*

### Flashcard SNT-5129 — Biofilm and resistance (2022 Q37)
**Front:** Why does biofilm formation matter for resistance? (PYQ: 2022 Prelims GS-I Q37)
**Definition (learn):** Biofilms are microbial communities on surfaces that resist antimicrobials and clearance; implants and food surfaces are examples (✅ owner 23 routed evidence; key unavailable locally).
**Breakdown:**
- Surfaces: implants, food
- Resistance connection
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 Prelims GS-I Q37; key unavailable locally.
**Links:** SNT-5102
**Trap:** Biofilm is not a single species.
**Mains hook:** Hospital infection control.
**Back:** *"Biofilms shield microbes."*

### Flashcard SNT-5130 — Trap: DNA versus RNA
**Front:** How do DNA and RNA differ?
**Definition (learn):** DNA contains deoxyribose and thymine and is the hereditary archive; RNA contains ribose and uracil and has functional and regulatory roles (✅ owner 23 Must-Know Facts; advanced owner).
**Breakdown:**
- Sugar: deoxyribose vs ribose
- Base: thymine vs uracil
- Role: archive vs functional and regulatory
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 (✅ owner 23).
**Links:** SNT-5066, SNT-5132
**Trap:** "RNA is only a copy of DNA" is wrong; microRNA is regulatory.
**Mains hook:** Regulatory RNA is the basis of the 2024 Physiology Nobel (SNT-5244).
**Back:** *"DNA: deoxy, thymine. RNA: ribo, uracil."*

### Flashcard SNT-5131 — Trap: antibody versus antigen
**Front:** Are antibodies and antigens the same?
**Definition (learn):** No. Antigen triggers the response; antibody is part of the response (✅ owner 23 trap list).
**Breakdown:**
- Antigen: recognised substance
- Antibody: produced protein that binds antigen
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 trap (✅ owner 23).
**Links:** SNT-5110
**Trap:** Antibodies are made by B cells; they are not themselves the disease.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Antigen triggers, antibody answers."*

### Flashcard SNT-5132 — Trap: virus versus bacterium
**Front:** Can viruses and bacteria be treated identically?
**Definition (learn):** No. Bacteria are cellular and independently living; viruses are acellular and host-dependent (✅ owner 23 trap list).
**Breakdown:**
- Bacteria: cellular, independent
- Viruses: acellular, host-dependent
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 trap (✅ owner 23).
**Links:** SNT-5101, SNT-5126
**Trap:** Antibiotics do not act on viruses.
**Mains hook:** Diagnosis changes treatment.
**Back:** *"Bacteria live alone; viruses borrow."*

### Flashcard SNT-5133 — Trap: hormones versus enzymes
**Front:** Are hormones and enzymes interchangeable?
**Definition (learn):** No. Hormones are signalling molecules; enzymes are biological catalysts (✅ owner 23 trap list).
**Breakdown:**
- Hormone: signal
- Enzyme: catalyst
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 trap (✅ owner 23).
**Links:** SNT-5078, SNT-5110
**Trap:** Insulin is a hormone, amylase an enzyme.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Hormone signals; enzyme catalyses."*

### Flashcard SNT-5134 — Trap: plants respire and photosynthesis is not respiration
**Front:** Do plants respire, and how is photosynthesis different?
**Definition (learn):** Plants respire continuously; photosynthesis is light-dependent and stores solar energy in chemical form (✅ owner 23 trap list; advanced owner).
**Breakdown:**
- Respiration: releases energy from food
- Photosynthesis: stores energy
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 trap (✅ owner 23).
**Links:** SNT-5063, SNT-5105
**Trap:** "Plants only photosynthesise" is wrong.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Plants breathe all the time."*

### Flashcard SNT-5135 — Trap: same DNA, different cells
**Front:** Why do cells differ if they share the same DNA?
**Definition (learn):** Differential gene expression creates functional specialisation (✅ owner 23 trap list; advanced owner).
**Breakdown:**
- Same genome
- Different gene expression
- Regulation creates cell types
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 advanced (✅ owner 23).
**Links:** SNT-5059, SNT-5066
**Trap:** "Same DNA means same function" is wrong.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Same genome, different expression."*

---

### GROUP — 24 S&T Institutions: DST, CSIR, ANRF and SERB (basic + advanced)

### Flashcard SNT-5136 — India's horizontal S&T architecture
**Front:** What is the horizontal S&T architecture, and which bodies belong to it?
**Definition (learn):** The horizontal layer funds, coordinates or enables multiple science sectors: DST (policy), DSIR (administers CSIR), CSIR (lab network), ANRF (statutory funding body) (✅ owner 24).
**Breakdown:**
- Policy steering: DST
- Strategic funding: ANRF
- Research performance: CSIR labs, universities
- Vertical missions draw on this layer (ISRO, DAE, DRDO, MeitY, DBT, ICMR, ICAR)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 24 architecture diagram (✅ owner 24).
**Links:** SNT-5137, SNT-5138, SNT-5139, SNT-5140, SNT-5155
**Trap:** Missions are not one class of "mission bodies"; constitutional form differs.
**Mains hook:** Horizontal capacity is what makes mission programmes durable.
**Back:** *"DST steers, ANRF funds, CSIR performs."*

### Flashcard SNT-5137 — Department of Science and Technology (DST)
**Front:** When was DST established, and what kind of body is it?
**Definition (learn):** DST was established in May 1971 as a nodal department to promote new S&T areas and coordinate activities; it is a Department, not a Ministry (✅ owner 24; ✅ Wiki: DST established 3 May 1971, a department within the Ministry of Science and Technology).
**Breakdown:**
- Established: May 1971
- Department within the Ministry of Science and Technology
- Policy, promotion, missions and coordination
- Administers several autonomous institutes
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** May 1971 (✅ owner 24; ✅ Wiki, 3 May 1971).
**Links:** SNT-5136, SNT-5139, SNT-5156
**Trap:** DST is not a lab network and not a Ministry.
**Mains hook:** DST is the policy anchor; funding and execution sit elsewhere.
**Back:** *"DST: a Department, since 1971, policy anchor."*

### Flashcard SNT-5138 — DSIR, the missing link above CSIR
**Front:** What is DSIR, and why must it appear in institutional diagrams?
**Definition (learn):** The Department of Scientific and Industrial Research (DSIR) administers CSIR and handles industrial-research promotion, technology transfer and recognition of in-house industrial R&D units (⚠️ owner 24 for the recognition role; ✅ owner 24 for administering CSIR).
**Breakdown:**
- Administers CSIR
- Industrial research promotion
- Technology transfer
- SIRO/RDI recognition
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** DSIR organisation page last updated 30 Dec 2025 (✅ owner 24).
**Links:** SNT-5139, SNT-5157
**Trap:** Omitting DSIR makes CSIR look like a direct peer of DST, which it is not.
**Mains hook:** The diagram error is itself a governance point.
**Back:** *"DSIR sits between the ministry and CSIR."*

### Flashcard SNT-5139 — CSIR: autonomous society with 37 national laboratories
**Front:** What is CSIR, and how many national laboratories does it have?
**Definition (learn):** CSIR is an autonomous society registered under the Societies Registration Act, administratively under DSIR; its profile records 37 national laboratories, 39 outreach centres, one innovation complex and three units (✅ owner 24).
**Breakdown:**
- Autonomous society, not a department
- Prime Minister is its President; Union Minister of S&T is Vice-President (✅ owner 24)
- Research-performing laboratory network
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 37 national laboratories (✅ owner 24; CSIR About page as verified by owner, 2 Aug 2026).
**Links:** SNT-5138, SNT-5049, SNT-5163
**Trap:** CSIR is not mainly a grant disbursal body (owner 24 trap).
**Mains hook:** Autonomy-plus-accountability is a governance design question.
**Back:** *"CSIR: society, 37 labs, under DSIR."*

### Flashcard SNT-5140 — ANRF Act, 2023 (Act 25 of 2023)
**Front:** What is the legal basis of ANRF, and when did it commence?
**Definition (learn):** The ANRF Act, 2023 (Act 25 of 2023) created the Anusandhan National Research Foundation as an apex body to seed, grow and promote research (✅ owner 24; owner states verification against the India Code PDF).
**Breakdown:**
- Act 25 of 2023
- Enacted 12 Aug 2023
- In force 5 Feb 2024 (S.O. 474(E))
- Statutory body, not a DST sub-unit
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Enacted 12 Aug 2023; commenced 5 Feb 2024 (✅ owner 24).
**Links:** SNT-5141, SNT-5143, SNT-5158
**Trap:** Enactment and commencement are different dates; use the commencement date for "in force."
**Mains hook:** The structural break from SERB dates to 2024 commencement.
**Back:** *"Act 25, passed Aug 2023, live Feb 2024."*

### Flashcard SNT-5141 — ANRF governance: PM and PSA
**Front:** Who heads ANRF's Governing Board and Executive Council?
**Definition (learn):** The Prime Minister is ex officio President of the Governing Board (s.5); the Principal Scientific Adviser is ex officio Chairperson of the Executive Council (s.7) (✅ owner 24).
**Breakdown:**
- Governing Board: PM as President (s.5)
- Vice-Presidents: Ministers for S&T and Education
- Executive Council: PSA chair (s.7)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** s.5 and s.7 (✅ owner 24).
**Links:** SNT-5140, SNT-5154
**Trap:** The PSA's office is advisory; ANRF's Executive Council chair is a role, not a separate funding body.
**Mains hook:** Separating strategy (Board) from execution (Executive Council) is a governance design point.
**Back:** *"PM heads the Board; PSA chairs the Council."*

### Flashcard SNT-5142 — ANRF financing design: ₹50,000 crore and ₹36,000 crore
**Front:** What is ANRF's announced financing design?
**Definition (learn):** ₹50,000 crore over 2023-28, of which about ₹36,000 crore (nearly 80%) was expected from non-government sources including industry and philanthropy (✅ owner 24; DST statement per owner).
**Breakdown:**
- Total: ₹50,000 crore over five years (2023-28)
- Non-government share: about ₹36,000 crore
- Government share: balance
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ₹50,000 crore; ~₹36,000 crore; 2023-28 (✅ owner 24).
**Links:** SNT-5140, SNT-5160
**Trap:** This is an announced financing design, not an appropriated budget (owner 24).
**Mains hook:** The test is whether private money actually arrives; if not, grant continuity suffers.
**Back:** *"50,000 announced; 36,000 hoped from non-government."*

### Flashcard SNT-5143 — SERB: the legacy grant body
**Front:** What was SERB, and what happened to it under the ANRF Act?
**Definition (learn):** The Science and Engineering Research Board was the statutory competitive-grants body under the SERB Act, 2008; section 27 of the ANRF Act repealed that Act and dissolved SERB, transferring assets, liabilities and staff to ANRF (✅ owner 24).
**Breakdown:**
- SERB Act, 2008
- s.27 ANRF Act: repeal and dissolution
- Functions moved to ANRF
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** SERB Act 2008; s.27 repeal (✅ owner 24).
**Links:** SNT-5140, SNT-5158
**Trap:** SERB references are not wrong for history, but are not current identities (owner 24).
**Mains hook:** Use SERB only to explain the transition.
**Back:** *"SERB 2008, dissolved by s.27."*

### Flashcard SNT-5144 — Grant versus fellowship versus mission-mode project
**Front:** How do a grant, a fellowship and a mission-mode project differ?
**Definition (learn):** A grant funds a specific project; a fellowship funds a person; a mission-mode project funds a directed, solution-oriented outcome across institutions (⚠️ owner 24 analytical distinction).
**Breakdown:**
- Grant: project, milestone-based
- Fellowship: person's stipend or salary
- Mission-mode: defined deliverables, multiple institutions
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-stated distinction (⚠️ owner 24).
**Links:** SNT-5145, SNT-5146
**Trap:** A fellowship is not a grant to a lab.
**Mains hook:** Mission-mode can crowd out curiosity research if unbalanced.
**Back:** *"Project, person, mission."*

### Flashcard SNT-5145 — PMECRG: early-career grant under ANRF
**Front:** What is PMECRG, and what does it provide?
**Definition (learn):** PMECRG is an early-career research grant under ANRF; the ANRF portal states a one-time grant up to ₹60 lakh excluding overheads for three years (✅ owner 24).
**Breakdown:**
- Early-career researchers
- One-time grant up to ₹60 lakh excluding overheads
- Three years
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Up to ₹60 lakh; three years (✅ owner 24, ANRF portal).
**Links:** SNT-5144, SNT-5146, SNT-5165
**Trap:** The amount excludes overheads; do not state it as all-inclusive.
**Mains hook:** Early-career support is a brain-drain tool.
**Back:** *"PMECRG: ₹60 lakh, three years, early career."*

### Flashcard SNT-5146 — MAHA mission-mode programme
**Front:** What is MAHA, and what were its early focus areas?
**Definition (learn):** MAHA is ANRF's mission-mode platform for priority-driven, solution-focused research; EV mobility was an early focus, with later calls for water and drones (✅ owner 24).
**Breakdown:**
- Mission-mode platform
- Early focus: EV mobility (MAHA-EV)
- Later calls: water (ANRF-Jal Shakti MAHA Water), drones
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** MAHA-EV call deadline recorded 25 Nov 2024 (📰 owner 24).
**Links:** SNT-5144, SNT-5165
**Trap:** MAHA is not a standalone ministry; it is an ANRF programme.
**Mains hook:** Mission consortia versus isolated projects.
**Back:** *"MAHA: mission-mode, EV first, then water and drones."*

### Flashcard SNT-5147 — PRIP: Department of Pharmaceuticals, not DST or ANRF
**Front:** Which department runs PRIP?
**Definition (learn):** The PRIP scheme supports pharmaceutical-MedTech research and is administered by the Department of Pharmaceuticals under the Ministry of Chemicals and Fertilizers (✅ owner 24).
**Breakdown:**
- Administered by Department of Pharmaceuticals
- Ministry of Chemicals and Fertilizers
- Not DST, not ANRF
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 24 (✅ owner 24).
**Links:** SNT-5136, SNT-5148
**Trap:** A pharmaceutical R&D scheme is not automatically a DST scheme.
**Mains hook:** Institutional mapping prevents misattribution.
**Back:** *"PRIP: Pharma Department, not DST."*

### Flashcard SNT-5148 — STI Policy 2013: the last clearly adopted policy
**Front:** Which national STI policy is the last clearly adopted reference point?
**Definition (learn):** The Science, Technology and Innovation Policy 2013 is the last clearly adopted national STI policy on DST's policy stack (✅ owner 24).
**Breakdown:**
- STI Policy 2013
- DST policy page reference (✅ owner 24)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2013 (✅ owner 24).
**Links:** SNT-5149
**Trap:** Do not replace STI Policy 2013 with a draft in answers without confirmed adoption.
**Mains hook:** Policy continuity matters for research funding.
**Back:** *"2013 is the last adopted STI policy."*

### Flashcard SNT-5149 — STIP 2020: drafted but not notified
**Front:** Was STIP 2020 adopted as national policy?
**Definition (learn):** STIP 2020 was drafted and consulted upon, but no official notification of adoption was located in the owner's research (⚠️ owner 24).
**Breakdown:**
- Draft and consultation
- No notification located
- Not current policy
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 24 status (⚠️ owner-stated).
**Links:** SNT-5148
**Trap:** Draft consultations are not adopted policy.
**Mains hook:** Use STI Policy 2013 as the adopted reference.
**Back:** *"STIP 2020: drafted, not notified."*

### Flashcard SNT-5150 — DST autonomous institutes
**Front:** Which autonomous institutes does DST administer, as named in the owner?
**Definition (learn):** DST administers a set of autonomous institutes, including IISER-linked bodies, ARIES, IIA, JNCASR, INST, SCTIMST and the S.N. Bose Centre, along with statutory and attached bodies (✅ owner 24).
**Breakdown:**
- IISER-linked bodies
- ARIES, IIA, JNCASR, INST, SCTIMST, S.N. Bose Centre
- Statutory and attached bodies
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 24 list (✅ owner 24).
**Links:** SNT-5137, SNT-5136
**Trap:** Autonomous institutes under DST are not CSIR labs.
**Mains hook:** DST's institutional portfolio is broader than its policy role.
**Back:** *"DST's autonomous institutes: IISER, ARIES, IIA, JNCASR, INST, SCTIMST, SN Bose."*

### Flashcard SNT-5151 — Vigyan Dhara: DST's umbrella scheme
**Front:** What is Vigyan Dhara?
**Definition (learn):** Vigyan Dhara is DST's consolidated umbrella scheme that merges earlier DST schemes (✅ owner 24).
**Breakdown:**
- Umbrella scheme
- Merges earlier schemes
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 24 (✅ owner 24).
**Links:** SNT-5137, SNT-5152
**Trap:** Umbrella scheme is not a body; it is a scheme.
**Mains hook:** Consolidation affects coordination.
**Back:** *"Vigyan Dhara merges DST schemes."*

### Flashcard SNT-5152 — Scientific Social Responsibility (SSR)
**Front:** What does Scientific Social Responsibility encourage?
**Definition (learn):** SSR guidelines encourage publicly funded institutions to share knowledge (✅ owner 24).
**Breakdown:**
- Knowledge sharing
- Publicly funded institutions
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 24 (✅ owner 24).
**Links:** SNT-5151
**Trap:** SSR is guidance, not a legal obligation (⚠️ owner 24 inference).
**Mains hook:** Public-funded knowledge sharing.
**Back:** *"SSR: share what public money funds."*

### Flashcard SNT-5153 — INSA: an academy, not a funder
**Front:** What is INSA?
**Definition (learn):** INSA is a scientific academy, not a funding body (✅ owner 24).
**Breakdown:**
- Academy
- Not a grant-maker
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 24 (✅ owner 24).
**Links:** SNT-5136
**Trap:** An academy does not fund projects in the same way as ANRF.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"INSA honours; it does not fund."*

### Flashcard SNT-5154 — Office of the Principal Scientific Adviser
**Front:** What role does the Office of the Principal Scientific Adviser play?
**Definition (learn):** The PSA office gives advice and coordinates, and the PSA chairs ANRF's Executive Council (✅ owner 24).
**Breakdown:**
- Advice and coordination
- Chairs ANRF Executive Council
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 24 (✅ owner 24).
**Links:** SNT-5141
**Trap:** The PSA office is not a funding agency.
**Mains hook:** Strategy-execution separation.
**Back:** *"PSA advises and chairs."*

### Flashcard SNT-5155 — Constitutional forms of sectoral bodies
**Front:** What is the constitutional form of ISRO, DAE, DBT, DRDO, MeitY, ICMR and ICAR?
**Definition (learn):** ISRO is an organisation under the Department of Space; DAE and DBT are Departments; DRDO is under the Ministry of Defence; MeitY is a Ministry; ICMR is under MoHFW; ICAR is under DARE (✅ owner 24).
**Breakdown:**
- Organisation: ISRO
- Departments: DAE, DBT
- Under ministry: DRDO (MoD), ICMR (MoHFW), ICAR (DARE)
- Ministry: MeitY
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 24 (✅ owner 24).
**Links:** SNT-5136, SNT-5161
**Trap:** Constitutional form matters in institutional questions.
**Mains hook:** Vertical missions need the horizontal layer.
**Back:** *"ISRO organisation; DAE and DBT departments; MeitY ministry."*

### Flashcard SNT-5156 — Trap: DST and CSIR are not interchangeable
**Front:** Are DST and CSIR the same thing?
**Definition (learn):** No. DST is a Department; CSIR is an autonomous society under DSIR (✅ owner 24 trap).
**Breakdown:**
- DST: policy
- CSIR: laboratories
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 24 trap (✅ owner 24).
**Links:** SNT-5137, SNT-5139
**Trap:** Naming both as "science bodies" hides the difference.
**Mains hook:** Institutional identity is a precise question.
**Back:** *"DST: policy. CSIR: labs."*

### Flashcard SNT-5157 — Trap: CSIR does not sit directly under DST
**Front:** Under which department does CSIR sit?
**Definition (learn):** CSIR sits under DSIR, a separate department of the same ministry (✅ owner 24 trap).
**Breakdown:**
- CSIR → DSIR → Ministry
- Diagrams omitting DSIR are wrong
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** DSIR page 30 Dec 2025 (✅ owner 24).
**Links:** SNT-5138
**Trap:** A diagram showing CSIR as DST's peer is a common error.
**Mains hook:** Use DSIR to correct diagram errors.
**Back:** *"CSIR under DSIR, not DST."*

### Flashcard SNT-5158 — Trap: ANRF is not just a renamed SERB
**Front:** Is ANRF just another name for SERB?
**Definition (learn):** No. ANRF is the successor statutory framework with a wider strategic remit; the SERB Act is repealed and SERB dissolved (✅ owner 24 trap).
**Breakdown:**
- SERB: competitive grants, 2008 Act
- ANRF: apex strategic funding, 2023 Act
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** s.27 (✅ owner 24).
**Links:** SNT-5140, SNT-5143
**Trap:** "ANRF is the same as SERB" fails the structural-change test.
**Mains hook:** The transition is about strategy, not renaming.
**Back:** *"ANRF widened what SERB did."*

### Flashcard SNT-5159 — Trap: ANRF is not a DST sub-unit
**Front:** Is ANRF a unit of DST?
**Definition (learn):** No. ANRF is a statutory body with its own Act; DST is its administrative anchor, not its parent office (✅ owner 24 trap).
**Breakdown:**
- Statutory body
- Chaired at board level by the PM
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 24 (✅ owner 24).
**Links:** SNT-5140, SNT-5141
**Trap:** Administrative anchor is not parent office.
**Mains hook:** Statutory independence is a design point.
**Back:** *"ANRF: statute, not sub-office."*

### Flashcard SNT-5160 — Trap: ₹50,000 crore is not a budget allocation
**Front:** Is ANRF's ₹50,000 crore a government budget allocation?
**Definition (learn):** No. About ₹36,000 crore (nearly 80%) was expected from non-government sources; the test is whether that share arrives (✅ owner 24 trap).
**Breakdown:**
- Announced design
- Non-government share
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ₹50,000 crore; ₹36,000 crore (✅ owner 24).
**Links:** SNT-5142
**Trap:** Headline numbers are not appropriations.
**Mains hook:** Crowd-in funding in a low private-R&D system.
**Back:** *"50,000 is design, not budget."*

### Flashcard SNT-5161 — Trap: DBT and DAE are departments, not mission agencies like ISRO
**Front:** Are DBT and DAE "mission agencies" like ISRO?
**Definition (learn):** No. DBT and DAE are Departments; ISRO is an organisation under the Department of Space; MeitY is a Ministry (✅ owner 24 trap).
**Breakdown:**
- Department: DBT, DAE
- Organisation: ISRO
- Ministry: MeitY
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 24 (✅ owner 24).
**Links:** SNT-5155
**Trap:** Constitutional form decides the answer.
**Mains hook:** Institutional questions test constitutional form.
**Back:** *"Departments, organisation, ministry: know which."*

### Flashcard SNT-5162 — Trap: every science mission is not inside DST
**Front:** Does DST control every science mission?
**Definition (learn):** No. Several mission bodies lie in different departments or ministries, though DST, ANRF and CSIR can support the wider ecosystem (✅ owner 24 trap).
**Breakdown:**
- Missions sit in several parents
- Horizontal bodies support
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 24 (✅ owner 24).
**Links:** SNT-5136, SNT-5155
**Trap:** Support is not ownership.
**Mains hook:** Coordination across parents is the real policy problem.
**Back:** *"Support is not ownership."*

### Flashcard SNT-5163 — CSIR laboratory examples
**Front:** Which CSIR laboratories are named for metrology, chemistry, molecular biology and electronics?
**Definition (learn):** NPL (metrology), NCL (chemistry), CCMB (molecular biology) and CEERI (electronics) are examples of discipline-specific CSIR labs (✅ owner 24).
**Breakdown:**
- NPL: metrology
- NCL: chemistry
- CCMB: molecular biology
- CEERI: electronics
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 24 (✅ owner 24).
**Links:** SNT-5139, SNT-5049, SNT-5050, SNT-5114
**Trap:** CEERI is electronics, CECRI is electrochemistry; do not swap them.
**Mains hook:** Lab depth complements grant funding.
**Back:** *"NPL metrology, NCL chemistry, CCMB biology, CEERI electronics."*

### Flashcard SNT-5164 — Trap: ANRF is a funder, CSIR is a lab network
**Front:** If ANRF exists, does CSIR become redundant?
**Definition (learn):** No. A funder and a research-performing network play different roles (✅ owner 24 advanced trap).
**Breakdown:**
- ANRF: funding
- CSIR: performance
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 24 advanced (✅ owner 24).
**Links:** SNT-5139, SNT-5140
**Trap:** Funding does not replace capacity.
**Mains hook:** Capacity and funding are complements.
**Back:** *"Fund does not replace lab."*

### Flashcard SNT-5165 — Current anchors: ANRF operational calls and first initiatives
**Front:** What ANRF milestones does the owner date?
**Definition (learn):** Board meeting chaired by PM on 10 Sep 2024; PMECRG and MAHA-EV announced as first initiatives on 14 Oct 2024; 2026 live calls including ANRF-Jal Shakti MAHA Water (1 Jun 2026) (📰 owner 24).
**Breakdown:**
- 10 Sep 2024: first Governing Board meeting
- 14 Oct 2024: PMECRG and MAHA-EV
- 2026: live competitive calls
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 10 Sep 2024; 14 Oct 2024; 1 Jun 2026 (📰 owner 24; status per owner, verify later).
**Links:** SNT-5145, SNT-5146
**Trap:** Calls and portal pages are activity, not outcomes.
**Mains hook:** Shows ANRF moving from statute to operation.
**Back:** *"Board Sep 2024, PMECRG Oct 2024, calls in 2026."*

---

### GROUP — 25 Computing Fundamentals: Hardware, Software, Networks and Cloud (basic) with Computer Architecture, Distributed Systems and Emerging Computing (advanced)

### Flashcard SNT-5166 — The computing stack: four layers and a governance layer
**Front:** What are the layers of a computer system, from device to governance?
**Definition (learn):** Physical layer (semiconductors, processor, memory, storage, I/O); system layer (firmware, OS, drivers, file system); application layer (programs, databases, AI, IoT); network layer (LAN, Internet protocols, cloud, edge); governance layer (security, privacy, interoperability, access, energy, e-waste, jobs) (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Hardware executes instructions
- System software manages resources
- Applications solve user problems
- Networks connect resources
- Governance judges impact
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 layers (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5169, SNT-5177, SNT-5182, SNT-5219
**Trap:** A computer is not only a device; UPSC tests the distinction between layers.
**Mains hook:** Evaluate computing policy through the full stack, not one layer.
**Back:** *"Physical, system, application, network, governance."*

### Flashcard SNT-5167 — Bit, byte and storage-unit prefixes
**Front:** What are a bit and a byte, and what is the difference between decimal and binary prefixes?
**Definition (learn):** A bit is 0 or 1; a byte is normally 8 bits. Decimal prefixes (kB, MB, GB, TB) use powers of 1,000; binary prefixes (KiB, MiB, GiB, TiB) use powers of 1,024 (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Bit: 0 or 1
- Byte: 8 bits
- Decimal: 1,000
- Binary: 1,024
- Bit rate in bits per second; storage in bytes
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 storage-unit discipline (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5166, SNT-5168
**Trap:** Bit rate is in bits per second, not bytes; mixing them misstates speed.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Bit is 0/1; byte is 8; kB is 1000, KiB is 1024."*

### Flashcard SNT-5168 — Compression, encoding, hashing and encryption: four different jobs
**Front:** How do compression, encoding, hashing and encryption differ?
**Definition (learn):** Compression saves space; encoding changes representation; hashing creates a fixed-size digest; encryption protects confidentiality and is reversed with the key (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Compression: lossless or lossy
- Encoding: format change, not security
- Hash: fixed-size one-way digest
- Encryption: confidentiality with key
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 trap (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5212, SNT-5211
**Trap:** Encoding is not encryption; it does not protect data.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Compress saves, encode converts, hash fingerprints, encrypt hides."*

### Flashcard SNT-5169 — CPU, control unit, ALU, registers and cache
**Front:** What are the components inside a CPU, and what does each do?
**Definition (learn):** The control unit directs execution; the ALU performs arithmetic and logic; registers are the fastest small working storage; cache sits between CPU and RAM (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Control unit: directs
- ALU: arithmetic and logic
- Registers: fastest
- Cache: fast memory near CPU
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 architecture (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5170, SNT-5171
**Trap:** Clock speed alone does not determine performance (owner 25 trap).
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Control directs, ALU computes, registers hold, cache buffers."*

### Flashcard SNT-5170 — Cores, parallelism and the clock-speed trap
**Front:** Does a faster clock or more cores always make a computer faster?
**Definition (learn):** A core is an independent processing unit; multiple cores enable parallel work only where software can use it (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Core: independent unit
- Parallelism depends on software
- Clock speed is one factor
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5169, SNT-5201
**Trap:** More cores do not help a serial workload.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Cores help only parallel work."*

### Flashcard SNT-5171 — RAM versus ROM and firmware storage
**Front:** Why is RAM volatile and firmware storage non-volatile?
**Definition (learn):** RAM is working memory and loses its contents without power; ROM and flash firmware storage keep instructions without power (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- RAM: volatile
- ROM and flash: non-volatile
- ROM is not ordinary working memory
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5173, SNT-5176
**Trap:** CPU is not permanent storage (owner 25 trap); RAM is not permanent.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"RAM forgets; flash remembers."*

### Flashcard SNT-5172 — GPU and accelerators
**Front:** What is a GPU good at, and what is its limit?
**Definition (learn):** A GPU runs many parallel operations, useful for graphics, AI and scientific work; it is not a universal replacement for the CPU (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Many parallel operations
- Graphics, AI, science
- Not universal
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5170, SNT-5201
**Trap:** GPU is not universally faster than CPU (owner 25 trap).
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"GPU: many small jobs at once."*

### Flashcard SNT-5173 — Storage: magnetic, solid-state and optical
**Front:** How do HDD, SSD and optical storage differ?
**Definition (learn):** HDD is magnetic with a mechanical head; SSD and flash have no moving head and are non-volatile; optical uses lasers on CD, DVD and Blu-ray (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Magnetic: HDD, tape
- Solid-state: SSD, flash, memory card
- Optical: CD, DVD, Blu-ray
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 table (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5171
**Trap:** Tape is archival, not a fast working store.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Magnetic spins, solid-state holds, optical reads light."*

### Flashcard SNT-5174 — Sensor, processor and actuator
**Front:** What is the difference between a sensor, a processor and an actuator?
**Definition (learn):** A sensor measures; a processor computes; an actuator changes the physical world (✅ owner 25 trap).
**Breakdown:**
- Sensor: measures
- Processor: computes
- Actuator: acts
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5203
**Trap:** An IoT device can contain all three; the terms are not interchangeable.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Sense, compute, act."*

### Flashcard SNT-5175 — Microprocessor, microcontroller and SoC
**Front:** How do microprocessor, microcontroller and System-on-Chip differ?
**Definition (learn):** Microprocessor: general-purpose CPU relying on external memory; microcontroller: CPU plus memory and peripherals for embedded control; SoC: integrates multiple components on one chip (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Microprocessor: external memory
- Microcontroller: integrated for control
- SoC: integrated system on one chip
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5176
**Trap:** An SoC is not just a bigger CPU; it integrates components.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Micro: CPU. Micro-controller: + memory. SoC: system on chip."*

### Flashcard SNT-5176 — Embedded system and firmware
**Front:** What is an embedded system, and what is firmware?
**Definition (learn):** An embedded system is designed for a specific function inside a larger product; firmware is low-level software in non-volatile memory that controls startup or device behaviour (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Embedded: single purpose
- Firmware: stored in non-volatile memory
- Firmware is software despite its hardware ties
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5171, SNT-5175
**Trap:** Firmware is software, not hardware (owner 25 trap).
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Firmware: software in chips."*

### Flashcard SNT-5177 — System software versus application software and OS functions
**Front:** What do system software and application software do, and what does an operating system manage?
**Definition (learn):** System software runs and manages the computer; application software performs user tasks; the OS allocates CPU, memory, storage and devices and provides security and interfaces (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- System: OS, driver, utility, runtime
- Application: browser, spreadsheet, GIS
- OS: process, memory, file, device management
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5178, SNT-5166
**Trap:** Browser is application software, not an OS.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"System runs the machine; application serves users."*

### Flashcard SNT-5178 — Kernel, device driver, file system and utility
**Front:** What do kernel, driver, file system and utility do?
**Definition (learn):** The kernel is the privileged core of the OS; a driver lets the OS talk to a device; a file system organises files and metadata; a utility handles maintenance such as backup or antivirus (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Kernel: privileged control
- Driver: device link
- File system: naming, permissions, allocation
- Utility: maintenance
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 table (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5177
**Trap:** A driver is not a file system.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Kernel rules, driver talks, file system files."*

### Flashcard SNT-5179 — Compiler, interpreter and assembler
**Front:** How do compiler, interpreter and assembler differ?
**Definition (learn):** A compiler translates source code to machine or executable code; an interpreter translates and executes during runtime; an assembler converts assembly language to machine code (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Compiler: ahead of time
- Interpreter: during runtime
- Assembler: assembly to machine code
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5180
**Trap:** Compiler and interpreter are not identical (owner 25 trap).
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Compile ahead, interpret live, assemble asm."*

### Flashcard SNT-5180 — Process, thread, multitasking and parallel processing
**Front:** What are a process and a thread, and how do multitasking and parallel processing differ?
**Definition (learn):** A process is a running program with allocated resources; a thread is an execution path within a process; multitasking shares processor time; parallel processing works simultaneously on multiple units (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Process: resources
- Thread: path within process
- Multitasking: time sharing
- Parallel: simultaneous
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5179, SNT-5201
**Trap:** Multitasking is not the same as parallel processing.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Process owns; thread runs; multitask shares time."*

### Flashcard SNT-5181 — API and open-source software
**Front:** What is an API, and does open-source mean free and secure?
**Definition (learn):** An API is a defined interface through which software components request functions or exchange data; it is not the same as a user interface. Open-source makes source code available under a licence; it does not mean no cost, no copyright or no security risk (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- API: software interface
- Open source: licence and source access
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5186, SNT-5199
**Trap:** API is not necessarily a public website (owner 25 trap).
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"API: interface. Open source: visible code, not free of risk."*

### Flashcard SNT-5182 — Network scales and client-server versus peer-to-peer
**Front:** What do PAN, LAN, MAN and WAN cover, and how do client-server and peer-to-peer differ?
**Definition (learn):** PAN: personal devices, very short range; LAN: home or campus; MAN: city; WAN: large area, with the Internet interconnecting networks globally. Client-server: clients request services; peer-to-peer: nodes act as both (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- PAN, LAN, MAN, WAN
- Client-server
- Peer-to-peer
- Packet switching: data in packets
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5183, SNT-5185
**Trap:** The Internet is a WAN of WANs; do not treat it as a LAN.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"PAN person, LAN room, MAN city, WAN world."*

### Flashcard SNT-5183 — Network devices: switch, router, modem, access point, firewall, repeater
**Front:** What does each network device do?
**Definition (learn):** Switch connects devices within a LAN and forwards frames; router connects networks and forwards IP packets; modem converts signals; access point gives wireless access; firewall permits or blocks traffic; repeater regenerates signal but does not route (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Switch: frames, LAN
- Router: IP, networks
- Repeater: signal only
- Firewall: rules
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5182, SNT-5187
**Trap:** Router connects networks; switch mainly connects devices within a LAN (owner 25 trap).
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Switch LAN, router networks, repeater signal only."*

### Flashcard SNT-5184 — Bandwidth, throughput, latency, jitter and packet loss
**Front:** How do bandwidth, throughput, latency and jitter differ?
**Definition (learn):** Bandwidth is theoretical or provisioned capacity; throughput is achieved rate; latency is delay; jitter is variation in delay; packet loss is failed delivery (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Bandwidth: capacity
- Throughput: achieved
- Latency: delay
- Jitter: delay variation
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5185
**Trap:** High bandwidth does not guarantee low latency (owner 25 trap).
**Mains hook:** Real-time voice and remote control need low latency.
**Back:** *"Capacity, achieved rate, delay, jitter, loss."*

### Flashcard SNT-5185 — Internet versus World Wide Web; browser versus search engine
**Front:** Are the Internet and the Web the same, and are browsers and search engines the same?
**Definition (learn):** The Internet is the global network of networks; the Web is one service on it, accessed mainly over HTTP(S). A browser renders web content; a search engine indexes and searches it (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Internet: infrastructure
- Web: one service
- Browser: client
- Search engine: indexing service
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5182, SNT-5186
**Trap:** Email and voice calls use the Internet but are not web pages (owner 25 trap).
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Internet is the road; Web is one vehicle."*

### Flashcard SNT-5186 — DNS, URL, HTTP and HTTPS
**Front:** What do DNS, URL, HTTP and HTTPS do?
**Definition (learn):** DNS resolves domain names to IP addresses; a URL identifies a resource and access scheme; HTTP is the web request protocol; HTTPS is HTTP protected using TLS (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- DNS: name to address
- URL: address of resource
- HTTP: web protocol
- HTTPS: TLS-protected
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5185, SNT-5187
**Trap:** HTTPS does not prove that a site's claims are true (owner 25 trap).
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"DNS names, HTTP asks, HTTPS locks transport."*

### Flashcard SNT-5187 — IP address versus MAC address
**Front:** How do IP and MAC addresses differ?
**Definition (learn):** An IP address is a logical address used for routing packets; a MAC address is a link-layer hardware identifier used on a local network (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- IP: logical, routing
- MAC: hardware, local link
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5183, SNT-5189
**Trap:** IP and MAC operate at different layers (owner 25 trap).
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"IP routes; MAC is the card."*

### Flashcard SNT-5188 — TCP versus UDP
**Front:** How do TCP and UDP differ?
**Definition (learn):** TCP is connection-oriented, reliable and ordered; UDP is connectionless with lower overhead and no built-in delivery guarantee (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- TCP: reliable, ordered
- UDP: low overhead
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5186
**Trap:** UDP is not "unreliable" in a banned sense; it has no built-in guarantee.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"TCP guarantees, UDP hurries."*

### Flashcard SNT-5189 — IPv4 and IPv6
**Front:** What is the difference between IPv4 and IPv6?
**Definition (learn):** IPv4 and IPv6 are Internet Protocol versions; IPv6 provides a vastly larger address space (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- IPv4: older address space
- IPv6: vastly larger
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5187, SNT-5196
**Trap:** Larger address space does not by itself make routing faster.
**Mains hook:** Address exhaustion is a transition issue.
**Back:** *"IPv6: much larger address space."*

### Flashcard SNT-5190 — Cookies: stored data, not a virus
**Front:** Is a cookie a program or a virus?
**Definition (learn):** A cookie is a small stored data item that a website asks the browser to store and return; it supports sessions and preferences, but may enable tracking; it is not an executable program (✅ owner 25; ✅ MDN, verified per owner 10 Aug 2026).
**Breakdown:**
- Stored data
- Sessions and preferences
- Tracking identifiers possible
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** MDN guidance (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5186, SNT-5211
**Trap:** A cookie is not inherently a virus; a session ID in a cookie is not the server session.
**Mains hook:** Cookies raise privacy issues, handled under DPDP (Topic 12).
**Back:** *"Cookie: data a site asks you to keep."*

### Flashcard SNT-5191 — LTE versus VoLTE (PYQ 2019 Q75)
**Front:** How do LTE and VoLTE differ? (PYQ: 2019 Prelims GS-I Q75)
**Definition (learn):** LTE supplies packet-based mobile data connectivity; VoLTE carries voice as an IP service over LTE; it is not a separate generation (✅ owner 25 routed evidence; key unavailable locally).
**Breakdown:**
- LTE: data radio standard family
- VoLTE: voice over LTE
- Not competing generations
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2019 Prelims GS-I Q75.
**Links:** SNT-5182
**Trap:** VoLTE does not replace LTE.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"LTE carries data; VoLTE carries voice on it."*

### Flashcard SNT-5192 — Short-range wireless: NFC, RFID, Bluetooth and Wi-Fi (PYQ 2022 Q36)
**Front:** How do NFC, RFID, Bluetooth and Wi-Fi differ in range and role? (PYQ: 2022 Prelims GS-I Q36)
**Definition (learn):** NFC is proximity-scale (centimetres) for tap exchanges; RFID identifies tags via reader, with passive tags needing no own battery; Bluetooth connects personal devices; Wi-Fi provides local network access (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- NFC: centimetres, tap payments
- RFID: tag and reader
- Bluetooth: personal device links
- Wi-Fi: LAN access
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 Prelims GS-I Q36 (✅ owner 25 routed evidence).
**Links:** SNT-5216
**Trap:** Classify by range, power and use.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"NFC touches, RFID tags, Bluetooth pairs, Wi-Fi networks."*

### Flashcard SNT-5193 — Visible Light Communication (PYQ 2020 Q39)
**Front:** What are VLC's strengths and limits? (PYQ: 2020 Prelims GS-I Q39)
**Definition (learn):** VLC uses visible light for data; it avoids radio interference but is limited by illumination, obstruction and line of sight (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Visible light
- Line of sight needed
- Blocked by opaque walls
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2020 Prelims GS-I Q39.
**Links:** SNT-5192
**Trap:** Light cannot pass through opaque walls (owner 25).
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"VLC: light data, line of sight."*

### Flashcard SNT-5194 — Cloud service models: IaaS, PaaS and SaaS (PYQ 2022 Q33)
**Front:** What does the user receive and manage in IaaS, PaaS and SaaS? (PYQ: 2022 Prelims GS-I Q33)
**Definition (learn):** IaaS gives virtual compute, storage and networking, user manages OS and applications; PaaS gives a managed runtime, user manages code and data; SaaS gives a ready application, user manages use and data (✅ owner 25; NIST SP 800-145 per owner).
**Breakdown:**
- IaaS: infrastructure
- PaaS: platform
- SaaS: application
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** NIST definition (✅ owner 25); 2022 Q33 (✅ owner 25 routed).
**Links:** SNT-5195, SNT-5197
**Trap:** SaaS is not a platform; the provider manages infrastructure.
**Mains hook:** Responsibility allocation matters for security.
**Back:** *"IaaS infrastructure, PaaS platform, SaaS software."*

### Flashcard SNT-5195 — Cloud deployment models
**Front:** How do public, private, community and hybrid cloud differ?
**Definition (learn):** Public: shared provider infrastructure; private: dedicated to one organisation; community: shared by organisations with common needs; hybrid: coordinated use of two or more distinct clouds (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Public, private, community, hybrid
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5194, SNT-5196
**Trap:** Hybrid means two or more distinct clouds, not just one cloud.
**Mains hook:** Data-localisation choices drive deployment.
**Back:** *"Public shared, private own, community common, hybrid mixed."*

### Flashcard SNT-5196 — Data centre, virtual machine, container, edge and fog
**Front:** How do data centre, VM, container, edge and fog computing differ?
**Definition (learn):** Data centre: physical facility; VM: software computer with guest OS; container: isolates an app while sharing host kernel; edge: processes near source; fog: distributed layer between edge and cloud (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Data centre: facility
- VM: guest OS
- Container: shared kernel
- Edge: near source
- Fog: intermediate layer
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5195, SNT-5197, SNT-5225
**Trap:** Virtualisation can enable cloud, but a VM alone is not a cloud.
**Mains hook:** Edge reduces latency and dependence.
**Back:** *"Facility, VM, container, edge near, fog between."*
