### Flashcard SNT-5197 — Trap: cloud is a service model, not "someone else's computer"
**Front:** Why is "cloud is someone else's computer" wrong?
**Definition (learn):** Cloud is an on-demand service model over shared configurable resources (NIST definition); a data centre is a facility, not a model (✅ owner 25 trap).
**Breakdown:**
- Service-delivery model
- On-demand, rapid provisioning
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** NIST (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5194, SNT-5196
**Trap:** Cloud is not automatically resilient.
**Mains hook:** Cloud concentration and jurisdiction are policy issues.
**Back:** *"Cloud: a model, not a machine."*

### Flashcard SNT-5198 — Structured, semi-structured and unstructured data; DBMS, relational, NoSQL, warehouse and lake
**Front:** What are the data types and database categories?
**Definition (learn):** Structured data follows a schema; semi-structured has tags but no rigid table; unstructured has no fixed schema. A DBMS stores, queries and recovers data; relational databases use tables; NoSQL covers non-relational models; a data warehouse is curated for analytics; a data lake stores raw data (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Structured, semi-structured, unstructured
- DBMS, relational, NoSQL
- Warehouse curated; lake raw
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5199, SNT-5200
**Trap:** A data lake is not a curated warehouse.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Schema, tags, none; warehouse curated, lake raw."*

### Flashcard SNT-5199 — Big data: volume, velocity, variety, veracity and value
**Front:** What characterises big data?
**Definition (learn):** Big data is commonly characterised by high volume, velocity and variety, with veracity and value often added; "big" is about processing, not file size (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Volume, velocity, variety
- Veracity, value
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5198, SNT-5181
**Trap:** Big data is not defined only by file size.
**Mains hook:** Data governance matters with volume.
**Back:** *"Three Vs plus veracity and value."*

### Flashcard SNT-5200 — Backup versus replication
**Front:** Why is replication not backup?
**Definition (learn):** A backup is a separate recoverable copy; replication maintains copies for availability and performance; replication is not a substitute for backup (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Backup: recovery point
- Replication: availability
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5198, SNT-5223
**Trap:** A corrupt write replicated everywhere is still corrupt.
**Mains hook:** Disaster recovery plans need backup.
**Back:** *"Replication copies errors; backup restores."*

### Flashcard SNT-5201 — HPC, supercomputer and cluster
**Front:** How do HPC, supercomputer and cluster differ?
**Definition (learn):** HPC uses powerful, often parallel systems for intensive workloads; a supercomputer is a high-end system for very large parallel work; a cluster connects multiple computers (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- HPC: intensive science
- Supercomputer: high-end parallel
- Cluster: connected machines
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5202, SNT-5170
**Trap:** A supercomputer is not simply a large storage server.
**Mains hook:** HPC supports climate, drug and materials science.
**Back:** *"HPC does big sums; supercomputer is the top tier."*

### Flashcard SNT-5202 — National Supercomputing Mission
**Front:** Who steers and implements the National Supercomputing Mission, and what was its reported capacity as of March 2025?
**Definition (learn):** NSM is steered jointly by DST and MeitY and implemented by C-DAC and IISc; as of March 2025 it reported 34 supercomputers with about 35 petaflops (✅ owner 25; dated figure per C-DAC as stated in owner).
**Breakdown:**
- Steering: DST and MeitY
- Implementation: C-DAC and IISc
- Network: National Knowledge Network
- Applications: climate, drugs, disaster, materials, astronomy, fluid dynamics, computational chemistry
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 34 supercomputers; 35 petaflops; as of March 2025 (✅ owner 25, dated).
**Links:** SNT-5201, SNT-5170
**Trap:** Treat the figure as a dated count, not a permanent total.
**Mains hook:** HPC sovereignty depends on the full capability stack (advanced owner).
**Back:** *"NSM: DST and MeitY steer; C-DAC and IISc build."*

### Flashcard SNT-5203 — Internet of Things pipeline
**Front:** What is the minimum pipeline of an IoT system?
**Definition (learn):** An IoT system connects identifiable devices that sense, process, communicate and sometimes act: sensor → local processing and connectivity → network or cloud → analytics → alert or actuator (✅ owner 25; PYQ 2018 Q66).
**Breakdown:**
- Sense
- Process
- Connect
- Analyse
- Act
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018 Prelims GS-I Q66 (✅ owner 25 routed).
**Links:** SNT-5174, SNT-5204
**Trap:** Connectivity alone is not IoT; an ordinary web page is not necessarily IoT.
**Mains hook:** IoT expands attack surface and surveillance risk.
**Back:** *"Sense, process, connect, analyse, act."*

### Flashcard SNT-5204 — Wearable technology (PYQ 2019 Q95)
**Front:** What can a wearable do, and what is it not? (PYQ: 2019 Prelims GS-I Q95)
**Definition (learn):** Wearables combine sensors, processing, communication and a body-worn form; they may track motion, location or physiological signals; they are not necessarily medical-grade diagnostics (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Sensors, processing, communication
- Consumer monitoring versus clinical diagnosis
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2019 Prelims GS-I Q95.
**Links:** SNT-5203
**Trap:** A fitness band is not a clinically validated diagnostic.
**Mains hook:** Health data privacy under DPDP.
**Back:** *"Wearable senses; clinical proof is separate."*

### Flashcard SNT-5205 — AR, VR, MR and metaverse (PYQ 2019 Q91, 2024)
**Front:** How do AR, VR, MR and metaverse differ?
**Definition (learn):** AR overlays digital information on the physical view; VR immerses the user in a computer-generated environment; MR lets digital and physical elements interact spatially; the metaverse is a persistent, interoperable shared virtual-world concept (✅ owner 25; 2019 Q91, 2024 metaverse PYQ).
**Breakdown:**
- AR: overlay
- VR: immersion
- MR: interaction
- Metaverse: shared persistent world
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2019 Q91; 2024 metaverse PYQ (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5206
**Trap:** AR overlays; VR substitutes. Metaverse is not a single game or headset.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"AR overlays; VR immerses; metaverse is shared."*

### Flashcard SNT-5206 — Blockchain and distributed ledgers (PYQ 2018, 2020 Q40, 2026 Q86)
**Front:** What is a blockchain, and what does it guarantee?
**Definition (learn):** A distributed ledger is replicated across nodes; a blockchain is one ledger design in which records are grouped and cryptographically linked; consensus is how participants agree on valid state (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Distributed replication
- Cryptographic linking
- Consensus
- Public or private
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018 Q64; 2020 Q40; 2026 Q86 (provisional key, answer not recorded) (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5207, SNT-5217, SNT-5224
**Trap:** Blockchain does not guarantee truthful input; not every blockchain is public or cryptocurrency.
**Mains hook:** Shared audit trails versus false claims of immutability.
**Back:** *"Linked, replicated, agreed; not necessarily true input."*

### Flashcard SNT-5207 — Smart contract and NFT (PYQ 2022 Q69)
**Front:** What is a smart contract, and what does an NFT not guarantee? (PYQ: 2022 Prelims GS-I Q69)
**Definition (learn):** A smart contract is code executing defined rules on a distributed platform; it is not automatically a legal contract. An NFT is a unique token or ledger record; it does not automatically transfer copyright (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Smart contract: code
- NFT: unique record
- Copyright not transferred automatically
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 Q69 (✅ owner 25 routed).
**Links:** SNT-5206
**Trap:** NFT ownership is not copyright ownership.
**Mains hook:** Legal status of digital assets.
**Back:** *"Smart contract is code; NFT is a token, not a deed."*

### Flashcard SNT-5208 — Web 1.0, Web 2.0 and Web3 (PYQ 2022 Q32)
**Front:** How do Web 1.0, Web 2.0 and Web3 differ? (PYQ: 2022 Prelims GS-I Q32)
**Definition (learn):** Web 1.0 is largely read-only static publishing; Web 2.0 is interactive, user-generated and platform-controlled; Web3 is a contested umbrella for decentralised or user-controlled designs (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Web 1.0: read
- Web 2.0: read-write, platforms
- Web3: decentralised, contested
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 Q32 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5206
**Trap:** Web3 is not the semantic Web; claims of full user control are not automatic facts.
**Mains hook:** Decentralisation is a design aspiration, not a guarantee.
**Back:** *"Web 1 reads, 2 posts, 3 claims decentralisation."*

### Flashcard SNT-5209 — AI, machine learning and deep learning (PYQ 2020 Q38, 2025)
**Front:** How are AI, ML and deep learning nested?
**Definition (learn):** AI is the broad system capability; machine learning is a subset of AI; deep learning is a subset of ML (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- AI: broad
- ML: subset
- Deep learning: subset of ML
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2020 Q38; 2025 deep-learning item (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5210, SNT-5219
**Trap:** AI does not equal human-level general intelligence (owner 25 2020 PYQ note).
**Mains hook:** Judge AI by pattern recognition, prediction, optimisation or automated perception.
**Back:** *"AI > ML > deep learning."*

### Flashcard SNT-5210 — Qubit and quantum computing (PYQ 2022 Q35)
**Front:** What is a qubit, and is quantum computing universally faster? (PYQ: 2022 Prelims GS-I Q35)
**Definition (learn):** A qubit is a quantum information unit described by amplitudes and measured as classical outcomes; quantum computers help for selected problem classes, not universally (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Qubit: quantum unit
- Quantum: selected problems
- Not a replacement for ordinary computers
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 Q35 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5209
**Trap:** A qubit is not merely a smaller or faster classical bit.
**Mains hook:** Quantum hardware claims need validation.
**Back:** *"Qubit: amplitudes, not just a faster bit."*

### Flashcard SNT-5211 — Confidentiality, integrity, availability, authentication and authorisation
**Front:** What do confidentiality, integrity, availability, authentication and authorisation mean?
**Definition (learn):** Confidentiality prevents unauthorised disclosure; integrity prevents unauthorised alteration; availability keeps systems accessible; authentication verifies identity; authorisation decides what an identity may do (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- CIA triad
- Authentication vs authorisation
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5212, SNT-5214
**Trap:** Authentication and authorisation are different steps.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Confidential, intact, available; who you are, what you may do."*

### Flashcard SNT-5212 — Symmetric versus asymmetric cryptography
**Front:** How do symmetric and asymmetric encryption differ?
**Definition (learn):** Symmetric uses the same secret key for encryption and decryption; asymmetric uses a public and private key pair (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Symmetric: one shared key
- Asymmetric: key pair
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5168, SNT-5213
**Trap:** Asymmetric is not automatically better; both serve different steps.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"One key versus a pair."*

### Flashcard SNT-5213 — Digital signature versus encryption (PYQ 2019 Q94)
**Front:** Does a digital signature hide the message? (PYQ: 2019 Prelims GS-I Q94)
**Definition (learn):** A digital signature is a private-key operation verified with the public key; it supports authenticity, integrity and non-repudiation; it does not hide content (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Signs with private key
- Verifies with public key
- Not confidentiality
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2019 Q94 (✅ owner 25 routed).
**Links:** SNT-5212, SNT-5214
**Trap:** Encryption provides confidentiality; a digital signature proves origin and integrity.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Signature proves who and what; encryption hides."*

### Flashcard SNT-5214 — Digital certificate and multi-factor authentication
**Front:** What does a digital certificate do, and what is MFA?
**Definition (learn):** A digital certificate binds a public key to an identity through a certificate authority; MFA uses factors from more than one category: knowledge, possession or inherence (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Certificate: key to identity
- MFA: multiple categories
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5211, SNT-5213
**Trap:** Two passwords are both knowledge factors, not MFA.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Certificate binds key; MFA uses three kinds of proof."*

### Flashcard SNT-5215 — Malware, phishing and patches
**Front:** What are malware, phishing and a patch?
**Definition (learn):** Malware is malicious software including viruses, worms, trojans and ransomware; phishing is deceptive attempt to obtain credentials; a patch corrects defects or vulnerabilities (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Malware types
- Phishing: deception
- Patch: fix
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5211
**Trap:** Malware is a category; a worm is not the same as a trojan.
**Mains hook:** Cyber resilience needs patch management.
**Back:** *"Malware harms, phishing tricks, patches fix."*

### Flashcard SNT-5216 — Kavach and RFID (PYQ 2025)
**Front:** What does RFID contribute in Kavach? (PYQ: 2025 Prelims GS-I)
**Definition (learn):** RFID tags enable radio-based identification or location references within a wider train-protection system; an RFID component does not describe the full safety architecture (✅ owner 25 routed evidence).
**Breakdown:**
- RFID: identification
- Kavach: wider system
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 Prelims GS-I (✅ owner 25 routed).
**Links:** SNT-5192
**Trap:** Do not treat a component as the whole system.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"RFID is a part of Kavach."*

### Flashcard SNT-5217 — Trap: blockchain immutability is practical, not magical
**Front:** Does blockchain make all records true and unchangeable?
**Definition (learn):** Changing old records is difficult because linked records and distributed agreement must be overcome; erroneous inputs can still be recorded (✅ owner 25; 2020 PYQ note).
**Breakdown:**
- Practical immutability
- Input can be false
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5206, SNT-5224
**Trap:** "Records can never be wrong" is false.
**Mains hook:** Oracle problem (advanced owner).
**Back:** *"Hard to change, not guaranteed true."*

### Flashcard SNT-5218 — Aadhaar Open APIs and biometric authentication (PYQ 2018 Q17)
**Front:** What do Aadhaar Open APIs enable? (PYQ: 2018 Prelims GS-I Q17)
**Definition (learn):** Open APIs allow electronic integration of services with Aadhaar authentication, including biometric authentication (✅ owner 25 routed evidence; digital-governance specialist owner).
**Breakdown:**
- APIs enable integration
- Biometric authentication
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018 Prelims GS-I Q17 (✅ owner 25 routed).
**Links:** SNT-5181, SNT-5211
**Trap:** An API is not a website.
**Mains hook:** Authentication design affects privacy (Topic 08 and 12).
**Back:** *"APIs plug services into identity."*

### Flashcard SNT-5219 — Evaluate computing technology: mechanism to verdict
**Front:** What is the evaluation sequence for a computing technology?
**Definition (learn):** Evaluate: mechanism → application → institution or standard → benefit → technical or social risk → realistic safeguard (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- Mechanism
- Application
- Institution or standard
- Benefit
- Risk
- Safeguard
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5166, SNT-5209
**Trap:** Naming a technology without its risk is incomplete.
**Mains hook:** Use this sequence in 10 and 15 mark answers.
**Back:** *"Mechanism, application, standard, benefit, risk, safeguard."*

### Flashcard SNT-5220 — Computing answer spine: 10 and 15 marks
**Front:** What is the answer spine for a 15-mark computing question?
**Definition (learn):** Stack and data flow → system architecture → application value → resilience, concentration and rights risks → standards, skills and inclusion verdict (✅ owner 25).
**Breakdown:**
- 10 marks: define by layer, contrast, application, one qualification
- 15 marks: five-stage spine
- Comparison in a table
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 core architecture (✅ owner 25).
**Links:** SNT-5219, SNT-5166
**Trap:** Prose lists of digital labels lose marks.
**Mains hook:** Use the stack as the spine.
**Back:** *"Stack, architecture, value, risk, verdict."*

### Flashcard SNT-5221 — Advanced: von Neumann bottleneck and RISC versus CISC
**Front:** What is the von Neumann bottleneck, and how do RISC and CISC differ?
**Definition (learn):** In the stored-program model, instructions and data share memory; the bottleneck is the limited rate of movement between processor and memory. RISC favours simple instructions and pipelines; CISC supports complex instructions (✅ owner 25 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Fetch, decode, operands, execute, store
- Caches and buses reduce but do not abolish the bottleneck
- RISC and CISC are tendencies, not complete rankings
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 advanced (✅ owner 25 advanced).
**Links:** SNT-5169, SNT-5222
**Trap:** Clock rate is cycles per second, not instructions completed.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Memory choke point; RISC simple, CISC rich."*

### Flashcard SNT-5222 — Advanced: memory hierarchy and locality
**Front:** Why do caches work?
**Definition (learn):** The memory hierarchy runs from registers to cache, RAM, SSD and HDD; locality of reference explains caching: temporal (reuse) and spatial (nearby) (✅ owner 25 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Registers, cache, RAM, SSD and HDD
- Temporal and spatial locality
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 advanced (✅ owner 25 advanced).
**Links:** SNT-5221, SNT-5171
**Trap:** Virtual memory does not create free physical RAM.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Fast small to slow large; locality makes caches work."*

### Flashcard SNT-5223 — Advanced: ACID and CAP
**Front:** What do ACID and CAP mean?
**Definition (learn):** ACID: atomicity (all or none), consistency (rules preserved), isolation (no improper interference), durability (committed changes survive). CAP: during a partition a distributed system trades consistency against availability (✅ owner 25 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- ACID: four transaction properties
- CAP: partition trade-off
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 advanced (✅ owner 25 advanced).
**Links:** SNT-5200, SNT-5224
**Trap:** CAP is not "pick two at all times."
**Mains hook:** Replication improves availability but adds conflict.
**Back:** *"ACID for transactions; CAP only under partition."*

### Flashcard SNT-5224 — Advanced: consensus families and the oracle problem
**Front:** How do proof of work, proof of stake and permissioned consensus differ, and what is the oracle problem?
**Definition (learn):** Proof of work uses computational work and is energy intensive; proof of stake links influence to committed stake; permissioned consensus uses known validators. The oracle problem: a ledger records what it receives but cannot verify that an external fact was true (✅ owner 25 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- PoW: work
- PoS: stake
- Permissioned: known validators
- Oracle: external input
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 advanced (✅ owner 25 advanced).
**Links:** SNT-5206, SNT-5217
**Trap:** Immutable record is not a true record.
**Mains hook:** Energy and governance trade-offs in consensus.
**Back:** *"Work, stake, known validators; oracle brings the outside truth."*

### Flashcard SNT-5225 — Advanced: VM, container and serverless trade-offs
**Front:** What trade-offs separate VMs, containers and serverless?
**Definition (learn):** VM: strong isolation with greater overhead; container: lightweight but weaker boundary, sharing host kernel; serverless: provider runs code on demand with lock-in and cold-start issues (✅ owner 25 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- VM: strong isolation
- Container: lightweight
- Serverless: convenience
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 advanced (✅ owner 25 advanced).
**Links:** SNT-5196
**Trap:** Elasticity does not mean infinite capacity or lower cost.
**Mains hook:** Vendor lock-in is a cloud concentration issue.
**Back:** *"VM isolates hard; container shares kernel; serverless bills by call."*

### Flashcard SNT-5226 — Advanced: emerging paradigms and federated learning
**Front:** What are the principal emerging computing paradigms and their limits?
**Definition (learn):** Quantum (noise, error correction), neuromorphic (immature standards), photonic (integration), DNA storage (cost, latency), confidential computing (hardware trust and side channels), federated learning (trains without centralising raw data; updates can still leak information) (✅ owner 25 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Quantum, neuromorphic, photonic
- DNA storage
- Confidential computing
- Federated learning
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 advanced (✅ owner 25 advanced).
**Links:** SNT-5210, SNT-5209
**Trap:** None of these is an immediate universal replacement for classical computing.
**Mains hook:** Federated learning addresses data-privacy trade-offs.
**Back:** *"Six paradigms, each with a catch."*

### Flashcard SNT-5227 — Advanced: availability, RPO and RTO
**Front:** What do RPO and RTO mean, and why is high availability not zero failure?
**Definition (learn):** RPO is the acceptable data-loss interval; RTO is the acceptable restoration time; high availability minimises downtime but does not mean zero failure (✅ owner 25 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- RPO: data loss
- RTO: restoration time
- Fault tolerance: continues through specified failures
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 25 advanced (✅ owner 25 advanced).
**Links:** SNT-5200, SNT-5223
**Trap:** A system can be available but leak data.
**Mains hook:** Resilience is not the same as security or privacy.
**Back:** *"RPO data, RTO time, HA is not zero failure."*

---

### GROUP — 26 Scientific Discoveries, Nobel Prizes and Scientists (basic + advanced)

### Flashcard SNT-5228 — Visual identification of a laureate: four clues
**Front:** Which four clues identify a laureate in a Prelims question?
**Definition (learn):** Biographical clue (birthplace, nationality), institutional clue (university or lab at award), scientific clue (field, discovery, technology) and prize-year clue; match all four to identify the laureate, then link back to the science (✅ owner 26).
**Breakdown:**
- Biographical: birthplace and nationality (only if officially stated)
- Institutional: affiliation at award time
- Scientific: discovery and field
- Prize year: official category and year
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 26 method (✅ owner 26).
**Links:** SNT-5229, SNT-5231, SNT-5233
**Trap:** A name list without the science fails when the name is stale.
**Mains hook:** Use laureates as entry points to durable mechanisms.
**Back:** *"Birth, base, bench, year."*

### Flashcard SNT-5229 — The six Nobel categories and their awarding bodies
**Front:** What are the six Nobel prize categories, and which is not one of Alfred Nobel's original five?
**Definition (learn):** Physics, Chemistry, Physiology or Medicine, Literature, Peace, and Economic Sciences (Sveriges Riksbank Prize in Economic Sciences in Memory of Alfred Nobel); the economics prize was established later and is not one of the original five (✅ owner 26).
**Breakdown:**
- Physics: matter, energy, quantum, astronomy, electronics
- Chemistry: molecules, materials, catalysis, biochemistry
- Physiology or Medicine: biological mechanism and therapy
- Literature, Peace
- Economic Sciences: established later
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 26 table (✅ owner 26).
**Links:** SNT-5230, SNT-5236
**Trap:** Do not infer a category from the word "Nobel" or from the laureate's employer.
**Mains hook:** Category decides what was recognised.
**Back:** *"Six categories; economics is the late addition."*

### Flashcard SNT-5230 — Minimum data card for each laureate
**Front:** What should every laureate data card record?
**Definition (learn):** Prize category and year; official spelling; official motivation; two-line mechanism; birth clue only if official; award-time affiliation; India link only if direct; status date (✅ owner 26).
**Breakdown:**
- Prize and year
- Name spelling
- Motivation
- Mechanism
- Birth clue
- Award affiliation
- India link
- Verification date
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 26 table (✅ owner 26).
**Links:** SNT-5228, SNT-5238
**Trap:** Affiliation changes; record it at award time, not a later employer.
**Mains hook:** Source discipline is part of the answer.
**Back:** *"Prize, name, motive, mechanism, affiliation, date."*

### Flashcard SNT-5231 — Trap: birthplace, citizenship and affiliation differ
**Front:** Do birthplace, citizenship and award-time affiliation mean the same thing?
**Definition (learn):** No. They are different facts; a laureate may work in a country other than the country of birth (✅ owner 26).
**Breakdown:**
- Birthplace: where born
- Citizenship: legal status
- Affiliation: institution at award
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 26 elimination rule (✅ owner 26).
**Links:** SNT-5238, SNT-5234
**Trap:** Options mixing these facts are the usual distractor.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Born, citizen, employed: three facts."*

### Flashcard SNT-5232 — Trap: shared prizes and co-laureates
**Front:** What happens when a prize is shared?
**Definition (learn):** A prize can be shared, and one option may name a co-laureate whose biography does not match the question (✅ owner 26).
**Breakdown:**
- Shared prize: several laureates
- Each share is a separate person
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 26 (✅ owner 26).
**Links:** SNT-5238, SNT-5239, SNT-5240
**Trap:** Matching the prize does not confirm every biography in the option.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Shared prize, separate biographies."*

### Flashcard SNT-5233 — Trap: match the year before the discovery
**Front:** Why match the prize year before matching the discovery?
**Definition (learn):** Year plus citation plus biography identifies the laureate; a remembered discovery alone can mislead (✅ owner 26).
**Breakdown:**
- Year first
- Official motivation next
- Biography last
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 26 rule (✅ owner 26).
**Links:** SNT-5228, SNT-5230
**Trap:** "Every famous scientist in the field won that year" is false.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Year, motive, then name."*

### Flashcard SNT-5234 — Trap: university affiliation does not prove nationality
**Front:** Does a university affiliation prove a laureate's nationality?
**Definition (learn):** No. It proves only the stated institutional association (✅ owner 26).
**Breakdown:**
- Affiliation: institution
- Nationality: separate fact
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 26 (✅ owner 26).
**Links:** SNT-5231
**Trap:** Employer is not identity.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Affiliation is not passport."*

### Flashcard SNT-5235 — Discovery versus invention; citation versus later application
**Front:** How do discovery and invention differ, and why do later applications not enter the citation?
**Definition (learn):** A discovery establishes a phenomenon; an invention creates a device or process; prize citations may recognise either; later applications are consequences and must not be rewritten into the citation (✅ owner 26 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Discovery: phenomenon
- Invention: device or process
- Citation: awarded work
- Application: consequence
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 26 advanced (✅ owner 26 advanced).
**Links:** SNT-5241, SNT-5246
**Trap:** A later device is not part of the prize citation.
**Mains hook:** Separate recognition from deployment.
**Back:** *"Phenomenon, device; citation, consequence."*

### Flashcard SNT-5236 — Individual prize versus organisational prize
**Front:** Can a Nobel be awarded to an organisation?
**Definition (learn):** Peace prizes can recognise organisations; science prizes are awarded to individuals under the applicable rules (✅ owner 26 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Peace: person or organisation
- Science: individuals
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 26 advanced (✅ owner 26 advanced).
**Links:** SNT-5229
**Trap:** Institution is not automatically a co-recipient.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Peace may honour bodies; science honours people."*

### Flashcard SNT-5237 — Discipline-to-topic routing
**Front:** Which owner should you revise for a quantum, protein or gene discovery clue?
**Definition (learn):** Quantum circuit, tunnelling or superconductivity: Topic 21 physics and Topic 10 quantum; protein structure or catalysis: Topic 22 and biotech; gene regulation or immunity: Topic 23 and Topics 13-15; AI foundations: Topic 09; climate or materials: Topics 18, 20-22 (✅ owner 26).
**Breakdown:**
- Quantum: 21 and 10
- Protein: 22
- Gene and immunity: 23, 13-15
- AI: 09
- Materials and climate: 18, 20-22
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 26 routing table (✅ owner 26).
**Links:** SNT-5241, SNT-5243, SNT-5244
**Trap:** A discovery's science lives in its discipline, not in the prize page.
**Mains hook:** Link biography to mechanism and then to policy.
**Back:** *"Quantum to 21 and 10; protein to 22; gene to 23."*

### Flashcard SNT-5238 — John Clarke: Nobel Prize in Physics 2025
**Front:** Who is John Clarke, and what is his 2025 Nobel link?
**Definition (learn):** John Clarke was born in Cambridge, United Kingdom, in 1942; at award time he was at the University of California, Berkeley; he shared the 2025 Physics prize with Michel H. Devoret and John M. Martinis (✅ owner 26).
**Breakdown:**
- Born: Cambridge, UK, 1942
- Affiliation at award: UC Berkeley, USA
- Shared with Devoret and Martinis
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Born 1942; 2025 Nobel Physics (✅ owner 26; 2025 Physics facts page per owner; NobelPrize.org unreachable during build).
**Links:** SNT-5239, SNT-5240, SNT-5241, SNT-5231
**Trap:** Birthplace is UK; affiliation is USA; do not swap them.
**Mains hook:** Exam link: superconductivity, Josephson-junction circuits and quantum hardware.
**Back:** *"Clarke: Cambridge-born, Berkeley at award."*

### Flashcard SNT-5239 — Michel H. Devoret: 2025 Nobel co-laureate
**Front:** Who is Michel H. Devoret?
**Definition (learn):** Devoret shared the 2025 Nobel Prize in Physics with John Clarke and John M. Martinis (✅ owner 26). His award-time affiliation is not stated in the owner (⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- Co-laureate, 2025 Physics
- Superconducting circuits
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 Nobel Physics (✅ owner 26).
**Links:** SNT-5238, SNT-5240, SNT-5232
**Trap:** A co-laureate's biography may not match the option text.
**Mains hook:** Shared prize shows team science.
**Back:** *"Devoret: Clarke's co-laureate."*

### Flashcard SNT-5240 — John M. Martinis: 2025 Nobel co-laureate
**Front:** Who is John M. Martinis?
**Definition (learn):** Martinis shared the 2025 Nobel Prize in Physics with John Clarke and Michel H. Devoret (✅ owner 26). His award-time affiliation is not stated in the owner (⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- Co-laureate, 2025 Physics
- Superconducting qubit work
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 Nobel Physics (✅ owner 26).
**Links:** SNT-5238, SNT-5239
**Trap:** Do not assign Martinis's name to the single-laureate option.
**Mains hook:** Team-based physics.
**Back:** *"Martinis: third in the 2025 trio."*

### Flashcard SNT-5241 — 2025 Physics citation: macroscopic quantum tunnelling and energy quantisation
**Front:** What did the 2025 Physics Nobel recognise?
**Definition (learn):** Discovery of macroscopic quantum mechanical tunnelling and energy quantisation in an electric circuit; their superconducting circuit showed quantum behaviour at macroscopic scale (✅ owner 26).
**Breakdown:**
- Macroscopic quantum tunnelling
- Energy quantisation in an electric circuit
- Superconducting circuit
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 Nobel Physics (✅ owner 26).
**Links:** SNT-5238, SNT-5242, SNT-5237
**Trap:** Quantum behaviour at macroscale is not a commercial quantum computer.
**Mains hook:** Recognition of physics does not prove deployment.
**Back:** *"Quantum tunnelling in a circuit you can hold."*

### Flashcard SNT-5242 — Superconducting circuits, Josephson junctions and qubits (exam link)
**Front:** How does the 2025 Physics work connect to superconductivity and quantum-computing hardware?
**Definition (learn):** The owner links the biography to superconductivity, Josephson-junction circuits, quantum tunnelling and quantum-computing hardware; the advanced owner notes a route to superconducting qubits and precision sensors (✅ owner 26; ⚠️ owner-inferred connection to Josephson junctions).
**Breakdown:**
- Superconductivity
- Josephson junction (⚠️ inferred from owner exam link)
- Qubits and sensors
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 26 exam link (✅ owner 26; advanced owner, Section 1).
**Links:** SNT-5241, SNT-5210
**Trap:** Do not stop at the name; connect the science.
**Mains hook:** Quantum hardware needs validation, not announcement (SNT-5210).
**Back:** *"Superconducting circuits lead to qubits."*

### Flashcard SNT-5243 — Nobel Prize in Chemistry 2024: protein design and structure prediction
**Front:** What did the 2024 Nobel Prize in Chemistry recognise?
**Definition (learn):** Computational protein design and protein-structure prediction; announced 9 Oct 2024 (✅ owner 22; 📰 owner 22 date).
**Breakdown:**
- Field: Chemistry
- Citation: protein design and structure prediction
- Laureate names: ⚠️ not verified (David Baker; Demis Hassabis and John Jumper, memory-only)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 09 Oct 2024 (📰 owner 22).
**Links:** SNT-5043, SNT-5244, SNT-5229
**Trap:** Chemistry is not Medicine, though proteins are biology.
**Mains hook:** Biomolecules are a live chemistry frontier.
**Back:** *"2024 Chemistry: protein design and prediction."*

### Flashcard SNT-5244 — Nobel Prize in Physiology or Medicine 2024: microRNA
**Front:** What did the 2024 Nobel Prize in Physiology or Medicine recognise?
**Definition (learn):** The discovery of microRNA and its role in post-transcriptional gene regulation; announced 7 Oct 2024 (✅ owner 23; 📰 owner 23 date). Laureate names: ⚠️ not verified (Victor Ambros and Gary Ruvkun, memory-only).
**Breakdown:**
- Field: Physiology or Medicine
- Citation: microRNA and post-transcriptional regulation
- Points to RNA as a regulator
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 07 Oct 2024 (📰 owner 23).
**Links:** SNT-5130, SNT-5243
**Trap:** RNA is not only an intermediate copy of DNA (advanced owner 23).
**Mains hook:** Regulation, not storage, explains cell types.
**Back:** *"2024 Medicine: microRNA regulates genes."*

### Flashcard SNT-5245 — Economic Sciences: established later, not Nobel's original five
**Front:** Is the Economic Sciences prize one of Alfred Nobel's original five?
**Definition (learn):** No. The Sveriges Riksbank Prize in Economic Sciences in Memory of Alfred Nobel was established later (✅ owner 26).
**Breakdown:**
- Riksbank prize
- Later establishment
- Not an original Nobel category
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 26 (✅ owner 26).
**Links:** SNT-5229
**Trap:** It carries the Nobel name but is not one of the original five.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Economics: later, Riksbank-funded."*

### Flashcard SNT-5246 — Maturity ladder: finding to scale
**Front:** What is the maturity ladder for a discovery?
**Definition (learn):** Finding → replication or engineering → regulated application → scale; a company claim, preprint or demonstration is not settled capability (✅ owner 26 core architecture).
**Breakdown:**
- Finding
- Replication and engineering
- Regulated application
- Scale
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 26 (✅ owner 26).
**Links:** SNT-5235, SNT-5247
**Trap:** A prize proves recognition, not engineering readiness.
**Mains hook:** Use the ladder to test claims.
**Back:** *"Finding, replicate, regulate, scale."*

### Flashcard SNT-5247 — Trap: a prize does not prove mature technology
**Front:** Does a Nobel prove that the technology is mature?
**Definition (learn):** No. It proves recognition of the cited contribution; engineering readiness, cost and regulation are separate tests (✅ owner 26 advanced trap).
**Breakdown:**
- Recognition
- Readiness
- Cost and regulation
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 26 advanced (✅ owner 26 advanced).
**Links:** SNT-5246
**Trap:** Naming a laureate does not validate a product.
**Mains hook:** Separate science from commercial claims.
**Back:** *"Prize is recognition, not readiness."*

### Flashcard SNT-5248 — PYQ: identify the 2025 Nobel recipient (2026 Prelims GS-I Q81)
**Front:** What is the 2026 Prelims question about Nobel recipients? (PYQ: 2026 Prelims GS-I Q81)
**Definition (learn):** Identify a 2025 Nobel Prize recipient through biographical and professional clues (✅ owner 26 routed; the 2026 Set-A key is provisional and no answer letter is recorded here).
**Breakdown:**
- Clues: birthplace, affiliation, field
- Year: 2025
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2026 Prelims GS-I Q81 (provisional key) (✅ owner 26).
**Links:** SNT-5228, SNT-5238
**Trap:** Use the official motivation, not popular labels.
**Mains hook:** Not a standalone Mains item.
**Back:** *"Clues to the 2025 laureate."*

### Flashcard SNT-5249 — Trap: Nobel count nationalism
**Front:** Does counting Nobel prizes measure a country's science?
**Definition (learn):** A stronger analysis examines funding, universities, laboratory autonomy, interdisciplinary teams and translation capacity, not Nobel counts (✅ owner 26 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Nobel count is a weak proxy
- Capacity matters more
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 26 advanced (✅ owner 26 advanced).
**Links:** SNT-5152, SNT-5136
**Trap:** Nobel count nationalism is not analysis.
**Mains hook:** Link to research funding architecture.
**Back:** *"Count is not capacity."*

### Flashcard SNT-5250 — Trap: a current-affairs name list is not enough
**Front:** Why is memorising current laureate names insufficient?
**Definition (learn):** The underlying mechanism remains examinable after the name becomes stale (✅ owner 26 elimination rule).
**Breakdown:**
- Names go stale
- Mechanism stays
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 26 (✅ owner 26).
**Links:** SNT-5228, SNT-5241
**Trap:** A list does not explain the science.
**Mains hook:** Link biography to mechanism.
**Back:** *"Names fade; mechanisms stay."*

### Flashcard SNT-5251 — Discovery answer spine: 10 and 15 marks
**Front:** What is the answer spine for a discovery or prize question?
**Definition (learn):** 10 marks: exact prize or discovery fact, two-line mechanism, owner link, status boundary. 15 and 20 marks: problem and method → discoverer and evidence → mechanism → applications and Indian relevance → uncertainty and maturity (✅ owner 26 core architecture).
**Breakdown:**
- Thesis: evidence matching
- Spine: prize, mechanism, owner, boundary
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 26 (✅ owner 26).
**Links:** SNT-5246, SNT-5237
**Trap:** Do not turn a biography into a technology-policy answer.
**Mains hook:** Discovery is evidence, not popularity.
**Back:** *"Prize, mechanism, owner, boundary."*
